## 2026-08-23

### 0216 — `TASK-260822-4p3dcq`: the build_ssh docs surface, and the scope-grammar finding that does not reproduce as filed

- SCOPE: worktree `.temp/TASK-260822-4p3dcq/worktree`, branch `task/TASK-260822-4p3dcq-docs-build-ssh` off `origin/main` 6a9b201, carrying the accepted `96m5pj + 2505vo + 3pkc80 + b0wg3a` chain applied verbatim from `TASK-260822-b0wg3a_final.patch` and staged as the baseline. Delta charged to this task: `docs/build-ssh.md` (new, 494 lines) and one README bullet. No Go code touched.
- CORRECTION TO `b0wg3a` REVIEW FINDING 3 (the derived-default-outruns-its-validator trap): the general lesson holds, but the concrete case does not reproduce for `build_ssh`. The reviewer cited `identifiers.PortableComponent`, which is the `internal/identity` path governing *skill* sources. External build repositories parse through `buildrepo.ParseSource`, and for the SSH transport it restricts the raw repository path to `^[A-Za-z0-9._/-]+$` (Spec §6.3) before Git or SSH starts — exactly the alphabet `scopeSegmentRE` admits. Verified at first hand: `ssh://git.example.com/team+infra/app.git` and `ssh://git.example.com/team@infra/app.git` are both refused at parse time with `repository SSH path must be portable ASCII`, and the scp form is refused by `scpRE`. So the *path* half of the divergence cannot occur on the only transport `build_ssh` ever selects for.
- WHAT DOES REPRODUCE, AND WHY THE PRESCRIBED REMEDY DOES NOT WORK: the divergence is in the **host**. `buildrepo.hostRE` is `^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9.-]*$`; `config.scopeHostRE` requires non-empty dot-separated labels that neither start nor end with a hyphen. `ssh://git.example.com./team/app.git`, `ssh://git..example.com/...` and `ssh://git-.example.com/...` all parse to a valid canonical identity whose derived default scope the CLI refuses — **and whose host is not a valid scope either**, so "widen the suggestion to the host" does not rescue it. No `build_ssh` entry can cover such a repository at all; the covering path is `--build-ssh-agent`/`--build-ssh-identity` or `CURATOR_BUILD_SSH_*`. An HTTPS identity such as `git.example.com/team+infra/app` *does* carry a rejected namespace, but HTTPS repositories never reach the scope suggestion, so it is unreachable from the diagnostic.
- SPEC DRIFT WORTH KNOWING BEFORE ANYONE CITES IT: `profiles/manager.md §11.3` on `curator-spec` **main** still admits only two authentication tails. The third, pinned-agent tail this repository accepted in 6a9b201 lives on the unmerged branch `spec/pinned-agent-authentication-tail` (`38232d3`). CI `SPEC_PIN` is `00b1688`, which also predates it. The conformance corpus does not encode the tail, so no pin or checksum is affected — but a doc that presents three tails as settled protocol would be wrong today. `docs/build-ssh.md` says so explicitly.
- METHOD, HOW THE EXAMPLES WERE MADE NON-FICTIONAL: every transcript in the page came out of the implementation rather than a keyboard. CLI output from a binary built in the worktree against a scratch `CURATOR_CONFIG`; the `build_ssh` JSON block is that scratch file verbatim after four `add` calls; the seven fail-closed parse messages from seven hand-written malformed configs; the prompt transcript, both missing-credential diagnostics and the provenance line from a throwaway `docsample_test.go` in `internal/install` driving `resolveBuildSSH` and `InteractiveBuildSSHResolver` directly, captured and then deleted. Raw capture attached as `TASK-260822-4p3dcq_docsamples.log`.
- ANOMALY, A FRESH WORKTREE FAILS `go vet` FOR A REASON THAT LOOKS LIKE A CODE FAULT: `git worktree add` does not populate submodules, so `internal/ui/ui_test.go` fails with `tuitestkit@v0.0.0-...: replacement directory ./agents/skills/skill-go-testing-tools/tuitestkit does not exist` and `go vet ./...` exits 1 while `go build ./...` exits 0. `git submodule update --init --recursive` inside the worktree fixes it. Worth doing immediately after any `git worktree add` in this repo.
- NOTE ON GATES: this repository has no markdown linter, no link checker and no docs build step — no `make docs` target, no docs job in `.github/workflows/ci.yml`. "Docs lint green" for a prose task therefore means the naming gate, the no-em-dash/no-guillemet prose rule of `docs/implementation-plan.md` section 3, and an explicit relative-link and in-page-anchor resolution pass. All three run clean; 13 relative targets and 10 anchors resolve, 0 misses.


### 0330 — `TASK-260822-b0wg3a` review: ACCEPTED, and the three places a derived default outruns the grammar that validates it

- VERDICT: **accepted** (`RUN-260822-fad8ab`, read-only, not goal-bound) on `.temp/TASK-260822-b0wg3a/worktree`, branch `task/TASK-260822-b0wg3a-install-precheck`, staged-but-uncommitted on `origin/main` 6a9b201. All AC met. Gates re-run at first hand in the worktree: `go build` 0, `go vet` 0, `gofmt` clean, `golangci-lint` v2.12.2 `0 issues.`, `gate-selftest.sh` 75/0, `go test -count=1 -timeout 30m ./...` **exit 0** (41 packages, `cmd/curator` 486.238s), and `-race` on the 36 named precheck/prompt/candidate tests clean. Statement coverage on the new code 78.9–100%; the only sub-90% functions are the two that shell out to a live `ssh-add`.
- PROVENANCE TECHNIQUE WORTH REUSING FOR STACKED TASKS: this branch carries three predecessor patches, so "what is this task actually accountable for" is not readable from `git diff origin/main`. Rebuilt the predecessor baseline independently — `git archive origin/main | tar -x` into a scratch dir, `git init`, apply `TASK-260822-2505vo_final.patch` — then `diff -rq` the whole worktree against it. That reduced a 4485-line patch to the real delta (2 new source files, 3 new test files, 3 edited files, resolver wiring, one `go.mod` promotion) and confirmed all seven files the implementer claimed byte-identical really are. The attached `final.patch` also re-diffs identical to the working tree.
- FINDING, A PROMPTED CREDENTIAL NEVER REACHES THE IN-MEMORY CONFIG: `operatorBuildSSHResolver`'s persist closure calls `config.SetBuildSSH(cfg.Path, …)`, which re-reads and rewrites the *file*; `cfg.BuildSSH` is untouched. `cmdInstallMode` captures the selection once (`cmd/curator/main.go:531-533`) and then loops over targets, so `install --all` re-asks the same question for every target that shares an uncovered repository. Harmless (same scope replaced) but it defeats *across* targets exactly the "one scope covers every sibling" property the prompt enforces *within* one. The implementer's own `TestThePromptPersistsThroughTheOrdinaryConfigWriter` has to `config.Load` a second time to see the write — the test shape is the tell.
- FINDING, A DERIVED DEFAULT CAN OUTRUN THE GRAMMAR THAT VALIDATES IT: `defaultBuildSSHScope` builds a scope from a canonical repository identity, but the two grammars do not agree. `identifiers.PortableComponent` admits any non-control rune except `:` `/` `\` (so `+`, `@`, `~`, non-ASCII); `config.ValidBuildSSHScope` restricts path segments to `^[A-Za-z0-9._-]+$`. For `git.example.com/team+infra/app` the derived default is `git.example.com/team+infra`, which the CLI refuses — so the non-interactive diagnostic emits a "ready-to-run" command that errors when pasted, and interactively the prompt offers a default it then rejects, re-asking with the same rejected suggestion until the operator guesses a host-only scope. Half of this predates the task (2505vo derived the command scope the same way); the prompt half is new. General trap: whenever one validator's output feeds another validator's input, the narrower grammar has to be the one that generates.
- FINDING CONFIRMED FROM THE IMPLEMENTER'S OWN NOTE: an agent-less identity built from a `*.pub` path cannot authenticate — `internal/buildrepo/admission.go:150-168` emits `IdentitiesOnly=yes IdentityAgent=none -i <path>`, and `ValidateOperatorSSHCredentials` proves only that the path is a regular file, never that it is a usable private key. So menu entries 3..N and the `--identity ~/.ssh/x.pub` diagnostic lines are selectable choices that fail at fetch time. The *default* entry (agent pinned to the first key) is the sound one and listing `*.pub` is what the task prescribes, so this is a docs item for `TASK-260822-4p3dcq`, not a precheck defect.
- NIT: `fmt.Sscanf(answer, "%d", &index)` (`internal/install/buildsshprompt.go:186`) accepts trailing garbage — `"1abc"`, `"2 x"`, `"1.5"` all resolve to a selection, contradicting the function's stated intent that an unparsable answer is re-asked. Forgiving rather than dangerous; `strconv.Atoi` would be exact.
- WHAT THE PRECHECK GETS RIGHT, VERIFIED BY READING NOT BY TRUSTING: `resolveBuildSSH` copies `selection.Scopes` before it can grow (`buildssh.go:151-154`), so a prompted answer cannot mutate loaded configuration behind the run; a resolver that returns a scope covering a *different* host still fails closed, because the second pass is the ordinary longest-scope match rather than a trust of what came back; and `externalPipelineRequest` copies `deps.GitTool` per row before setting `SSHCredentials`, so no repository inherits a sibling's selection.
- NO NEW READ-ONLY REGRESSION: `curator status`/`doctor` build plans with `productionExternalDeps(cfg, true)` and no resolver, so an uncovered SSH repository fails those surfaces — but it already did under 2505vo via `SSHPolicyFor`. This only moves the refusal earlier and gives it a better message.
- EVIDENCE: `TASK-260822-b0wg3a_review-verdict-RUN-fad8ab.md` and `TASK-260822-b0wg3a_review-fullsuite-RUN-fad8ab.log` (board outcome resources); local logs `.temp/TASK-260822-b0wg3a/review-fad8ab/`.


### 0140 — `TASK-260822-b0wg3a`: credential discovery lists, it never selects — and the `cmd/curator` suite is not hanging

- DECISION, DISCOVERY IS NOT SELECTION: the install precheck reads two operator-owned sources — the live `SSH_AUTH_SOCK` and `~/.ssh/*.pub` — and turns neither into a credential. `internal/install/buildsshcandidates.go` returns a list; `internal/install/buildsshprompt.go` reaches `persist` only after *both* the credential question and the scope question are answered. An abort, or EOF on stdin, returns `ErrBuildSSHAborted` carrying `build_repository_ssh_credential_missing`, so refusing to choose fails exactly as having no terminal does. Ambient SSH state is never a fallback.
- DECISION, THE DEFAULT MENU ENTRY IS AGENT-PINNED: entry 1 is "agent, pinned to <first key>", not "agent". A bare agent offers every key it holds to the destination; pinning reuses a loaded key while naming the single identity the destination may see. Both shapes remain selectable — the default is the one that leaks least.
- DECISION, THE PROMPT NEEDS A REAL TERMINAL, NOT A CHARACTER DEVICE: `cmd/curator/main.go` gates the resolver on `term.IsTerminal` for *both* stdin and stderr. `< /dev/null` is a character device; an `isatty`-by-mode test would have made a scripted run block on a question nobody can answer instead of failing closed with pasteable commands. A dry run never gets a resolver either — persisting mid-report would make a read-only surface write.
- FINDING, `ssh-add -l` EXIT 1 IS AN ANSWER: an agent holding nothing exits 1. Treating that as unreachable would report "key count unavailable" for a perfectly healthy empty agent. `sshAgentKeyCount` distinguishes exit 1 from every other failure; everything else degrades the *count*, never the candidate.
- FIX: the `plan.messages` → `Result.Messages` hop was duplicated in `internal/install/install.go:539` and `internal/install/global.go:283` with no test — dropping either loop would leave the suite green while the operator saw no dry-run provenance. Extracted as `externalPlan.credentialReport(label)` in `internal/install/buildssh.go`, covered by `TestTheCredentialReportIsLabelledByTheScopeThatProducedIt`.
- ANOMALY RESOLVED, THE SUITE THAT LOOKS DEADLOCKED: `go test ./cmd/curator/` takes ~10 min on this host (`TASK-260822-2505vo_full-suite-final.log` records `588.652s`). At `-timeout 5m` it panics with a stack in `godriver.digestToolchainRecords` → `os.(*File).Stat` and reads as a hang. It is an unfinished run: `internal/godriver` fingerprints the whole GOROOT per toolchain session. Use `-timeout 30m`. Two runs were spent diagnosing this.
- NOTE: `go test ./...` emits package results in package order, so a redirected log stays **empty** until `cmd/curator` finishes. An empty log is not a stalled run either.
- SCOPE: worktree `.temp/TASK-260822-b0wg3a/worktree`, branch `task/TASK-260822-b0wg3a-install-precheck` off `origin/main` 6a9b201. Carries the 96m5pj + 2505vo + 3pkc80 stack; every file shared with the accepted 2505vo patch verified byte-identical.
- STATUS: handed to review. `go build` 0, `go vet` 0, `gofmt` clean, `golangci-lint` `0 issues.`, `gate-selftest.sh` 75 passed / 0 failed.

### 0008 — `TASK-260822-a6jpu9` review: ACCEPTED, and the check that decides whether the vendor answer means anything

- VERDICT: accepted on the gap branch. Every load-bearing claim re-derived by command rather than read from the report. Gates re-run standalone with direct exit codes: `go build` 0, `go vet` 0, `gofmt -l internal cmd` 0 empty, `go test -count=1 ./...` 0 with 31 `ok` / 0 `FAIL`, `golangci-lint run` `0 issues.` Evidence: `TASK-260822-a6jpu9_review-verdict.md`.
- THE CHECK THAT MATTERS, AND THAT THE REPORT LEFT IMPLICIT: "the audit does not read `vendor/`" is only interesting if `vendor/` is *there* when the audit runs. `internal/install/install.go:261-277` builds every `audit.Subject` from `node.Snapshot` — the full repository snapshot, `vendor/` included — so the vendored files are skipped by **scope**, not by absence. Had the audit run over the whitelisted context instead (`internal/whitelist` drops `vendor` outright, `IncludeRoots` is an allowlist), the whole finding would have been a tautology about a directory that was never presented. Verify *what the subject was built from* before believing any "the scanner does not see X" result.
- METHOD: the negative control is what makes the positive result admissible. `TestAuditAdmitsVendoredThirdPartyText` installs clean under `strict`/`fail_on=low`, then moves the identical curl-pipe line into first-party `scripts/`, retags, and the install fails with `network-undeclared`. Without that second half, "install succeeded" is equally consistent with the audit being switched off.
- DECISION: the missed AC half (an executable file below `vendor/` should block, and does not) was *not* sent back as rework. The task description's conditional fires on its second clause — the external-repository audit subject does not exist (`SupportedSchemaVersions` caps at 5; no `go-repository-v1`, no `go list`/`go build` child) — and making a vendor executable block requires widening the detector scope past the literal `scripts/` prefix, which changes install outcomes for every existing skill. That is the follow-up policy decision the task routes to, not a bugfix; forcing it is the forced fit the role contract refuses.
- CARRIED, UNOWNED: the `runtime_roots` audit-coverage gap (sibling entry 0001) is in this logbook and the research note but has **no board item** — `STORY-260822-2jdj9o`'s children are `27bvo4`, `5wfdfx`, `a6jpu9` and nothing else. A finding that lives only in prose is a finding that ships. Orchestrator to open one; a reviewer run should not widen the board unilaterally.
- NOTE: `TestVendorTextIsOutsideTheDetectorScope` is a deliberate change-detector — its `len(findings) != 1` assertion fails the moment anyone widens the scope. Documented in the test comment; the follow-up owner edits it rather than reading the failure as a regression.
- SCOPE: read-only reviewer run `RUN-260822-2559c8`; no worktree file modified. No `commit_ack` supplied.

### 0001 — `TASK-260822-a6jpu9`: the vendor-inert-text question has no audit to answer it, and the detector scope is narrower than anyone assumed

- FINDING, THE ASKED QUESTION: the external-repository static audit does **not** block on non-executable text below `vendor/` — because it does not exist here. `internal/skillspec/types.go:9` caps `SupportedSchemaVersions` at 5, and `grep -rnE "go-repository-v1|build_repository|skill-build|BuildRoot" --include='*.go' internal cmd` returns nothing: no build driver, no `skill-build.json`, no `go list`/`go build` child. The schema 6/7 audit subject of `curator-spec@a2d44eb protocol/core.md:844` is unimplemented. No demotion-to-advisory code was written; there is no blocking behavior to demote.
- FINDING, WIDER AND NOT VENDOR-SPECIFIC: the audit detector scope is the **literal** prefix `scripts/` plus `csk-skill.json` (`internal/audit/audit.go:307`) — *not* the manifest's declared `runtime_roots`. Measured against `detect` directly: a snapshot with `runtime_roots: ["bin"]` and `bin/tool` containing `curl https://evil.example.net/x` yields **0 findings**. Every skill that names its runtime root anything but `scripts` ships with zero audit coverage of its real executable surface, today, at schemas 1-5. Six-line probe reproduces it.
- CONSEQUENCE: this implementation reaches the task's desired vendor outcome (inert vendor text admitted) by accident of scope, not by an authored rule, and misses the other half entirely — an **executable** file below `vendor/` is admitted too. Not exploitable while `vendor/` is not a declared `runtime_root`: `internal/whitelist` keeps it out of installed context and `internal/skillspec/parse.go:546` rejects command paths outside `runtime_roots`.
- DECISION: recorded as a gap rather than fixed. Widening the scope — to `runtime_roots` first, then to the whole snapshot per `protocol/core.md:531` — changes install outcomes for every existing skill and is a policy call, not a bugfix. Spec cover for leaving it: `profiles/manager.md:616`, "Detectors and analysis backends may differ; decisions do not."
- SCOPE: tests only, no product code. `internal/audit/vendor_test.go` (4 tests: detector scope, strict gate blocks first-party only, vendor-only snapshot admitted silently, revocation still blocks a vendored snapshot) and `internal/install/install_test.go::TestAuditAdmitsVendoredThirdPartyText` (end-to-end install under `strict`/`fail_on=low`, plus a negative control moving the identical curl-pipe line into `scripts/`, which fails the install).
- STATUS: `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./...` (31 ok)/`golangci-lint run` (0 issues)/`gofmt -l` all exit 0. Evidence: `TASK-260822-a6jpu9_vendor-inert-text-audit-policy.md` (board outcome resource).

## 2026-08-22

### 2340 — `TASK-260822-27bvo4` rework: a ledger row is only worth what a real `-json` stream proves

- CONTEXT: the symlink-launcher fix (`identity.go` canonicalizing with `physicalPath` instead of `filepath.EvalSymlinks`) was accepted on the merits; review blocked on two CI-wiring gaps. Both closed without touching product code.
- METHOD WORTH COPYING: `make ledger-check` only proves a row's case is *compiled into* the named GOOS build — it never runs anything. Added rows can therefore be green in `ledger-consistency.sh` and still not be enforced. Ran `platform-case-gate.sh` against two real `go test -json` streams instead (darwin locally, windows on `ssh win`), with `CI_EXCLUDED_PKGS` set to every other ledger package and `CI_GATE_GOOS=windows` to drive the darwin host against the Windows stream. All seven new cases observed `ok`, zero skips among them. That is the difference between "the row parses" and "the runner executed the case".
- GOTCHA: running the gate without `CURATOR_CONFORMANCE_ROOT` makes every `root-unset` skip fatal unless `CI_DEFERRED_PKGS` names the package, and one ledger row that tolerates a `root-content` skip then fails with `skipped for the wrong reason` — the class the test *printed* is `root-unset`. Both are artifacts of the local no-root lane, not of the change; do not chase them, and do not report the gate as green because of them.
- DESIGN: `Parent/*` ledger rows are the lever for subtest skips. Their `must_run_on` is ignored by both scripts, but their `skip_allowed_on` decides whether a *subtest* skip is fatal — so `-  windows  host-capability` makes a darwin subtest skip fail the gate (darwin can create both link kinds, so a skip there is a defect) while still tolerating a Windows host without `SeCreateSymbolicLinkPrivilege`.
- REUSED, NOT WIDENED: the unclassified skip reason was fixed by rephrasing the test to `creating Windows symlink requires host support`, an existing `skip-classes.tsv` row, rather than adding one. Third task this week where phrasing a skip onto an existing class beat growing the table.
- CMD TRAP: `ssh win 'go test ... & echo TESTEXIT=%ERRORLEVEL%'` prints the *previous* command's code — `cmd.exe` expands `%VAR%` when it parses the whole line. It reported 0 before the test had run. Let `ssh` propagate the remote exit status instead; the honest re-run was 94.95s, exit 0.

### 2205 — `TASK-260822-2505vo` review: ACCEPTED, and the skip predicate that has to read the *effective* transport

- VERDICT: accepted on `task/TASK-260822-2505vo-per-repo-credentials` off `origin/main` `6a9b201`, 14 files `+1937/-9`. Every AC clause maps to a named test. Reviewer re-ran the gates standalone rather than reading the report: `gofmt -l` empty, `go vet` 0, `golangci-lint` v2.12.2 `0 issues.`, targeted SSH tests ok in `install`/`buildrepo`/`config`, `.github/ci/gate-selftest.sh` `75 passed, 0 failed`, attached full-suite log 41 `ok` / 0 `FAIL`. Evidence: `TASK-260822-2505vo_review.md`.
- DESIGN, WORTH KEEPING: `needsBuildSSH` keys on `effective.Transport`/`effective.IdentityKind`, not the declared spelling. That is the only version that stays correct in both directions — an operator substitution can move a declared-SSH repository onto HTTPS or a local path, *and* move a declared-HTTPS repository onto SSH. `effectiveRepository` produces exactly the transport the acquire closure later hands `AcquireNetwork`, so the skip predicate and the fetch cannot disagree. A predicate written against `declared` would have passed the obvious tests and leaked credentials to a mirror.
- DESIGN: credentials bind to `GitTool` **per repository** inside `externalPipelineRequest` (`tool := deps.GitTool; tool.SSHCredentials = ...`), not per run. A closure spanning two hosts cannot offer either host the other's key. `deps.GitTool` stays credential-free because the copy is a value.
- NOTE, DELIBERATE PRECEDENCE ASYMMETRY: run-wide flags/env beat config scopes for identity and agent, but a scope's `known_hosts` beats a run-wide `--build-ssh-known-hosts` (`BuildSSHSelection.knownHosts` tries the scope's value first). Rationale: a scope selects authentication material, host keys are a fact about the destination. Defensible and tested, but it is the kind of thing an operator discovers the hard way — `TASK-260822-4p3dcq` should say it out loud.
- NOTE: `CURATOR_BUILD_SSH_IDENTITY=~/.ssh/id` is **not** expanded — only config paths are. A shell expands the flag spelling, so the env spelling fails with "SSH identity path must be absolute". Matches the AC as written; document it.
- NOTE, NO PRODUCTION REGRESSION FROM A NEW FAIL-CLOSED GATE: `AcquireNetwork` now refuses an SSH source whose tool carries no selection. Checked before accepting that this breaks nothing live: `productionGitTool()` never sets `SSHWrapper`, and `AcquireNetwork` already refused SSH without the exact manager wrapper. External SSH repositories were unreachable before this change too.
- METHOD: verified the board patch artifact **is** the branch rather than a description of it — `diff <(git apply --stat <worktree diff>) <(git apply --stat *_final.patch)` identical. Also confirmed the branch applies the accepted `TASK-260822-96m5pj` patch verbatim (only `MatchBuildSSH` added). The extra `SetBuildSSH`/`RemoveBuildSSH`/`ValidBuildSSHPath` sitting uncommitted in the primary checkout are *not* in that artifact — they are untracked `TASK-260822-3pkc80` work, so their absence here is correct, not a drop.
- NOTE, SKIP VOCABULARY DONE RIGHT FOR ONCE: the new test helpers introduce four skip reasons; two got new `skip-classes.tsv` rows, and the other two were **phrased** to land on the pre-existing `host-capability  this host cannot create` class instead of widening the table. Worth copying.
- SCOPE: read-only reviewer run `RUN-260822-13d94b`; no file in the worktree modified. No `commit_ack` supplied — the commit-owning mover lands `TASK-260822-2505vo_final.patch`.

### 2137 — `TASK-260822-3fkfmf` cycle-2 review: ACCEPTED, and a vector file three documents cite that exists nowhere

- VERDICT: accepted at `curator-spec` `spec/sw-manager-security` `e5df43d`, four signed commits, base `b92b105`, diff exactly 2 files `+502/-4`. All four cycle-1 blocking findings re-verified fixed by command rather than read from the report. Eight gates re-run standalone, all exit 0. Evidence: `TASK-260822-3fkfmf_review-cycle2-verdict.md`.
- FINDING, NEW AND UNOWNED: `protocol/core.md:425-427` (`spec/sw-core-prose` `78d544d`) names `conformance/v1/vectors/script-host-execution-policy.json` as *the authority* for `script-worker-v1-native-control-inventory-v1`. `git ls-tree -r` over `origin/main`, `spec/sw-core-prose`, `spec/sw-schema` `ebfed81`, and `spec/sw-manager-security` finds **no such path on any branch**. `TASK-260822-c0rxj7` must not merge core.md before `f4qv7w` lands that vector, or the spec ships a normative forward reference to a missing file. Neither of 3fkfmf's two files cites it — both reference only the inventory version string — which is why `validate.py` and `lychee` are both clean and nothing local caught it.
- FINDING: `${PIPESTATUS[0]}` under zsh returns **empty**, so a gate harness written as `cmd 2>&1 | tail -5; echo "exit=${PIPESTATUS[0]}"` prints `exit=` and reads as a pass at a glance. Re-ran every gate under bash with a direct `$?` before trusting any of them. Same zsh trap already recorded for `gh pr merge`; it generalizes to any piped gate.
- DECISION, NORMATIVE LAYERING: `e5df43d` **removes** manager.md's mirror of core.md's new "a per-user resource limit is not a private aggregate domain / MUST NOT back `active-process-count-limit` or `aggregate-memory-limit` with `RLIMIT_NPROC` or `RLIMIT_AS`" rule, on `1f533i`'s request as owner of §4.1.1. Verified this leaves no behavioural gap: the rule survives in core.md, and a manager reading the profile alone still cannot reach `RLIMIT_NPROC` because the inventory cell names the mechanism (`delegated cgroup v2 pids.max`) and the inventory is declared exhaustive. A second copy of a normative MUST is only a place for two documents to drift.
- FINDING: the fix for a delegated-but-underspecified set is *both* axes, not either. core.md 4.1.1 states an **open criterion** for manager-owned environment names and delegates only the enumeration; manager.md had declared its enumeration **closed**. Prefix closure alone (`LD_`, `DYLD_`, `PYTHON`, `NODE_`, `NPM_CONFIG_`) still asserts an unprovable completeness claim over two interpreters × three platforms; minimum-plus-criterion alone leaves per-manager divergence on names anyone could have listed. Taking both makes the enumeration the interop floor and the criterion the backstop, and turns the residual divergence fail-closed and reported instead of fail-open and silent.
- NOTE, CARRIED SEAM: core.md 4.1.1 still says "The **exact** reserved set … is defined by the manager profile" while the profile now supplies a *minimum* and defers the rest back. No input makes the two documents produce different manager behaviour, so it is a one-word core.md fix for `c0rxj7`, not a rework of this task.
- NOTE, CROSS-DOC VERIFICATION THAT PAID OFF: extracting the process graph from all three files and `diff`ing showed byte-identity across `core.md`/`profiles/manager.md`/`SECURITY.md`; the same method over the 8×3 inventory table confirmed the mirror survived core.md moving `41cf556` → `78d544d` mid-review. When one document is mirrored into two others, diff the extracts — do not read them.

### 2130 — `TASK-260822-27bvo4` review: the skip-vocabulary trap fired a second time, and a Windows-only fix that no runner is required to prove

- FINDING, THE SAME TRAP AS THE 2129 ENTRY, DIFFERENT TASK: `internal/godriver/identity_test.go:24` skips with `this account cannot create a file symbolic link`, which matches **nothing** in `.github/ci/skip-classes.tsv`. Matched all three of the task's new reasons against every regex in the table the way `platform-case-gate.sh` TIER 2 does: the junction and hard-link reasons hit `host-capability :: this host cannot create`; this one is unclassified and therefore `FATAL`. `internal/godriver` runs on the darwin and windows runners, so on a Windows host without `SeCreateSymbolicLinkPrivilege` it takes nine subtests down and reddens CI for the wrong reason. The classified phrasing for the identical situation already exists two packages away — `internal/buildcache/protection_windows_test.go:272`, `creating Windows symlink requires host support`.
- NOTE: two independent tasks tripped this within one day (`TASK-260822-2505vo` at 2129, this one). It is not a per-author slip, it is a missing guardrail: nothing greps a new `t.Skip` reason against `skip-classes.tsv`, and no darwin/linux run ever surfaces it. Worth a `no-broad-suppression`-style gate that scans `t.Skip` reason literals statically instead of waiting for the reason to fire on a runner.
- FINDING: on unix `physicalPath` **is** `filepath.EvalSymlinks(filepath.Clean(path))` (`platform_unix.go:16`), so this task's one-line fix is a literal no-op on darwin and linux. Verified by mutation: reverting `identity.go:56` to the pre-fix `EvalSymlinks` leaves all six darwin identity tests **passing**. The behaviour change exists only on Windows, where `physicalPath` is `GetFinalPathNameByHandle`. Corollary for reviewing any `physicalPath`/`EvalSymlinks` change: a green unix suite is not evidence, it is silence.
- FINDING: no `platform-cases.tsv` row names the new identity cases, so no runner is **required** to execute the only tests that can catch a regression of a Windows-only fix. The junction case's own `t.Skip` classifies `host-capability`/`allow`, so a runner that stops producing junctions drops it silently while CI stays green. Sibling row already exists at `platform-cases.tsv:107` for `TestExecutableIdentityRejectsSubstitutionAndMutation`.
- FIX (the task's, confirmed correct): `internal/godriver/identity.go:56` canonicalizes with `physicalPath(absolute)`. Reproduced red-to-green myself on the native Windows host — pre-fix, a manager under a directory junction is refused `worker_identity_invalid: cannot canonicalize the manager executable` before any substitution check runs, with `EvalSymlinks` returning `The system cannot find the path specified`; post-fix both junction cases pass and the full `internal/godriver` suite is exit 0 in 93.781s. Resolution-first does not widen trust: `artifactHasMultipleLinks` on Windows opens the resolved path with `FILE_FLAG_OPEN_REPARSE_POINT` and still rejects reparse points and `NumberOfLinks != 1`.
- FINDING: `os.Executable` reports the **launch path**, not the physical file, on darwin *and* Windows — observed from a started process on both, not inferred. Canonicalizing before the identity checks is load-bearing on every platform this ships to.
- STATUS: `to-dev`. Product fix and test set accepted; the two gate-wiring rows are the rework. Artifact `TASK-260822-27bvo4_review-RUN-1f3d1d.md` plus four verification logs on the board.

### 2129 — `TASK-260822-2505vo`: per-repository SSH credential resolution, and the skip vocabulary a POSIX-only fixture has to enter before CI sees it

- DECISION: the whole run's credentials are resolved in `planExternalBuilds` **before the first repository is reached** (`internal/install/external.go:126`), not lazily per fetch. A closure holding one unselected private repository therefore fails closed naming every unselected repository at once, instead of part way through the network with the others already cloned.
- DECISION: one predicate covers both AC skip cases — `needsBuildSSH` is `effective.IdentityKind == "network-git" && effective.Transport == "ssh"` (`internal/install/buildssh.go:158`). Reading the **effective** state rather than the declared one makes substitution decide the transport in both directions: a substitution redirecting a declared SSH repository onto HTTPS or a local path moves it off SSH, and one redirecting a declared HTTPS repository onto SSH moves it on. `TestSubstitutionMovesARepositoryOffAndOntoTheSSHTransport` pins both directions.
- DECISION: `GitTool.SSHCredentials` is bound **per repository** in `externalPipelineRequest`, never per run, so a closure spanning two hosts cannot offer either host the other's key (Spec §12.2). `AcquireNetwork` independently refuses an SSH source whose tool carries no selection (`internal/buildrepo/admission.go:261`) — the backstop for a caller that forgot to resolve, so no fetch can silently fall back to ambient SSH state.
- DECISION: `~/` expansion is **config-scoped only**, matching the AC wording. Flag/env values are not expanded; `ValidateOperatorSSHCredentials` requires an absolute path, so a `~/…` passed on the command line fails with "path must be absolute" rather than being silently reinterpreted. The shell already owns that expansion for a flag.
- DECISION: an entry recording `agent: true` with no socket resolves to the **live** `SSH_AUTH_SOCK`, and errors when there is none rather than degrading to identity-only. A macOS agent socket is per login session, so a persisted path is the one thing a config cannot keep current. `known_hosts` resolves scope → run-wide → `$HOME/.ssh/known_hosts`; a run-wide `--build-ssh-known-hosts` governs a scoped selection too, because a scope selects authentication material, not who the destination is allowed to be.
- FINDING, THE ONE THAT WOULD HAVE GONE RED IN CI: five `t.Skip` reasons introduced by this task's tests matched **nothing** in `.github/ci/skip-classes.tsv`, and an unmatched reason is `FATAL-unclassified` in `platform-case-gate.sh:233`. `an operator agent socket is a unix-domain rendezvous point` fires unconditionally on the Windows runner in two packages, so the Windows test *and* race lanes would both have failed the gate — while every local gate on darwin stayed green, because the reasons never fire here. Reproduced before fixing with the gate's own `CI_GATE_GOOS=windows` override against a synthetic `go test -json` stream (`.temp/TASK-260822-2505vo/logs/skip-gate-red-01.log`, 4/4 unrecognised), then re-proved at 0/6 after (`skip-gate-green-01.log`).
- METHOD: three of the five were fixed by **rewording onto existing vocabulary** (`this host cannot create …`, an already-declared `host-capability` row) rather than by growing the table; only two genuinely new concepts got rows — `platform-control` for the POSIX unix-domain agent socket, `host-capability` for `no home directory`. `gate-selftest.sh` still passes 75/0 after the edit. **A new `t.Skip` in this repo is a CI-visible API change, not a test detail**: grep the reason against `skip-classes.tsv` when you write it, because no local darwin/linux run will ever tell you.
- ANOMALY, PROCESS: the previous spawn run on this task (`RUN-260822-c60921`) exited **0** having written a complete-looking `TASK-260822-2505vo_results.md` whose verification table still read `RESULT_PLACEHOLDER` for `go test ./...`, next to a **0-byte** `full-test-02.log`. A clean exit code plus a polished artifact is not evidence the gates ran; the placeholder and the empty log were the only things that gave it away. Re-verified everything from scratch rather than inheriting the claim.
- NOTE, INTEGRATION: `TASK-260822-3pkc80` also edits `cmd/curator/main.go`, but in the `cmdConfig`/`cmdConfigBuildSSH*` region, disjoint from this task's `installFlags` / `cmdInstallMode` / `runGlobalInstallMode` / `productionExternalDeps`; a textual merge should be clean. Both it and `TASK-260822-96m5pj` live only as **uncommitted files in the primary checkout** on `handoff/cocoaskills-parity-20260731`, which is 50 commits behind `main` and carries neither `internal/buildrepo` nor `internal/install/external.go`. This task's branch is cut from `origin/main` at `6a9b201` and carries the 96m5pj patch applied verbatim, with one line diverging: the reworded `no home directory` skip.
- NOTE: the env prefix `CURATOR_BUILD_SSH_{IDENTITY,AGENT,KNOWN_HOSTS}` is exactly what `TASK-260822-3pkc80` already committed `buildSSHUsage` to (2048 entry above), so the documented precedence flags > env > scopes is now implemented as documented, and `buildSSHUsage` needs no amendment.
- SCOPE: new `internal/install/buildssh.go`, `internal/buildrepo/credentials.go` (+ tests); modified `internal/install/external.go`, `internal/install/install.go`, `internal/install/global.go`, `internal/buildrepo/admission.go`, `internal/config/config.go`, `cmd/curator/main.go`, `.github/ci/skip-classes.tsv`. The manager's own SSH wrapper is still not materialized or exec'd — `GitTool.SSHWrapper` still comes from the ambient `GIT_SSH`, as it did before this task. This builds the policy that wiring will consume and proves all three shapes reach it.
- STATUS: `to-review`. Gates, each run standalone with its real exit code: `gofmt -l cmd internal` empty, `go build ./...` 0, `go vet ./...` 0, `golangci-lint run` (v2.12.2, CI's pin) 0 / "0 issues.", `go test ./... -count=1` 0 (41 packages), `go test -race` on the three affected packages 0, `bash .github/ci/gate-selftest.sh` 0 (75 passed, 0 failed). `test-gate.sh` itself not run — no `CURATOR_CONFORMANCE_ROOT` in this session.

### 2125 — `TASK-260822-3fkfmf` rework: a "closed" delegated set is only closed if the delegating criterion says so

- FIX: `110e1f6` then `e5df43d` on curator-spec `spec/sw-manager-security`, both signed and pushed. All four blocking review findings closed inside `profiles/manager.md` and `SECURITY.md`, nothing else touched. All eight gates re-run standalone on each commit's content, every one exit 0.
- ROOT CAUSE OF THE ONE FINDING WITH TEETH: `core.md` 4.1.1 states an **open criterion** for manager ownership of an environment name and delegates only the enumeration; `profiles/manager.md` 3.1 then declared its enumeration **closed**. A closed set that discharges an open criterion is only sound if it is provably complete — and over two interpreters and three platforms it was not: Linux enumerated three `LD_` names while macOS got `DYLD_` prefix closure, and Windows reserved `WINDIR` but not its twin `SystemRoot`. An `env_read` entry naming anything in the gap passed the caller's inherited value straight through, **fail-open and silent**, which is the exact hole the delegation existed to close.
- DECISION — BOTH HALVES, NOT EITHER: widened the enumeration (`LD_` by prefix, plus `LOCALDOMAIN`, `SYSTEMROOT`, `__PYVENV_LAUNCHER__`, plus a stated Windows case-insensitivity rule) **and** demoted "closed" to "reserved minimum" with the core criterion still governing names it does not reach. Prefix closure alone leaves an unprovable completeness claim; the minimum-plus-criterion framing alone leaves per-manager divergence on names anyone could have listed. Together the enumeration is the interop floor and the criterion is the backstop, and the residual divergence is fail-closed and reported rather than fail-open and silent. General pattern for delegated normative sets: **the delegate may not narrow the delegator's criterion by enumeration, and "closed" is a claim that needs proving, not a default.**
- FINDING — CONSEQUENCE FOR `TASK-260822-f4qv7w`: a negative vector asserting "a name outside the reserved set passes through" is no longer sound and MUST NOT be written. Positive vectors over the enumerated minimum and over the withheld-entry reporting rule are unaffected.
- FINDING — SIX BACKTICKS PAIR LEFT TO RIGHT: `SECURITY.md` inlined the four-node process graph in one code span and put a second backtick pair around `exec` inside it, so the graph the whole section turns on rendered as three broken fragments in a public-facing file. `core.md` and `profiles/manager.md` both carry the identical graph in a fenced ```text block and render fine. Now all three copies are the same fenced block. Nothing in CI lints Markdown, so this was invisible to every gate.
- NOTE — THE MIRROR MOVED WHILE THIS BRANCH SAT IN REVIEW: `spec/sw-core-prose` went `41cf556` → `78d544d` and fixed two of the findings this task had handed forward (H1 Linux `active-process-count-limit` → `host-conditional: delegated cgroup v2 pids.max`; H2 "complete diagnostic set" → "policy-level diagnostic set"). Re-mirrored the inventory cell and scoped the rc5-overlap sentence to macOS/Windows verdicts; the 8-row inventory is now byte-identical to `core.md`'s. **When a profile mirrors a core section, a review cycle is long enough for the mirror to go stale — re-diff the source before re-committing, do not assume the base you branched from is still the base you mirror.**
- DECISION — INHERIT, DO NOT RESTATE: `110e1f6` also mirrored `core.md`'s new normative paragraph (no per-user resource limit may back either aggregate control) into the profile; `e5df43d` removed that copy at the explicit request of `TASK-260822-1f533i`, which owns 4.1.1. The core section owns the rule, the profile inherits it, and a second copy of a normative MUST is only a place for two documents to drift. Kept: the corrected inventory cell, and this profile's own rc5-overlap sentence, which the correction made false rather than duplicated. **A mirroring profile should copy the table it must apply, not the argument for why the table says what it says.**
- STILL UNOWNED: decision 0008 line 117's audit-record extension for the policy identity. `profiles/manager.md` 7 now states normatively that the record carries it, but `audit-record-v1.schema.json` is `additionalProperties: false` and no task owns the change — implementable today only through the open `audit` object, so no schema version pins it and no vector can assert it.
- EVIDENCE: `TASK-260822-3fkfmf_rework.md` (board outcome resource); gate logs `curator-spec/.temp/STORY-260822-3k3hbs/manager-security-worktree/.temp/gate3-*.log` and `.temp/gate4-*.log`.

### 2205 — `TASK-260822-1yz9ug` cycle 2: three prose sentences in decision 0009 that one `git` command each falsifies

- LANDED: `be7861c` on curator-spec `main`, single-parent squash of PR 26, decision-only, amending `decisions/0009-first-party-module-roots.md` in place. No revert of `b92b105` or `3dc9ca6`; the Decision section's rules were not touched this cycle. All 8 PR checks pass, `Release target provenance` green post-merge on `main`.
- CONFIRMED F4, AND IT IS THE ONE THAT MATTERS: the previous amendment's own Status section claimed "The Decision section is unchanged". `git diff b92b105 3dc9ca6` has a hunk at `@@ -111,9 +136,11 @@`; `## Decision` spans lines 68–135 in `b92b105`, so that hunk is inside it, at point 5 — and it is the load-bearing edit, swapping the false laundering claim for the clause bounding it. **A document that denies its own diff hands the next task a false stability guarantee exactly where the text moved.** The rules genuinely did not move, so the fix is to say that precisely rather than to widen the claim. Same correction applied to the 2052 entry above.
- CONFIRMED F5: "at `ca5c4fd3` the repository carries no `replace` directive at all" is over-broad — `pkg/board/go.mod` there replaces `skill-agent-facing-api/agentquery` with `../../../skill-agent-facing-api/agentquery`, three levels up and outside the repo. Repo-wide at that revision: `pkg/board` 1, all five other modules 0. The operative claim survives (non-main-module replaces take no part in resolution, `pkg/board` is vendored, the stream still shows `Module.Replace == nil`), but "neither build root carries a `replace` directive" is both true and stronger, since it is the build root's `go.mod` the manager reads.
- CONFIRMED F3: the Consequences bullet told the first consumer to restore "the `replace` directives and vendor trees its `main` branch currently lacks". `origin/main` of `skill-project-management` carries four across its build roots (board-cli 3, board-tui 1) plus one in `pkg/providerlimits` — which the amended Context states correctly two screens above. `git ls-tree -r origin/main` returns zero paths under either build root's `vendor/`, and `.gitignore:64-67` excludes `tools/*/vendor/`. It lacks only the vendor trees.
- PATTERN, THREE CYCLES RUNNING: every defect on this task has been an unqualified prose claim about state a reviewer could falsify in one command — `type: "system"` (cycle 1), the laundering guarantee (cycle 1), and these three (cycle 2). The normative rules have survived every cycle untouched. Decision documents fail on their *factual* sentences about consumer repositories and their own history, not on their rules; those sentences deserve the same "re-derive, don't recall" discipline as the rules.
- ANOMALY, ENVIRONMENT: the data volume hit **100% full** (126Mi free of 926Gi) mid-task, and the harness could not even write a Bash tool's output file — every tool call failed `ENOSPC` until space was freed. `go clean -cache` recovered 34Gi and unblocked it (it exits non-zero with "directory not empty" while still reclaiming). Worth checking `df -h /` before blaming tooling when tool calls start failing for no reason.
- NOTE, METHOD: `${PIPESTATUS[0]}` is a bashism and expands to **empty** under this zsh, so `cmd | tail; echo rc=${PIPESTATUS[0]}` silently reports no exit code at all rather than a wrong one. Redirect gate output to a log file and read `$?` instead of piping — that preserves the real status and satisfies the evidence contract.
- GATES on `bb064ae`, each standalone with its real exit code: `validate.py` 0 (49 schemas, 471 vector files), `unittest discover -s tools` 0 (91 tests), `go test ./tools/...` 0, `generate-vectors` 0, `git diff --exit-code` on `conformance/v1` + release json 0, `gofmt -l tools` empty, `git diff --check` 0, and `lychee` with the exact CI arguments 0 (40 OK / 0 errors). Commit signed, `%G? = G`, `oparin@me.com`, no AI attribution.
- NUMBERING WARNING, STILL LIVE AFTER THREE CYCLES: `draft/TASK-260728-12pnm1-rust-driver`, `draft/TASK-260728-168smo-kotlin-native-driver` and `draft/TASK-260728-1yhuqi-swift-driver` hold `0009`/`0010`/`0011` locally against a `main` that now owns `0009`. `decisions/` already carries a duplicate `0005` from exactly this failure mode.

### 2126 — `TASK-260822-27bvo4` verification: on macOS the manager needs canonicalization even without a package manager, because `/var` is a symlink

- CONTEXT: third spawn (`RUN-260822-50e157`) on a task two earlier runs had already finished. `RUN-260822-507162` merged both lanes, attached its evidence and checked every board item, then ended `exit=124` — a timeout — before the handoff. That is the `2115` lesson repeating one run later, from a different cause: the first time the handoff was deferred behind a gate, this time the process was killed before reaching it. Either way the board status, not the spawn exit code, is the only thing that says the role handed off. No product code changed in this run.
- FINDING: the darwin tests are load-bearing, which the earlier runs' evidence did not establish. Their red-to-green was measured only on Windows, where the `EvalSymlinks` → `physicalPath` difference lives; on unix `platform_unix.go:16` makes the two the same function, so that mutation is a literal no-op on darwin. The mutation that bites here is removing canonicalization altogether (`canonical := filepath.Clean(absolute)`): `go test ./internal/godriver -run '<identity set>' -v` exits **1** with all six identity tests red.
- FINDING (the reason, and it widens the defect): the mutant also reddens `TestExecutableIdentityRejectsSubstitutionAndMutation`, which uses no link at all. `t.TempDir()` returns `/var/folders/...` and `/var` is itself a symlink to `/private/var`, so the resolved identity and the installed file disagree by that one component and `readExecutableIdentity` refuses with `the manager executable is not a canonical regular file`. So canonicalization is not only about the operator's launch shape: without it the manager cannot identify itself through an ordinary absolute path whose *parent* is a link — `/var`, `/tmp`, any `/opt`-style install published behind one. Same class as the Windows junction, reachable with no package manager involved.
- GOTCHA: a shared `GOCACHE` is not a safe substrate for a mutation check while other spawns run. Three attempts died in the linker, not in a test — first `link: mapping output file failed: no space left on device` (data volume at 100%, 1.0 GiB free, two concurrent full-suite runs), then twice `could not import io (open <GOCACHE>/dd/dd72...-d: no such file or directory)` when something purged 34 GiB of cache mid-link. A build failure reported as `FAIL` is indistinguishable from a red test at a glance; the log has to be read. Fixed by pointing `GOCACHE` at a private per-run directory (337 MB, deleted after) — the mutant then ran and failed for the intended reason.
- NOTE (method): product code was mutated in place rather than in a module copy, so every attempt was wrapped to restore from a byte backup unconditionally, and the tree re-diffed identical afterwards (3 files, 70 insertions). The attached patch artifact was re-generated at the end and is byte-identical to the working tree, so the evidence is faithful rather than asserted.
- NOTE (repo geometry, for whoever lands this): `internal/godriver` does not exist on the main checkout's branch (`handoff/cocoaskills-parity-20260731` at `74fe162`) — that lineage predates the build-driver packages, which is also why `make ledger-check` is red there on rows naming it. It exists on `main` (`0d9fe6e`), still carrying the unfixed `filepath.EvalSymlinks`, so nothing has landed yet. The task branch's base `6a9b201` is one board-metadata commit behind `main`.
- SCOPE: verification only. `gofmt -l ./cmd ./internal` 0, `go build ./...` 0, `go vet ./...` 0, `go test ./internal/godriver/ -count=1` 0 twice (61.3s, then 108.6s after mutate/restore), six identity tests PASS with 0 skips, `golangci-lint run` 0 issues, `GOOS=windows` and `GOOS=linux go vet ./internal/godriver/` both 0. NOT RUN: `go test ./...` (host disk), Windows execution (no host reached from this run; `RUN-260822-507162`'s native evidence stands), conformance gates (`CURATOR_CONFORMANCE_ROOT` unset).
- STATUS: ready for review.

### 2124 — `TASK-260822-3pkc80` review: accepted; the org can supply a `build_ssh` default it cannot enforce, and one operator `add` erases it

- VERDICT: `accepted` (`RUN-260822-0dff48`, read-only). Every gate re-run standalone by the reviewer: `go build ./...` 0, `go vet ./...` 0, `gofmt -l cmd internal` empty, `go test -count=1 ./...` 0, `golangci-lint run` 0 issues. Beyond the suite, the built binary was driven against four scratch configs — bare `--agent` genuinely keeps the scope positional, a re-`add` leaves no `agent` key behind in the JSON, and 13 malformed invocations all exit 2 with the file byte-identical afterwards. Evidence: `TASK-260822-3pkc80_review.md`.
- FINDING, WIDENS THE 2048 NOTE: that entry records that a system-supplied scope is listable but not removable. The sharper half is what happens on the *first* `add` — `build_ssh` is a top-level key, so the merge in `applySystem` is whole-key, and the operator's first user-config scope masks the organization's `build_ssh` **entirely**, silently, with no warning. Verified live: system `org.example.com/identity=/org/key` lists fine, then `add mine.example.com --agent` leaves `list` showing only `mine.example.com`. Identical semantics to `projects` and `allowed_sources`, so it is Spec §7.2 working as designed rather than a defect — but `build_ssh` is in `managerKeys` and **not** in `LockableKeys`, so an org can ship a credential default it has no way to enforce, and the operator gets no signal when it stops applying. `TASK-260822-4p3dcq` should say so out loud; whether `build_ssh` belongs in `LockableKeys` is a spec question, not a CLI one.
- FINDING: the help documents `CURATOR_BUILD_SSH_*`, but `STORY-260822-2v23mv`'s own text says `CURATOR/CSK_BUILD_SSH_*`. If `TASK-260822-2505vo` honours a `CSK_` alias for cocoaskills parity, `buildSSHUsage` needs a second sentence — a precedence claim that names only one of two accepted prefixes is worse than naming neither, because the operator will trust it.

### 2115 — `TASK-260822-1f533i` rework: `core.md` 4.1.1 now refuses to back an aggregate control with a per-user rlimit

- FIX: `spec/sw-core-prose` `41cf556` → `78d544d` (signed, pushed, `protocol/core.md` +32/-13), answering the `RUN-260822-ecf868` verdict. Linux `active-process-count-limit` goes `available: RLIMIT_NPROC` → `host-conditional: delegated cgroup v2 pids.max`. All four review items in one commit; all eight gates re-run standalone at `78d544d`, every exit 0.
- DECISION, WIDER THAN THE ASK: added a normative paragraph after the script inventory — a per-user resource limit is not a private aggregate domain; `RLIMIT_NPROC` and `RLIMIT_AS` bound every process of the invoking *user*, not the descendants of one invocation, so a manager MUST NOT back `active-process-count-limit` or `aggregate-memory-limit` with either on any platform; `RLIMIT_FSIZE` legitimately backs `per-file-size-limit` because that control bounds one file write, not an aggregate over a domain. Reason: the cell was already "fixed" once by inheritance from the analysis, and a bare table cell carries no argument — the next editor reverts it. This also gives the macOS `unavailable: no-private-aggregate-domain` verdicts on those two rows a stated reason instead of an assertion.
- FINDING: the rule generalises past this task. The distinguishing question for an inventory verdict is not "does the platform have a limit" but "is the limit's domain the invocation" — `RLIMIT_FSIZE` passes because per-file size is not a domain claim at all, and both aggregate controls fail on every rlimit. Three copies of this table exist (`core.md`, `manager.md:710`, and the vectors `f4qv7w` has yet to write); only `core.md` is corrected.
- STATUS, LOCKSTEP OPEN: `profiles/manager.md:710` on `spec/sw-manager-security` (`c2371d3`, `TASK-260822-3fkfmf`, `reviewing`) still says `available: RLIMIT_NPROC`. `3fkfmf` diagnosed it first and deliberately mirrored rather than diverge unilaterally in one of three copies; `core.md` has now diverged, so the profile is free to follow. If `3fkfmf` closes first, `TASK-260822-c0rxj7` owns the fix at landing or the merged spec asserts two verdicts for one cell. Coordination note posted to `3fkfmf`.
- FIX, THREE CHEAP ONES: "The **complete** diagnostic set" → "The **policy-level** diagnostic set" (section 4.2.1, the mirror target, claims completeness nowhere, and `manager.md` defines seven codes against `core.md`'s four); `execution_policy` and `interpreter` now named where each is introduced, with the `dependentRequired`-in-both-directions co-requirement stated normatively; the `network-isolation-domain` non-aliasing sentence now names `script-total-network-denial` (this policy's deferred guarantee, the name a script surface would actually misuse) alongside `total-network-denial` (section 4.2.1's, forbidden on any script surface).
- NOTE, PROCESS: the reviewer's real complaint on item 1 was not the wrong cell but that a sibling's named handoff arrived *unfixed and unrecorded* — absent from the divergence list and from the 2110 entry. Both now carry it: `TASK-260822-1l4r4f_analysis.md:560` is cited as the source and the deviation is written up as a deviation, not silently corrected.
- STATUS, STILL UNOWNED: schema 8 remains unreachable from `core.md` section 4 (v1–v7 list, Added-behavior table stops at row 7, "schemas 2 through 7 reject unknown fields"). Not charged to this task by the reviewer's own routing; recorded in the results handoff list. Needs an owner before `c0rxj7` merges.

### 2113 — `TASK-260822-3fkfmf` review: the one set `core.md` delegated to the manager profile came back narrower than the criterion that delegated it

- VERDICT: `changes_requested` → `to-dev`, `RUN-260822-bac22e`, read-only. `spec/sw-manager-security` @ `c2371d3`, `profiles/manager.md` + `SECURITY.md`, +476/-4, both commits signed and pushed. AC is literally met — both files on the branch, all gates green re-run standalone, no-kernel-guarantee criterion verified mechanically (6 `kernel` and 15 `guarantee` occurrences in added lines, every one a negation, deferral or prohibition). Four fixes, all inside the two owned files. Evidence: `TASK-260822-3fkfmf_review.md`.
- FINDING, BLOCKING: `core.md` 4.1.1 states an **open criterion** for manager-owned env names — "every name that selects a program, a library or module search path, an interpreter startup file or option, a temporary or configuration root, or proxy or resolver configuration" — and delegates only the enumeration. `manager.md:654-676` answers with a **closed** set that is narrower than that criterion, so the two documents assert different things. macOS gets prefix closure (`every DYLD_-prefixed name`); Linux enumerates exactly `LD_PRELOAD`, `LD_LIBRARY_PATH`, `LD_AUDIT`, leaving `LD_ORIGIN_PATH` ($ORIGIN → library search path) and `LD_TRACE_LOADED_OBJECTS` (loader does not run the program) outside it. Windows reserves `WINDIR` but not its twin `SystemRoot`, which is the spelling the loader and the Win32/WinSock/crypto APIs actually consume. Under `manager.md`'s own rule an `env_read` entry naming any of those passes the inherited value through.
- NOTE, SEVERITY HONESTLY BOUNDED: defence-in-depth, not a package-controlled escape — the package picks the *name* via `env_read`, the *caller's* environment supplies the value, so it needs a hostile manifest and a poisoned caller env together. Blocking anyway because this set is the one artifact `core.md` delegated here and nowhere else, and because it contradicts the implementer's own stated design principle: the set was made prefix-based (`DYLD_`, `PYTHON`, `NODE_`, `NPM_CONFIG_`) "so the closed set survives interpreter evolution", and `LD_` is the single loader family left enumerated.
- FINDING, BLOCKING: `SECURITY.md:350-353` nests backticks inside a code span. Six backticks pair left to right, so the process graph renders as a code span ending at "named by the ", then bare unstyled `exec`, then a stray code span containing " capability". The graph the entire section turns on is unreadable in any renderer, in a public-facing `SECURITY.md`. `core.md` and `manager.md` both render the identical graph correctly in a fenced `text` block — `SECURITY.md` is the only copy that inlines it.
- FINDING: `manager.md:511` — "The launcher rules of this section govern declared-only commands". `declared-only` is defined in 3.1 as a *script command* category, but section 3 also carries the build and system command launcher rules, and 3.1 itself says build launchers are unchanged. As written the carve-out removes build and system commands from section 3 entirely.
- CORRECTION TO THE RECORD: the 2110 and 2255 entries both frame the Linux `active-process-count-limit` = `available: RLIMIT_NPROC` cell as diverging from an rc5 verdict. `rc5-native-control-inventory-v1` (`manager.md:251`) has **no Linux column at all** — macOS and Windows only. The cell originates in `core.md` 4.1.1 and has no rc5 precedent to contradict; the diagnosis is still right, the provenance was not. Mirroring it verbatim rather than diverging unilaterally in one of three copies remains the correct call.
- FINDING, STORY-LEVEL, UNOWNED: decision 0008:117 lists "audit-record extension for the policy identity" as a required consequence. `manager.md` §7 now states normatively that the audit record "carries the effective execution-policy identity or its absence", but `schemas/v1/audit-record-v1.schema.json` is `additionalProperties: false` and `spec/sw-schema` @ `ebfed81` does not touch it. Satisfiable today only by stuffing the identity into the open `audit` object, so no schema version pins it and no vector can assert it. Third unowned story-level item on this epic after the schema-8 prose gap and the `host-capability` platform-case class.
- NOTE: `spec/sw-schema` @ `ebfed81` adds no `script_execution_*` enum anywhere, so the `core.md` "complete diagnostic set" (4) vs `manager.md` 3.1 (7) contradiction is still unconstrained by any artifact and lands whole on `TASK-260822-f4qv7w`.
- NOTE, METHOD: the handoff write-up's six self-reported findings all held up under check, and the one place it overstated something it overstated *against itself*. None of the four blocking defects appear in it — they are in the prose the author was closest to. Self-reporting bought accurate triage of the cross-task risks and bought nothing on the owned files.

### 2255 — `TASK-260822-1f533i` review: 4.1.1 is strong prose with one cell that mandates a false `applied` claim

- VERDICT: `changes_requested` → `to-dev`, `RUN-260822-ecf868`, read-only. `spec/sw-core-prose` @ `41cf556`, `protocol/core.md` +388/-2. Structure, 4.2.1 mirror and honesty discipline all hold; one table cell blocks. Evidence: `TASK-260822-1f533i_review-verdict.md`.
- FINDING, BLOCKING: the script inventory's Linux `active-process-count-limit` cell says `available: RLIMIT_NPROC`. 4.1.1's own rule "An `available` control MUST report `applied`" then *requires* every Linux manager to emit `active-process-count-limit / available / applied` in each `script-capability-evidence-v1` record. `RLIMIT_NPROC` bounds processes for the **real UID**, shared with every other process that user owns — the literal content of `no-private-aggregate-domain`, which is what the same row's macOS cell says, on a platform that has the same rlimit with the same semantics. One row, two standards for one primitive. Fix: `host-conditional: delegated cgroup v2 pids.max`, in `core.md`, `manager.md:710`, and the vectors together.
- FINDING, CORROBORATING: the row directly beneath settles it. `aggregate-memory-limit` on Linux is `host-conditional: delegated cgroup v2 memory.max` — `RLIMIT_AS` was correctly *not* claimed as an aggregate memory limit. If cgroup delegation is host-conditional for the memory controller, the `pids` controller is host-conditional by the same argument, and an rlimit is no more sufficient for process count than it was for memory. The analysis stated the rule itself ("both are mapped to `unavailable`/`host-conditional`, so neither can produce a false `applied` claim") and then broke it in exactly one cell.
- ANOMALY, PROCESS: this was not missed silently — `TASK-260822-3fkfmf` handed it over by name ("two things you own that 3fkfmf could not"), with the diagnosis and the recommended fix, after mirroring it rather than diverging unilaterally in one of three copies. It arrived unfixed *and* unrecorded: absent from the outcome resource's divergence list and from the 2110 logbook entry. A handoff note that names an owner is not a tracked item; when a sibling defers a correctness call to you, it needs a checklist row, not a paragraph in `notes`.
- FINDING: `core.md` says "The **complete** diagnostic set of this policy is" over four rows while `manager.md@c2371d3` defines seven and pre-reconciles ("the first four are the policy-level set [...] the last three are worker-session codes of this profile"). Section 4.2.1, the mirror target, claims completeness nowhere — `core.md` names three `build_execution_*` codes and `manager.md` adds three. The word "complete" is a divergence from the mirror that manufactures a contradiction on merge. One-word fix.
- FINDING: 4.1.1 normalizes an opt-in surface without naming its fields. `execution_policy` and `interpreter` are fixed in `common.schema.json@ebfed81` (with `dependentRequired` both ways) and used throughout `manager.md`, but appear nowhere in the subsection — which says only "an OPTIONAL per-command field". Section 4.1 above names `unix_path`/`win_path` and 4.3 names all six capability fields, so protocol core is a document that names manifest fields; the section defining the opt-in cannot be read standalone.
- FINDING, STORY-LEVEL, UNOWNED: 4.1.1 says "manifest schema 8 or later" and `spec/sw-schema` adds `agent-skill-v8.schema.json`, but `core.md` section 4 still says manifests conform to "v1 through v7", its Added-behavior table stops at row 7, and the downward gate paragraph says "schemas 2 through 7 reject unknown fields". `spec/sw-schema` touches no `.md` outside `schemas/v1/README.md`. Reads as `TASK-260822-1mwy10`'s prose scope (also in `reviewing`); needs an owner before `c0rxj7` merges, or merged core.md references a schema its own section 4 does not admit.
- NOTE, RESOLVED: the board's coordination note lists identifier spellings that match neither file any more. `c2371d3` reconciled `manager.md` to core.md's names, and extraction confirms deferred guarantees, warning classes, inventory version, record version, availability values and unavailable reasons are now identical across both branches. Section-number handoff is closed too — `manager.md` already cites 4.1.1 in five places. Re-derive identifier sets from the files, never from the note that described them.
- NOTE: all seven CI gates re-run by the reviewer at `41cf556`, not taken on trust — `validate.py` (49 schemas, 471 vectors), `unittest`, `go test ./tools/...`, generate-vectors + `git diff --exit-code`, `git diff --check`, `lychee --offline` (36 OK / 0 errors). All exit 0. The AC clause "formatting and link gates pass" is confirmed independently.

### 2210 — `TASK-260822-1yz9ug` review cycle 2: the amendment fixed both findings and introduced two of its own

- VERDICT: `changes_requested` → `to-dev`, `RUN-260822-5f6beb`, read-only. No revert of `b92b105` or `3dc9ca6`. Cycle-1 requests 1–4 all landed correctly and the normative core is sound; what remains is three sentences of prose that one `git show` falsifies. Evidence: `TASK-260822-1yz9ug_review-verdict-2.md`.
- ANOMALY, SELF-FALSIFYING: decision 0009's Status, the `3dc9ca6` commit message, the 2052 logbook entry and the board note all say "the Decision section is unchanged". `git diff b92b105 3dc9ca6` has a hunk at `@@ -111,9 +136,11 @@`, inside `## Decision`, point 5. And it is the load-bearing edit — the removed lines were the false laundering claim, the added ones the bound "It does not, and by construction cannot, reach a package that presents no replacement at all". The rules did not move; the sentence bounding what point 5 delivers did. Claiming otherwise tells `TASK-260822-3nvx91` the normative source of truth is stable exactly where it moved.
- ANOMALY, INTERNAL CONTRADICTION: the Consequences bullet says the first consumer "restores the `replace` directives and vendor trees its `main` branch currently lacks". `main` has four first-party replaces — the amended Context says so two screens above. It lacks only the vendor trees. The bullet that tells the consumer what to do asserts the opposite of the Context about half its subject.
- FINDING: "at `ca5c4fd3` the repository carries no `replace` directive at all" is over-broad. `pkg/board/go.mod` at that revision replaces `skill-agent-facing-api/agentquery` with `../../../skill-agent-facing-api/agentquery` — three levels up, outside the repository, at a sibling checkout. The operative claim survives (replaces in non-main modules take no part in resolution, `pkg/board` is vendored, the stream still presents `Module.Replace == nil`), but the true statement is narrower and stronger: neither *build root* carries one.
- CONFIRMED, AGAINST CYCLE 1: the implementer's two corrections hold. `git show ca5c4fd3:tools/board-cli/go.mod` is already replace-free with a committed `vendor/` — no packaging-time rewrite, so cycle 1's "stripped" was wrong to drop. And `ca5c4fd3` (2026-08-07) is an ancestor of `main`, 117 commits behind, so "has since drifted" is accurate.
- NEW EVIDENCE FOR A CLAIM NOBODY HAD CHECKED: "it builds under `go-v1` today" is substantiated on this host — `~/.curator/cache/build/go-v1/` holds three receipts whose `bin/` entries are exactly `task-board`, `tb-sessiond`, `task-board-tui`. That also independently kills the original `type: "system"` premise: a system command produces no build receipt.
- CARRIED, NOT BLOCKING: the `EPIC-260822-18ylpq` note still says the snapshot shipped "STRIPPED" and that `2ed3acd` restored the replaces — both disproved in cycle 1's rework, and the amended decision explicitly denies the first. It is the input `TASK-260822-3nvx91` reads; align it before that task spawns or the normative producer inherits a framing the decision it implements refutes.
- TRAP: in `zsh`, `git show "$c:agent-skill.json"` applies the `:a` history modifier and resolves `$c` to an absolute path — it fails with a mangled filename that reads like a bad revision. Brace it: `"${c}:agent-skill.json"`.
- LESSON, SECOND TIME IN TWO CYCLES: a document that corrects factual claims needs its *own* claims held to the standard it just applied. Both new defects are statements about verifiable state written from intent rather than from a check, and both survived green CI — CI reads schemas and links, never whether a sentence about another repository is true.
- SCOPE: review-only, `RUN-260822-5f6beb`. Status `to-dev`. Requested: one decision-only PR amending three sentences of `decisions/0009-first-party-module-roots.md` in place, or folding them into the normative PR if the maintainer prefers not to spend a third CI cycle.

### 2110 — Script-worker prose: core.md called its diagnostic set "complete" and it is not; three concurrent branches converged only because one of them landed first and said so

- SCOPE: `TASK-260822-3fkfmf`. Branch `spec/sw-manager-security` in `curator-spec`, commits `b9ca2ad` then `c2371d3`, base `origin/main`=`b92b105`. `profiles/manager.md` gains section 3.1 (enforced `script-worker-v1` command launch) plus a section 3 launcher carve-out and two section 7 audit warning classes; `SECURITY.md` gains the "Enforced script execution boundary" peer section. 2 files, +445/-12.
- FINDING, TENSION LEFT VISIBLE: `protocol/core.md` 4.1.1 says "The complete diagnostic set of this policy is:" and lists four codes. `profiles/manager.md` 3.1 carries seven — those four plus `script_execution_worker_identity_invalid`, `script_execution_worker_protocol_invalid`, `script_execution_package_influence_forbidden`. That extension is the *established* shape: core.md 4.2.1 names three `build_execution_*` codes and manager.md 2.2.1 carries six, including exactly these three analogues. But 4.2.1 never claims completeness and 4.1.1 does. Dropping them would leave the identity-verified worker's own failure modes undiagnosable, which is the point of verifying identity. Labelled the split in the prose rather than papering over it. Landing task must either soften 4.1.1 to "policy-level diagnostic set" or promote the three.
- FINDING, INVENTORY CELL THAT LOOKS FALSE: the script native-control inventory marks Linux `active-process-count-limit` as `available: RLIMIT_NPROC`. `RLIMIT_NPROC` caps processes per real UID across the whole session — it is not a private aggregate domain — and the rc5 ledger marks **macOS unavailable: `no-private-aggregate-domain` for that same primitive**. An `available` entry MUST report `applied`, so the cell as written manufactures exactly the false applied-claim decision 0006 exists to forbid. Recommended `host-conditional: delegated cgroup v2 pids.max`. Mirrored verbatim rather than diverged, because the identical table lands in three places (`core.md` 4.1.1, `manager.md` 3.1, and the vectors) and a unilateral edit in one copy reads as a transcription error, not a fix. Raised on the board instead.
- DECISION: `SECURITY.md`'s blanket "Capability declarations and source auditing are review and policy surfaces; they are not runtime sandboxes" was true for every command until this change and is now false for enforced script commands. Split it rather than delete it: source auditing stays a review surface; capability declarations stay one *except* under `script-worker-v1`, where the same bytes additionally feed a manager-mechanism containment profile. The sentence "Neither form is a kernel sandbox" carries the honesty that the deleted clause used to.
- FINDING, DELEGATION THAT WOULD HAVE DANGLED: core.md 4.1.1's `env_read` bullet ends "The exact reserved set per platform and per interpreter identifier is defined by the manager profile" — a forward reference to a set nobody had written. Section 3.1 now defines it, prefix-based where a vendor keeps adding names (`DYLD_`, `PYTHON`, `NODE_`, `NPM_CONFIG_`) so the closed set survives interpreter evolution, plus the rule that a manager rejects an interpreter identifier it has no reserved set for rather than launch with an unfiltered environment. Cross-check a delegating document for its unsatisfied forward references before declaring a profile task done.
- FINDING, CONCURRENCY: three prose/schema tasks ran simultaneously against one analysis artifact with no live channel between them. `spec/sw-manager-security` landed first and therefore fixed identifier spellings on the wire — then `spec/script-worker-v1-normative` (`41cf556`) landed core.md 4.1.1 with *different* spellings for four of them. Core.md is normative, so `c2371d3` reconciled: `script-unconditional-network-denial`→`script-total-network-denial`, `script-unconditional-write-confinement`→`script-private-runtime-area-only-writes`, `script-verified-interpreter-tree`→`script-read-only-runtime-tree`, `script_execution_deferred_claim_forbidden`→`script_execution_hardened_claim_forbidden`, snake_case audit classes→kebab-case, three-node graph→four-node, and a separate exec-denial guarantee folded away (eight→seven). "Landed first" is not authority; the normative document is. Budget a reconciliation pass whenever sibling prose branches run concurrently, and re-derive the identifier sets mechanically rather than by eye — a regex diff of both files against core.md found every one of them in seconds.
- NOTE: the venv trap from 2052/2100 hit a third time. `curator-spec` has no `.gitignore`, so `.temp/venv` inside the worktree shows up untracked; staged the two files explicitly and never `git add -A`.
- GATES on `c2371d3`, each standalone with its real exit code: `python tools/validate.py` 0 (49 schemas, 471 vector files), `unittest discover -s tools` 0 (91 tests), `go test ./tools/...` 0, `generate-vectors` 0 then `git diff --exit-code -- conformance/v1 release/*.json` 0, `gofmt -l tools` empty, `git diff --check` 0, and `lychee` over the two changed files 0 (1 link, 1 OK, 0 errors).
