### 2100 — Manifest schema 8 lands as one shared bump; `install-marker-v3` pins `skill_schema_version` to a `const`, so a manifest bump is never free

- DECISION: manifest schema 8 carries **both** decision 0008 (`execution_policy` on script commands) and decision 0009 (first-party module roots). Not sequential 8/9. Five reasons, strongest first: `skill_schema_version` is a `const` per install-marker version, so a second manifest version forces a second marker version; the legacy rejection matrix (every schema below N rejects N's surface) would need a second full pass; sequential numbering leaves schema 8 born superseded, since any manifest wanting both features must use the higher one; no release has ever carried two manifest versions (rc.4=6, rc.5=7); and the two surfaces are disjoint. `COMPATIBILITY.md` freezes *released* bytes only, and schema 8 has not shipped, so a shared bump violates no freeze.
- FINDING, THE ONE THAT COST SCOPE: `schemas/v1/install-marker-v3.schema.json:20` is `"skill_schema_version": {"const": 7}` and marker v2 is `minimum 0, maximum 6`. A new manifest schema with no marker version is **uninstallable** — `core.md:1153-1154` requires a specific marker version per manifest band. Every future manifest bump drags an install-marker bump with it. Delivered `install-marker-v4` as marker v3 with `schema_version` 4 and `skill_schema_version` 8 and no other difference; a test strips those two properties and asserts the documents are equal.
- FINDING: the marker-v3 example generators in `tools/generate-vectors/main.go` were already version-neutral — `markerV3WithExternalRecord` only clones the marker passed in — so marker v4 inherits all 27 build-record branches by calling the same helper. Renamed to `markerWithExternalRecord` / `installMarkerBuildRecordSchemaExamples` to stop the names lying.
- FINDING: two parallel spawns converged on the same closed interpreter set without coordination. `spec/sw-schema` (`ebfed81`, schema) admits `node-v1` and `python3-v1`; `spec/sw-core-prose` (`41cf556`, normative) independently writes "Protocol 1.0 admits exactly `python3-v1` and `node-v1`" and defers `bash-v1`/`powershell-v1` on the same platform-resolution argument. The analysis (`TASK-260822-1l4r4f`) had left the exact set open to "the prose task's call" — a shared, well-argued analysis artifact was enough to make two agents agree on an unassigned decision.
- FINDING, F1 OF THE ANALYSIS CONFIRMED AND LEFT VISIBLE: `$defs.capabilities` has `"default": "repo"` on `filesystem`, which contradicts decision 0008 §3's deny-by-default. Draft 2020-12 `default` is an annotation, never applied, so the schema cannot resolve it — and it *must not*, because capabilities are manifest-wide while enforcement is per command. Recorded in `schemas/v1/README.md` prose instead of silently changing the annotation. `spec/sw-core-prose` independently landed the matching normative sentence: "the schema default for that field MUST NOT widen a derived control".
- SCOPE: branch `spec/sw-schema`, commit `ebfed81`, base `origin/main`=`b92b105`. New `schemas/v1/{agent-skill-v8,csk-skill-v8,install-marker-v4}.schema.json`, new `$defs.{scriptExecutionPolicyV1,scriptInterpreterV1,scriptCommandV8,commandV8}`, 186 new generated cases, 6 new tests.
- FROZEN-BYTES EVIDENCE WORTH REUSING: `git diff --name-only -- conformance/v1/schema-cases/` returning exactly `index.json` is the cheap proof that a schema bump redefined nothing in place. All 186 other case changes were additions.
- GATES on `ebfed81`, each standalone with its real exit code: `python3 tools/validate.py` 0 (52 schemas, 656 vector files), `unittest discover -s tools` 0 (94 tests, ~42s), `go test ./tools/...` 0, `gofmt -l tools` empty, and `generate-vectors` + `git diff --exit-code` run **twice**, 0 all four times. `release/1.0.0-rc.5/6/7.json` unchanged; only rc.8's candidate pin moves, which is what that generated field is for.
- STATUS: pending — `protocol/core.md` on no branch yet renumbers the manifest series (§4 preamble still says "v1 through v7", §4 table has no row 8, §10 knows no marker v4). Schema 8 and core.md contradict each other until `TASK-260822-c0rxj7` lands those four deltas. Recorded on the landing task.
- NOTE: the venv-and-`__pycache__` trap from the 2052 entry hit again. Put the venv **outside** the worktree (`curator/.temp/<TASK-ID>/venv`); `curator-spec` has no `.gitignore` at all, so anything dropped in the tree shows up untracked and is one `git add -A` from being committed.

### 2052 — `TASK-260822-1yz9ug` rework: decision 0009 amended; the reviewer was right on both findings and wrong on two supporting details

- LANDED: `3dc9ca6` on curator-spec `main`, single-parent squash of PR 25, decision-only, amending `decisions/0009-first-party-module-roots.md` in place. No revert of `b92b105`. The Decision section's rules are unchanged; point 5 gains the bound that its scoping does not reach packages presenting no replacement. Status, Context, Rejected alternatives, Compatibility impact, Security impact, Consequences and a new open question 7 carry the corrections. (Corrected 2026-08-22 in cycle 2: this entry originally claimed the Decision section was "byte-unchanged", which `git diff b92b105 3dc9ca6` falsifies at `@@ -111,9 +136,11 @@`, inside point 5.)
- CONFIRMED F1: `skill-project-management` has never shipped a `type: "system"` command. `git log --all -S'"type": "system"' -- agent-skill.json` on that repo returns **nothing**; `origin/main` is schema 6 with three `type: "build"`/`go-v1` commands. The decision's motivating premise was false in all three places it appeared.
- CONFIRMED F2, AND IT IS THE REAL FINDING: decision 0009 §5 scoped decision 0005's vendored `SFiles`/`cgo_import_dynamic` exceptions to unreplaced results claiming that stops `go mod vendor` laundering first-party assembly under a third-party allowance. It does not. A package with no `replace` directive presents `Module.Replace == nil`, declares no `modules`, never triggers the bijection, and keeps the exceptions. Resolved by doing **both** halves the review offered rather than picking one: narrow §5/Compatibility/Security to the guarantee actually delivered, **and** record the residual as open question 7. Nothing in the fixed `go list` stream separates first-party from third-party vendored code once the replacement is gone, so closing it needs a mechanism, not a predicate — a design question that belongs to the normative change, not to a decision whose subject is the declared form.
- CORRECTION TO THE REVIEW ENTRY ABOVE (2145), FIRST DETAIL: "the packaged snapshot has the `replace` directives **stripped**" implies a packaging-time rewrite. There is none. At `ca5c4fd3` the **committed git tree itself** carries zero `replace` lines, requires `pkg/board v0.0.0` and `pkg/remoteconfig v0.0.0` as ordinary requirements, and commits `tools/board-cli/vendor/` — the installed snapshot is a faithful copy. Verified both ways: `git show ca5c4fd3:tools/board-cli/go.mod` and the on-disk snapshot agree. The amended decision says "not a packaging-time rewrite" explicitly, because a manager-side reader would otherwise look for a transformation step that does not exist.
- CORRECTION, SECOND DETAIL: "`2ed3acd` added the directory-form replaces" is wrong — `2ed3acd` (2026-07-30) *removed* an `agentquery` replace and touched nothing first-party. The replaces returned to `main` via `1e655eb` (2026-08-18, a trunk carry), and `b6c7404` (2026-08-20) touched only `.gitignore`/docs. Neither belongs in a spec decision, so the amended text states the two present-state facts instead and cites no commit archaeology: `main` has the replaces and no vendor tree; `ca5c4fd3` has neither replaces nor a missing vendor tree.
- TRAP WORTH KEEPING: `git log -S<pat> -- <path>` under default history simplification silently hid the commit that reintroduced those replaces across a merge; only `--full-history` surfaced `1e655eb`. Pickaxe on a merge-heavy branch is not proof of absence.
- FINDING RECORDED IN THE DECISION: the unreplaced pre-vendored shape is now a named rejected alternative, not a hypothetical — it is the status quo decision 0009 displaces. Its three failure modes: the packaged tree diverges from the source repo with nothing gating the drift (and nothing *can* gate it, since the replacement that would name the source directory is gone); first-party code enters under an allowance written for audited third-party dependencies; and a committed vendor tree auto-enables `-mod=vendor` for every local build, which is why the consumer's `main` now excludes `tools/*/vendor/` and thereby made itself unbuildable under `go-v1`.
- GATES on `5cb234b`, each a standalone process with its real exit code: `python tools/validate.py` 0 (49 schemas, 471 vector files), `python -B -m unittest discover -s tools -p 'test_*.py'` 0 (91 tests, 120s — exceeds a 2-minute tool timeout, budget for it), `go test ./tools/...` 0, `go run ./tools/generate-vectors -root .` 0, `git diff --exit-code -- conformance/v1 release/*.json` 0, `gofmt -l tools` empty, `git diff --check` 0. PR 25: 8 checks pass, `Release target provenance` skipped on PRs by design (`ci.yml:87`).
- ANOMALY, STILL LIVE: `python tools/validate.py` needs `jsonschema`, absent from the host interpreter; a throwaway venv inside the worktree works but **must be removed before `git add`**, along with `tools/__pycache__/` which the unittest run creates untracked.
- NUMBERING WARNING, STILL LIVE: `draft/TASK-260728-12pnm1-rust-driver`, `draft/TASK-260728-168smo-kotlin-native-driver` and `draft/TASK-260728-1yhuqi-swift-driver` hold `0009`, `0010`, `0011` locally and now collide with `main`. `decisions/` already carries a duplicate at `0005` from the same failure mode.
- SCOPE: doc-writer rework, `RUN-260822-2bf138`. Status `to-review`. Evidence: `TASK-260822-1yz9ug_amendment-results.md`.

### 2110 — `TASK-260822-1f533i`: core.md 4.1.1 defines `script-worker-v1`, and three of its rules are not what decision 0008 says

- DECISION: the subsection lands as `#### 4.1.1` under section 4.1, not as a sibling of 4.2.1. Decision 0008's first consequence offers both; under 4.1 it sits next to the script-command definition it constrains and leaves the 4.2 build numbering untouched. Commit `41cf556` on `spec/sw-core-prose` (pushed, no PR), 388 insertions in `protocol/core.md`.
- DECISION: enforcement derives from the **declared manifest bytes**, not the schema-defaulted object. `common.schema.json` `$defs.capabilities.filesystem.default` is `"repo"` while decision 0008 §3 says an absent field means no writes outside the private runtime area. Deriving from declared bytes resolves the conflict without touching frozen schema-7 bytes; the schema default stays the declared-only audit reading.
- DECISION: the first-release interpreter set is exactly `python3-v1` and `node-v1`. `bash-v1` and `powershell-v1` are named in the text and deferred because neither a POSIX shell nor PowerShell resolves on all three supported platforms, so an enforced command declaring one would be uninstallable on at least one conformance lane. The analysis left this set to the prose task.
- FINDING: `env_read` had to be given a reservation rule the analysis never raised. Without it `env_read: ["PATH"]` passes the inherited `PATH` through and defeats mandatory control 5 (the manager-built `PATH`) from inside the manifest. 4.1.1 now says a manager-owned name never passes the inherited value; the per-interpreter reserved set is deferred to the manager profile.
- FINDING: three rules of 4.2.1 must NOT be inherited by a script policy, and silence would have meant inheriting them: closed stdin (breaks every interactive and piped shim), bounded combined output (breaks streaming), and the wall-clock deadline (a script may legitimately run forever). Each is written as a positive control instead of a dropped one.
- NOTE: 4.1.1 diverges from decision 0008's literal text in five places, all deliberate and listed in the task's outcome resource: new `script-capability-evidence-v1` record instead of reusing `capability-evidence-v1` (`core.md:817` makes a foreign `execution_policy` a forbidden hardened claim); the `filesystem` default conflict above; host filtering deferred instead of entering the inventory per platform; "per invocation-policy identity" read as one record per invocation; and a Linux platform column, which no execution-policy ledger has ever had.
- ANOMALY: the task description named the prepared worktree branch `spec/script-worker-v1-normative` while the board notes and DoD named `spec/sw-core-prose` and asked for a push. Reconciled by keeping the prepared worktree and renaming its local-only branch to `spec/sw-core-prose`. Worth checking at spawn time: a prepared worktree and a spawn note can disagree on the branch, and only the notes said to push.
- NOTE: `tools/validate.py` needs `jsonschema==4.25.1`, absent from the system Python on this machine. A venv from `requirements-dev.txt` under `.temp/TASK-260822-1f533i/venv` is what every Python gate in curator-spec has to run through here.
- STATUS: ready for review. `validate.py`, `unittest`, `go test ./tools/...`, regenerate-check, `git diff --check`, and `lychee '**/*.md'` all exit 0 at `41cf556`. The subsection names `conformance/v1/vectors/script-host-execution-policy.json` as its machine-readable authority and that file does not exist yet, so this branch cannot land before `TASK-260822-f4qv7w`.

### 2051 — `TASK-260822-2v5e80`: an operator remedy on `toolchain_executable_mismatch` is visible on the error line and truncated in the report row

- DECISION: the remedy is a new `Diagnostic.Remedy` field rendered behind the detail by `Error()` (`internal/godriver/errors.go:26`), not text folded into `Detail`. Both sites keep their protocol string byte for byte (`internal/godriver/session.go:521`, `internal/godriver/session.go:669`); a reader matching on the detail is unaffected. Rendered form is `go-v1 <code>: <detail>; <remedy>`.
- FINDING: `install.RedactDiagnostic` bounds a rendered detail at 240 runes (`internal/install/diagnostics.go:13`). The operator failure line (`Result.Errors`) is 193 runes with the remedy and survives whole, including `PATH="$(go env GOROOT)/bin:$PATH"` — the `/` after `)` is not a path opener, so nothing is redacted to `<path>`. A build report row prefixes 101 more runes (`cmd/curator/builds.go:424`) and truncates at 294 → the remedy is exactly what `...` eats. Remedy kept short for that reason; a fully visible row needs the bound or the prefix changed.
- FINDING: `goToolchainGuidance` may not contain the substring `path` outside its closed-rule sentence (`cmd/curator/builds_test.go:678`), so a PATH remedy cannot live in the CLI guidance layer at all — it has to be carried by the driver diagnostic.
- ANOMALY: two spawn runs (RUN-260822-88df86, RUN-260822-3b7262) were queued 18s apart on the same task and both implemented it, in `.temp/TASK-260822-2v5e80/worktree-r88df86` and `.temp/TASK-260822-2v5e80/worktree`. Both arrived at the same `Remedy`-field design and identical operator text; the branches are duplicates, and a reviewer has to discard one.
- NOTE: the go-v1 conformance vectors skip at SPEC_PIN 00b1688a — that suite publishes no `vectors/build-drivers.json`. The harness compares diagnostic codes only, never detail text (`internal/godriver/builddriver_rejection_conformance_test.go:513`).
- STATUS: `to-review`. `go vet ./...`, `golangci-lint run`, `go build`, and the three affected package suites exit 0; host data volume at 99% caused unrelated `No space left on device` failures that re-ran green and pass on a pristine baseline worktree.
- VERIFIED (`RUN-260822-f42998`, fourth run after two exit-124 deaths and one exit-1): the change on `task/TASK-260822-2v5e80-toolchain-shim-remedy` is byte-identical to the attached `TASK-260822-2v5e80_remedy.patch` (sorted-diff comparison; `git apply --check` exit 0 against pristine `6a9b201`), and the *whole* suite is green on this host, not just the three touched packages: `go test -count=1 ./...` **exit 0**, 41 packages ok, `cmd/curator` 527s / `internal/install` 119s / `internal/godriver` 82s. `gofmt -l cmd internal` empty, `go build ./...` 0, `go vet ./...` 0, `golangci-lint run` 0 issues. Every gate ran as a standalone process under the approved absolute toolchain (`GOROOT=/Users/iv/.goenv/versions/1.25.5`, `GOTOOLCHAIN=local`, `GOENV=off`) — the bare host `go` is a goenv shim, i.e. the exact selection this diagnostic now remedies.

### 2048 — `TASK-260822-3pkc80`: the build-ssh CLI is the first thing to commit to an env-var prefix that has no implementation yet

- DECISION: `curator config build-ssh -h` states precedence as flags > `CURATOR_BUILD_SSH_*` environment values > configured scopes, and `TestConfigHelpDocumentsPrecedenceAndSubcommands` pins that ordering in the text. No flags/env resolution layer exists yet — `TASK-260822-2505vo` builds it. The prefix follows the repo's only env convention (`CURATOR_CONFIG`, `CURATOR_SYSTEM_CONFIG`, `CURATOR_REGISTRY_TOKEN`, `cmd/curator/main.go:1042`); the resolver must adopt it or update `buildSSHUsage` in the same change.
- DECISION: `add` replaces the whole entry under a scope rather than merging fields. A merge would leave a previous `agent` selection able to authenticate after the operator re-declared the scope as identity-only. Proved by mutation: patching `SetBuildSSH` to merge turns the suite red.
- FINDING: `--agent [SOCKET]` needs `AcceptsOptionalValue` to claim the next token *only when it reads as a credential path* (`config.ValidBuildSSHPath`, newly exported), otherwise `config build-ssh add --agent git.example.com` swallows the scope as a socket. Same `flag.Value`+`IsBoolFlag` pattern as `--audit`, but the discriminator has to be the value grammar, not a fixed enum.
- DECISION: exit codes split by fault — 2 for a malformed invocation (validated with `config.ValidateBuildSSH` *before* the config file is read, so nothing is attributed to the file), 1 for the operation failing (unwritable config, removing a scope that is not configured). Removing the last scope drops the `build_ssh` field entirely rather than leaving `{}`.
- NOTE: `list` reads the effective config (system overlay included) while `add`/`remove` write the user config, so an unlocked system-supplied scope can be listed yet not removable. The remove error names the user config path, which is what makes that legible.
- SCOPE: `cmd/curator/main.go` (`cmdConfig` dispatcher replaces the inline `config show` case), `internal/config/write.go` (`SetBuildSSH`, `RemoveBuildSSH`), `internal/config/buildssh.go` (`ValidBuildSSHPath`), tests in `cmd/curator/main_test.go` and `internal/config/write_test.go`.
- STATUS: `to-review`. `go test -count=1 ./...` exit 0, `golangci-lint run` exit 0, `go vet` exit 0, `gofmt -l cmd internal` empty. `make ci-test`/`check-ci` not run — no `CURATOR_CONFORMANCE_ROOT` available in this session.

### 2145 — `TASK-260822-1yz9ug` review: decision 0009's laundering fix does not bind, and its first consumer is already through the hole

- ANOMALY: decision 0009 states three times that `skill-project-management` "installs through a `type: "system"` manifest" until module roots land. It does not. `origin/main:agent-skill.json` is schema 6 with three `type: "build"`/`go-v1` commands over `build_roots` `tools/board-cli` and `tools/board-tui`, and the snapshot curator actually installs (`~/.curator/cache/project-management/ca5c4fd3.../snapshot`, the revision pinned by `origin/main:Skillfile.json`) carries the same manifest. No `type: "system"` command exists in that repo or its history. The claim was inherited verbatim from the `EPIC-260822-18ylpq` analysis note.
- FINDING: what actually ships is a third packaging shape nobody wrote down — the packaged snapshot has the `replace` directives **stripped** and the first-party modules pre-vendored as ordinary `v0.0.0` requirements. At `ca5c4fd3`: zero `replace` lines in `tools/board-cli/go.mod`, zero `=>` lines in `vendor/modules.txt`, copies under `vendor/github.com/relux-works/skill-project-management/pkg/`. The git tree diverged from it when `2ed3acd` added the directory-form replaces and `TASK-260819-3vr8j3` gitignored `tools/*/vendor/`, which is why `main` as it stands is unbuildable under `go-v1` — the accurate form of "`go-v1` cannot package it".
- REGRESSION IN THE ARGUMENT: decision 0009 §5 scopes decision 0005's vendored `SFiles`/`cgo_import_dynamic` exceptions to results whose module carries no replacement, "so that `go mod vendor` cannot launder package-controlled assembly ... under a third-party allowance". The replacement predicate cannot carry that. Strip the replaces and pre-vendor, and `Module.Replace == nil`: the 0005 exceptions apply to the package's own first-party code and the new bijection never fires, because there is no directive to biject against. The named first consumer is in exactly that state in production right now. §5 closes the path only for packages that *keep* their replace directives — i.e. only for the shape 0009 newly admits.
- NOTE: "replaced modules are rejected outright today, so nothing shipped can depend on the wider reading" is literally true and still misleading, for the same reason. A shipped, accepted package already routes first-party code through the third-party allowance by having no replacement at all.
- LESSON: an inherited analysis note is not evidence. Both errors survived a careful implementer and green CI because nobody opened the consumer's manifest — the one file the whole motivation rests on. This repo rejects vendor metadata "checked against nothing"; the same standard applies to a decision's Context.
- SCOPE: review-only, `RUN-260822-c8550c`. Verdict `changes_requested` → `to-dev`, evidence in `TASK-260822-1yz9ug_review-verdict.md`; `EPIC-260822-18ylpq` note corrected so `TASK-260822-3nvx91` does not inherit the premise. No revert of `b92b105` — the fix is a follow-up decision-only PR.

- VERDICT: accepted (`RUN-260822-bc9f3a`, read-only). One production line, `internal/closure/closure.go:288`. - METHOD: `CURATOR_CONFORMANCE_ROOT` is unset here, so instead of declaring the conformance gate "not run" and stopping, the residual risk was checked directly — `internal/closure/conformance_test.go` is 53 lines asserting provider order with no error-string assertion, and no vector, doc or README pins the closure manifest-error text. That converts an unrunnable gate into a bounded, stated risk rather than an unknown.

Outside this AC; worth folding into any future closure-diagnostics pass.
- HANDOFF: reviewer supplies no `commit_ack`. The working tree carries unrelated uncommitted work — `internal/skillspec/parse.go` (+ test) is `TASK-260822-5wfdfx`, `internal/config/config.go` is the cocoaskills parity line — so the commit-owning mover must commit only the four files of this task's scope before the final `done` transition.

### 2038 — `TASK-260822-3j4bcb`: closure manifest errors get provenance at the call site, not in the parser

- ROOT CAUSE: `closure.resolveNode` wrapped a failed `skillspec.Load` with a bare `fmt.Errorf("%s: %w", item.name, err)`. A broken transitive manifest produced the whole diagnostic `leaf: commands: must be an object` — no resolved ref, no requirement chain, and the `leaf:` prefix reads like one more verr field-path segment rather than a node name, so it looks like the *declaring* skill is broken.
- FIX: `internal/closure/closure.go:288` now emits `invalid skill manifest for <name> <kind> <ref> -> <short commit> (via <chain>): <verr>`. Measured: `invalid skill manifest for leaf tag v1 -> d0775b5f70d0 (via <project> -> mid -> leaf): commands: must be an object`. The `<kind> <ref> -> <short commit>` shape is copied from the sibling `version conflict for ...` / `cannot resolve ...` messages in the same file.
- DECISION: provenance is added at the call site, never inside `skillspec.Load`. The only other caller is `skillcheck.go:32`, which backs standalone `curator skill check` and emits the protocol issue `skill.manifest_invalid`; pushing closure wording down would leak closure-only context ("via `<project>` -> ...") into a command that has no closure, and change a protocol payload. `internal/skillcheck/skillcheck_test.go` now pins that boundary explicitly.
- FINDING: `skillspec.Load` has exactly two call sites repo-wide, so the single closure wrap covers every install planning path (`install.Project`, `install.Global`, `cmdAudit`) — all of them reach manifest parsing only through `closure.Build`. `install.validateNodes` -> `skillcheck.Validate` re-parses the same snapshot but is unreachable for manifest errors: `closure.Build` runs first over the same directory with the same deterministic parser. Left alone rather than given a second, divergent provenance format.
- GOTCHA: `gofmt -l .` at repo root is useless in this checkout — `.temp/` holds an unpacked go1.25.1 source tree plus a vendored worktree from earlier tasks and it reports thousands of unrelated files. Scope it: `gofmt -l ./cmd ./internal`.
- SCOPE: `internal/closure/closure.go` (only production change), plus tests in `internal/closure`, `internal/install`, `internal/skillcheck`. `go build ./...`, `go test ./...`, `go vet ./...`, `golangci-lint run` all exit 0. Conformance gates not run — `CURATOR_CONFORMANCE_ROOT` is unset here and `conformance_test.go` self-skips.
- STATUS: pending review (`to-review`).

### 2115 — `TASK-260822-27bvo4`: the duplicate-run collision resolved by merging, and a spawn that finishes is not a spawn that hands off

- FOLLOW-UP to the `2038` entry below. `RUN-260822-1e48d3` completed with `exit=0`, attached three artifacts and checked the board items — and its own final message says "I'll run the board handoff once `go test ./...` reports". It never did. The run ended, the task stayed in `development`, and its work sat uncommitted in `.temp/TASK-260822-27bvo4/worktree`. LESSON: a spawn log ending `exit=0` proves the process finished, not that the role handoff happened; the board status is the only signal that means that. Deferring the handoff behind a long-running gate is where the two come apart.
- CONVERGENCE, SECOND TIME TODAY (cf. `2035`): two runs that never saw each other's code reached the same one-line `physicalPath` diagnosis, the same test-only probe-mode technique for observing `os.Executable` in a started process, and near-identical test names. Independent convergence on the fix is worth more than either run's own confidence in it.
- DECISION: merged rather than shipped two branches. Adopted the peer's test files and probe mode wholesale — better naming (`identity_test.go` beside `identity.go`), wider junction coverage — and grafted in the three cases this run had that they lacked: a launcher link **loop**; the physical file **swapped for a link** after resolution (`Verify()` must refuse); and `TestWorkerProvesTheInstalledIdentityWhenLaunchedThroughALink`. That last one covers a boundary the peer's end-to-end test cannot reach: with `managerExecutable` pointed at a link the manager still re-execs the *physical* file, so the worker's own `resolveExecutableIdentity` at `workerserver.go:88` never sees a link unless the worker process itself is started from one.
- GOTCHA: a cross-compiled `go test -c` binary is not a native gate. Shipped to the Windows host it runs the pure-identity tests fine, but every fixture test that compiles a stub `go` dies with `fork/exec \Users\iv\.goenv\versions\1.25.5\bin\go.exe: The system cannot find the path specified` — the binary carries the *build* host's toolchain path. Full-suite numbers have to come from a real checkout on the host.
- MEASURED on that checkout (`C:\Users\admin\TASK-260822-27bvo4-507162`, go1.25.5 windows/amd64): merged tree `go test ./internal/godriver/ -count=1` exit **0** (93.7s); same tree with `identity.go` reverted to `filepath.EvalSymlinks`, junction test exit **1**. Red→green on one host, one command apart.
- SCOPE: `internal/godriver/identity.go`, `main_test.go`, `worker_test.go`, new `identity_test.go`, new `identity_windows_test.go`. Branch `task/TASK-260822-27bvo4-symlink-launcher-507162`.
- STATUS: ready for review.

### 2038 — `TASK-260822-27bvo4`: the manager could not identify itself when installed behind a Windows junction

- ROOT CAUSE: `godriver.resolveExecutableIdentity` canonicalized with `filepath.EvalSymlinks`, not with `physicalPath` — the rule the rest of the driver resolves by since `BUG-260731-33v6zz`. On Windows a directory junction is `ModeIrregular` (Go 1.23+), `EvalSymlinks` follows only `ModeSymlink`, and `walkSymlinks` refuses to descend through a non-directory component, so a path *through* a junction fails outright. An install directory published behind a junction — the GitHub Actions tool-cache shape, and the ordinary way a machine-wide install points at a versioned directory — made the manager reject its own executable with `build_execution_worker_identity_invalid: cannot canonicalize the manager executable`, killing every `go-v1` build.
- MEASURED ON A REAL WINDOWS HOST (`ssh win`, go1.25.5 windows/amd64), before/after on the same box: pre-fix binary FAILs `TestExecutableIdentityResolvesAnInstallDirectoryJunction` with that diagnostic and records `filepath.EvalSymlinks(...) = The system cannot find the path specified.`; post-fix binary PASSes. Cross-compiled test binary shipped over scp — no repo sync needed for a `-test.run` slice that touches no testdata.
- FIX: `internal/godriver/identity.go:56` → `physicalPath(absolute)`. No-op on unix (`platform_unix.go:16` is literally `EvalSymlinks(Clean(path))`); on Windows it is `GetFinalPathNameByHandle`, which resolves every link kind of every component in one call. Resolving *more* makes the boundary stricter, not looser: the substitution checks then run against the physical file, so retargeting the link afterwards cannot move the identity.
- FINDING — `os.Executable` does not canonicalize on macOS or Windows, measured rather than assumed: darwin returns the runtime's `executablePath` verbatim (`$GOROOT/src/os/executable_darwin.go`), Windows returns the `GetModuleFileName` launch path, Linux `readlink`s `/proc/self/exe` and is already physical. Launched through a link, a darwin process reports `.../curator-launcher.test` and a Windows one `...\curator-launcher.exe`. So the canonicalization step in `resolveExecutableIdentity` is what stands between an ordinary Homebrew/Scoop-style install and self-rejection — proved by mutation: strip it and all three positive launch-shape tests go red with `the manager executable is not a canonical regular file`.
- NOTE — method: a test-only second hidden mode (`launchShapeMode` in `main_test.go`, beside `WorkerMode`) lets a test start this binary through a link and read back what the started process was told about itself. Nothing in-process can observe that; an in-process test can only assume the platform's behavior.
- ANOMALY — DUPLICATE SPAWN, SECOND OCCURRENCE TODAY: `RUN-260822-507162` and `RUN-260822-1e48d3` were both queued for this task and both live. The peer was mid-edit inside `.temp/TASK-260822-27bvo4/worktree` while this run had just created it, converging independently on the same `physicalPath` fix. Followed the `2035` entry's resolution — separate run-scoped worktree and branch (`task/TASK-260822-27bvo4-symlink-launcher-507162`), merge the supersets later. Board has no lock; the collision is only visible as two spawn-log files under `.task-board/.resources/<TASK-ID>/`.
- ANOMALY — HOST DISK: `go test ./...` failed across five packages with `no space left on device`. Data volume at 99% (10 GiB free), ~6.6 GiB of stale per-task worktrees under this repo's `.temp/`, 32 GiB Go build cache. Unrelated to any change; the full suite is not reliably runnable at default parallelism on this host right now.
- SCOPE: `internal/godriver/identity.go`, `main_test.go`, `worker_test.go`, new `identity_launcher_test.go` and `identity_launcher_windows_test.go`.
- STATUS: ready for review.

### 2037 — `TASK-260822-96m5pj` review: accepted; one determinism claim in the new parser does not hold

- VERDICT: `accepted`. `build_ssh` config field, scope grammar, longest segment-prefix match and serialization all meet AC, re-verified independently: `gofmt`/`go build`/`go vet`/`golangci-lint`/`go test ./...` (31 packages) all exit 0, `-race` on `internal/config` clean, 100.0% statement coverage on all nine `buildssh.go` functions. The attached patch artifact re-diffs identical to the working tree, so the evidence is faithful rather than asserted.
- FINDING: `buildssh.go:251` ranges a `map[string]*string` literal to validate `identity` and `known_hosts`, so an entry with two invalid paths names a different field on each load — measured 172 `identity` / 28 `known_hosts` over 200 loads in a throwaway copy of the tree. The file's own comment at `buildssh.go:208` claims a config with several faults always reports the same one; that holds for the sorted scope loop, not for the fields inside an entry. Fail-closed behaviour is unaffected, only the message. Non-blocking, folded into the sibling CLI/docs tasks.
- NOTE: `BuildSSHObject` normalizes rather than round-trips a struct the parser can never produce (`Agent:false` + `AgentSocket` set serializes to `"agent": "<socket>"`, which reparses as `Agent:true`). `TASK-260822-3pkc80` has to call `ValidateBuildSSH` before `BuildSSHObject` so the CLI cannot write what the validator refuses.
- OPEN POLICY: `build_ssh` was deliberately left out of `LockableKeys` — an organization cannot lock operator SSH credential scopes from a system config. Defensible under §12.2 (credentials are operator-owned) but it is an unratified policy call; confirm with the spec owner before `STORY-260822-2v23mv` closes.
- METHOD: reviewing read-only while the tree is dirty and uncommitted — copy `internal cmd go.mod go.sum` into `.temp/<TASK-ID>/review/probe`, add probe tests there, run, delete. No probe file ever touches the reviewed checkout, and `git add -N` used to render the untracked-file diff was reset immediately after.

### 2035 — `TASK-260822-96m5pj`: the duplicate-run collision resolved — isolate in a worktree, then merge the supersets

- FOLLOW-UP to the `2024` entry below, written by the second of the two runs (`RUN-260822-fad725`). Both runs went to completion. Fighting over one checkout is not recoverable by care alone: `git diff` showed a state that was already stale by the time it printed, and a `go test` result describes a tree the other run is mid-edit on, so it is not evidence. The working move is `git worktree add .temp/<TASK-ID>/worktree HEAD --detach`, finish and gate there, then diff against whatever the peer left and land the merge once.
- GOTCHA: a fresh worktree does **not** carry untracked-but-required content. `agents/skills/skill-go-testing-tools/tuitestkit` is a `replace` target in `go.mod` and is not tracked, so `go vet ./...` fails in the worktree with "replacement directory ... does not exist" until it is copied across. `go test ./internal/config` passes without it, which makes the gap easy to miss until the full-repo gate.
- CONVERGENCE: the two independent implementations arrived at nearly the same file — same exported names, same `identity.MatchesPrefix` reuse, same `ValidCanonical` guard, in places the same comments. The only substantive fork was the `agent` field's type.
- DECISION, superseding the `2024` entry's shape: `agent` accepts `true` **or** a socket path, backed by `BuildSSHCredential{Agent bool, AgentSocket string}`. Socket-path-only looks more spec-literal (§11.3 wants a concrete `IdentityAgent=<socket>`), but on macOS the agent socket is per-login-session — `/private/tmp/com.apple.launchd.*/Listeners` — so a socket path persisted into a machine-global config goes stale at the next relogin, and the sibling CLI task specifies `--agent [SOCKET]` with the socket optional for exactly that reason. `agent: true` records the operator's *choice* of an agent; resolution reads the live socket and still passes it explicitly. `agent: false`/`null` stay rejected, so identity-only keeps one spelling.
- EVIDENCE: `TASK-260822-96m5pj_implementation-notes.md` and `TASK-260822-96m5pj_build-ssh-config.patch` on the board; both peer files preserved verbatim under `.temp/TASK-260822-96m5pj/`. Post-merge gates in the main checkout: `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...`, `golangci-lint run`, `go test -count=1 ./internal/config`, `go test -race -count=1 ./internal/config`, `go test ./...` — all exit 0.

### 2028 — `TASK-260822-27bvo4`: the manager executable identity boundary used the one canonicalization rule the driver already rejected as incomplete

- ROOT CAUSE: `resolveExecutableIdentity` canonicalized the running manager with `filepath.EvalSymlinks` (`internal/godriver/identity.go:39`, pre-fix), while every other canonicalization in the driver — `selectToolchain`, `verifySelectedRoot`, the `mustPhysical` test helper — goes through `physicalPath`. On unix the two are the same function. On Windows they are not, and `internal/godriver/platform_windows.go:20-36` already documents why in its own comment.
- REGRESSION (Windows only): a manager installed under a directory published as a **junction** was refused at the identity boundary with `build_execution_worker_identity_invalid: cannot canonicalize the manager executable`, before any substitution check ran. Since Go 1.23 `os.Lstat` reports a junction as `ModeIrregular`, not `ModeSymlink`, so `walkSymlinks` refuses to descend and `EvalSymlinks("<junction>\\curator.exe")` fails outright with "The system cannot find the path specified." That is the operator's launch shape treated as a fault — the inversion `profiles/manager.md` orders against: resolution first, rejection targets substitution. Not hypothetical: the GitHub Actions Go tool cache is published exactly this way.
- FIX: `resolveExecutableIdentity` canonicalizes with `physicalPath(absolute)` (`internal/godriver/identity.go:53`). Unix behavior bit-identical; on Windows every link kind resolves in one `GetFinalPathNameByHandle` call. Resolving makes the downstream checks stricter, not looser — they apply to the physical file, so retargeting the link cannot move the identity the manager and worker agree on.
- FINDING: `os.Executable()` reports the **link** path, not the target, on both darwin/arm64 and windows/amd64 — measured, not assumed, by re-executing the test binary through a shim symlink in a new test-only hidden mode (`identityProbeMode`, `internal/godriver/main_test.go`) and reading back what the started process resolved for itself. Only linux hands back an already-resolved `/proc/self/exe`. Canonicalization is what makes manager and worker agree on darwin and Windows, not a no-op.
- SCOPE: `internal/godriver/identity.go`, `internal/godriver/identity_test.go` (new), `internal/godriver/identity_windows_test.go` (new), `internal/godriver/main_test.go`. darwin: `go test ./internal/godriver/` 0, `go build ./...` 0, `go vet ./...` 0, `golangci-lint run` 0, `gofmt -l cmd internal` empty. windows/amd64 over `ssh win`: full `go test ./internal/godriver/` 0 (248.8s); the junction test against a pre-fix baseline of the same tree exits **1**, which is the intended red.
- NOTE: `internal/managerlock/identity.go:63,84` also canonicalizes with `filepath.EvalSymlinks`, but for the *lock path*, not the executable. Out of scope here; worth a separate look at whether the same junction shape reaches it.
- STATUS: ready for review.

### 2025 — `TASK-260822-5wfdfx`: manifest precedence probed with `Stat` turns a broken manifest into a silently wrong spec

- ROOT CAUSE: `skillspec.Load` decided manifest precedence with `os.Stat` and treated **every** stat failure as absence (`internal/skillspec/parse.go:28-40`, pre-fix). A dangling `csk-skill.json` symlink therefore loaded `agents/runtime.json` and returned `SourceFile: agents/runtime.json` with the legacy commands; an unreadable snapshot directory returned the empty pure-context spec. Both are the silent-empty-spec failure mode recorded for the Python side in §1615 — wrong context/script inclusion downstream, and vacuous assertions instead of red ones.
- FINDING: the *other* half of the task was already correct at HEAD. A present-but-unparseable manifest (malformed JSON, `schema_version: 99`, invalid body) has always been terminal; nothing fell back. The defect was strictly in presence detection, not in parse-error handling.
- FIX: `Load` now probes through `manifestPresent` (`internal/skillspec/parse.go:50-59`), which uses `os.Lstat` and returns an error for any failure that is not `fs.ErrNotExist`. A manifest symlink counts as present even when its target is gone. Same rule applied to `agents/runtime.json`, so an unreadable legacy manifest is an error rather than a pure context skill.
- FINDING (method): mutation-checked in a throwaway module copy, never in the live checkout. Swallowing the parse error kills 12 cases; reverting only presence to `os.Stat` kills exactly the three new presence tests and leaves the schema-99 tests green — the tests diagnose which defect regressed, not merely that one did.
- DECISION: no `.github/ci/platform-cases.tsv` row added. `internal/skillspec` has no tier-1 rows, and all three mode-000/symlink skip reasons were verified to match `allow` rows of class `host-capability` in `.github/ci/skip-classes.tsv`, so tier 2 covers them.
- ANOMALY: two implementer spawns (`RUN-260822-c1807e`, `RUN-260822-b89c6b`) ran concurrently on this single task and both wrote `internal/skillspec/parse_test.go` in the same checkout; a neighbouring run separately left `internal/config/buildssh_test.go` NUL-truncated long enough to redden `go vet ./...`. Reconciled by hand, but a same-task duplicate spawn is a board-level hazard, not a local one.
- SCOPE: `internal/skillspec/parse.go`, `internal/skillspec/parse_test.go`. `go test -count=1 ./...` 0, `go vet ./...` 0, `golangci-lint run` 0, `gofmt -l cmd internal` empty, build 0. `make ledger-check` is **red (exit 2)** on six pre-existing rows naming packages absent from the module (`internal/transaction`, `internal/install/atomicity`, `internal/godriver`) — untouched by this task.
- STATUS: ready for review.

### 2024 — `TASK-260822-1yz9ug`: decision 0009 landed — `-mod=vendor` makes local `replace` mean something other than what the surface suggests

- FINDING: `go mod vendor` copies a locally replaced module **into** `vendor/`, and under `-mod=vendor` the compiled bytes come from that copy. The replacement directory is never a compiler input. Measured with a two-module repro using the spec's exact fixed argument vectors.
- FINDING: `Module.Replace.Dir`/`GoMod` in the `go list` stream are derived lexically from the `go.mod` text — Go never stats them. With the replacement directory renamed away, `go build` and `go list` both still exited 0 and still reported the same `Dir`. A manager cannot read `Module.Replace` as evidence about the tree; decision 0009 §4 forbids it explicitly and validates the *declared* directory against the snapshot instead.
- FINDING: directory-form replacement and module-to-module redirect are exactly separable from the same stream. Directory form → `Dir`+`GoMod`, no `Version`. Redirect → module path + `Version`, no `Dir`/`GoMod`. Precise enough for Go and Python implementations to share one predicate.
- FINDING: no `replace` directive can hide by going unused. A directive absent from `vendor/modules.txt` fails the fixed `go list` with `inconsistent vendoring: ... is replaced in go.mod, but not marked as replaced in vendor/modules.txt`, before `go build`. The complete effective replace set is materialised in a file below the build root that `curator-build-source-v1` already hashes — the bijection is observable with no new argument vector.
- DECISION: because a replaced first-party module *is* vendored, it matches decision 0005's "vendored non-standard package" predicate. Left unscoped, module roots would let package-controlled `SFiles` and `//go:cgo_import_dynamic` reach the build under an allowance written for audited third-party deps — `go mod vendor` as a laundering path. 0009 §5 scopes 0005's exceptions to results whose module carries **no** replacement. This narrows the current trust boundary; not a regression, since replaced modules are rejected outright today.
- SCOPE: `curator-spec` `decisions/0009-first-party-module-roots.md`, merged to `main` as `b92b105` via PR 24, decision-only. Local CI parity green before push (validate 0, 91 unittests 0, `go test ./tools/...` 0, deterministic regeneration 0, gofmt 0); all PR checks and both post-merge `main` workflows green.
- STATUS: unblocks TASK-260822-3nvx91 (core.md prose + schema). Six open questions written for that task to settle.

### 2024 — Decision numbering: `origin/main` is the only safe source, and three unmerged drafts already claim 0009-0011

- ANOMALY: `curator-spec` `origin/main` carries a duplicate at `0005` — `0005-external-build-repositories.md` and `0005-vendored-go-boundary-relaxation.md`. Two prior collisions are why the task mandated verifying against `origin/main`.
- FINDING: `draft/TASK-260728-12pnm1-rust-driver` holds `0009`, `draft/TASK-260728-168smo-kotlin-native-driver` holds `0010`, `draft/TASK-260728-1yhuqi-swift-driver` holds `0011`. None are on `main`, so `0009` was correctly free and is now taken by `b92b105`.
- NOTE: whoever lands those drafts must renumber. Checking `git ls-tree origin/main decisions/` alone will not warn them — the conflict only surfaces at merge, and Git will not flag it because the filenames differ.

### 2024 — `TASK-260822-96m5pj`: two implementer runs were dispatched for one task and co-edited the same new file, leaving `config.go` non-compiling

- ANOMALY: `progress.md` for the task records **two** started implementer spawns, `RUN-260822-13cbfb` and `RUN-260822-fad725`, both `[implementer] developer (claude)` under `max_parallel=20`. Both wrote `internal/config/buildssh.go`. The file went through three distinct designs within minutes: `BuildSSHCredential` with string `Agent`, then a `BuildSSH` struct with `Agent bool` + `AgentSocket`, then back to `BuildSSHCredential`.
- REGRESSION: both runs applied the same `Parse` wiring, so `internal/config/config.go` ended up with a **duplicated** `buildSSH, err := parseBuildSSH(data["build_ssh"])` block — a redeclaration that does not compile — while `Config.BuildSSH` named a type the other run had renamed away. Neither fault is visible in a diff review of the new file alone; only `go build ./...` catches it.
- FINDING: a same-second concurrent rewrite reads exactly like a deliberate harness edit ("changed on disk since you last read it"). The tell is the *type* mismatch against the caller, not the rewrite itself. Check `go build` before trusting a file that changed under you.
- DECISION: converged onto the third design and kept it — it reuses `identity.MatchesPrefix` (the segment-aware matcher the source allowlist already uses at `internal/identity/identity.go:125`) instead of a second hand-rolled prefix rule, and validates credential paths as rooted. Added a `identity.ValidCanonical` guard to `BuildSSHFor` so a local source, bare host, or non-canonical value selects no credential, and made a present-but-empty `agent`/`identity`/`known_hosts` string a fault rather than a silently absent field.
- SCOPE: `internal/config/buildssh.go` (new), `internal/config/buildssh_test.go` (new), `internal/config/config.go`. `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...`, `golangci-lint run`, `go test ./...` all exit 0; 100% statement coverage on every `buildssh.go` function.
- STATUS: task work resolved and handed to review. The dispatch duplication itself is unresolved and belongs to whoever configured the spawn.

### 2020 — `TASK-260822-1l4r4f`: decision 0008 is not implementable as written — three conflicts found while resolving its five open questions

- FINDING: capability *values* have no normative semantics anywhere in curator-spec. `"repo"` and `"home-config"` occur exactly twice repo-wide — `protocol/core.md:557` and `schemas/v1/common.schema.json:535` — both syntactic. Decision 0008 turns that object into a containment profile, so the script prose has to define every value it consumes before any vector can be written.
- ANOMALY: 0008 §3 says absent capability fields mean "no writes outside the private runtime area", but the schema default for a missing `filesystem` is `"repo"`. Decision text and schema default already disagree.
- ANOMALY: 0008 §4 reuses `capability-evidence-v1` for script evidence, but that record is closed — `core.md:437` makes any `execution_policy` other than `manager-worker-v1` a `build_execution_hardened_claim_forbidden`. A record naming `script-worker-v1` is a forbidden hardened claim by the current normative text. Resolution recommended: new `script-capability-evidence-v1` over a new `script-worker-v1-native-control-inventory-v1`, reusing 0006's shape and rules but none of its version constants.
- ANOMALY: 0008's Context cites "unshare/netns on Linux". No execution-policy ledger in this spec has a Linux cell — `core.md`/`profiles/manager.md` never mention Linux, and `rc5-native-control-inventory-v1` has exactly `[macos, windows]`. The example implies a platform column that does not exist.
- FINDING: today's script launcher blocks enforcement outright. `profiles/manager.md:503-509` requires it to preserve the inherited `PATH`, and on Unix it may be a bare symlink (confirmed in `internal/runtimestore/runtimestore.go:119-145`) — a symlink has no process in which to install controls, a POSIX-shell wrapper puts a shell in the graph the build boundary forbids, and the Windows `.cmd` routes through `cmd.exe`. Enforced commands need a manager-owned native launcher.
- DECISION: Q2 answered as neither posed option. §8.2's whole-tree fingerprint does not transfer (site-packages churn), and an `exec`-declared interpreter hands program selection to the package (`core.md:461-468`). Recommended the `driver: "go-v1"` precedent instead — closed package-declared `interpreter` identifier, manager-owned resolution under §8.2's trust rules, per-invocation identity check of the launched file only, library tree declared TCB, shebang and file association inert.
- DECISION: Q4 answered per invocation, not per install generation — `core.md:412-414` says a cached result is not a probe, and install-generation evidence is definitionally cached. Probe once at launch, never re-probed mid-session; install-time preflight added on top, same diagnostic. Evidence stays off the command's stdout/stderr.
- DECISION: Q5 — Windows at full parity in the mandatory portable set; every asymmetry in the inventory. One new cell, `descendant-exec-denial`: available on Windows only when the effective `exec` set is empty (all-or-none child-process policy), unavailable on macOS under a new reason `no-unprivileged-per-process-exec-policy`. Needs an `applicable_when` predicate and a `not-applicable` status — both safe only because the script evidence record is a new closed version.
- NOTE: the Windows child-process-policy and macOS deprecated-sandbox facts are corroborated only in-repo, by decision 0006's rejected alternatives. Vendor-doc confirmation still owed before either becomes a normative mechanism string.
- SCOPE: analysis only. Deliverable `TASK-260822-1l4r4f_analysis.md` on the board; no spec or code touched.

### 2016 — `TASK-260822-5wfdfx`: manifest fail-loud already held; pinned it, and the task description names a file that does not exist

### 0631 — `BUG-260801-nzpar0`: WinError 87 is the Win32 relative-root boundary, not the rename record or handle rights

- FINDING: CocoaSkills PR 18 heads `0314ab5` and `4cd1589` fail identically on Microsoft Windows Server 2025 build `10.0.26100`: `SetFileInformationByHandle(FileRenameInfo)` returns `ERROR_INVALID_PARAMETER` 87 when given a non-NULL root handle and simple relative target. `4cd1589` isolates the prior rights hypothesis by reopening that root with exactly `FILE_TRAVERSE | FILE_READ_ATTRIBUTES` (`0xa0`), checking its volume/file ID and final path against the selected directory, then receiving the same five-test failure. Source opens already request `DELETE` and share read/write/delete.
- ABI: Windows-target compiler layouts prove the modern DWORD/union and legacy BOOLEAN first fields are byte-compatible for no-replace: Win64 offsets `0/8/16/20`, size 24; Win32 offsets `0/4/8/12`, size 16. UTF-16LE byte lengths and `sizeof(struct)+name_bytes` are correct, and the Win64 allocation leaves four zero bytes after the name. Layout, padding, encoding, length, and buffer size are not the cause.
- ANOMALY (primary-source conflict): Microsoft's 2022 `FILE_RENAME_INFO` doc correction records that `RootDirectory` was effectively unusable/inconsistent in the Win32 implementation and told callers to use NULL (`MicrosoftDocs/sdk-api@ada04eef`). A 2026 docs-only change (`d1debc5`, PR 1123) now advertises relative-root support, but its submitter expressed uncertainty and it supplies no Server 2025 implementation evidence. The exact runner result agrees with the older implementation warning for this call path.
- DECISION: the smallest identity-safe correction is native `NtSetInformationFile(FileRenameInformation=10)` using the exact verified source and `0xa0` destination-root handles, a native BOOLEAN/aligned-HANDLE/ULONG/UTF-16 record, `ReplaceIfExists=FALSE`, and raw NTSTATUS translation. Do not ship `RootDirectory=NULL` plus an absolute path: pre/post pathname checks cannot bind the kernel's destination lookup across a root exchange.
- LIMITATION: `ssh win` was offline (SSH 255, ping 2, Tailscale ping 1), and the operator directed research not to wait. The unrun JSONL matrix is attached for later granted-access/type/identity/raw-status evidence; exact reproduction comes from hosted job logs at both heads. Evidence and full citations: `.research/260801_windows-handle-rename-winerror87.md`.
