### 1810 — `BUG-260731-1rldqv` rework: the `exist_ok` regression is closed, and the two findings the earlier entry omitted

- FIX (rework 1): `locking.provision_new_manager_home` restates the condition `mkdir(exist_ok=True)` carries — `if not csk_home.is_dir(): raise LockError(...)` — inside the `except FileExistsError` it had replaced with a bare `return`. Verified **differentially against `8a02e17` rather than by reading**: the same five-shape probe (absent / existing dir / symlink→existing dir / symlink→missing dir / regular file) run against the pre-fix module and the fixed one now agree on all five rows, and the two rows that had flipped to accept are `LockError` again. Five tests, red-then-green: 3 fail without the fix, and the 2 adoption rows pass either way, which is what proves the condition did not over-tighten.
- FINDING: the reviewer's "raw `FileExistsError` escapes on a regular file" row is right about the *symptom* but not about where it comes from — `provision_new_manager_home` itself returned cleanly on that input; the traceback surfaced from a later `mkdir` once locking used the accepted non-home. Immaterial to the fix (both are fail-open), but worth stating exactly, and the new `GlobalLock` test pins the `LockError`-not-`OSError` boundary that `cli.main` depends on.
- FIX (rework 2): the *pairwise-scan-is-gone* contract is now pinned by counting every read of the state a namespace is compared by, plus every parts-tuple equality. Three equally spaced sizes must grow by equal steps: measured **156 / 296 / 436** units for 32 / 60 / 88 namespaces, against **3,500 / 12,446 / 26,880** for a restored pairwise scan over the same memoised probes. Verified red by actually restoring that scan — it fails this test **alone**, both older canonicalisation tests staying green, exactly as the reviewer predicted.
- LESSON, generalisable: **a cost contract belongs to the commit that establishes it.** Memoising the probes and indexing them were two separate wins, and pinning only the first left the second reversible without any test going red. When an optimisation lands in stages, each stage needs its own tripwire, or the last one silently becomes optional.
- FINDING (missing from the entry below, which stopped at `7a66c73`): the deeper CocoaSkills defect was never Windows-specific. `_validate_namespace_independence` compared every namespace with every other and canonicalised **both** operands per comparison, and the pass re-runs on every journal save — **twice per 32 KiB staging chunk**. One install measured **750,620** canonicalisations, 182 s of its 210 s, *on macOS*. Windows opens a handle per path component for the same question, so only there did quadratic-with-a-cheap-constant become fatal. A POSIX-latent cost defect that a slower syscall promotes to an outage is worth looking for before the next change near a hot revalidation path.
- NOTE (tooling anomaly, cost us a wrong conclusion once): `gh run view --json jobs` returned `completedAt` values implying the three green Windows cells took ~34 minutes. The job **logs** said `8323.00s`. The API field was simply wrong. Trust the logs; do not time a matrix from that field. Corollary found this round: `gh run view --log` and `gh run view --job <id> --log` returned **empty or single-job output** for a completed run, while `gh api repos/.../actions/jobs/<id>/logs` returned the full 233 KB. When a job log looks missing, try the API endpoint before concluding anything.
- FINDING (platform truth, and I had it backwards): **`CreateDirectoryW` on a dangling symlink returns `ERROR_ALREADY_EXISTS` — it does not reparse to the link destination and create a directory there.** With `ssh win` offline I reasoned it *would* reparse, since a final path component is normally reparsed when `FILE_OPEN_REPARSE_POINT` is absent, and had a platform-tolerant version of the test staged rather than the strict one. The runner settled it: both symlink tests **ran** on `windows-latest` (0 new skips, +6 tests on every cell), so the strict `LockError`-on-every-platform assertion is correct and shipped. Worth remembering before weakening a test to accommodate a Windows behaviour that has only been *reasoned about* — Curator has the same reparse-point questions in `internal/godriver`, where a junction genuinely does behave differently from a symlink.
- STATUS: signed `f8b90a5` on `ivanopcode/cocoaskills` only, PR 16 head. **PR 16 run `30641011440` is 14/14 green** — 12 test cells, mypy strict, Build artifacts. Windows cells report **1217 passed / 152 skipped** each (672 / 851 / 493 / 660 s), against 1211 / 152 at `32737a8`. Local: `mypy` strict exit 0 (67 files); focused suites exit 0, 162 passed / 4 skipped; full suite exit 0, **1151 passed / 100 skipped** against a 1145 / 100 baseline — the delta is exactly the six new tests on both. CocoaSkills `LOGBOOK.md` carries the two code reworks; the platform fact above is recorded here rather than there, to avoid churning a green PR head for a documentation line.

### 1746 — `BUG-260731-1rldqv` CHANGES REQUESTED; the Windows fix is right, and the same commit quietly un-hardened manager-home creation

- DECISION (reviewer, `RUN-260731-2bd01b`): **changes requested → `to-dev`**. Every AC claim re-derived from primary sources rather than carried over, and all of it held: PR 16 run `30637483316` on head `32737a8` is **14/14 green**, all three commits carry good GPG signatures, `origin` is `ivanopcode/cocoaskills` only, and locally at `32737a8` `mypy` strict is exit 0 (67 files) with the full suite **exit 0, 1145 passed / 100 skipped**, matching the reported figure exactly. The five root causes are correctly diagnosed and fixed at the producer, never at the guard.
- FINDING — **the "guard is not weakened" claim is testable, so I tested it rather than reading it.** `32737a8` replaces the O(n²) pairwise namespace scan with three index lookups, the riskiest edit in the change. I copied the **old** pairwise algorithm verbatim out of `8a02e17` and ran it against the **shipped** indexed scan over identical namespace sets on a real filesystem — duplicates, containment at several depths, hardlink alias, symlink-to-dir, symlink-to-file, dangling symlink, absent paths, absent-under-absent-parent, `entry`/`bytes` mixtures. **1771 exhaustive pair/triple sets + 4000 randomized sets = 5771, zero disagreements.** The indexed form is in one respect a *superset*: the old code reached the physical-identity check only for pairs surviving the parts checks. This is the cheap, decisive way to review an algorithmic rewrite of a guard — diff the *decisions*, not the code.
- FINDING: the "no-op on POSIX" digest claim is also a compatibility claim, since digests persist in receipts and install markers, so it was measured too — `digest_path` over a tree of `.cmd`/`.exe`/`.bat`/plain files across five modes is **byte-identical** at `8a02e17` and `32737a8`. Nothing on POSIX is invalidated.
- REGRESSION (the reason this is not accepted): `7a66c73` rewrote `csk_home.mkdir(mode=0o700, parents=True, exist_ok=True)` as a bare `mkdir` plus `except FileExistsError: return`. **`Path.mkdir(exist_ok=True)` does not swallow that exception** — CPython's body is `if not exist_ok or not self.is_dir(): raise`, so it tolerates an existing *directory* and re-raises for anything else. Dropping the `is_dir()` condition means a non-directory at the manager-home path is now accepted: a symlink → *missing* directory went from `LockError: cannot create manager home` to **silently materialising the home at the link destination at mode `0o755`, never provisioned** — skipping both the `0o700` create mode and, on Windows, the entire ownership/DACL stamp that `7a66c73` exists to apply. A regular file went from `LockError` to a raw `FileExistsError` that `cli.main` does not catch.
- FINDING: severity is **latent, not live** — reachability was checked, not assumed. Every `GlobalLock(cfg.path.parent)` site in `cli.py` is preceded by `config.load_config()`, which fails first on a non-directory home, and `csk bootstrap` already died on this input *before* the change. Still rework: the task scope names ownership/provenance guards explicitly, the defect is inside the new ownership-provisioning code, `provision_new_manager_home` was newly promoted to a public two-caller function, and no test covers the case — which is what let it through. Fix is two lines and a test.
- LESSON, generalisable: **an `exist_ok=True` → `except FileExistsError` rewrite is never a refactor.** `pathlib` attaches a type check to that flag that the bare `except` silently discards. Worth grepping for wherever a "create if absent" call gets restructured.
- NOTE: independent cross-language corroboration — the CocoaSkills diagnosis (`0x410` directory-junction reparse point; a manager home made by a plain `mkdir` inherits its parent's ACEs and can never satisfy an owner-only de-inherited DACL) matches what `BUG-260731-33v6zz` found in the Go implementation, arrived at separately the same day. Two codebases, two languages, same platform truths.
- ALSO ROUTED: `32737a8`'s cost contract (that the *pairwise comparison* is gone) is unpinned — a pairwise scan over memoised probes would leave both existing cost tests green while reinstating the 364,635 comparisons that were the dominant Windows term. And the entry below stops at `7a66c73`; the quadratic `_validate_namespace_independence` and the `gh run view --json jobs` `completedAt` anomaly (reported ~34min against an actual 8323.00s in the job logs — trust the logs) belong in this record too.
- EVIDENCE: `BUG-260731-1rldqv_independent-review-verdict.md`.

### 1551 — `BUG-260731-33v6zz`: Curator could never build or publish a compiled command on Windows

- FINDING: the 14 unowned `Test (windows-latest)` failures were not one bug but six, stacked. Each fix uncovered the next, and the `cmd/curator` half turned out to be a chain in which **no compiled command could be built or published on Windows at all**. Six CI rounds; no local Windows host exists, so every verdict came from raw `go test -json` in the artifact, never a job summary.
- ROOT CAUSE (go-v1 boundary): the GitHub Actions tool cache publishes Go behind a **directory junction**. Runner facts: `C:\hostedtoolcache\windows\go\1.25.5\x64`, `attributes=0x410`, `reparseTag=0xa0000003` (`IO_REPARSE_TAG_MOUNT_POINT`), `statDir=true`, `lstatDir=false`. Since Go 1.23 (`os/types_windows.go` `fileStat.mode()`) a junction is a reparse-tag *name surrogate*, so the `ModeDir` branch is skipped, and its tag is not `IO_REPARSE_TAG_SYMLINK`, so it falls to `default: m |= ModeIrregular`. `filepath.EvalSymlinks` follows only `ModeSymlink`, so `selectToolchain` resolved links, found something neither directory nor link, and refused the host's own working toolchain.
- FINDING: `filepath.EvalSymlinks` is worse than incomplete for a junction that is **not** the last component — `walkSymlinks` refuses to descend through a non-directory mode, so `<junction>/bin/go.exe` fails outright with `ENOTDIR`. Any `EvalSymlinks` + `Lstat().IsDir()` pair in this repo has the same blind spot on Windows.
- FIX: `physicalPath` — `EvalSymlinks` on unix, `GetFinalPathNameByHandle` on Windows — for the root, the launcher and `absolutePhysical`, so all three agree what a path is. Stricter, not looser: the fingerprint and the `verifySelectedRoot` `os.SameFile` anchor now bind to the physical directory, so retargeting the junction cannot move the trusted toolchain under a live session. `internal/godriver/{platform_windows,platform_unix,session}.go`.
- ROOT CAUSE (build cache): nothing in Curator ever attached a protected DACL to the manager home. It is made by `os.MkdirAll`, a Windows directory made that way inherits its parent's ACEs, and `ensureProtectedBase` requires an owner-only de-inherited DACL — so **no publication on Windows could ever succeed against a real manager home**. `internal/buildcache`'s own suite passed only because `newTestStore` calls `protectTestHome` first. Unix has no gap: the `mkdir(0o700)` `managerlock` uses on the same home already satisfies `validateUnixDir`.
- REGRESSION (caught in CI, fixed in the same branch): hardening the home with a `NO_INHERITANCE` owner-only ACE locked the owner out of its own new files — `open ...\global\.curator-txn-...: Access is denied`, same on a manager lock. A protected DACL that propagates nothing gives every later child an **empty** DACL, unopenable even by its owner. The home is the parent of manager locks, transaction files, the global root and the registry cache, all created by ordinary `os` calls that rely on inheritance.
- FIX: a directory's owner-only entry propagates to sub-containers and objects, a file's stays non-inheritable, and `validateWindowsDACL` accepts propagation flags while still rejecting `INHERITED_ACE`. An inherit-only entry contributes no effective rights, so the existing inherit-only refusal is unchanged. `internal/buildcache/protection_windows.go`.
- ROOT CAUSE (concurrency): `ensureProtectedBase` created a directory then applied its DACL. In that window a concurrent publisher sees inherited entries and correctly refuses the publication as untrusted provenance — for state the manager had just created itself. Fixed by `CreateDirectory` + `SECURITY_ATTRIBUTES` carrying `SE_DACL_PROTECTED`, giving Windows what `mkdir`'s mode argument gives Unix.
- FINDING: three of the 14 were Unix-shaped *expectations*, not defects — an identity compared against the on-disk spelling where `identity_windows.go` folds case-insensitive components by design, POSIX path literals that are neither absolute nor clean under `filepath` on Windows, and a runtime profile pinned to `"unix"` on a host with no POSIX execute bit.
- DECISION: `internal/identity`'s drive-letter fix was taken **byte-identical** from `BUG-260731-27h1yc` rather than written independently, so the two PRs merge without conflict; that branch keeps the unit coverage.
- DECISION: `internal/godriver` was outside this bug's scope because `BUG-260731-lepevi` was working in it concurrently. That PR merged mid-task; this branch rebased onto it and PR 11 touched `controls_other.go`, not `session.go`. Crossing the boundary was flagged in the PR rather than assumed, and the change restores the boundary's existing intent that a Go 1.23 stdlib change had broken.
- SCOPE: `internal/godriver/{platform_windows,platform_unix,session}.go`, `internal/buildcache/protection_windows.go`, `internal/identity/identity.go`, plus test-side fixes in `internal/{managerlock,staging,globalbins}` and a new `cmd/curator` host-toolchain diagnostic.
- NOTE: `cmd/curator` is the only place the real `build.Default.GOROOT` path runs — `internal/install` uses `fakeToolchain` and `internal/godriver` injects a synthetic `hostFacts` — which is why seven cases failed identically there and nowhere else. The diagnostic case that prints the selection inputs is what made the last three rounds possible.
- STATUS: `.github/ci/platform-cases.tsv` and `skip-classes.tsv` byte unchanged. PR 13. Still red and now unowned: `internal/godriver TestFingerprintReportsUnreadableDirectoryIdentically` and `internal/transaction` (5) were mapped to `BUG-260731-lepevi` and survived that PR, whose AC covered only the Linux lane.

### 1537 — `BUG-260731-27h1yc`: the Windows install lane was 70 fake assertions, and one real product defect underneath

- ROOT CAUSE: `internal/install` and `internal/install/atomicity` pinned `opts.Platform = "unix"` in their harnesses and ~25 call sites. On a Windows host that is not a portable default — it is a shape the platform cannot build. `internal/runtimestore/scripts.go:172` validates a staged **unix** script runtime for a POSIX execute bit no Windows file carries, and `internal/runtimestore/targets.go:96` refuses a natively compiled artifact against a unix shim. Every baseline install failed before its case asserted anything: 68 of the 70 in-scope failures are those two error strings.
- FINDING: the ledger's Windows rows for these packages were nominal, not real. `internal/install TestEndToEndInstall` is required on `windows` with `skip_allowed_on -`, and it had never once materialised a Windows shim. A required row proves nothing if the fixture cannot construct the platform it names.
- ROOT CAUSE (product, not test): `internal/identity` refused every local Windows checkout. `identity.Parse` reaches an scp-style fallback whose path group is `[^\\]+`, so `C:\repos\skill` never matched, fell to the "contains a colon" branch and came back `malformed or unsupported network source`. `C:/repos/skill` was already local by the existing single-letter-host rule; the backslash spelling was not. **`curator` could not install a skill from a local path on Windows at all.**
- FINDING: `TestCanonical` carried a `C:\repos\skill` case that passed the whole time, because `Canonical` discards `Parse`'s error and returns `""` for both "local" and "malformed". A helper that swallows an error can keep a defect green for the life of a repository.
- FINDING: `os.OpenRoot` pins its directory on Windows. Go 1.25.5 `syscall/syscall_windows.go:396` opens with `FILE_SHARE_READ|FILE_SHARE_WRITE` and never `FILE_SHARE_DELETE`, so while a `buildsource` token is frozen the kernel refuses to rename or delete that directory — and every enclosing one, since Windows blocks renaming a directory with an open handle in its subtree. `TestFrozenTokenRejectsRootReplacement` failed in its *setup*, never reaching `Recheck`. Same mechanism holds an open declaration document against an atomic rename in `internal/install`.
- FINDING: `bad:name` is not a filename on NTFS — the colon opens an alternate data stream, so the entry that lands is the perfectly portable name `bad` and `Validate` returns `nil`. The Windows analogue is a trailing dot, which `identifiers.PortableComponent` refuses precisely because Win32 normalisation strips it; creating it needs the `\\?\` extended-length prefix, and `os.RemoveAll` cannot delete it afterwards.
- DECISION: no case skipped or platform-excluded. `TestFrozenTokenRejectsRootReplacement` tolerates no skip, so its Windows half asserts the kernel refusal *and* drives the replacement the way Windows does allow one — repointing a directory reparse point in the path prefix, junction fallback, `t.Fatal` (not `t.Skip`) if the host can create neither.
- FIX: platform derived from the host, shim assertions named through a `shimName()` helper, user-bin PATH prepared on every runner, `identity.Parse` accepting a drive-letter prefix in either spelling, `buildsource` and `generation` fixtures platform-split.
- SCOPE: `internal/identity/identity.go` (only product change), `internal/buildsource/{buildsource,rootreplacement*}_test.go`, `internal/install/*_test.go`, `internal/install/atomicity/*_test.go`.
- STATUS: resolved on `windows-latest`. Run 30626331508: all three packages **pass**, all 8 required ledger rows report `ok`, `skips-observed.tsv` identical to the `main` baseline. Baseline run 30624569953 on `main` @ `3a047d5` had 91 failures, 70 of them here. PR 12, awaiting review.
- NOTE: the ledger is strengthened, not relaxed — `TestGlobalInstall` and the global rollback sweep now assert the PATH-visible forwarding shim and user-bin mirror ledger on Windows, a class they previously bypassed with a `runtime.GOOS != "windows"` guard.
- NOTE: `internal/install/atomicity` costs 1081 s on `windows-latest` against 71 s on macOS once its installs actually run. It was 16 s while every install failed instantly. Inside the 30 m per-package timeout, but the Windows lane is now ~15x slower than it looked.


### 1520 — `BUG-260731-3a5q1p`: the rc.6 dry-run gap is 1 unbound scope **plus 18 unbound effects**, and the whole binding table was unfalsifiable

- ROOT CAUSE: rc.6 adds a third authoritative dry-run case, `compiled-cache-miss-is-read-only` / `scope: multi-project`, to `vectors/manager-lifecycle.json`. `internal/install/dryrun_conformance_test.go` failed on its own `default:` at the scope switch — but that was only the *first* wall. The case publishes **28** forbidden persistent effects against rc.3's 9, and **18 of them had no binding either**, so `assertNoEffect`'s `default:` was the next failure. The bug report's "no executable binding for the scope" understates the work by an order of magnitude.
- FINDING: rc.6 also adds five per-case fields the older cases never carried — `allowed_go_commands`, `forbidden_go_commands`, `artifact_executed`, `logical_cache_key`, `operation_private_state_after`, `reported_build_outcomes`. `internal/interop` (on PR 9) gates their *vocabulary*; nothing bound them to a run.
- DECISION: Curator's multi-project operation **is** `install --all` / `upgrade --all` (`cmd/curator/main.go:520`, `:635`) — every target planned in one run, one manager home, one skills root, one shared `FetchedRepos` set, in canonical project-identity byte order per `profiles/manager.md` §2.5. A dry run acquires **no** lock (`managerlock.ErrDryRun`), so canonical order is §2.5's only remaining obligation for planning. No new product API was invented for the binding.
- FINDING: the real toolchain probe runs **exactly** `go telemetry off`, `go version`, `go env -json` (`internal/godriver/session.go:220`–`248`) — the published `allowed_go_commands` verbatim. And `goBuilder.Stage` is the only thing in an installation that runs `go list`/`go build` — the published `forbidden_go_commands`. Those two facts let the binding use the **real** toolchain and the **real** `buildcache.Store` and still assert the Go-command contract exactly, with only the builder replaced by one that records and refuses.
- FINDING: `buildcache.New` and `Store.Inspect` create nothing, so `<home>/cache/build` stays absent through a real read-only miss. That is what makes `compiled-artifact-cache` assertable against the production cache rather than a fake.
- DECISION (the load-bearing one): each effect binding is now a **probe** returning where its surface is visible, not an assertion that fires. `TestDryRunEffectBindingsSeeWhatARealOperationWrites` runs the same probes backwards — a real project install + real global install + real non-dry `managerlock` operation produce **21 of 27** surfaces in production locations, and every binding must then see them. Before this, a binding pointed at a path Curator never writes would have passed forever. Completeness is asserted too, so a new effect cannot gain a binding without gaining a witness.
- FINDING: a real armed install writes, in production paths, all of `<home>/audit`, `<home>/cache`, `<home>/cache/registry`, `<home>/consumers.json`, `<home>/runtime`, `<home>/state/{locks/v1{,/projects,/build},registry,transactions}`, `<home>/global/{bin,skills,env.sh}`, `<project>/.agents/{bin,skills,env.sh}`, `.csk-install.json`, `.claude/skills/.csk-managed.json`, and `<skillsRoot>/clonable` + `.git/FETCH_HEAD`. That single dump is the map the whole effect table was derived from — worth re-running before adding any effect.
- DECISION: dropped `t.Parallel()` from `TestAuthoritativeDryRunCasesMutateNothingPersistent`. `t.Setenv` (via the package's existing `isolateTempDir`) forbids a parallel ancestor, and TMPDIR isolation is the only race-free way to bind the published `operation_private_state_after: absent`. Scheduling change, not an assertion change; ~0.2 s at rc.3. Non-parallel top-level tests never overlap the parallel batch, so the isolation is sound.
- FINDING: several effects share one root because that is Curator's layout, not laziness — `permission-repair`/`quarantine`/`compiled-artifact-cache` → `<home>/cache/build` (the protected boundary is the only surface whose modes a manager establishes or repairs; an untrusted entry is quarantined, never permission-repaired), and `revocation-state` → `<home>/state` (Curator keeps **no** separate revocation store; a revocation is decided from signed registry records plus configuration).
- FINDING: six mutations each made the new assertions fail, then were reverted — vacuous binding, single project, diverging cache keys, planted surface, renamed scope, unbound effect. Both `default:` branches confirmed intact.
- FINDING: `root-artifacts.tsv` declared only `vectors/build-drivers.json` for `internal/install`, yet this test has always read `vectors/manager-lifecycle.json` unguarded. Pre-existing table gap; now declared, so a root without it **defers** the package instead of failing it on `open`.
- REGRESSION (not mine): the full rc.6 gate on this branch exits 1 on `internal/interop TestManagerLifecycleVectors` — `golden_test.go:488` hardcodes `len(vector.DryRunCases) != 2`. That is `BUG-260731-3gm8kc` / PR 9's scope, fixed there by `fee35c8`. On `main` + `bd6ba08` + this patch the same gate exits **0** with `CI_REQUIRE_FULL_ROOT=1`, `served=40 deferred=0`. So the two bugs together are what clear rc.6, and **neither alone does**.
- BLOCKED (needs a human, not a decision): `~/.ssh/ivanopcode` is passphrase-protected and the passphrase is not in this session's keychain — `ssh-add --apple-load-keychain` loads nothing, `security find-generic-password` misses, `ssh -T git@github.com` is `Permission denied (publickey)`. No signed commit, no push, so no PR and no Linux/Windows CI. Note this differs from the earlier memory that the keychain trick works: it does **not** work in a non-interactive spawn. Change saved as a patch artifact plus the exact push/dispatch commands.
- STATUS: `internal/install` green at rc.6 (`go test ./internal/install/...` exit 0), rc.3 green, `-race` green, lint/vet/gofmt/ledger/no-broad-suppression green. macOS only; three-platform proof pending the push.


### 1450 — `BUG-260731-1rldqv`: CocoaSkills PR 16 Windows failures are four platform facts, none of them transaction-engine logic

- ROOT CAUSE (28 of 45): CPython `Modules/posixmodule.c:update_st_mode_from_path` ORs `0o111` into `st_mode` for `.bat/.cmd/.com/.exe`, and it is reachable **only** from path-based stat. `Python/fileutils.c:_Py_fstat_noraise` has no path, so it cannot. Measured on `windows-latest`: `tool.cmd` → `lstat=0o777`, `fstat=0o666`, same inode. `transactions._digest_file` compared those two directly, so every command shim tripped the corruption guard. Never `.csk-install.json` — that name has no executable extension. That asymmetry in the bug report was the whole tell.
- ROOT CAUSE (12 of 45): Windows records a symlink's type on the reparse point, and a **file** link that lands on a directory cannot be traversed — `os.stat` → `WinError 5`. A staged adapter link is dangling by construction (its destination is relative to the *live* location), and `transactions._staging_tree_entry` derived `link_is_directory` by resolving that destination → `False` → file link. Probe confirms: dangling dir link has `st_file_attributes=0x410` (DIRECTORY set) while `is_dir()` returns `False`.
- FINDING: the 3.14 cell's 34-vs-45 delta is *exactly* those 12 and is independent confirmation — `Path.exists()` on 3.14 swallows the `PermissionError` and returns `False` where 3.11 propagates it. Same broken reparse point, different reporting.
- ROOT CAUSE (4 of 45): new Windows objects belong to the token's **owner**, not its user. On the runner `_current_user_sid()` is RID 500 (built-in Administrator) while every created object is owned by `S-1-5-32-544`. The manager therefore owned neither the home it created nor the artifact its own compiler produced, and the protected build cache correctly rejected both. POSIX hands the manager that state for free via `st_uid` + `mkdir(0o700)`; Windows grants neither, and CocoaSkills never established it.
- FINDING: none of the ownership path was reachable on `main` — `main`'s installer only *plans* builds (`builds.planner` and nothing else). `c4131bd` is the first commit where `installer.install` compiles and publishes, so it is the first code to reach those guards on Windows. Latent gap, newly exposed, not a newly written bug.
- DECISION: fix the **producer**, never the guard. Manager provisions a home it creates (`locking.provision_new_manager_home`) and makes a fresh artifact private before publication (`builds.cache.make_publication_source_private`). An established home is never re-provisioned, so `test_windows_manager_home_permissions_and_owner_drift_fail_closed` still holds and real drift still fails closed.
- DECISION: digests and change guards now use the *permission identity* Windows can actually hold (the writable bit) instead of the synthesized mode. No-op on POSIX — full macOS suite byte-identical at 1136 passed. This also fixes a latent defect where identical bytes digested differently under a staging sidecar name vs. a live `.cmd` name.
- FINDING: the fixes were validated by staged `windows-latest` probe jobs, each removing exactly its own signature and exposing the next, with no signature reappearing. Final probe: `253 passed, 55 skipped`, success.
- NOTE: `ssh win` (tailscale `mbpro-win`) was **offline** for the whole task. GitHub Actions on a throwaway `debug/**` branch with `on: push` was the substitute Windows harness — put diagnostics in their own **job**, not step, since job logs are only downloadable after the whole job ends.
- BLOCKED (product decision, not this task): a Windows home created by an elevated shell *before* this change stays Administrators-owned and fails closed. Adopting it would blunt the drift guard. Explicit opt-in repair vs. documented manual re-provisioning is unresolved.
- STATUS: PR 16 full matrix `30624304158` running on `7a66c73`; `mypy strict` already green.

### 1442 — `BUG-260731-11bpa4` ACCEPTED; the Windows lane is now observable, and landing PR 10 is what unblocks its two owners

- DECISION (reviewer, `RUN-260731-d744df`): **accepted → `done`**. Every claim re-derived from primary sources — Actions API step conclusions, both uploaded gate artifacts, the GitHub contents API, the source tree — rather than carried over from the developer report. All of it held.
- FINDING: the percent coverage that survives the fixture reduction is **stronger than the developer reported**. `targets_windows_test.go:57` asserts `helperPath` = `dependency path %PATH% Юникод` **verbatim** through the `set` line, `:48` exercises the `call` line with `immutable cache % Юникод`, and all three shim destinations (`:62`–`:64`) carry a `%`. Only the *argument* side lost its `%VAR%` case — precisely the narrowed contract, and not one byte more.
- FINDING: the three new `PrepareScriptRuntime*` skips are justified **at source**, not asserted — `scripts.go:172` requires `Perm()&0o111 != 0` when `platform == "unix"`, which no file on a Windows host can carry. They classify `allowed-platform-control` under an existing regex, none is in `platform-cases.tsv`, and none ever ran on Windows because the package never compiled. No coverage regression.
- FINDING: `main` has **no branch protection** — `GET /repos/relux-works/curator/branches/main/protection` returns `404 Branch not protected`. There is no GitHub-enforced required-check set, so "land only after required CI is green" is a project-policy judgement, not a gate. Worth knowing before the next agent treats a red job as a hard merge block.
- DECISION: **land PR 10.** Zero of the 117 red Windows cases are attributable to the diff, and holding it *blocks* `BUG-260731-27h1yc` and `BUG-260731-33v6zz` — until it lands, `main`'s Windows job still dies at step 7 `go vet` and `go test` never runs there, so neither owner can obtain a baseline. This PR is the prerequisite that makes the whole Windows lane observable.
- NOTE: the reviewer archetype is read-only and supplies no `commit_ack`, so the merge was **not** performed here; it is the commit-owning mover's action and the verdict artifact is its acceptance evidence. Checklist item 4 left **unchecked** — the review half is done, the landing half is not, and ticking it would be false.
- NOTE: three non-blocking observations recorded rather than sent back as rework — `parseHelperOutput` uses `fmt.Errorf` with no format verbs where `errors.New` fits; `scripts.go` supports `platform == "windows"` script runtimes but no test constructs one (**pre-existing**, belongs with the Windows-lane bugs); and the path-`%`-pairs-with-argument-`%` scenario is deliberately no longer exercised, since a correctly escaped path cannot pair and a `%`-bearing argument is now out of contract.
- STATUS: `BUG-260731-11bpa4` **done**. PR 10 open and unmerged — the single remaining action. Evidence: `BUG-260731-11bpa4_review-verdict_RUN-260731-d744df.md`.

### 1426 — The derived `call`-line quadrupling rule survived the Windows runner; runtimestore is clean there

- MILESTONE: `BUG-260731-11bpa4` AC met. On CI run 30622852198 job 91131187109 (`windows-latest`), `internal/runtimestore` reports **37 case results and 0 failures**, `go vet` success, and **`TestWindowsPostInstallWrappersForwardArgumentsPathAndExitCode` PASSES** — the case that had never once executed in repo history.
- FINDING: the 1400 entry's caveat is discharged. The quadrupling rule was *derived, not executed*; it is now executed and green. The runner did not disagree, so no empirical override was needed.
- FIX: `escapeCMDCallValue` at `internal/runtimestore/runtimestore.go:216`, applied only to the `call` line (`:191`); `set "PATH=..."` (`:182`) stays at one pass. Written as `escapeCMDValue(escapeCMDValue(v))` so the code says "one escape per expansion pass" rather than a magic `%%%%`. Signed commit `3cee1c1` on PR 10.
- FINDING: both conformance tests pass **on the Windows runner**, confirming the 1400 prediction that context-correct escaping is byte-identical on `%`-free fixture paths. No `SPEC_PIN` re-pin, no protocol change.
- DECISION: `internal/globalbins/globalbins.go:353` byte-compares stored shim bytes against a fresh `WindowsShimContent`. A shim installed by an older manager under a `%`-bearing path now compares unequal and is treated as unowned. **Accepted as-is, no globalbins change**: such a shim is provably non-functional (its single-doubled `%` is deleted by pass 2, so it points at a mangled path), and both call sites emit a non-fatal advisory rather than failing. Declining to adopt a broken artifact is correct; loud beats silent overwrite.
- FINDING: the 1402 entry's prediction is **confirmed**. Baseline-vs-head diff of the uploaded gate artifacts (117 failures each) shows exactly one new failure, `cmd/curator :: TestCompiledInstallFollowsTheNativeControlInventoryExactly`, with the same `go_toolchain_missing: trusted GOROOT is not a real directory`. It is not from this diff — `git grep` proves the test exists **nowhere on this branch**; it arrived via `main` (`b6e523b`, sibling PR 11) into the PR merge commit. `BUG-260731-33v6zz` should count it, as 1402 said.
- FINDING: `internal/globalbins :: TestSafeSelectionFeedsStagedForwardingTargetWithoutLiveMutation` fails **identically at baseline and head**. Worth naming because that package consumes `WindowsShimContent` — it is pre-existing, not escaping fallout.
- NOTE: reproducing the suite locally with `go test ./...` and `CURATOR_CONFORMANCE_ROOT` exported for every package yields 8 **spurious** failures (`vectors/build-drivers.json: no such file or directory`). `.github/ci/test-gate.sh` deliberately splits served packages (root exported) from deferred (`env -u`), because pinned spec `00b1688` does not publish those vectors. Run the gate script; a blanket `go test ./...` is the wrong gate.
- ANOMALY: `golangci-lint` caches by module path, and every task worktree here shares `github.com/relux-works/curator`. A stale cache reported findings against **another agent's worktree** by absolute path. Re-run with a fresh `GOLANGCI_LINT_CACHE` when paths look wrong.
- STATUS: PR 10 ready for review, **not landed**. `Test (windows-latest)` stays red on 116 pre-existing out-of-scope failures plus the one from `main`; all other jobs green, including Lint (the two sibling `unused` findings were fixed by PR 11 landing). Landing is a review decision gated on `BUG-260731-27h1yc` / `BUG-260731-33v6zz`, not on anything in this diff.

### 1402 — The six Linux-carved-out compiled cases FAIL on Windows, for a reason that is not the inventory

- FINDING: `BUG-260731-lepevi` carved six `cmd/curator` compiled cases out of Linux with `must_run_on=darwin,windows` and stated the Windows half was "a correct declaration, not an observed pass". It is obtainable: PR 10's run 30620739038 got the Windows lane past `go vet`, and its `test-evidence-windows-latest/test/observed-cases.tsv` records **all six as `fail`** — not skip.

- FINDING: this is **not** `rc5-native-control-inventory-v1`. `InventoryPlatform("windows")` returns `"windows"`, so `requireNativeControlInventoryPlatform` is inert on Windows and the new carve-out is not implicated. Pre-existing from `cfffd7c`, observed on a branch that does not contain the carve-out.
- DECISION (reviewer): this **validates** keeping `must_run_on=darwin,windows`. Narrowing those rows to `darwin` to make them look satisfied would have hidden a real Windows defect behind a ledger edit. Consistent with the 1400 entry's "ledger rows are opt-in strictness".

The other two are `TestAuthoritativeUpgradeCasesAreExecutable` and `TestDryRunNeverClaimsACompletedCompilerCheck` — the latter a compiled case deliberately **not** carved out (it needs no completed compilation) and correctly absent from the ledger.
