### 0416 — Build-source Windows recheck omits root ADS and ignores failed stream-handle cleanup

- REVIEW: `TASK-260720-3c0ss2` cycle 3 requests implementation rework. Replacing cached Windows `DirEntry.stat()` identity with no-follow `os.lstat()` is correct, but the named-stream boundary is incomplete.
- ROOT MUTATION: `_validated_root_stat` enumerates root streams only during initial freeze. `FrozenSnapshot.recheck()` uses raw root `lstat` plus a descendant scan, so a persistent ADS added to the snapshot root during the consumer callback survives the last-child recheck. The independent seam recorded root checks `('.', 'file', '.')` during freeze, only `('file',)` during recheck, and `root_stream_mutation_failed_closed=False`.
- CLEANUP: `_windows.named_data_streams` calls `FindClose` in `finally` but ignores its Boolean result. A seam returning zero with error 5 produced `find_close_failure_result=()` and `find_close_failure_failed_closed=False`.
- REQUIRED: enumerate/reject the root on every recheck and map that failure to `SnapshotMutationError`; add native root-directory ADS initial/mutation cases; surface `FindClose` failure with immediate last-error capture and deterministic chaining; cover first/next/close error branches in the platform-independent seam.
- REWORK: the root is now enumerated before every descendant re-scan, so persistent root and descendant ADS additions during `FrozenSnapshot.use()` fail as `SnapshotMutationError`. `FindClose` failure captures `GetLastError` immediately; when enumeration also fails, the enumeration `OSError` stays primary and the cleanup `OSError` is its explicit cause. Platform-independent first/next/close seams and native root/descendant ADS tests cover the boundary. Final local gates: source 28 passed / 4 Windows skips, accepted-root focused 207 passed / 4 skips, strict mypy 58 files, full 716 passed / 5 skips, build/package/compile/diff checks green. Native Windows CI remains sequenced after review-authorized publication.
- GREEN CONTROL: accepted-root focused pytest passed 198 with 1 Windows-only skip, strict mypy passed 58 source files, the signed `2734beff` base and three-file product scope match, and tracked/new-file diff checks pass. Evidence: `TASK-260720-3c0ss2_review-verdict-cycle-3.md` and the cycle-3 probe/test/provenance outcomes.

### 0318 — Telemetry containment followed a relocated config symlink outside the private operation root

- REVIEW: `TASK-260720-3j8pp5` requests implementation rework. The candidate verifies `GOTELEMETRYDIR` below `config_root.resolve()`, but never proves that the resolved config root remains below the operation-private root created before the probes.
- REPRODUCTION: an isolated `ProbeRunner` replaced the Darwin `HOME/Library/Application Support` directory with a symlink to an external directory during `go telemetry off`, then returned the exact version and fixed `go env -json` shape with `GOTELEMETRY=off`. `establish_toolchain` returned a trusted session even though `reported_telemetry.is_relative_to(operation_root)` was false. `close()` removed the nominal operation root but the external telemetry directory remained.
- ROOT CAUSE: `_validate_probe` receives only `layout.config` and compares two resolved paths; once the config path is repointed, both values agree outside the private boundary. The ordinary outside-directory test keeps the config root intact and therefore misses this case.
- REQUIRED: retain and validate the operation-root/config-directory identity across all three probes, require the physical config root and reported telemetry directory to remain within the private operation root at the platform-expected location, reject symlink/reparse replacement, and add this outward-repoint regression including cleanup assertions.
- GREEN CONTROL: independent focused pytest passed 59 tests, strict mypy passed all 57 source files, full pytest passed 633 with 19 skips, the task-file style gate passed, and a real Go 1.25.5 Darwin/arm64 probe reproduced the producer identity and clean nominal teardown. The verdict is based on a security false accept, not a failed existing gate.

### 0312 — A marker-consistent pre-exclusion install bypasses schema-6 build-root isolation

- REVIEW: `TASK-260720-3c0ss2` requests implementation rework. The candidate excludes declared build roots when it rebuilds context, but `_install_skill_context_to_root` returns `up-to-date` before that selector runs.
- ROOT CAUSE: `_marker_is_current` validates source/ref/locale/activation metadata and then compares the installed tree with the marker's own `content_sha256`; it never proves that `plan.spec.build_roots` are absent from the installed tree or marker `files`. A pre-patch schema-6 marker can therefore describe and authenticate the contaminated tree to itself.
- EVIDENCE: an isolated probe seeded a valid candidate install with the exact files and recomputed marker that the pre-exclusion installer would have written. The next install exited cleanly, reported `skill-build ... up-to-date`, and left `assets/build-tool/go.mod` agent-visible. Focused rc.5 + task tests still passed 151/151, strict mypy passed 57 files, and the full suite with both accepted roots passed 702 with 1 skip; the existing up-to-date test only repeats an already-sanitized candidate install and cannot detect migration/currentness failure.
- REQUIRED: currentness must fail when a declared build-root subtree is present (and should reject stale marker-file entries beneath one), forcing a sanitized reinstall. Add the marker-consistent legacy-tree regression and rerun the independent gates.
- ANOMALY: the producer result artifact transcribes two vector digests incorrectly. Authoritative/candidate values are empty `sha256:3a518980ed122b2139e46152d9c4dda7426a42572f3235cde8cbe781566f5753` and binary/empty/marker `sha256:68008c9a1131c1295d78f4f7d184c3df5f7382a88d8d40333be7cf02b2ee4de9`; the implementation and tests use those correct values.

## 2026-07-29

### 2340 — golangci-lint's cache is keyed on content, not path, so a fresh mirror replays another tree's file paths

- ANOMALY: the first `golangci-lint run` inside a byte-exact mirror of the accepted composite (`.temp/BUG-260729-1o0m8f/worktree`) reported all four findings against `../../TASK-260720-1pvfj5/rework/composite/...` — paths outside the tree being linted. Exit code and finding set were correct; only the locations were another checkout's.
- ROOT CAUSE: the result cache is keyed on file content. An rsync'd mirror hits every cache entry the source tree populated, and the cached issue carries the path recorded when it was stored.
- DECISION: `golangci-lint cache clean` before **every** measured run. Any lint evidence gathered in a copied or worktree-mirrored tree without that step is unciteable — the paths may name a tree the reader cannot inspect, and a stale hit could mask a real difference.
- NOTE: applies to any workflow that measures a candidate by mirroring it. `go test` is unaffected; its cache keys include the package's import path and build inputs.

### 2338 — The three accepted-composite lint findings were all removable in source; two were false positives worth deleting anyway

- FIX: `internal/protocoljson/ccj.go:211-212` — the `G115` rune→byte narrowing existed only to index a 16-entry hex table under a `character < 0x20` guard. Indexing with the rune directly (`hexadecimal[character>>4]`) removes the conversion. Ranging a string never yields a negative rune, so this is not a bounds risk; for `0x00..0x1f` the emitted bytes are identical.
- FIX: `internal/transaction/journal.go:398` — `G602` fired on `entries[index-1]` behind a short-circuit `index > 0`. `validateRemovalEntries` now carries `previousPath` forward instead of reading behind the cursor. Predicate, error text and fail-closed behaviour unchanged.
- FIX: `internal/godriver/builddriver_positive_conformance_test.go:176-179` — the `ineffassign` was genuinely dead: `environment` was never read after its own nil check, and `indispensableEnvironment()` is a pure reader on both platforms. Four lines removed; the function keeps three other callers.
- DECISION: no `nolint`, no `.golangci.yml` exclusion. A suppression would have preserved two conversions/indexings that bought nothing; deleting them is smaller than annotating them. Pinned v2.12.2 goes **exit 1 → exit 0**.
- FINDING: proof of semantic equivalence is direct, not argued. The two new test files (`ccj_test.go`, `journal_order_test.go`) pass **against the original accepted sources** (exit 0) as well as the refactored ones, and the C0 case is exhaustive over `0x00..0x1f` with byte-exact expectations. Three mutations — upper-cased hex table, guard narrowed to `< 0x1f`, dropped `previousPath` update — each go red, so the coverage is not vacuous.
- FINDING: `TestFixedEnvironmentAndFiveDirectArgvFormsVector/fixed_environment` **cannot run against the committed `SPEC_PIN`** — that root publishes no `vectors/build-drivers.json`, so `loadPositiveVectors` skips. Exercising it needs the rc.5 candidate root (`.temp/TASK-260729-3nx97g/worktree/conformance/v1`, manifest `b6f56aac…`, 28 `fixed_environment` keys). Before/after subtest ledgers there are identical apart from wall time.
- SCOPE: exactly five files differ from the accepted composite — the three above plus two new test files. `.golangci.yml` and `.github/workflows/ci.yml` compare byte-identical.
- STATUS: resolved, handed to review as `BUG-260729-1o0m8f`. Closes finding (1) of the 2322 entry in full, including the `ineffassign` left without an owner at 2333.

### 2334 — A racing repetition is not a gate: 100,000 attempts missed a bug a 0.47 s deterministic sweep pins at budget 17

- FINDING: the first fix attempt gated the cancellation contract by repeating the racy subtest 200× per run. Measured against **deliberately reverted** product code, that gate never goes red: `-race -count=50` (10,000 racing attempts) **exit 0**, `-count=500` (100,000 attempts) **exit 0**, and 2,000 attempts with a freshly written 256 KiB fixture per attempt **exit 0**.
- ROOT CAUSE: the window only opens under CPU contention. The original flake was observed while this run's focused race **overlapped `TASK-260720-1pvfj5`'s final race gate**; on an unloaded machine `go cancel()` is scheduled before the first file is ever read, so the racy loop samples the wrong phase every time. Repeating a race harder does not widen a scheduling window — loading the machine does, and no test may depend on that.
- DECISION: the proof is deterministic. `countdownContext` (`fingerprint_equivalence_test.go`) returns nil for N `Err()` calls then cancels, placing cancellation on an exact check — including one taken between two chunks of a single file. `TestFingerprintCancellationStaysFailClosed/every_cancellation_point_stays_a_deadline` walks the budget 0→N until a budget completes cleanly, asserting every outcome is `nil` or `toolchain_timeout`. Against the reverted code it fails at **budget 17 in 0.47 s**; it is self-extending, so a cancellation check added later is covered the moment it exists.
- NOTE: the racy subtest is kept as a ~5 ms smoke check with a comment stating plainly what it does *not* prove. Keeping it silent would have left the next reader trusting it.

### 2333 — Cancellation inside the digest copy loop was misreported as toolchain mutation; the sentinel makes the precedence narrow

- ROOT CAUSE: `digestToolchainRecords` (`internal/godriver/fingerprint.go:104`) collapsed every non-clean record copy into one bucket — `copyErr != nil || closeErr != nil || written != size` → `toolchain_mutated`. `copyWithContext` checks `ctx.Err()` at the top of each chunk and returns the partial count with that error, so a cancellation landing *inside* a file's bytes reported `toolchain_mutated`, while a cancellation landing anywhere else in the same pipeline (walk descent, per-entry, per-record) correctly reported `toolchain_timeout`. Which one appeared was pure scheduling — the intermittent race-gate failure recorded at 2322.
- FIX: `copyWithContext` tags **its own** context check with package-private `errCopyAbandoned` wrapping the real cause; new `digestCopyDiagnostic` branches on that sentinel alone. Everything else — read error, close error, a length that moved on its own, and a bare `context.Canceled` that did not come from that loop — still fails closed as `toolchain_mutated`.
- DECISION: precedence is deliberately *narrow*, not "cancellation wins". Only the abort this package raises counts, so a concurrent write cannot be laundered into a deadline; pinned by `TestDigestCopyDiagnosticPrecedence/bare_context_error_is_not_this_loop's_abort`. This is a reclassification between two refusals — both codes fail closed, no fingerprint is produced, no toolchain is trusted — and `toolchain_timeout` was already emitted by the walk, so no consumer sees a new value. The error still unwraps to `context.Canceled`.
- SCOPE: `internal/godriver/fingerprint.go` + `internal/godriver/fingerprint_equivalence_test.go` only; every other godriver file byte-identical to the accepted `TASK-260720-jrrgw9` candidate. Patch applies clean to `.temp/TASK-260720-1pvfj5/rework/composite` (`git apply --check` **exit 0**).
- STATUS: resolved, handed to review. `-race -count=1000` on the cancellation + precedence tests **exit 0** (28.0 s; 200,000 racing attempts, 1,000 deterministic sweeps). One of the two findings from 2322 is now closed; the `ineffassign` at `builddriver_positive_conformance_test.go:178` is **confirmed pre-existing** — the CI-pinned linter gives byte-identical output and exit 1 on the accepted baseline worktree — and still needs an owner.

### 2322 — The CI gate must derive its own shape from the conformance root, because the product is ahead of the released pin

- FINDING: with the committed released pin (`00b1688a`, rc.3) exported to every package, the accepted compiled-build composite is **red by construction**: `go test -count=1 ./...` **exit 1**, 1577 pass / 33 skip / **10 FAIL** across 7 packages. All ten are unguarded reads of rc.5-only artefacts (`vectors/build-drivers.json`, `vectors/external-repository-lifecycle.json`, `schema-cases/install-marker-v2`, `expected/build-driver/{marker,context_files}.json`, `schema-cases/agent-skill-v6`, `fixtures/go-build-skill`). The implementation is simply ahead of the released protocol; no CI wiring can wish that away.
- DECISION: resolved entirely in `ci.yml` + `.github/ci/` with **zero product-code changes**, by making the run *plan itself from the supplied root*. `root-artifacts.tsv` declares each package's unguarded artefact reads; `suite-plan.sh` splits the module into served (root exported) / deferred (root unset, missing artefact named) / excluded. Against the pin: 33 served, 7 deferred. Against the rc.5 candidate: **40 served, 0 deferred**. `CI_REQUIRE_FULL_ROOT=1` in the candidate lane makes any deferral fatal, so a candidate run cannot be green while a package quietly ran without its vectors.
- DECISION: the Linux exclusion of `internal/godriver` is read from the **root's own** `vectors/conformance-claim-v3-qualification.json` (`{"name":"linux","status":"excluded","until_task":"TASK-260728-1skseh"}`), not hardcoded — so `TASK-260728-1skseh` flipping that vector restores Linux coverage with no CI edit. `default_excluded_on` covers only a pre-vector root like the committed pin. The exclusion is **asserted, never assumed**: `TestProbeRejectsAnUncoveredPlatformBeforeTheWorker` runs on the excluded runner and must pass (measured **exit 0**), proving `probeNativeControlsFor("linux")` refuses before any worker starts.
- FINDING: `go list` with `GOOS=` set reports the exact per-platform test file set, so **every ledger claim about Linux and Windows is checkable from a macOS host with no runner**. `ledger-consistency.sh` caught two invented case names during authoring (`adapters::TestSymlinkModeUsesRelativeLinks` and an `install/atomicity` case that exists only on `origin/main`) — exactly the class of error the superseded `RUN-260729-9ead43` outcome shipped. Go's implicit `_windows_test.go` filename constraint also matters: `targets_windows_test.go` carries no `//go:build` line yet is windows-only.
- DECISION: skip enforcement is two-tier rather than one enumerated list. Tier 1 = 49 named ledger rows (37 required on darwin, 32 windows, 31 linux). Tier 2 = 35 reason-regex classes derived from **every** skip literal in the composite; an unrecognised reason is fatal, so a new skip is caught the first time it runs. `root-unset` is `deferred-only`, which is what makes the candidate lane's no-silent-skip guarantee mechanical. Measured darwin: candidate lane 12 skips (0 root-unset), pin lane 45.
- STATUS: pending, **two open findings in accepted product code, neither fixable inside a CI-scoped task.** (1) `golangci-lint` v2.12.2 **exit 1**: `G115` ×2 at `internal/protocoljson/ccj.go:211,212` (guarded by `character < 0x20`) and `G602` at `internal/transaction/journal.go:398` (guarded by short-circuit `index > 0`) are false positives — note the identical pattern at `journal.go:385` is *not* flagged; plus a genuine `ineffassign` at `internal/godriver/builddriver_positive_conformance_test.go:178`, which v2.4.0 also reports and so predates the version pin. (2) `TestFingerprintCancellationStaysFailClosed/cancelled_between_the_walk_and_the_digest` (`fingerprint_equivalence_test.go:519`) is an intermittent race-suite flake: it admits `nil` or `toolchain_timeout` but the racy `go cancel()` can also yield `toolchain_mutated`. Failed 1 of 2 full `-race` runs; **0 failures in 185 targeted `-race` executions** including `-cpu 1,2,8`. Both routed to the owner of those files.
- NOTE: race timings on the composite are comfortable — `internal/install/atomicity` 86.7 / 87.5 s against the 480 s bar, `internal/transaction` 34.6 / 35.3 s, long pole `cmd/curator` ~415 s inside the 30 m per-package timeout. No timeout was inflated, no pin moved, `.golangci.yml` untouched, and the 356 product manifest entries are byte-identical to the accepted `jrrgw9` candidate (`cmp` exit 0).

- MILESTONE: `TASK-260720-jrrgw9` verifier 4 ran the exact integrated candidate under the immutable rc.5 conformance root. Focused authoritative 12-package barrier **exit 0, 15.26 s**; exact `go test -count=1 ./...` **exit 0, 352.86 s**; exact `go test -count=1 -race ./...` **exit 0, 441.11 s**. No timeout override, shared-cache clearing, overlapping Go process, skip, or assertion change.

The full race log contains no `WARNING: DATA RACE`, `DATA RACE`, or `FAIL`.

### 2028 — `golangci-lint` capped its own report at 3, so a "fix these three" constraint was structurally incomplete

- FINDING: `.golangci.yml` sets neither `max-same-issues` nor `max-issues-per-linter`, so golangci-lint's defaults apply — **`max-same-issues: 3`**. `TASK-260729-365r5r`'s full-repo run reported exactly three `revive unused-parameter` findings in `internal/transaction/namespace_pass_test.go`. That was a **truncated view, not a complete count.** A fourth instance existed at line 144 and was invisible until the first three were fixed and a scoped re-run went red on it.
- ROOT CAUSE: the review verdict and the follow-up rework constraint both enumerated "the three" because they read the linter's output as exhaustive. It never was. A cap that silently elides same-class findings makes "fix everything the linter reported" and "make the linter green" different tasks.
- FIX: four renames, not three — `t *testing.T` -> `_ *testing.T` at the `nested live paths`, `repeated live path`, `backup sidecar` and `cleanup tomb` cases of `TestValidateIndependentTargetNamespacesRejectsOverlappingPaths` (`internal/transaction/namespace_pass_test.go:119,127,136,144`). The other three closures use `t` and were left alone.
- NOTE: the cap also gives the completeness proof. The scoped re-run reported **1** issue while the cap allows 3, so that count could not have been truncated a second time. Under a capped linter, "N < cap findings reported" is the evidence that N is complete; "N == cap" never is.
- DECISION: exceeded the constraint's literal three-item list to hit its own stated acceptance bar (zero introduced findings). Stopping at three would have honoured an enumeration derived from a capped report and guaranteed a fourth red handoff. Flagged explicitly for the reviewer rather than absorbed silently.
- MILESTONE: `TASK-260729-365r5r` lint blocker cleared. `golangci-lint run ./internal/transaction/...` **exit 0, `0 issues.`** Full repo **exit 1** with one issue — the inherited `ineffassign` in `internal/godriver/builddriver_positive_conformance_test.go:178`, byte-identical to the never-edited baseline twin and absent from the delta manifest. Zero introduced findings.
- SCOPE: one file, eight lines, one hash moved across 392 manifested files. `namespace.go` bit-identical (`bb332038…`) before and after, so the accepted 66 s / 84-76-75 s atomicity evidence is not staled — a `_test.go` file in `package transaction` is not an input to any `internal/install` build.
- STATUS: pending independent review. Only open design question is the mid-pass detection tradeoff inherent to per-pass identity reuse.

### 2015 — The 12.5% ceiling that killed the fixture trim was arithmetic fiction; the trim died at 493 s instead

- ROOT CAUSE: entries 1935 and the `TASK-260729-rfrdfo` cycle-2 artifact both rejected the `references/info.md` trim with `3 files x 5 saves = 15` and a "self-limiting" `reduction_max(C) = 5C/(60+20C)` capped at 12.5% against a required 14.4-18.8%. **Both numbers are wrong.** Removing the file removes **2** staging entries, not 1 and not 3 — the file *and* the now-childless `references/` directory, because `captureRemovalEntries` walks directories too (`internal/transaction/journal.go:506-539`). So `3 saves x 2 entries + 2 saves x 1 chunk = 8` per affected context, not 5. Corrected best case at the measured C=3 is **17.14%**, *above* the 14.44% required. **The arithmetic could never have excluded the lever.** Review finding R2-1 caught it.
- FINDING: the denominator was wrong too. `saves = 3N + 5F` used `N = 20` (an alarm-frame figure never re-derived; measured `staged_targets` is **12-18** per transaction) and charged nothing for directory entries. Correct staging count is `3*entries + 2*chunks`, which reproduces all eight measured rows exactly: saves 100->84, 140->116, 110->94, 107->91, every delta exactly `8 x affected contexts`. Real save reduction **14.55-17.14%**.
- DECISION: fixture trim **still rejected — on measurement, not arithmetic**. `TASK-260729-2afulh` built the 14-file patch and ran it: focused count-one race **exit 0 at 493 s** (`ok ... 492.231s`, no `DATA RACE`) against the strict `<=480 s` bar. **13 s over, negative margin.** Non-race 286 -> 273 s. Realized 12.12% off the best inherited run vs 14.44% required.
- FINDING: **model calibration, the durable output of the whole detour.** Corrected save model predicts 14.55-17.14%; the race arm delivered 13.91% against the inherited mean. Journal saves are a large but not exclusive share of race-instrumented wall time, so a save-count model runs ~1-3 points optimistic as a wall-clock predictor. That constant did not exist before this measurement.
- NOTE: honest limit on the rejection. The acceptance predicate is universally quantified over repetitions, so one run at 493 s falsifies it and reps 2/3 were correctly not completed. But that proves the trim **fails to demonstrate margin** — not that it can never land under 480 s. The inherited spread was **32 s**, larger than the 13 s miss. Recorded so nobody re-opens it on "you only ran it once", and nobody repeats the over-claim of impossibility.
- FINDING: the successor settles the order of magnitude. `TASK-260729-365r5r` stored gates: atomicity race **84 / 76 / 75 s**, non-race 66 s vs 306 s same-session baseline — **~5.7x headroom** where the trim was 13 s *over*. Every test-side lever moves the **save count**; only the production fix moves the per-save **O(P^2) -> O(P)**. Its own acceptance is still open (lint red, four gates need re-run, review pending).
- DECISION: `rfrdfo`'s 13-file patch **not widened**. Patch A accepted (226.191-235.124 s). Patch B kept as recorded: exit 0 at 591.280/560.828/564.022 s, misses the bar, not over-claimed. No timeout changed, no skip, no assertion weakened, no full-repo race.
- NOTE: method lesson. When a lever is cheap to measure, **measure it**. Cycle 2 dressed an unmeasured bound as a structural proof and its own honesty statement flagged the weakness (`F` "statically bounded, not measured") while drawing the opposite conclusion from it. The reviewer was right to refuse. The measured answer landed in the same place — but only by 13 seconds, and the model said it should have cleared.
- SCOPE: `TASK-260729-rfrdfo` cycle 3, evidence-only. **No Go command of any kind run**; no product or prototype file edited. Partial probe artifacts from cancelled `RUN-260729-5e854d` (`.temp/TASK-260729-rfrdfo/measure/`) excluded and not cited.

### 1945 — Allocation scaling, not wall clock, is what proves an O(P²)→O(P) claim

- FINDING: `TASK-260729-365r5r` hoists per-path work out of the pairwise namespace sweep in `internal/transaction/namespace.go`. Wall-clock speedup is real but *ambiguous* — 6.1x/10.0x/15.4x/20.6x at P=57/113/225/449 — because the in-memory pairwise comparison stays O(P²) by design, so the time column stays super-linear and proves nothing on its own. **Allocation counts settle it**: baseline x3.47/x3.70/x3.84 per P doubling (→4 = quadratic), prototype x1.99/x1.99/**x2.00** (= linear). Per-path allocations: baseline 362→2267, prototype flat **95.7→96.4**. When a change only removes the *filesystem* half of a quadratic, allocs isolate that half and wall clock does not.
- DECISION: reuse is **per validation pass only** — `resolvedNamespacePath` lives in a slice local to `validateIndependentTargetNamespaces`, never on `Engine`. Two earlier attempts on this task were rejected for cross-save verdict caches (`namespaceGraphAccepted`/`acceptNamespaceGraph`, `Engine.namespaceChecked`). Every `saveJournal` still re-resolves live filesystem facts. Verified: `Engine` byte-identical, 4 fields; zero package-level vars in `namespace.go`.
- FINDING: the identity read must stay **lazy** (`resolveNamespacePath` deliberately does not stat). The baseline only asks for identity *after* a pair survives the containment test, so an eager read would surface an inspection failure for a path a containment overlap would have rejected first. Hoisting the cheap pure work while leaving the expensive impure work lazy is what preserves error precedence.
- DECISION (tradeoff, disclosed not hidden): per-pass reuse is **strictly weaker mid-pass** — baseline could catch a mutation landing between pair (A,B) and pair (A,C); the snapshot cannot. Accepted because neither version is atomic or locked, the window is one pass, and the property that matters (mutation *between* saves fails closed before any write) is asserted behaviourally in `namespace_pass_test.go:222,289` including on-disk-bytes-unchanged checks. Inherent to the approach — if rejected, reject the prototype, not patch around it.
- ANOMALY: inherited `evidence/call-path-proof.md` §6 cited `evidence/equivalence-check.md` as behavioural confirmation. **That file never existed and the check had never been run.** The `equivcheck/` tree was correctly built (product byte-identical to baseline, test minus the 2 white-box prototype-only cases) but nobody ran it. Also §1 annotated a `wc -l` as 24 when it returns 25. Both would have failed a reviewer's re-run. **A cited artifact is not evidence until `ls` finds it** — audit citations for existence, not just plausibility.
- FINDING: `run()` in the gate driver wrote `99` and marched on when the process barrier was busy, so a foreign Go process merely *between* two of its own gates could poison a whole run — this is what produced the earlier `gate-atomicity-structure=99` non-result. Now retries acquisition ~40 min. `99` is barrier refusal, never a test outcome.
- SCOPE: product delta is **one file**, `internal/transaction/namespace.go`; plus new `namespace_pass_test.go`. `engine.go`/`journal.go`/`types.go` byte-identical, journal schema untouched, no `-timeout` token. Patch reproduces the tree byte-identically, exit 0, no fuzz/offset/reject.
- STATUS: pending. The decisive AC clause — atomicity margin ≤480 s — has **no measurement yet** for this prototype; §5's 20x is a different question from whether `internal/install/atomicity` drops from ~561-593 s under 480 s. Gates queued behind the `TASK-260729-2afulh` Go slot. Do not integrate on the benchmark alone.
- NOTE: `golangci-lint` is **not installed** on this host, so `make lint` cannot run; `gofmt` + `go vet` are the runnable parts of `make check`.

### 1941 — Seven cycles of contract review, and the eighth blocker was the title line

- FINDING: `TASK-260729-osjeay` revision 7 closed every cycle-6 executable-contract finding and its 55-case harness reproduced green under an independent reviewer — and was still rejected, because lines 1/3/5/25 said "revision 6", "rework cycle 5", "Supersedes: revision 5", "Cycle 5 (this revision)". Six revisions of edit-in-place discipline updated the body's correction tables every time and never touched the header. The header is what a producer reads first, so it outranks the sections that contradicted it.
- ROOT CAUSE: the document's per-cycle sections (`§1.2` … `§1.2e`, `§1.3`, `§10` blocks) are *append*-shaped, so each cycle naturally adds one; the header is *replace*-shaped, so each cycle must remember to rewrite it. Append survives inattention; replace does not.
- FINDING: the same class was in three more places the verdict did not name — `§10`'s preamble still scoped "this cycle" to rows 53-57 in a revision that had added 58-62; line 16 called cycle-5 `ssh`/`scp` stubs "new this cycle"; five correction tables were headed "Correction in this revision" across revisions 2-6. All fixed by naming the cycle/revision explicitly and deleting every relative "this".
- DECISION: relative self-reference ("this revision", "this cycle", "new") is banned from any multi-cycle artifact header or table heading in this audit line. Write the absolute cycle number. A reader who opens the artifact cold has no way to resolve "this".
- FINDING: `§10` row 61 cites line numbers for each `1.25.x` hit, so any header edit silently falsifies it. Header edits were kept line-neutral and the `grep` re-run after: **11** hits (was 10; the eleventh is the sentence describing the re-search), all classified, **zero** requirement-shaped. Line-number citations are a maintenance liability that only pays off if re-measured every edit.
- SCOPE: metadata only. Map `d6e2c6a9…` → `89613c58…`, 3096 → 3109 lines; harness `c2391ab7…` byte-identical and re-run from the board resource, `ALL 55 EXPECTATIONS MET`, real exit **0**; logs identical after normalizing the `$TMPDIR` PID. No Go, no network, no host contacted. Pin `00b1688a` unmoved at `ci.yml:28,81`; `TASK-260720-1pvfj5` still `backlog`.
- STATUS: cycle-7 rework handed to review. Checklist item 13 `Tests green` stays unchecked — read-only no-Go scope, and the append-only board CLI still has no `remove_checklist_item`. Fourth occurrence of that constraint; decision D7-a in the map is the open class fix.

### 1935 — Atomicity's test-only ceiling is real: all six gates in, and the last lever is 12.5% short of 14.4%

- FINDING: all six `TASK-260729-rfrdfo` focused race gates completed, each `.exit` = **0**, verified this cycle from `gates/*.exit` + `*.seconds`. `internal/install` **232.088 / 235.124 / 226.191 s** — Patch A clears the 480s bar by 245-254s and is done. `internal/install/atomicity` **591.280 / 560.828 / 564.022 s** — three greens, all above 480s, worst margin **8.720 s** under the 600s alarm. The three-run spread is **30.45 s (5.4%)**, so that margin is inside the measurement's own noise; it is a coin flip, not a margin.
- DECISION: §4.3 (drop the unasserted `references/info.md` fixture write) is **NOT promoted** to a required patch target. It removes 3 files from a context target but **not a single target**, so the O((7N)²) factor is untouched and only the save count shrinks: `3 files x 5 saves = 15` of `120-210` total ⇒ **7.1-12.5%**, against a required **14.4-18.8%**. Best case on every free parameter still lands 490.7-517.4 s. Widening a 13-file allowlist to 14 for a gain that cannot reach the bar is the move the allowlist exists to prevent.
- ROOT CAUSE (of why nothing test-side is left): the patched package is **contention-bound, not chain-bound**. Twelve per-class outer subtests span 196.25-247.78 s against a 78.02 s isolated chain — **~x3 self-contention** — at **x9.6** effective concurrency on an M3 Max with **12 performance cores** (`hw.perflevel0.physicalcpu`). Each chain is already `baseline + 1 injection + final`, the minimum §5 allows. More partitioning: impossible. More parallelism: oversubscribes 12 cores 19 ways. Less parallelism: longer serial tail. The only lever left is **reducing total CPU work**, and test-side that is §4.3 alone.
- DECISION: test-only boundary declared **exhausted** for `internal/install/atomicity`. Route the production-side fix — hoist or memoise the target-namespace independence validation out of `Engine.saveJournal` — as its own blocking story. It also protects `cmd/curator`, at **557.779 s** of a 600 s alarm and next in line to fail. No `-timeout` override, build tag, skip, or assertion cut.
- ANOMALY: cycle 6 opened by re-diagnosing from scratch and writing a duplicate `.research/260729_install-race-timeout-diagnosis.md` before reading the cycle 1-5 history. It independently reproduced the same root cause and the same 88/19 split — useful corroboration — but its projections were already refuted by measurement. Deleted from disk; the board copy could not be deleted (unrelated board validation error, `unknown status: todo`) and was neutralised in place with a SUPERSEDED pointer. **Read the logbook and the task's own prior outcomes before producing a "fresh" artifact on a task at cycle 6.**
- SCOPE: `TASK-260729-3dr6hw` cycle 6. §1-§9 of the diagnosis verified **byte-identical** to the cycle-5 board copy (sha `0b96cd53...`); only §10 edited, §11-§12 added. No Go command run in any cycle.


### 1912 — The hosted CI lane can download a toolchain, and the fix is one line, not an action bump

- FINDING: `actions/setup-go` **v6.0.0** landed a *breaking* change (PR #460, "Improve toolchain handling") whose first bullet is: *"force `go` to always use the local toolchain … via setting the `GOTOOLCHAIN` environment variable to `local`."* `.github/workflows/ci.yml` pins **`setup-go@v5`** at three sites (lines 31, 61, 84). **So every hosted Curator job runs with the default `GOTOOLCHAIN=auto` today** and is free to fetch a toolchain from the proxy — the exact behaviour the native-host discipline (LOGBOOK 1800) forbids. The hazard is not just native.
- DECISION: close it with a workflow-level `env: GOTOOLCHAIN: local`, **not** with an action-major bump. One visible line next to `SPEC_PIN`, zero action-version surface for `TASK-260720-1pvfj5`, survives a later setup-go upgrade unchanged, and states the invariant explicitly instead of inheriting it from a third party. Recorded as decision D8.
- FINDING: current upstream majors, read 2026-07-29 — `actions/checkout` **v7** (v7.0.1, 2026-07-20), `actions/setup-go` **v7** (v7.0.0, 2026-07-16), `golangci/golangci-lint-action` **v9** (v9.3.0, 2026-06-29). Repo pins v4 / v5 / v7. Checkout and setup-go each cut a new major **in the nine days before this audit**; a review that stated "checkout v6, setup-go v6" was already one major stale when written. Action-major facts have a half-life measured in days — re-measure, never inherit.
- FINDING: `macos-latest` is **arm64** (macOS 26); `ubuntu-latest` is Ubuntu 24.04 x64; `windows-latest` is Windows Server 2025 x64. Evidence lines that say "macOS" without an architecture conflate the hosted lane (arm64) with `ssh relux` (`Darwin x86_64`).
- ANOMALY: a proposed `golangci-lint` pin of `v2.4.0` (released **2025-08-14**) would have been an **eight-minor downgrade** against what `version: latest` resolves to today (**v2.12.2**, 2026-05-06). "Pin it" and "pin it to the version a sibling task happened to run" are not the same instruction.
- SCOPE: `TASK-260729-osjeay` cycle 4, read-only. No CI/Makefile/pin edit. Six read-only network reads (5 × `gh api`, 1 × `curl`) — disclosed, since revisions 1–4 had each claimed "no network fetch to GitHub".

### 1904 — A gate that only prints is not a gate: three shapes that passed review four times

- FINDING: the `require-toolchain` recipe carried through revisions 3 and 4 **accepted every failure it claimed to prevent**, proved by running the verbatim recipe against `/bin/sh` stubs: compiler from root A paired with formatter from root B → **exit 0**; launcher whose real `GOROOT` is not the operator-approved one → **exit 0** (it *printed* the drift, then accepted it); `GOTOOLCHAIN=auto` in force → **exit 0**. It checked that *some* `bin/gofmt` existed under the reported root, which is true of every Go install in existence.
- ROOT CAUSE: the recipe compared nothing. It had no expected-root input, so there was no second operand for `go env GOROOT` to be checked against, and the environment it required was documented in prose rather than supplied by the recipe.
- FIX: one operator input, `GOROOT_EXPECTED`; `GO` and `GOFMT` **derived** from it, so a cross-root pairing is unrepresentable. Byte-for-byte root comparison, launcher-is-`$GOROOT/bin/go`, formatter-is-`$GOROOT/bin/gofmt`, environment **supplied** by a `$(GOENVPREFIX)` and **read back** (`go env GOTOOLCHAIN` must say `local`, `go env GOENV` must say `off`), plus a `go.mod`-vs-`Makefile` version reconciliation. Read-back and not just export, because a shim launcher can override its caller — this host already runs bare `go` through one.
- FINDING: the same class reappeared in transport. `tar -cf - … | tar -xf - -C dest` under `set -e` **exits 0 having staged 1 of 3 files** when the producer dies mid-stream; `set -e` sees only the extractor. Fixed with an intermediate archive, three separately checked statuses, and a complete-set assertion — an origin-side per-file inventory (catches changed/missing) **plus** a destination file count (catches extra, which `shasum -c` structurally cannot see).
- NOTE: the same defect shape has now recurred three cycles running — `$(shell …)` discarding `go list` status, `grep -q` short-circuiting a failing producer, `tar|tar` masking a failed producer. The invariant worth keeping is narrower than "check exit codes": **materialize before you filter, and never let a pipe stand between a producer and its status.**
- STATUS: harness extended 7 → **21 cases**, `ALL 21 EXPECTATIONS MET`, real exit 0, reproduced twice. The `cmd.exe` lane remains the one contract that **cannot** be executed from here (`ssh win` unreachable, exit 255) — four mandatory producer-time negative injections stand in for it.

### 1853 — All 13 focused gates in: Patch A is a landslide, Patch B is the whole problem

- FINDING: `TASK-260729-rfrdfo`'s driver completed (1758→1846). **All 13 gates real exit 0, zero `DATA RACE` markers across every log.** The split the 1839 entry could not yet see: `internal/install` under `-race` is **232.088s / 235.124s / 226.191s** — clears the 480s bar with a **245–254s** margin, beating even the plan's optimistic 12× row. `internal/install/atomicity` is **591.280s / 560.828s / 564.022s** — three green runs, all above 480s, all within 40s of the 600s alarm. **Patch A is not the problem and never was; the entire residual risk is Patch B.**
- FINDING: the plan's §4.1 projection for `internal/install` was pessimistic by **>2×** (worst-plausible row said 510–558s; measured 226–235s). The same document was optimistic by ~25% on atomicity. Projections built from static work-weighting missed in *both* directions on the *same* patch — the useful lesson is not "it was wrong" but that the sign of the error is not predictable from the method, so a margin narrower than the projection band's own width is not a margin.
- FINDING: third atomicity repeat lands at 564.022s, so the measured race factor stabilises at **×1.97–2.07** across all three (non-race baseline 285.434s). The ×3.33–4.27 assumption is retired by three independent same-tree observations, not one.
- DECISION: §4.3 (dropping the unasserted `references/info.md` fixture write) was **not attempted**, and that is deliberate. It makes `atomicity/fixture_test.go` a 14th file; this task's scope names that file as forbidden and caps the allowlist at 13. Quietly widening an allowlist to rescue a margin is exactly the move the allowlist exists to prevent. Routed as a separate task with a declared 14-file allowlist and a required before/after `StagingEntries` measurement.
- FINDING: the plan's §8.3 rsync recipe (`rsync -rin --delete --exclude='.git/'`) does **not** reproduce verifier-3's 23-line artifact — it emits **119** lines. Three causes, each visible in the recorded output: the recorded itemization carries the `c` bit, which only appears under `--checksum`; `--exclude='.git/'` with a trailing slash matches directories only, and `.git` is a worktree *pointer file* here; `.temp` and `.task-board` are agent scratch present in one tree only. Reconciled flags reproduce it byte for byte. **A verification recipe nobody has replayed against its own recorded output is not a verification recipe.**
- FINDING: `git diff --check` cannot be run inside a copied worktree. The copy's `.git` is a pointer file into the *source* repo's `worktrees/` admin dir, so any git command there resolves `core.worktree` back to the **source candidate** and refreshes the shared index as a side effect — i.e. the integrity check mutates the thing it is checking. Covered instead by `gofmt -l` plus a trailing-whitespace/CR scan of the generated patch.
- SCOPE: 13 files, test-only, `aba_test.go` and `atomicity/fixture_test.go` byte-identical; source candidate byte-identical over 391 files; conformance root byte-identical over 448; accepted-worktree delta 23 → 34 lines exactly as the corrected §8.3 predicts (20 `*deleting` + 3 pre-existing + 11 new).
- STATUS: prototype ready for review. `./...` race gate deliberately not run — verifier's gate. Expect atomicity to remain the failing package there.

### 1838 — Both censored race ratios are the same kind of object, and both roundings were invalid

- ROOT CAUSE: entry 1816's closing line is **corrected**. It called `internal/install`'s ×1.77 "the only honest same-run datum … a floor, not a factor" while dismissing atomicity's ×1.37 as "a censored value read as a measurement". Both race sides are **right-censored by the same alarm**; they are the same kind of object — a one-sided lower bound on the completed factor — differing only in how severe the censoring is. Treating one as a floor and the other as empty was internally inconsistent.
- FINDING: atomicity is **> ×1.3685** with **severe** censoring (binding sweep unfinished *plus* three whole tests never started), which makes it too weak to compare against `cmd/curator`'s completed ×1.45 or to estimate the completed factor — but **not** information-free. `internal/install` is **> ×1.7670** with **moderate** censoring (72 of 107 finished, 1 in flight, **34** never started), which is usable as a floor. Atomicity's completed in-gate factor comes from the separate phase-level solve (×3.33 sweep, ×4.14 activation), never from its own row.
- FINDING: **both two-decimal figures round the wrong way.** `603.306/341.415 = 1.767075…` and `603.701/441.122 = 1.368557…` round **up** to ×1.77 and ×1.37, so neither "> ×1.77" nor "> ×1.37" is a strictly true floor — each asserts marginally more than the arithmetic supports. Bounds are now stated as the truncated **> ×1.7670** and **> ×1.3685**; the 2-dp values appear only as roundings. Cheap to check, easy to miss, and it survived four review cycles on both rows.
- NOTE: "purely because the alarm truncated the numerator" is also withdrawn. Truncation explains why the *observed* value is low; how far above the bound the completed factor sits is **not derivable from the row**, and no claim is made either way.

### 1816 — The same-run race factors refute the mechanism they were cited to prove

- ROOT CAUSE: entry 1756's "imports no cross-tree assumption" finding was over-read, and its causal half is **withdrawn**. It is consistent with the alarm stacks (`P` = 8 install, 19/20 atomicity) and with `internal/transaction`'s ~33 `Prepare` sites carrying one-to-a-few-target literals, but nothing measures its share of wall time. The discriminator is **journal target count**, not in-process-versus-subprocess. No projection band moves; the patch is unchanged.

### 1800 — Two agents, one host, one `go` command, two toolchains

- FINDING (F1, the sharpest fact this cycle): bare `go` is **not a stable reference on this host**. The cycle-3 reviewer's shell resolved `command -v go` to `/Users/iv/.goenv/shims/go`; mine resolved it to `/opt/homebrew/bin/go`. Same host, same repo, same week. `which -a go` returns **three** launchers. Every "it works on my machine" argument about a Go gate here is unfalsifiable until the launcher is absolute.
- FINDING: the third launcher is the trap. `/usr/local/go` is **`go1.25.1`** (read from its `VERSION` file) while `go.mod:3` says `go 1.25.5` with **no `toolchain` line**. Under the default `GOTOOLCHAIN=auto` that combination **downloads** a toolchain — a network fetch, on a host that is supposed to be hermetic, triggered by nothing more than PATH order. `GOTOOLCHAIN=local` turns it into an immediate error. Separately, `GOROOT` is **exported** to the goenv tree while bare `go` resolved to the Homebrew tree; both are 1.25.5 today, so it works **by luck**.
- FIX: §5.0 of the final-CI map — one operator-approved absolute `GO_EXE` + matching `GOROOT` per host, `GOTOOLCHAIN=local`, `GOENV=off`, verified by `version` + `env GOROOT` before any gate. `require-toolchain` enforces it; `where go` is gone from the Windows lane in favour of transferred-in `WIN_GOROOT`/`WIN_GO_EXE` constants. Hosted GitHub runners are the **documented** exception (`TOOLCHAIN_ALLOW_PATH=1`), because `actions/setup-go` owns PATH in a fresh image — and even there the version/GOROOT assertions still run. Invariant I11.
- ROOT CAUSE (F2): `LINUX_PKGS = $(shell $(GO) list ./... | grep -v …)` cannot fail. Make's `$(shell)` discards exit status, so a `go list` that printed 3 of 40 packages and then exited 1 became a **green 2-package test lane**. The `grep -q` importer guard was worse — it returns on first match, potentially before `go list` even reaches its error. **Both reproduced at exit 0** with `/bin/sh` stubs. FIX: discovery moved inside the recipe behind `rows="$(…)" || exit 1`, exclusion asserted to be exactly one package, safe set asserted non-empty. Invariant I12.
- DECISION: recipes get executed before they get written down. All seven cases (healthy 0, partial-list 2, relative `GO` 2, wrong version 2, hosted exception 0) run from one attached script, `TASK-260729-osjeay_verify-recipes.sh`, with **no Go**. This is the direct application of entry 1740's lesson.
- ANOMALY (self-inflicted, caught by running it): that harness was **red on its first execution** and the recipe was fine. `STUB_VER=go1.25.1 run …` — a prefix assignment before a shell **function** — persists in the caller's shell under POSIX `sh`, so case H's downgraded version leaked into case I. **A harness that lies about the thing under test is the same defect class it was built to catch.**
- FINDING (F3): the race numbers were sitting in `.temp/` unread for a day. `.temp/TASK-260720-2284br/gates-rework1/gate-race.log` records `-timeout 45m` **passes**: `internal/install` **609.117 s**, `internal/install/atomicity` **1422.407 s**, exit file `race exit=0`. My "the only executed race numbers are timeouts" claim was false, and entry 1732 had already superseded the 2.75× / 1121 s model with 2.67× / 4.02×. Correcting it made the risk **worse, not better**: 1422.407 s against a 1800 s alarm is **1.27× headroom**, measured with 5 packages in flight, and the AC's gate runs 40. `go test -race ./... -timeout 30m` has still never been executed.
- FINDING (F4): board checklists are **append-only**. The mutation set is `add_checklist_item` / `check_item` / `uncheck_item` — no remove, no edit. So "replace the inapplicable checklist item" is not executable by any agent through the CLI, only "leave unchecked" or "check it". The board owner had independently reached the same conclusion and recorded the not-applicable reconciliation in task notes. Second occurrence after `TASK-260729-2sxx7k` (entry 0510) — the class fix belongs in the role definition, not in another per-task note.
- SCOPE: `TASK-260729-osjeay` rework cycle 3, read-only. Map revision 4, SHA-256 `73a2da0ea21b71a0da5723e13436767850c8aff58d419257fba06776b113c36e`. **No Go command of any kind** — toolchain versions read from `VERSION` files, `make` drove `/bin/sh` stubs. No product/spec/CI/Makefile/pin/target-task mutation.

- ROOT CAUSE: entry 1732's post-patch integrity expectation was unfireable. `internal/install/cache_conformance_test.go` and `dryrun_conformance_test.go` are **candidate-only**, so they already sit in `candidate-source-delta-post.txt` as `*deleting` lines. rsync has nothing in the accepted tree to compare them against — editing them changes their line **not at all**. A second check that structurally cannot cover two of its thirteen targets must say so, not imply coverage.

Its denominator (`228.344 s`) comes from `gate-gotest.log` — 25 lines, truncated, and line 21 is `FAIL … internal/godriver 99.383s` with two subtests dying on `go-v1 process_timeout` (`boundary_test.go:66`), i.e. a **loaded** host. The atomicity ×4.02 pair survives — both its sides are in the same batch and `gate-acceptance-verbose.log` lets its composition be read directly (zero `TestStableHybridActivation…`, so that tree predates `activation_test.go`).

The split is the mechanism: subprocess-dominated packages (`git`, `go build`) barely move under `-race`; in-process CPU — the O(P²) `validateIndependentTargetNamespaces` — does. Sub-10 s packages are noise (`internal/audit` reads ×4.66 on a 1.4 s baseline) and are excluded.

Two independent mechanisms, not one.

This is defence in depth on top of `before.diff(snapshotState)` at `:178`, which still runs on every injection; the residual exposure is a defect that both escapes `snapshotState`'s hand-maintained path list *and* only manifests through a later class. Sanctioned by review cycle 2, now named as **intentionally retired**, with a two-class fallback (restores 9 of 31 pairs, ~384 s race) recorded as the escalation, not the recommendation.

Package sizes 107 and 8 match the alarm dumps' `0x6b` and `0x8`. ### 1740 — A transport command nobody ran, and a red-gate claim nobody re-measured

- ROOT CAUSE (self-inflicted, F1): revision 2 of the final-CI map archived the frozen candidate with `tar -cf … -C "$(dirname "$DST")" conformance` where `DST=…/candidate/conformance/v1`. One `dirname` lands on `…/candidate/conformance`, so the operand resolves to `…/conformance/conformance` — nonexistent. Reproduced in a scratch dir: **exit 1**, `tar: conformance: Cannot stat`. The fix is two `dirname`s, plus a `test -d` preflight and a `tar -tf | grep -qx 'conformance/v1/manifest.json'` listing assertion. **A shell command written into a plan and never run is not a plan.**
- FINDING: the POSIX tree pipeline's *intermediate* stream, written to a file, hashes to the same digest the pipeline emits — the final `shasum` hashes exactly those bytes. So `candidate-inventory.sha256` is simultaneously the 448-file per-file manifest **and** its own authenticity seal: its SHA-256 **is** `CANDIDATE_TREE_SHA256` = `e6a13215…2fae`. One constant now drives POSIX recompute, inventory authentication, and Windows per-file set comparison. No second digest was invented to reach Windows.
- DECISION (F2): a frozen candidate whose digests differ from the accepted ones is a **different candidate**, not a new baseline. Revision 2 told the producer to "adopt the new values"; that makes every downstream identity check tautological. Withdrawn — mismatch aborts, records the measured values, and escalates for a refreshed accepted input.
- FINDING (F3): a Make target labelled "mirrors the CI gate exactly" ran `go test ./...` with `CURATOR_CONFORMANCE_ROOT` **unset**, so every conformance test silently skipped, and it added a Linux-only guard the macOS/Windows job never runs. A `require-pin-root` guard now makes the root a hard input for every suite target. **A green gate that skipped the tests it claims to run is worse than a red one.**
- ROOT CAUSE (self-inflicted, caught by re-reading this logbook rather than by review): D6 asserted "without `-timeout 30m` the composite CI is red regardless of everything else", inheriting a **pre-rework** `2kaopg` measurement. Entry 1637 records the exact non-race command exiting **0 in 444 s** (`cmd/curator` 384.270 s) after the shared compiled-fixture rework. The gate that *is* red at the default alarm is the **race** lane — `internal/install` **603.306 s**, `internal/install/atomicity` **603.701 s**, no `DATA RACE` — which is precisely the clause the AC names. Recipe unchanged; the claim was not.
- TRAP: `-timeout 30m` on the race lane rests on 918 s / 1121 s **projections** (entry 1659), while the only executed numbers are timeouts. The race job is now labelled *specified but unproven*, with its live `TASK-260720-jrrgw9` / `TASK-260729-3dr6hw` dependency named, and the producer is told to measure before writing the job — and never to widen the alarm to make it pass.
- MEASURED: `ssh win` and `ssh lev` are **unreachable** (exit 255, twice each, `Operation timed out`); `ssh relux` is reachable with a Go binary at `/usr/local/bin/go` that is **not on the non-interactive PATH**. So the Windows lane now has two named prerequisites, not one, and every relux command must use the absolute launcher. Version unmeasured — running `go version` is running Go, and this audit ran none.
- FINDING: `go test` on a remote host needs more than the conformance root. The module has 4 direct + 17 indirect deps, no `vendor/`, and a `replace` into a git submodule; the source tree also holds exactly two symlinks (`.claude/`, `.codex/`) that Windows cannot extract without Developer Mode. The Windows bundle therefore ships a second archive built from a staging copy with `go mod vendor` applied and those two dirs excluded — never vendoring in the repo worktree.
- SCOPE: `TASK-260729-osjeay` rework cycle 2, read-only. `.research/260729_final-curator-ci-execution-map.md` revision 3, SHA-256 `d93e155edf2a4ddf2b23b353f5c411bb40223a9c4be8295973a03bc2990c7d93`. No Go command of any kind, no product/spec/CI/Makefile/pin/target-task mutation. Every command in the artifact is a future producer gate; nothing in it is a green result.

### 1738 — Final-CI map still trusts ambient Go and masks package-discovery failure

- REVIEW: `TASK-260729-osjeay` cycle 3 requests analysis rework. Revision 3 fixes the prior archive, identity, root-plumbing, and Windows-option-set defects, but its native commands contradict its own toolchain invariant: W1/W6 use `where go` and bare `go`, C3 runs bare `go mod vendor`, and the primary local gates use bare `go`/`make` even though this host resolves `/Users/iv/.goenv/shims/go` first under an exported goenv `GOROOT`. Required fix is one absolute approved Go executable/GOROOT per host, `GOENV=off`, `GOTOOLCHAIN=local`, and reuse of that exact executable in every gate.
- REVIEW: `LINUX_PKGS := $(shell go list ./... | grep …)` and both importer guards mask `go list` status through pipelines/Make expansion. A partial list can become a green partial test lane. Materialize and status-check complete `go list` output before filtering; then assert exactly one exclusion and derive every safe package.
