Existing `.temp/TASK-260720-2284br/gates-rework1/gate-race.log` records race passes for `internal/install` at **609.117 s** and `internal/install/atomicity` at **1422.407 s**; entry 1732 already supersedes the 2.75× / 1121 s atomicity model. Full `-race ./... -timeout 30m` remains unproven, but focused-package evidence is measured, not projected.
- EVIDENCE: board outcome `TASK-260729-osjeay_review-verdict-cycle-3.md`. No Go/build/test/lint/fetch/install/product/CI/Make/pin/target-task mutation during review.

### 1732 — The install-race projections were measurable all along, and two of them were wrong

- FINDING: entry 1659's projections (918 s / 1121 s) were pure extrapolation. Prior first-hand gate logs already in `.temp/` carry **measured** race/non-race pairs nobody had opened: `.temp/TASK-260720-2284br/gates-rework1/gate-race.log` (`-timeout 45m`, real exit 0) records `internal/install` **609.117 s** against `gate-gotest.log`'s **228.344 s** → **×2.67**, and `internal/install/atomicity` **1422.407 s** against **353.629 s** → **×4.02**. A focused pair (`gate-race-activation.log` 97.813 s vs 22.92 s) gives **×4.27**.
- ROOT CAUSE (of the bad model): entry 1659 claimed both packages share one race factor (~2.7×). They do not. Atomicity's journals carry **19–20 targets** against `internal/install`'s **8** (read off the alarm frames), and the hot leaf is the O(P²) `validateIndependentTargetNamespaces` — in-process instrumented Go, which `-race` inflates — not `git` subprocesses, which it does not. Corrected projections: install **890–1000 s**, atomicity **1284–1494 s** (the old 1121 s omitted the three post-sweep tests, which sort *after* the sweep and so were never reached in the failing gate).
- FINDING: `TestStrictRegistryPolicyFailsUnknown` is `registry_e2e_test.go:110`, the **third** declaration in that file, so it is test **73 of 107**, not 71. **72** tests completed before the alarm, not 70. The unstarted tail (`revalidation` + `stage`) holds 35 % of install invocations and 44 % of fixture repos in 33 % of tests, so the uniform-cost extrapolation is a floor, not an estimate.
- MEASURED (this closes an open inference): `.temp/logwork/TASK-260729-2kaopg/…RUN-260729-051449.log:4697-4765` is an isolated `-v` atomicity run, `ok … 371.708 s`. Per-subtest wall reconstruction `22.92 + 301.67 + 17.89 + 18.46 + 9.97 = 370.91 s` accounts for all but 0.80 s. The `project-hybrid-auto` chain alone is **81 %** of package wall. The 7 project injections span 34.99–40.20 s (±7 %) and the 5 global 23.78–31.27 s — **injection cost is class-independent**, exactly as predicted from `Prepare` staging every target before the commit-phase fault fires. Entry 1659 listed this as inference; it is now measurement, and it yields the separable phases Patch B needs (project baseline+final **40.74 s**, mean injection **37.28 s**).
- FINDING: `internal/install/atomicity` does **not** appear in `candidate-source-delta-post.txt` — it is byte-identical to the accepted worktree. Its 1422 s race time predates this candidate and was masked by `-timeout 45m` on the focused gates. **Pre-existing debt, not a `jrrgw9` regression.** The candidate's only additions inside `internal/install` are 2 of its 107 tests.
- DECISION: candidate-integrity gating moves off `git diff` entirely. The candidate is an intentionally dirty delivery worktree at HEAD `17804ce`, so HEAD-relative diffs report the delivery, not the patch; and the 448-file digest covers only the immutable conformance root. Replaced with a pre/post path-sorted SHA-256 manifest of the worktree plus a regenerated accepted-worktree rsync delta, gated by a standalone exit-coded script with explicit added/deleted/modified split. Required patch allowlist tightened to **13 files** — `aba_test.go` (all 5 tests excluded) and `atomicity/fixture_test.go` (optional unmeasured lever only) are **not** targets.
- TRAP: entry 1659's "even the pessimistic 2× concurrency case clears the alarm" clears **600 s** but not the **480 s** pass bar it set itself — 2× lands at 510–558 s. And focused per-package numbers are measured with far fewer packages in flight than `./...`, so they read optimistic against the real gate. The 480 s bar exists to absorb that gap; the `./...` gate goes back to the verifier, not to the producer.
- BLOCKED (reviewer decision, not an implementation blocker): partitioning the atomicity sweep to one class per scenario removes the cross-class residue link — today's 7-long chain exercises 21 ordered (X-then-Y) class pairs, one-per-entry exercises none. The per-injection `before.diff(snapshotState)` digest check and the post-sweep ordering assertion survive intact (the latter runs 7×/5× instead of once), but the behavioural property is genuinely narrowed. If that is not acceptable, atomicity cannot reach the default alarm test-only and the choice escalates to a documented `-timeout`, a build tag, or the production-side fix.
- SCOPE: `TASK-260729-3dr6hw` rework cycle 2, read-only. `.research/260729_install-race-timeouts.md` revised in place. **No Go command executed** — a foreign `go test -count=1 ./...` (PID 1686) was live throughout, which is a second reason beyond the task boundary.

### 1702 — Every macOS resource bound fails a different way, and only executing them separates the three

- FINDING: `TASK-260729-3jmqgl` rework cycle 1 replaced a declared platform table with executed per-resource probes, and the three failure modes turned out to be genuinely distinct. Per-process-with-a-fresh-copy: `RLIMIT_NOFILE` bound the setter at 61 of a declared 64 while its child opened a fresh 61 (aggregate 122), and `RLIMIT_CPU` signalled `SIGXCPU` at 1003 ms of a declared 1000 ms while its descendant burned a fresh 1002 ms. Not-expressible: this kernel refuses `setrlimit` for `RLIMIT_AS` and `RLIMIT_DATA` at 256 MiB with `EINVAL`, lowest accepted value ≈ 421 707 907 058 bytes (1570× the declared budget). Wrong-scope: `RLIMIT_NPROC` installs cleanly, then refuses the domain's *first* descendant under a declared budget of 4, because it is accounted per real uid.
- FINDING: a soft limit is a floor the bounded process consented to. A domain member raised its own soft limit back to the inherited hard limit for both `RLIMIT_CPU` and `RLIMIT_NPROC` (`raised`); the matched control lowering the hard limit too was refused with `EPERM`. A supervisor that lowers only soft limits has bounded nothing.
- FINDING: lowering `rlim_max` is irreversible for the process that does it. Limits must be set in the child before `exec`, never in a process the supervisor intends to reuse.
- FINDING: a wall-clock deadline ends one process, not a build. Measured on a 3 s deadline: the domain root died at 3.002 s, and **both** the attached and the detached descendant survived cancellation — cancelling a supervised process signals that process alone. A group-directed `SIGKILL` reached the attached one; the detached one survived everything a plain macOS supervisor can issue.
- DECISION: report platform teardown and harness cleanup as two separate checks. The harness kills the survivor by pid, but a production implementation cannot rely on that — it only reaches descendants the supervisor already recorded. Folding them into one "cleaned up" line would hide exactly the gap that matters.
- ROOT CAUSE (self-inflicted, caught by its own control): an earlier draft declared the CPU bound in milliseconds and passed the same number to `setrlimit`, which takes whole seconds — installing a limit 1000× the declared one. Every CPU measurement came back unrefused and read as a clean platform finding. `FIX`: one named conversion with its own test, plus an invariant test that every kind's stress ceiling sits above its declared budget. A stress stopping at its ceiling is otherwise indistinguishable from one the bound refused.
- DECISION: mechanism inventory entries now carry `exercised` plus an `observation` naming the checks behind them. 9 of 20 were exercised this run. Without the split, a status written by hand next to a mechanism nobody probed reads exactly like one the run established — the precise defect the reviewer flagged.
- TRAP: Go's `syscall` package does not export `RLIMIT_NPROC` on darwin. It is 7 in `<sys/resource.h>`; transcribed with the header named rather than guessed, because a wrong resource number silently bounds something other than what the report claims.
- TRAP: code that only runs in a child process is not credited to the parent's coverage profile. `internal/inside` read 65.1% until the safe orchestration paths were driven in-process; the fix is not to stop using subprocesses, since the subprocess is what makes a self-terminating bound measurable at all.
- SCOPE: prototype only, `prototypes/macos-hardened-probes/` in the task-owned worktree. Headline platform result unchanged — macOS establishes 1 of 6 hardened guarantees, harness rejects fail-closed with exit 1. Not committed, not staged, nothing in Curator imports it.

### 1659 — Both install race timeouts are one quadratic in `saveJournal`, and `internal/install` never had a single `t.Parallel`

- ROOT CAUSE: `internal/install` (`FAIL 603.306s`) and `internal/install/atomicity` (`FAIL 603.701s`) under `go test -count=1 -race ./...` are cumulative duration, not deadlock — no `DATA RACE` marker anywhere in the 320-line log. All three CPU-`runnable` goroutines across both alarm dumps sit in the *same* leaf: `strings.FieldsFunc` ← `namespaceComponents` (`internal/transaction/namespace.go:217`) ← `namespaceContains:203` ← `namespacePathsOverlap:175` ← `validateIndependentTargetNamespaces:26` ← `saveJournal` (`journal.go:71`) ← `stageTarget`/`copyStagingFile` (`staging.go:37`/`:161`).
- FINDING: `Engine.saveJournal` validates the whole target-namespace independence graph **twice** per journal write — once at `journal.go:344`, again with the reserved journal root at `:354`. Each run rebuilds up to `targets*7` canonical paths (`EvalSymlinks` + `unix.Pathconf` + `unix.Statfs` per path, `namespace_case_darwin.go:13,25`) and then does an O(P²) pairwise loop (`namespace.go:100-111`); on APFS `normInsensitive` is true so every path component of both sides of every comparison is NFD-normalized. `saveJournal` fires 3× per staging entry (`staging.go:26,33,56`) and 2× per 32 KiB chunk (`:141,161`).
- FINDING: target counts are readable straight off the dump frames — `0x8` = 8 targets for `internal/install`, `0x13`/`0x14` = 19/20 for the two atomicity scenarios. That is the ~6× quadratic gap between the two packages per journal save.
- FINDING: the fault injects at `PointAfterBackup`, a **commit**-phase boundary, so every injection first runs a complete `Prepare` staging all 20 targets. Injection cost is therefore ~independent of which class is faulted — the sweep's cost is `chain length × per-install cost`, and chain length is the only test-only lever.
- FINDING: `internal/install` has **zero** `t.Parallel()` across 107 tests / 109 install invocations — one serial thread on a 16-core host. 88 are safe to parallelize; 19 are not: 11 touch `t.Setenv` (6 direct, 5 via `isolateTempDir`, `private_test.go:20`), 7 assign the package-level `afterDocumentOpen` hook (`generation.go:57`, read unsynchronized at `:81`, set by `aba_test.go:37` and `generation_test.go:44`), 1 is the re-exec child `TestInstallHelperProcess` (`commit_test.go:760`).
- FINDING: derived race factor is **2.69×** for `internal/install` (70 of 107 tests in 600s vs 341.415s non-race) and **2.75×** for atomicity (sweep 1026s projected vs 373s non-race) — two independent derivations agreeing, so projections are `918s` and `1121s` against an unchanged 600s alarm.
- DECISION: test-only mitigation is `t.Parallel()` on the 88 allowlisted tests plus partitioning the atomicity sweep to **one class per scenario** (chain 9→3 project, 7→3 global, with 5 distinct global user homes to keep the `sharedUserHome` non-overlap invariant). No assertion removed — residue is already asserted directly by `before.diff(snapshotState)` at `commit_atomicity_test.go:178`, so the shared-baseline chain was defence in depth, not the proof.
- DECISION: the cheap real fix (hoist namespace validation out of `saveJournal`, or memoize per journal) is **production** code behind a safety invariant. Routed as a separate story, not smuggled into a test-only rework.
- ANOMALY: adding `t.Parallel()` to 88 tests exercises production code concurrently for the first time. `afterDocumentOpen` proves unsynchronized package-level hooks exist here. A new `WARNING: DATA RACE` from the focused run is a genuine defect, not a reason to revert.
- SCOPE: diagnosis only, no execution, no edits. `.research/260729_install-race-timeouts.md`, `TASK-260729-3dr6hw`.

### 1653 — The final CI task is blocked by the pin's *contents*, not by Linux; and the audited "main" was a divergent branch

- ROOT CAUSE (self-inflicted, corrects my own 1627 entry): I read `HEAD` and called it main. `main` = `origin/main` = `17804ce` with committed suite pin `00b1688a…`; `c06aa1a` is the tip of the divergent branch `agent/link-curator-skill-registry` carrying `e72defe…` (merge base `ecb6c1a`, neither is an ancestor of the other). Invariant I6 was therefore exactly backwards: composing on `c06aa1a` is what reverts the pin — from 14 vectors down to 10 — and `e72defe` is missing `manager-lifecycle.json`, which the composite's `internal/closure` test hard-fails on. `git rev-parse origin/main` before `git log`, always.
- FINDING: the composite is statically red on **all three** platforms under the committed pin, and Linux is only the third-largest cause. Six conformance sites read an rc.5-only artifact and `t.Fatal` when it is missing — they skip only when `CURATOR_CONFORMANCE_ROOT` is *unset*, not when the artifact is absent: `buildsource/conformance_test.go:16`, `buildcache/conformance_test.go:15`+`readJSONObject:63`, `scopes/gc_conformance_test.go:38`, `marker/marker_v2_test.go:37`, `whitelist/conformance_test.go:20,25`, `skillspec/conformance_test.go:106`. CI exports the root for `go test ./...`. Verified by `git cat-file -e` per path per revision, not inferred.
- FINDING: the candidate's **own** new tests already solved this. Five of the 20 delta files guard with `t.Skipf("%s publishes no build-drivers vector", root)` before touching the artifact (`skillcheck:24`, `whitelist/builddriver:25`, `skillspec/builddriver:39,301`, `buildcache/builddriver_positive:31`). The six older sites were never retrofitted. The fix is to propagate the pattern the composite already established, not to invent one.
- FINDING: `vectors/build-drivers.json` exists at **no** published curator-spec revision — it is untracked even inside the rc.5 candidate worktree. So the build-driver conformance suite can only ever run under an explicitly supplied candidate root, and any pin-rooted job that expects it is red by construction. This is the concrete shape of what `TASK-260720-2qqq0w` routed to 1pvfj5 as "pin/gate reconciliation".
- ANOMALY: the committed pin `00b1688a` is `v1.0.0-rc.2-1-g00b1688` — one commit *past* rc.2, described by no release tag. 1pvfj5's AC calls it "one immutable currently released pin": immutable yes, released no. The nearest real release with a byte-identical vector set is `v1.0.0-rc.3` = `57c1f56`, but promotion is `TASK-260720-38l1sy`'s, so the AC wording is what should move.
- FINDING: `go test ./...` on this composite exits 1 at Go's fixed 10-minute default in `cmd/curator` (measured twice by `TASK-260729-2kaopg`, different late victim each time; both passed in isolation at 40.0 s and 9.3 s). Every gate needs `-timeout 30m` or it is red before any of the above matters.
- DECISION: no hosted candidate job. The rc.5 root is an uncommitted worktree (3 modified, **354** untracked — my 1627 entry said 18, understating it 20×), so no `ref:` can fetch it and no hosted runner can see the path. Candidate evidence is native-only, over a frozen read-only snapshot identified by two digests: manifest `b6f56aac…04c` and whole-tree `e6a13215…2fae` over 448 files. Today that means **macOS arm64 only** — `ssh win` and `ssh lev` both have no Go.
- DECISION: the Linux exclusion list is *derived* (`go list ./...` minus `internal/godriver`), never hand-maintained, so new-package drift is structurally impossible. The guard covers the drift that can actually happen: the godriver importer set growing past `{cmd/curator, internal/install}`.
- TRAP: a Go `-run` selector matching nothing exits 0. Any gate written as `-run 'TestProbeRejects…'` is vacuous the moment the test is renamed, and reads exactly like a pass.
- SCOPE: `TASK-260729-osjeay` rework cycle 1, read-only. `.research/260729_final-curator-ci-execution-map.md` revision 2. No Go run, no product/spec/CI/Makefile/pin/target-task mutation. Every command in the artifact is a future producer gate; nothing in it is a green result.

### 1645 — A research artifact that quotes live board state has a shelf life shorter than its own review

- FINDING: `TASK-260729-35tb37` cycle 2 requested exactly one correction, and it was not a technical error. Every provenance, packaging, CI, file-plan, and rc.2/rc.5 regression claim in revision 2 was independently confirmed. The defect was that `TASK-260729-v5hqnv` finished its own second review *after* revision 2 was written, so the artifact reported `to-review`/unaccepted for a task that had become `done`.
- ROOT CAUSE: the artifact embedded another task's mutable lifecycle state as a fact. Board status moves on its own clock; a reconnaissance document does not. Any concurrently-reviewed dependency read into prose is stale by the time a reviewer opens it.
- DECISION: keep quoting the state — a producer needs it — but always cite the accepting verdict resource beside it (`TASK-260729-v5hqnv_review-verdict-cycle-2.md`) and re-query the live projection as the last step before every reviewer handoff. The citation is what survives; the status word is a snapshot.
- FINDING: acceptance of a board-metadata task changes nothing downstream. `v5hqnv` touched only seven briefs' `description`/`scope`/`ac` plus two `blockedBy` edges. Both roots stayed `backlog` behind `TASK-260720-1pvfj5`, `TASK-260720-12r55p` stayed fail-closed on blocked rc.4 `TASK-260720-3ag6pi`, and no test ran in either task. Board `done` is not an upstream ref, a CI pin, or start clearance.
- TRAP: the board DSL has no batch `get`. `get([ID,ID,…])` exits 1 with `element … not found` rather than projecting; scoped per-ID `get(ID) { … }` is the working form. Field projection is also space-separated inside braces — commas are a parse error, and `{ full }` is rejected by `m` entirely.
- SCOPE: research artifact only. Revision 3 of `TASK-260729-35tb37_cocoaskills-baseline-file-map.md`, SHA-256 `929098bec7303b5b7545e4ad469c47aa9afe484776f8d6f0cb870dd1b0b41b57`. No repository, brief, dependency, diagram, pin, protocol artifact, or historical rc.2/rc.5 result altered; no test run.

### 1632 — A cleanup guard that reads the variable the run overwrote can never fire

- FINDING: `TASK-260729-1bf72u` revision 1 published a Windows cleanup barrier that computed `$ExpectedTempParent` from `$env:TEMP` — but the native matrix had already executed `$env:TEMP = $RunTmp`. The guard therefore compared `C:\Users\admin\AppData\Local\Temp\TASK-260729-1bf72u-native-windows` against `C:\Users\admin\AppData\Local\Temp` and refused, forever, to remove the exact root it owned. Measured on `ssh win`: `old_guard_passes:false` at process exit 0.
- ROOT CAUSE: the guard's reference value and the run's mutable state were the same variable. A safety check is only a check if what it reads cannot be changed by the thing it is guarding against.
- FIX: capture `$OriginalTemp`/`$OriginalTempParent` **before** any override, build `$RunTmp` from the immutable parent, and validate against that parent — never a live `$env:TEMP` read. Re-measured `cleanup_guard_passes:true` on the same host.
- FINDING: the same block also only ran cleanup on the success path. Everything after `New-Item` is now inside `try`, with `finally` restoring `TEMP`/`TMP` and invoking the guarded cleanup. Proven by throwing inside the `try`: `simulated_failure` non-null with `finally_ran:true` and `cleanup_verified_absent:true`.
- DECISION: the guarded cleanup exists exactly once as `Invoke-TaskWindowsCleanup`; the in-run `finally` and the aborted-run recovery path call the same definition. A second pasted copy in the barriers section is how the two drift apart. The harness must persist `$OriginalTempParent`/`$RunTmp` durably so recovery has an immutable parent to validate against.
- TRAP: `ssh win "powershell -EncodedCommand <b64>"` dies at exit 1 with a cp866 "command line too long" from remote `cmd.exe` past the ~8191-char limit. An 8,660-char encoding failed; 6,372 worked. Compact the script or transfer it.
- EVIDENCE: cleanup-guard probe standalone (not piped) exit 0; remote absence sweep exit 0 with 0 `TASK-260729-1bf72u-*` leftovers; both published PowerShell blocks parse-checked on Windows PowerShell 5.1 at 0 errors with their probe files removed and verified absent. Board outcomes `TASK-260729-1bf72u_runner-readiness.md` revision 2 SHA-256 `d6e1ce11508bd88b8ae356839b071eba75a19e46f434ad53f11b3a47dcc239ad` and `TASK-260729-1bf72u_cleanup-guard-evidence.md`.
- SCOPE: documentation correction only. No prerequisite installed, no product source edited, no macOS suite rerun; all independently accepted revision 1 readiness findings preserved.

### 1631 — A parser test authored beside the parser cannot gate the parser; the owner-published vector can

- DECISION: `TASK-260729-35tb37` rework cycle 1 added a required regression gate to the schema-v6 root plan — `CURATOR_CONFORMANCE_ROOT=<rc.3>/conformance/v1 pytest tests/test_protocol_conformance.py::test_skill_manifest_resolution_vectors` — instead of relying on `tests/test_skillspec.py`. Reason: schema 6 rewrites `_load_skill_manifest()`, the exact seam whose earlier gap produced a *silent empty spec*, and producer-authored cases written in the same commit can regress in lockstep with the parser. The eight rc.3 vectors are owner-published, so they cannot.
- FINDING: the gate costs nothing new. `00b1688a9b2457ca397a0bb550acf47cad8ee967` is already the committed conformance `ref` at upstream `6fc2fd97`; no pin moves, no dependency is added, no candidate root is substituted.
- FINDING: the base determines whether the rc.2/rc.5 conformance regression exists at all. Local `edce8816` pins rc.2 `cbe912d0…` (protocol `1.0.0-rc.2`, manifest `728f7729…`, 81 files) and has the single-name parser; upstream `6fc2fd97` pins rc.3 `00b1688a…` (`1.0.0-rc.3`, manifest `7951cda1711d34d2a9dd9a873cf9d537c41ca4e9527e94f138f38743610a379e`, 93 files) and already carries the `deb971f` canonical/legacy fix plus the new test. So the red result is historical local evidence and a regression gate — not work to redo and not authorization to move a pin.
- TRAP: `tests/test_protocol_conformance.py` carries `pytestmark = pytest.mark.skipif(not ROOT_TEXT, …)`. A bare local run of that module skips everything and exits 0. Any gate citing it must export `CURATOR_CONFORMANCE_ROOT` or it proves nothing.
- CORRECTION: the upstream delta is **19 distinct paths**, not 20. There are 20 commit-level touch events only because `.github/workflows/ci.yml` is modified by both `deb971f` (19 paths) and `6fc2fd9` (1 path).
- SCOPE: research artifact only. Revision 2 of `TASK-260729-35tb37_cocoaskills-baseline-file-map.md`. No CocoaSkills, Curator, spec, pin, dependency, checkout, or other board task was changed; no test suite was executed.

### 1630 — A length-framing test is vacuous unless the two inputs concatenate identically

- FINDING: the hardened kernel-build fixture `macos-host-build-source-boundary-shift` was built to prove `curator-hardened-host-build-v1` length framing by moving one byte across a source-value boundary. It cannot: the construction hashes `name || value` pairs, so the declared source **names sit between the values** and act as separators. Removing all framing still leaves the base and the shifted probe distinct.
- FINDING: proven by mutation, not inspection. `construction-probe-r4.py` deleted `writeFramed` from `hostBuildDigest` (`tools/generate-hardened/main.go`) and `go test` stayed green; only the Python/Go cross-implementation fixture check caught it — a different property (two implementations agreeing), not framing.
- FIX: a genuine framing test needs two field lists whose whole hashed byte stream concatenates identically — `a|bc` vs `ab|c`. Added in both implementations (`main_test.go` `TestHostBuildDigestIsLengthFramed`, `test_validate_hardened.py`). The fixture was restated as what it does prove: an implementation hashing the observed values as one blob would alias two different observations.
- FINDING: the hashed source count was likewise overclaimed as anti-aliasing. Per-field framing already separates a truncated list; the count states cardinality explicitly. Prose and both tests corrected.
- DECISION: for any domain-separated digest, write the mutation that removes the property before writing the test that claims it. The test that survives the mutation is the only one that means anything.
- SCOPE: `protocol/hardened-execution.md` §2.3.3, `tools/generate-hardened/main.go`, `tools/validate_hardened.py`, both test files. `TASK-260728-1jafds` rework cycle 4.

### 1629 — Four review cycles on one spec kept finding the same class: a MUST nobody parses

- FINDING: cycles 2–4 of `TASK-260728-1jafds` each rejected a different instance of one pattern. Cycle 2: a TCB record narrower than what is trusted. Cycle 3: `minimum_version` "at or above" with nothing comparing it, and component algorithms named but not constructed. Cycle 4: `host.build` required-but-nullable, and a re-verification MUST whose executable phase list contradicted it.
- ROOT CAUSE: normative prose and the executable form were mirrored by hand. Nothing read the document, so any statement not backed by a check drifted or was born unenforced.
- FIX: the document is now parsed. `validate_host_build_declaration_document` reads §6.3's per-platform build-identity source table and requires it to match what the tools hash; `validate_reverification_documents` requires the §7.2 re-verification section to name every re-verified member and the manager row to state the complete record, byte identity, the subset prohibition, and the joined domain; `hardenedPhaseV1` is pinned to the one ordered phase list.
- FINDING: derive coverage sets from the closed record rather than restating them — `reverifiedMembers()` is computed from `tcbBoundFields()` minus the profile constants, so a member added later is covered the moment it exists. The cycle-4 defect was exactly a hand-written subset (4 of 12) drifting from a hashed record.
- DECISION: for spec work, an assertion is only shipped once a mutation probe shows removing it fails something. Cycle 4 shipped 21/21 rule+document mutations detected with 21 distinct messages, 13/13 adversarial validator instances, 7/7 construction mutations.
- SCOPE: `tools/validate_hardened.py`, `protocol/hardened-execution.md`, `profiles/manager-hardened.md`. Evidence: `TASK-260728-1jafds_rework-cycle-4-{results.md,validation.log,probes.tar.gz}`.

### 1627 — The final CI task's Linux `go test ./...` cannot pass; the rc.5 candidate has no revision to pin

- FINDING: `internal/godriver` is statically Linux-red against the `TASK-260720-jrrgw9` candidate. `rc5-native-control-inventory-v1` records exactly macOS and Windows (`internal/godriver/controls.go:75`), `InventoryPlatform` returns `""` for linux (`controls.go:200`), and `probeNativeControlsFor` rejects with `build_execution_control_unavailable` before the worker (`controls.go:241`). `Build` probes at `build.go:161`. The tests that drive it — `build_test.go`, `worker_test.go`, `boundary_test.go` — carry no build constraint and no Linux skip, and `newSnapshotFixture` (`main_test.go:134`) has no platform guard. `.github/workflows/ci.yml:51` runs `go test ./...` on `ubuntu-latest`. Static prediction, not measured: no Go was run under this audit's scope.
- FINDING: blast radius is confined to `internal/godriver`. Only `cmd/curator` and `internal/install` import it, and neither's tests call `godriver.Build` — they use constants (`SelectionCuratorGo`, `WorkerMode`, `TestedFamilies`) and a `Diagnostic` fixture. So 1pvfj5's named Linux race packages (transaction, buildcache, install, conformance) are all safe, and `internal/interop` imports no godriver either.
- FINDING: the rc.5 candidate cannot be pinned as a revision. `.temp/TASK-260729-3nx97g/worktree` is an *uncommitted* curator-spec tree (3 modified, 18 untracked) on top of `v1.0.0-rc.3`. Only `CURATOR_CONFORMANCE_ROOT` + manifest digest `b6f56aac…04c` (verified) identify it. A hosted GitHub runner cannot reach that path, so candidate jobs are either self-hosted or locally-executed evidence — writing a hosted candidate job that can never fire would be a false gate.
- ANOMALY: the committed CI pin `e72defe…` (`ci.yml:28,81`) is described by no tag and predates `v1.0.0-rc.1` by 24 minutes (`git describe` → "No tags can describe"; `merge-base --is-ancestor 4f24639 e72defe` → NO). `TASK-260720-1pvfj5`'s AC calls it "one immutable currently released pin" — immutable yes, released no. `TASK-260720-38l1sy` owns promotion and should be told.
- FINDING: only rc.1/rc.2/rc.3 tags exist in curator-spec. There is no rc.4 and no rc.5 tag, so all rc.4 wording in 1pvfj5 (checklist item 1, AC, notes) refers to a release that was never cut.
- FINDING: both candidate worktrees sit on `17804ce` and still carry the superseded pin `00b1688a…`; repo HEAD `c06aa1a` carries `e72defe…`. Composing 1pvfj5 on the worktree base would silently revert the committed pin.
- DECISION: recommended shape is to scope the ubuntu `test` step to an explicit package list excluding `internal/godriver`, keep `go vet ./...` at full breadth so Linux still compiles everything, and add one Linux step asserting the fail-closed path (`TestProbeRejectsAnUncoveredPlatformBeforeTheWorker`, `worker_test.go:434`). Adding Linux skips inside godriver was rejected — those files are owned by `jrrgw9`/`1nlmvv` and editing them breaks 1pvfj5's conflict-free surface.
- SCOPE: producer edit surface is exactly `.github/workflows/ci.yml` and `Makefile`. The candidate delta is 23 files, all `_test.go`, zero production drift (`diff -rq` accepted `2kaopg` vs candidate `jrrgw9`). Intersection is empty.
- BLOCKED: `TASK-260720-1pvfj5` is not startable — `TASK-260720-jrrgw9` is still `development`. Linux native validation stays non-gating pending an operator-approved absolute Go 1.25.x GOROOT + trusted identity on `ssh lev` (distro offers 1.26, outside the accepted family) and `TASK-260728-1skseh`, the `until_task` named by the rc.5 exclusion vector.
- SCOPE: `.research/260729_final-curator-ci-execution-map.md`, attached as `TASK-260729-osjeay_final-ci-execution-map.md`.

### 1618 — Spawn logs are the only durable provenance for reverting an out-of-scope board field write

- FINDING: `task-board` keeps no mutation journal, and `.task-board/` content in this repo is **untracked** in git. Neither `git show` nor a board API can recover a field's prior value after a write.
- FINDING: the recoverable provenance is spawn logs. `TASK-260720-12r55p.notes` was reconstructed byte-for-byte — 515 bytes, `3a04adc70cd2e5499608172203a7d3ed17b4ec33e281c4c7ca7b51f24ace2500` — from two independent sources that agree exactly: the original 2026-07-20 `set_notes` mutation result in `STORY-260720-1uv5gi_spawn-log_-analyst--solution-architect--codex-.log:11337`, and a read-only 2026-07-28 board projection captured in `TASK-260729-1kq1rd_spawn-log_-analyst--researcher--codex-_RUN-260728-b72cf7.log:4720`.
- ROOT CAUSE: `TASK-260729-v5hqnv` was scoped to brief `description`/`scope`/`ac` + dependency edges, but cycle 1 also appended a paragraph to `12r55p.notes`. That put a real mutation outside both the authorized scope **and** the before/after JSONL evidence, so the "exact audit" was not exact.
- DECISION: capture the evidence projection over **every field a run might touch**, not just the fields it intends to touch. A before-snapshot narrowed to intended fields cannot detect or undo an unintended write.
- FIX: notes restored via `set_notes(..., set=true)`; the substantive fail-closed gate content rewritten into the authorized `scope` field (1383 → 1979 chars). Net notes change across the task is zero.
- SCOPE: board metadata only. `TASK-260729-v5hqnv`, rework cycle 2. Evidence `TASK-260729-v5hqnv_rework-cycle-2-corrections.md`.

### 1618 — An "exact audit" died on one unverified absolute; scope absence claims to the fields you actually mutated

- ROOT CAUSE: `TASK-260729-v5hqnv` cycle 1 claimed stale rc.4 receipt hash `750f5f75…` was "removed from the board entirely". `task-board grep 750f5f75` returns **29** lines across historical outcome resources and progress records (`TASK-260720-12iigs`, `1nvomm`, `1s1vr6`, `3mrm4z`, `poa3ze`, `cw39jh`, `TASK-260729-1t1z2l`, `3nx97g`). Reviewer disproved it with one grep and returned changes-requested.

- DECISION: scope removal/absence claims to the exact fields actually mutated, and run the grep **before** writing an absolute. A board-wide negative is a claim about ~29 files nobody checked.
- NOTE: same discipline surfaced a second honest caveat — the retargeted `12r55p.scope` now contains one literal `rc.4` string, intentionally, because it describes the still-rc.4 `TASK-260720-3ag6pi` gate rather than retargeting the brief. Recorded explicitly rather than left to look like residue.

### 1615 — CocoaSkills conformance is already red against rc.5, and the cause is a fixture filename, not protocol semantics

- REGRESSION: `tests/test_protocol_conformance.py` is **98 passed** against the committed default pin (`relux-works/curator-spec@cbe912d0`, protocol `1.0.0-rc.2`, manifest `728f772950414b9c3ddf38a8f1e9f2c7d2953bdca1d8c135c7e1a9abf40fff06`, 81 files) but **1 failed / 97 passed** against the immutable rc.5 root (manifest `b6f56aacc0e37dcc6692f73f641bff761e89b645adfe20a47a06d81c6fda204c`, 447 files).
- ROOT CAUSE: the shared fixture manifest was renamed `csk-skill.json` → `agent-skill.json`. The two bodies are semantically identical (schema 5, `runtime_roots: ["scripts"]`, one script command) and `expected/marker.json` is byte-identical between the roots. `csk.skillspec.load_skill_spec` only knows `csk-skill.json` and silently returns `SkillSpec(commands={}, source_file=None)`, so `include_scripts` flips to true and `whitelist.copy_context` pulls `scripts/golden-tool` into agent context.
- FINDING: the failure mode is worse than the red test. A silent empty spec makes `fixtures/go-build-skill` (schema 6, `build_roots: ["assets/build-tool"]`) assertions **vacuous** rather than red — build-root context exclusion would "pass" while exercising nothing.
- DECISION: `TASK-260720-z9j4c9` owns the fix (its description already says "canonical and legacy manifest parity") but its **AC never names `agent-skill.json`**, and it is not one of the seven briefs `TASK-260729-v5hqnv` retargeted. Exact recommended AC clause is in §10.2 of the design artifact. Not applied — this task is read-only design.
- FINDING: independence is provable today with zero product edits. `csk.audit_registry.canonical_bytes` reproduces the 869-byte `build-input.ccj.json` and the 1120-byte `receipt.ccj.json` byte-for-byte, re-derives all three `cache_identity` keys (`529370…` valid, `3fcd714a…` and `13736230…` invalid, `aliases: false`, distinct), and `expected/build-driver/marker.json` equals `portable_identity.marker`. `hashing.content_sha256(fixtures/skill)` reproduces `expected/snapshot_sha256.txt` (`f2c21f31…`) exactly.
- FINDING: the two framed preimages decode in pure Python — one-byte record tag (`D`/`F`/`L`/`V`) + uint64 **big-endian** path and payload lengths after a `curator-build-source-v1\0` / `curator-go-toolchain-v1\0` domain prefix. No Go source needs reading or copying to specify the writer.
- FINDING (structural): `schemas/v1/` lives **outside** `CURATOR_CONFORMANCE_ROOT` and is not covered by `conformance/v1/manifest.json` (which covers only `schema-cases`, `expected`, `fixtures`, `vectors`). Every schema-derived assertion in the `12r55p` AC is instead satisfiable from manifest-covered `schema-cases/`, so no `jsonschema` dependency and no second oracle is introduced.
- DECISION: cluster gating is **fail-on-missing, never skip-on-missing**. Parity-map §3.3 documented Curator's `TestCandidateBuildMetadataArtifacts` silently SKIPping for the whole rc.5 candidate line while reporting exit 0. The Python harness declares a per-cluster introducing protocol version: below it → honest skip naming both versions; at or above it with the files absent → hard `AssertionError`.
- NOTE: `TASK-260720-3pemm6` converged independently. `v5hqnv` retargeted it mid-run to "Ubuntu … prove the source-aware go-v1 path fails closed with an unavailable-control error and no worker launch", which is the same three-tier boundary §7 of the design derives — tier A data assertions everywhere, tiers B/C on macOS/Windows, Linux asserted fail-closed rather than skipped.
- SCOPE: read-only. `TASK-260729-1b9tc3`. Artifact `TASK-260729-1b9tc3_rc5-conformance-consumer-design.md` (`aa2513c5a1bc138024eb59a858db67aa19ef3e350e41a6c7d22d71272e0b55ec`), byte-identical to `.planning/260729_csk-rc5-conformance-consumer-design.md`. No CocoaSkills or Curator file created, edited, staged or committed; no pin, release or publication touched.
- STATUS: ready for review.

### 1609 — A compiled-status plan may be frozen and replayed only for evidence the planner does not read; protected-cache state is not that

- FINDING: `planOne` (`internal/install/plan.go:469-530`) derives its `buildmeta.Input` from build source, build root, command, source dir, target, toolchain and fixed policy only. It never reads the install marker and never reads the installed skill dir — so a marker rewrite, a marker refusal, or a directory materialized inside the installed tree cannot change what a plan derives. Those cases are safely replayable from one acquired plan.
- FINDING: the planner *does* read protected-cache evidence — `cache.Inspect` at `plan.go:504-509`, embedded into the planned facts at `plan.go:521-528` → `factsOf` (`cmd/curator/builds.go:173-190`). `recheckBuildCache` (`builds.go:241-262`) re-takes that lookup and `statusReport` (`cmd/curator/main.go:795-808`) overwrites the classified state with `build-state-changed` on any difference. Replaying a pre-tamper plan over moved cache state therefore publishes `build-state-changed`, never the case's own code.
- ROOT CAUSE of the cycle-1 audit defect: the permission case at reviewed-snapshot `cmd/curator/status_test.go:523-539` (`chmod 0o777` on cache entries) was grouped with the marker cases. `0o777` trips the `stat.Mode & 0o022` boundary test (`internal/buildcache/protection_unix.go:239,259`), flipping the inspected outcome from `cache-hit` to untrusted provenance (`internal/buildcache/cache.go:167-168`). All **three** protected-cache mutations need a plan acquired after their own tamper, not two.
- FINDING: for a cache case, a live in-process plan + replay and a real `status --json --check` cost exactly the same one plan — the plan is the whole cost. The real CLI is strictly better: same price, and it additionally proves the published document and the fail-closed process exit.
- FINDING: taking a live post-tamper plan is always safe for these three. `blocking()` (`plan.go:56-60`) is true only for `unsupported` and `toolchain-unavailable`, so `corrupt`, `would-preflight-and-build` and `would-rebuild-untrusted-cache` all still return `Status == "ok"`.
- NOTE: the accepted precedent already states the rule — `cmd/curator/global_status_test.go:338-343` and its live-plan cache group at `:466-480`.
- SCOPE: `TASK-260729-1y7okj` cycle-1 rework, read-only. Artifacts `TASK-260729-1y7okj_runtime-optimization-audit.md` (rev. 2) and `TASK-260729-1y7okj_reviewed-snapshot-provenance.md`.
- STATUS: ready for review.

### 1608 — Auditing a live worktree owned by another in-flight producer is not reproducible; freeze the snapshot instead

- ANOMALY: `.temp/TASK-260720-jrrgw9/worktree/cmd/curator/status_test.go` moved three times in one afternoon — `4c2339dd…` (what verifier 2 failed on and what the audit was written against) → `bb21c15c…` (15:54:27) → `487b12bd…` (mtime 15:54:47) — none of it written by the auditing or reviewing agent.

Base `c62d0791…` (`.temp/TASK-260729-2kaopg/worktree/cmd/curator/status_test.go`) plus board diff `TASK-260720-jrrgw9_status-timing-patch.diff` (`03c637da…`) reproduces `4c2339dd…` byte-exactly. All three digests were already recorded in `TASK-260720-jrrgw9_status-timing-patch-results.md:21,23,25`, which is what made the reconstruction auditable.
- FINDING: the moving candidate is on a different cost axis — a shared-fixture rework folding five top-level tests into `TestCompiledProjectStatusRepairRollbackRecovery` over one `compiledProjectFixture`, removing duplicate *installs*. The audit removes duplicate *plans*. Additive, not competing. It has already implemented the audit's R1 (`snapshotBuildCacheAfter` replaced `t.Cleanup(reinstall)`) and deliberately retains the per-case plan R2 targets.
- REGRESSION risk recorded: that rework renames tests, so `-run '^TestStatusReportsCompiledCurrentnessAndFailsCheck$'`-style selectors in any preserved allowlist now match nothing — and a Go `-run` matching nothing exits 0. Any timing measured with a stale selector is silently meaningless.
- NOTE: second trap — the repo-root main checkout `cmd/curator/main.go` (`1d2cc9c9…`) is a pre-feature file whose `cmdStatus` has no compiled-command surface. Product line references must come from the worktree file (`a0798e7d…`).
- SCOPE: `TASK-260729-1y7okj`, read-only. No file in either worktree was written.
- STATUS: recorded.

### 1606 — Withdrawn build-cache entries are never collected by the ordinary sweep, and `Revert`'s doc comment says they are

- FINDING: `internal/buildcache/publish.go:366` documents on `Revert` that "the withdrawn entry is quarantined exactly like any other unusable one and is collected by the ordinary sweep". `Sweep` does not collect it.
- ROOT CAUSE: `internal/buildcache/collect.go:133` classifies cache-root members three ways — `entryNameRE` (`^[0-9a-f]{64}$`, `collect.go:31`), `sweepPrefix` (`".sweep-"`, `collect.go:29`), and `default`. `quarantinePrefix` is `".quarantine-"` (`publish.go:156`) and matches neither, so every withdrawn entry falls to `default`, is warned about as an *unrecognized cache root member* (`collect.go:148`), and is retained forever. Each `gc` re-emits the warning.
- FINDING: unguarded today. No test asserts the sweep's treatment of `.quarantine-*`, and the corrupt-repair and untrusted-repair paths both withdraw entries, so a long-lived manager home accumulates them silently.
- DECISION: ROUTED, not fixed. `TASK-260720-jrrgw9` is test-focused; the defect belongs to the owning build-cache implementation task. Fix is either teaching `Sweep` to collect `quarantinePrefix` members under the same grace rule, or correcting the `Revert` doc comment and documenting an operator reclaim path — plus a regression test for whichever is chosen.
- SCOPE: observed while reworking `cmd/curator/status_test.go` onto one shared compiled fixture. Because the cache root is now shared across cases, the rollback assertion `quarantinedEntries(t, home) != 1` became a delta against a baseline sampled before the failing run; the claim ("exactly the one this run published") is unchanged.
- STATUS: pending — needs an owning implementation task.

### 1545 — cmd/curator runtime is 24 cold compiles + 55 double-GOROOT fingerprints; ~100s is recoverable test-side

- ROOT CAUSE: two product mechanisms own essentially all `cmd/curator` wall time, and neither memoizes. (1) Every CLI invocation against a scope with a compiled command runs `planBuilds` (`internal/install/plan.go:363`) -> `Probe`/`Establish` -> `godriver.establish` (`internal/godriver/session.go:157`), which runs three `go` subprocesses and then `fingerprintToolchain` (`internal/godriver/fingerprint.go:34`) over the whole GOROOT — **233 MB / 14,502 files, re-opened and SHA-256'd** — and `Session.Close` (`session.go:332`) fingerprints it a *second* time via `VerifyToolchain`. `Probe` is documented as not memoizing (`session.go:140`). (2) `bootstrapEnvironment` (`session.go:404`) points `GOCACHE` at the operation-private root, so every real compiled install compiles from an empty build cache.
- FINDING: derived unit costs — one plan-only compiled invocation `P ~ 3.2s`, one real rebuild `B ~ 8-11s`, a cache-hit install ~4s. Solved from three preserved measurements (167.062s / 69.816s / 267.207s). Static inventory of the package gives ~24 real builds + ~55 plan-only invocations, which predicts ~463s against the known accepted clean baseline of 465.944s — the model reproduces reality to within 1%.
- FINDING: `builds_test.go`, `gc_test.go`, `main_test.go` and `lifecycle_conformance_test.go` activate **no** compiled command (`lifecycle_conformance_test.go:233` publishes `"commands": {}`). All compiled cost is in `status_test.go` + `global_status_test.go`.
- FINDING: `TestStatusJSONKeepsTheLegacyShapeWithoutCompiledCommands` was only *active* at the alarm — it uses `legacyProject`, no toolchain session, and the stack below it was a live `os.Lstat`, i.e. forward progress. Not a hang.
- FINDING: the biggest remaining duplicate is `t.Cleanup(func() { reinstall(t) })` at `cmd/curator/status_test.go:1216` — four real compiled reconciliations used purely to reset a fixture, when all four moves mutate only `home/cache/build` and the accepted `snapshotBuildCacheAfter` (`cmd/curator/global_status_test.go:185`) restores exactly that tree byte-and-mode.
- FINDING: the project drift matrix can be replayed from one frozen plan the way `global_status_test.go:386` already does, because `planOne` (`internal/install/plan.go:400`) takes source token, target, toolchain and cache — **never** the install marker. The two cache-damage cases must keep their own live plans; the eleven marker/context cases cannot change what a plan derives.
- FINDING (trap): in a replayed project-scope case `markerDigests` must be taken *after* the tamper. Taken before, every marker case classifies as `build-state-changed` instead of its own code — the exact mechanism `TestStatusReportsProtectedCacheStateThatMovedDuringTheCheck` exploits. This is the likeliest way to produce a green-but-wrong patch.
- ANOMALY: `internal/install/atomicity` measured **543.719s** and `internal/install` **411.094s** against the same unchanged 600s deadline. `atomicity` sits 56s from its own timeout — a second latent failure site on the same gate, independent of `cmd/curator`.
- DECISION: no new board element created. The rework is already owned by `TASK-260720-jrrgw9` and the product-side fingerprint work closed under `TASK-260729-1zex8r`; a parallel task would duplicate an in-flight owner.
- SCOPE: read-only audit of `.temp/TASK-260720-jrrgw9/worktree` at HEAD `17804cea`. No file in either worktree was modified; `status_test.go` digest unchanged at `4c2339dd…`. `TASK-260729-1y7okj`.
- STATUS: pending — ranked test-only plan projects ~86s clean / ~101-111s at `./...` load, attached as `TASK-260729-1y7okj_runtime-optimization-audit.md`. Projection only; no Go command was run by this audit.

### 1527 — macOS measured: 6 of 11 hardened capability classes available, 1 of 6 guarantees established

- FINDING: executable probes on macOS 26.5 / arm64 / SIP enabled / go1.25.5, unprivileged uid 502. Available: `network-syscall-denial`, `read-only-source-view`, `read-only-toolchain-view`, `write-path-confinement`, `exec-path-allowlist`, `active-capability-probe`. Unavailable: `domain-membership-enforcement`, `domain-atomic-termination`, `preexisting-endpoint-revocation`, `filesystem-view-restriction`, `aggregate-resource-bounds`. Only `fail-closed-capability-preflight` is established; harness exits 1.
- FINDING: no unescapable domain. A descendant that calls `setsid` leaves the process group/session — the only grouping a plain macOS supervisor can create — and survives the group-directed `SIGKILL`; the non-detached control is destroyed, so the teardown itself works. The survivor *does* keep its seatbelt policy (inheritance is real and cannot be renounced), but that binds access rules only, not accounting or termination membership. `domain-membership-enforcement` is required by 4 of 6 guarantees, so this one fact blocks network denial, private writes, resource bounds and exec allowlisting even where the control itself measured available.
- FINDING: the dynamic loader forces the root namespace open. A `(deny default)` profile omitting read access to the literal `/` aborts every dynamically linked program during `dyld` startup with `SIGABRT`; metadata-only access is insufficient, `dyld` needs `file-read-data`. Granting it leaves `/` enumerable, so `filesystem-view-restriction` fails. Property of the backend, not fixable with a better profile. Individual undeclared reads (`/etc/passwd`, `/Users`, home, `.ssh`) are still denied, confirmed against an uncontained negative control.
- FINDING: `RLIMIT_NOFILE` binds per process, not per tree — a child gets a fresh budget under the same soft limit, so parent+child open past the declared cap of 64. Nothing refused a write past a declared 4 MiB budget below the private build root: macOS has no per-directory byte cap. `RLIMIT_NPROC` is per user, not per operation.
- FINDING: a socket connected before the domain existed stays writable inside it. Seatbelt is evaluated at `exec` and inherited descriptors are not re-evaluated, so network *denial* is real for new endpoints but *revocation* of a pre-existing one is not available.
- FINDING: two traps for any reimplementation. (1) A nil `Stdout`/`Stderr` makes `os/exec` open `/dev/null`, which `(deny default)` denies; the `EPERM` from `Start` is indistinguishable from the exec denial being probed, so every stream must be wired explicitly. (2) `PROT_EXEC` on any file returns `EPERM` on Apple Silicon without a JIT entitlement, so the `mmap`-exec escape fails for a platform reason and cannot be credited to the allowlist.
- FINDING: a seatbelt filter matches the absolute path the kernel resolved. A relative root, or one reached through a symlink (`/tmp` and `/var` are symlinks on macOS), matches nothing and turns every probe into a denial — which looks exactly like perfect enforcement. `NewEnvironment` rejects unresolved paths for this reason.
- DECISION: every available verdict rests on the seatbelt profile language, which is private and unversioned, reached through `sandbox_init`, which `<sandbox.h>` declares deprecated. Neither is a basis for a production guarantee independent of what was measured. Virtualization.framework is the only public macOS mechanism that satisfies the three blocking classes; `hdiutil` fixed-size images are the only public way to bound bytes below a build root.
- NOTE: measured confirmation of the macOS finding in `.research/260727_go-v1-host-execution-decision.md`, reached there by documentation review. No contradiction — macOS must reject `go-v1` before starting any worker or Go process.
- SCOPE: `prototypes/macos-hardened-probes/` in the `TASK-260729-3jmqgl` worktree only. Separate Go module, not built by the `Makefile`, imported by nothing. Not staged, committed or published. `TASK-260729-3jmqgl`.
- STATUS: handed to review. `go build`/`go vet`/`gofmt -l`/`go test`/`golangci-lint` all exit 0; coverage 81.2–100% per package; the 11-class fail-closed sweep passes.

Loop cost 44 -> 17 compiled plans.

### 1131 — Only protected cache state is observable by the read-only plan; markers and context are not

- FINDING: the currentness verdict is derived from two independent sources, and which one a state comes from decides whether a CLI test can reuse an already acquired plan. `buildReport.CacheOutcome` is `facts.Outcome`, taken from `install.PlannedBuild` (`cmd/curator/builds.go:173`), so the read-only plan itself inspects the protected cache. Install markers and installed-directory contents are read only during classification (`classifySkillBuilds`, `contextExposure`, `cmd/curator/builds.go:345`).
- CONSEQUENCE: `build-source-drift`, `build-input-drift`, `build-command-drift`, and `build-context-exposed` are fully reproducible from one plan acquired before the tamper. `corrupt-build-cache` and `missing-build-artifact` are not — a replayed plan would publish cache evidence that no longer exists — so each needs its own acquisition while the tampered state is live.
- DECISION: `cmd/curator/global_status_test.go` classifies each case by that boundary instead of re-planning per assertion. `curator global status` split into parse / acquire / classify-render-check phases (`cmd/curator/main.go:1145`) so a test drives the production classifier from a plan it already holds; production still acquires exactly one fresh plan per invocation, in the same order relative to the marker-fingerprint bracketing.
- RESULT: focused `TestGlobalStatus*` coverage run 110.541s -> 43.022s; compiled-scope plans 26 -> 8; real compiled installs 4 -> 2. Two cache cases now snapshot and restore protected cache bytes and permission bits instead of running a real reinstall as fixture cleanup.
- SCOPE: `cmd/curator/main.go`, `cmd/curator/global_status_test.go`. No change to `builds.go`, `statusReport`, project-scope `cmdStatus`, `internal/`, or the documented `--json`/`--check` contract.

### 0845 — A contract-stated defence that no check asserted, and the binding model it needed

- FIX: `TASK-260728-1yhuqi` cycle-5 rework. The compile permit is now a step in the normative session order — `AdmitSources` → `swiftc -###` → `VerifyPlan` → **permit** → `swiftc compile_argv` — and `compile_argv` is reachable only through it. The session records that the step ran and over how many bindings, so the call is measured rather than inferred.
- ROOT CAUSE: the mechanism existed and every unit test called it; the thing that actually runs did not. No check went red because no check asserted the step. Cheap generalisation: a security step needs an assertion on the *integrated path*, not only on the function.
- FINDING: a binding must record the path whose identity was **actually** established, not the token the plan carried. They differ for a not-yet-created output, where the operation-private parent was verified; re-resolving the raw token there rejects every valid happy path, so the naive integration forces you to weaken or bypass the check. Split into `raw` and `checked`; measured 5 of 33 plan bindings are absent outputs re-checked at their parent.
- FINDING: `ENOENT` is evidence of absence and nothing else is. A permission or I/O error while re-checking an absent plugin path now fails closed.
- FINDING: file identity (size/mode/mtime) is restorable by a writer, so Stage B binds the **digest** of the bytes it scanned and the permit re-reads every admitted source. Unit test writes one byte in place, restores mtime, asserts identity is unchanged, and still requires the finding.
- ANOMALY: two measurement mistakes worth remembering — a shim invoked by the session must use shell builtins only, because the contract runs children with an empty `PATH` and `cat` does not resolve; and a live-vs-retired comparison must run *at* the permit, because the graph command's own `TemporaryDirectory` does not survive the compile command.
- EVIDENCE: 23/23 cases, 32 closure checks with no verdict, **17/17** controls red and each replayed individually, **70/70** structural checks, executed P2 admission, `green: true`. New `S65`-`S72` run whole sessions with an adversary acting after plan verification; each adversarial case must name its own mechanism and prove 1 manager command and no artifact. New controls `C16` (session without the permit still starts 2 commands) and `C17` (retired binding model: 5 vs 0 findings on a valid happy path, 0 vs 1 on an unreadable absent plugin). Probe sha256 `4465da8689f68031ef7c1908369c6cdf44aa8e18ebdc3f6db5de9dbfef37f5b9`.
- STATUS: handed to review. Residual added in both documents — the permit closes the graph-to-permit interval, not the permit-to-`open` interval.

### 0856 — Cycle-4 cache compensation still loses post-rename quarantine failures

- REGRESSION: `TASK-260720-1nlmvv` cycle 4 compensates selection/validation/root-sync and restore faults, but `quarantinePath` renames the live entry before syncing its parent and returns `("", error)` if that sync fails. `Publish` has not yet recorded `displaced`, so its defer skips compensation; `restoreDisplaced` likewise has no withdrawn path to return.

The advertised focused buildcache/install/CLI gates, build, vet, formatting, and diff checks all exit 0, proving the gap is an uncovered interior mutation boundary rather than a general mechanical failure.

Add a deterministic post-rename/pre-sync fault point; both expected-red regressions must preserve a non-empty live slot. Board artifact: `TASK-260720-1nlmvv_tester-evidence-cycle-4.md`.

### 0818 — Scoped fingerprint walk can reinterpret a replaced directory as a file leaf

- REGRESSION: `TASK-260729-1zex8r` now re-resolves digest-phase files from GOROOT and closes the prior detached-handle failure, but collection still derives descent from the later canonical record kind. If an entry was listed/Lstat'd as a directory and its root path becomes a file before the root-relative recheck, the scoped walk records `F`, skips descent, and can omit the directory's former descendants.
- LEGACY: `fs.WalkDir` retains the listed `DirEntry` for its post-callback descent decision. It therefore still attempts to read that path as a directory and fails closed instead of accepting the replacement leaf.
- EVIDENCE: new `TestToolchainWalkRejectsDirectoryReplacedByFile`; focused command exits 1 at `fingerprint_equivalence_test.go:854` with `scoped walk accepted a file in place of a listed directory`. The prior digest-phase regression test exits 0. Gofmt/diff checks exit 0. Board artifact: `TASK-260729-1zex8r_tester-collection-race.md`.
- REQUIRED: keep the listed-directory descent decision independent of the root-relative record classification and reject a directory-to-nondirectory mismatch before appending a leaf. Then rerun godriver, conformance/vector, build/vet/style, coverage, benchmark, and clean `cmd/curator` gates.

### 0809 — Swift cycle-5 review found permit-time re-binding outside the exercised session

- REVIEW: `TASK-260728-1yhuqi` changes requested and routed to `analysis`. Cycle 4's two findings are closed in the normative documents, and independent replay is green: 23/23 cases, 32 closure checks with no verdict, 15/15 controls (also replayed individually), 62/62 structural checks, and executed P2 admission.
- ROOT CAUSE: the probe's authoritative `session` performs source admission → `swiftc -###` → `VerifyPlan` → `swiftc compile_argv`, but never calls the mandatory `Reverify` gate immediately before compile. S20/S21 exercise `Reverify` only in isolated demonstrations, so S60/S62/S64 prove cardinality and output, not that the second command received a binding-reverified permit.
- FINDING: integrating the current function unchanged would reject the normal absent-output path. Output verification records the existing parent identity but retains the absent final output as `Raw`; `Reverify` calls `EvalSymlinks(Raw)` and fails because the output correctly does not exist yet. The binding must retain the actual checked path. An absent-plugin recheck must also reject non-`ENOENT` `Lstat` errors instead of treating them as continued absence.
- REQUIRED: integrate fail-closed permit-time re-binding into the session; repair output-parent binding; add integrated mutations for appearing plugins, changed/re-pointed sources (including introducing `@`/`#` after Stage B), executable/search changes, and output-parent replacement; prove the compile command does not start; add an expected-red skipped-rebind control and replay all gates.
- EVIDENCE: board outcome `TASK-260728-1yhuqi_review-verdict-cycle-5.md`; submitted probe SHA-256 `3446bfa42c24bad7102982b0b5e117f9a7f31cf9b3956c45eed37bb1510ba16b`; standalone format/vet/test/build all exit 0; degraded run exits 0 with 23 `not_run`.

### 0755 — A one-command execution policy forces the Swift macro boundary onto the source bytes

- DECISION: `swift-v1` / `swift-repository-v1` reject any compiled source carrying the byte `0x40` (`@`) or `0x23` (`#`) anywhere, and any source that is not well-formed UTF-8, at Stage B before the graph phase. `curator-swift-source-admission-v1`. `TASK-260728-1yhuqi`, decision 0011 §4, reference §3.3.
- ROOT CAUSE: cycle 3 closed the macro surface by executing the `swiftc -###` plan's jobs from the manager with the plugin flags deleted. That works and is **not** `manager-worker-v2`: accepted decision 0008 §7 admits at most one graph command and exactly one compile command, started by the driver's own trusted launcher. Measured: the design starts **4** manager commands for the default two-source build (1 graph + 3 plan jobs) and starts `swift-frontend`/`clang` directly, changing both cardinality and parentage while still binding the v2 label.
- FINDING: the completeness of a two-byte rule is not an argument from the Swift grammar — **the compiler states it**. `externalMacro(module:type:)` without the sigil → `error: expansion of macro … requires leading '#'`; a `macro` declaration with no role attribute → `error: macro … must declare its applicable roles via '@freestanding' or '@attached'`. Those two diagnostics are the whole proof.
- FINDING: no side channel reaches the sigil. Fullwidth `＠` U+FF20 is an identifier character (parse error), `\u{40}` outside a literal is not syntax (parse error), a bare attribute name is not an attribute (parse error), and an overlong UTF-8 encoding of U+0040 (`0xC1 0x80`) carries no `0x40` byte but is rejected as `invalid UTF-8 found in source file`. The last one is why the encoding rule must run **before** the byte scan: "no `0x40` byte implies no U+0040" only holds on well-formed UTF-8.
- FINDING: under one `swiftc` compile command the injected plugin search paths cannot be removed, so they are stated **inert** rather than absent — 5 components in the plan, **0** macro-load remarks for an admitted source set, artifact runs. `import Observation` with no macro use also loads nothing: a macro-bearing module is a prebuilt `.swiftmodule` whose expansion already happened in the library build.
- DECISION: the byte rule over-rejects and the collateral is inventoried, not discovered — `@main`, `@available`, `@escaping`, `@inlinable`, `@_cdecl`, `@_silgen_name`, property wrappers, `#if`, `#available`, `#file`, `#selector`, raw string literals `#"…"#`, extended regex `#/…/#`, and any sigil in a comment or string literal. Two matrix rows moved from *admit, bounded* to *reject*. A narrower rule needs a Swift lexer in the manager — a second implementation of a grammar the compiler owns.
- ANOMALY: `swift-frontend -scan-dependencies` reports `macroDependencies` as the module's dependency **closure**, not its use — a macro-free file still lists `SwiftMacros` while loading nothing. Compiler-side macro *detection* is unusable as a boundary; only byte rejection or channel removal work.
- REGRESSION: restoring `compile_argv` gives up cycle 3's literal "the plan verified is the plan executed" — it is back to an equality of inputs (one builder, one source set, one environment, one inserted token) plus permit-time re-binding. Stated as a residual in decision §14 / reference §11 rather than papered over; closing it needs a new execution-policy identity, which is a decision 0008 change.
- SCOPE: `.temp/TASK-260728-1yhuqi/probe-module/{sourceadmission.go,sourceadmission_test.go,structural_cycle4.go}`; probe 56→62 structural checks, 14→15 expected-red controls, 55→63 tests. New controls: `C13` (retired Stage B admits macro source to a permit), `C15` (retired plan execution starts 4 commands).
- STATUS: cycle-4 rework ready for review.


### 0730 — GOROOT fingerprint: 84% of its cost was `os.Root` path resolution, not hashing

- ROOT CAUSE: `fingerprintToolchain` opened one `os.Root` over all of GOROOT and re-resolved every root-relative path one `O_NOFOLLOW` component at a time **three times** per entry — once in `fs.WalkDir(root.FS(), …)`, once in `root.Lstat(name)`, once in `root.Open(record.filesystem)`. At depth ~5 that is ~15 `openat` calls per file instead of ~3. Only 16% of the profiled time was reading and hashing bytes.
- FIX: `internal/godriver/fingerprint.go` — phase 1 descends through one `*os.Root` per directory (`toolchainWalk.descend`); phase 2 resolves each file through `scopedDirs`, a stack of ancestor handles. Measured A/B in one process over the host GOROOT: **3.42–3.60 s → 0.99–1.04 s, 3.4x**. `TASK-260729-1zex8r`.
- FINDING: sorted full-path order is **not** the same as sorted pre-order DFS. `a/b`, `a/b/c`, `a/bb` interleaves a directory with its own subtree whenever a sibling name starts with a byte below `/` (`-`, `.`, `!`, digits are all below `0x2F`). A single-slot directory cache thrashes on exactly that pattern — this is why the ancestor **stack** lands at 3.4x where the `TASK-260729-2kaopg` single-slot prototype measured 2.2x. The explicit `sort.Slice` on records is load-bearing and cannot be replaced by relying on walk order.
- DECISION: symlink validation stays anchored at GOROOT (`walk.root.Stat(protocolPath)`), not at the link's own scoped directory. The accepted RC4 vector contains `pkg/tool-link -> ../bin/go`, which resolves inside the root but **outside its own directory**; a scoped `Stat` would reject a conforming toolchain. Comment left in code against a future "simplification".
- FINDING: per-component `parent.OpenRoot(name)` is **stricter** than full-path `root.OpenRoot(dir)` — it refuses a component that escapes its own parent even when the target stays inside the root. Unreachable for real toolchains (phase 1 records only lstat-confirmed directories, so no descended component is a link) and fail-closed under a TOCTOU swap. No containment guarantee weakens.
- FINDING: `os.Root.FS().ReadDir` sorts by name (`os/root.go:393`), so `fs.WalkDir` never saw unsorted entries. The canonical record set is order-independent, but **which of several violations reports first** is not — error precedence had to be reproduced explicitly and is now pinned by a dedicated test.
- SCOPE: `internal/godriver/fingerprint.go`, new `internal/godriver/fingerprint_equivalence_test.go`. The pre-change traversal is preserved verbatim in-package as `legacyFingerprintToolchain`, so equivalence is asserted against a real reference — identical digest, identical record set, identical diagnostic code and detail — over 13 tree shapes, 5 fail-closed cases, 4 error-precedence cases and the real host GOROOT (16093 records, identical digest).
- NOTE: `internal/godriver/testdata/realbuild/build/vendor/example.test` is a vendored **module directory**, not a compiled binary. `rsync --exclude='*.test'` silently drops it when seeding a worktree.
- STATUS: ready for review.

### 0724 — A build-cache entry cannot be swapped atomically with a journaled install

- ROOT CAUSE: `runCommit` publishes build winners before target staging because a launcher can only point at a protected entry that is already live. The cache is not a transaction target, so every fault between publication and durability left the displaced entry quarantined and the replacement live — a persistent change by an operation that committed nothing, and one that silently rewrites what `status` says about the *previous* install (`corrupt-build-cache`, which install repairs → receipt/artifact drift against an entry nobody asked for).
- DECISION: compensation, not a journaled cache target. Journaling the entry would move the swap after preparation and require the transaction engine to own protected 0700 directories, their sidecars, and their interaction with the sweep. `buildcache.Revert` + `PublicationResult.Quarantined` withdraw the replacement and restore the predecessor by rename only — nothing is deleted, the sweep collects the leftovers. `internal/buildcache/publish.go`, `internal/install/commit.go`. `TASK-260720-1nlmvv`.
- FINDING: a returned `Journal.Commit` error does **not** by itself mean the transaction rolled back. `engine.commit` rolls back in-process on a target fault, but a journal-write failure at `Prepared → Committing` leaves every target at its preimage while recovery will commit **forward**. "Revert on any commit error" is therefore wrong. `reversiblePublication` requires two facts: every journaled target back at its recorded preimage, **and** no live journal still referencing the published keys (`ReferencedBuildKeys`). The second closes exactly that window.
- DECISION: the refusal is asymmetric on purpose. Keeping a valid unreferenced entry is always safe (the sweep takes it); restoring an unusable predecessor over an entry a recovered commit points at turns a reported failure into a broken install. `Result.BuildCacheRetained` carries that case out so `repairNotices` never repeats "the live build cache is unchanged" when it is not.
- FINDING: a **same-key cache replacement** — new artifact bytes plus the canonical receipt that exactly describes them — stays a valid `Hit`, so nothing about it looks broken. It is invisible to every check except re-reading the receipt identity, and it is the only cache change that produces a stale *drift* verdict (`corrupt-build-receipt`) rather than a stale *current* one. `status` now re-takes the exact planning lookup after classification (`PlannedBuild.Expectation` → `recheckBuildCache`); marker fingerprints alone never caught it.
- FINDING: redacting *every* commit error would be wrong — journal and target failures name the operator's own project paths, which are the actionable part. Only failures that originate at the build-cache boundary carry manager-private state, so they are marked (`buildPhaseError`) and routed through `Result.failCommit`. `runtimestore.CompiledTargetFromCache` is in that set: it receives the private absolute artifact path.
- ANOMALY: `golangci-lint` v2.4.0 reported 2 gosec `G304` hits on files that are byte-identical to the accepted base and carry `// #nosec G304` on the offending line. Linting those packages alone → 0 issues; after `golangci-lint cache clean`, full `./...` → 0 issues. Its own cache, not `GOCACHE` (2.2 GiB, untouched). Treat a sudden `#nosec`-suppression failure as a stale lint cache before hunting the code.
- SCOPE: `internal/buildcache/publish.go`, `internal/install/{commit,install,global,plan,targets}.go`, `cmd/curator/{builds,main}.go`, README. Gates: `go test ./...` 0 (40 pkgs), lint 0 issues, gofmt/build/vet 0.
- STATUS: cycle-3 rework ready for review.

### 0723 — Cached GOROOT directory handles can hash a detached tree after replacement

- REGRESSION: `TASK-260729-1zex8r`'s `scopedDirs` reuses a directory handle solely because its cached component string still matches. Rename that directory out of GOROOT and install a replacement at the same path: legacy `root.Open("pkg/second")` reads the replacement, while the scoped lookup reads `"old-second"` through the detached handle.
- IMPACT: the file-level `os.SameFile` guard also passes because both the recorded identity and opened file belong to the detached old tree. The optimization therefore widens the mutation window from one file open to the lifetime of the cached directory handle and violates the task's legacy-equivalence/no-race-regression requirement.
- EVIDENCE: new `TestScopedDirsDoesNotReadFromAReplacedDirectory`; focused `go test -count=1 ./internal/godriver -run '^TestScopedDirsDoesNotReadFromAReplacedDirectory$'` exits 1 at `fingerprint_equivalence_test.go:667`. Formatting and diff checks exit 0. Board artifact: `TASK-260729-1zex8r_tester-mutation-regression.md`.
- REQUIRED: rework reused-handle path-binding validation, then rerun differential mutation, go-v1 conformance/vector, performance, and default-timeout gates. The tester continuation forbids product edits, so no workaround assertion or product change was added.

### 0700 — Swift macro loading cannot be suppressed by argv; the manager must execute the plan itself

- FINDING: on Apple Swift 6.3.2 / macOS 26.5 arm64, `swiftc` injects plugin search paths into every frontend job unasked, and **nothing in the command line moves or disables them**. `-resource-dir <other>` moves 0 of 10 plugin components; invoking `swiftc` through a manager-owned symlink moves 0 of 10 because the driver resolves its own executable first; pointing `-in-process-plugin-server-path` at an absent file does not stop the load because the implementation is `dlopen`ed directly; and no `swiftc`/`swift-frontend` flag disables plugin-path injection.
- FINDING: the channel is nevertheless entirely flag-driven. `swift-frontend -frontend -c` with **no** plugin flag rejects `@Observable` with `plugin for module 'ObservationMacros' not found` and loads nothing; add `-plugin-path <toolchain>/usr/lib/swift/host/plugins` and it loads `libObservationMacros.dylib`.
- DECISION: Curator's `swift-v1` / `swift-repository-v1` driver executes the **verified `-###` plan job by job** with the closed plugin-flag set deleted, instead of handing the compile vector to `swiftc`. Measured end to end: plain and Foundation/Codable/regex sources build and the artifact runs; `@Observable` fails closed with 0 load remarks; two passes to the same output path are byte-identical despite different random intermediate temp dirs. Side effect: "the plan verified is the plan executed" stops being an inference about a deterministic planner and becomes enforced.
- FINDING: `swift-frontend -scan-dependencies` is **not** a usable macro-use oracle. Its `macroDependencies` reports the dependency closure, not use — a source file with no macro at all still lists `SwiftMacros` from the stdlib while its compile emits zero load remarks. Rejecting on non-empty would reject every build.
- FINDING: `-load-pass-plugin=<path>` is a real joined flag in this toolchain that loads an LLVM pass plugin. A verifier total only over path-shaped tokens accepts it silently, because the token itself is not path-shaped. Same hole for `-Xllvm`/`-Xcc`, which hand their value to a second option parser.
- SCOPE: `.temp/TASK-260728-1yhuqi/` — `curator-spec-worktree/{decisions/0011-swift-driver-pair.md,docs/swift-build-drivers.md}` and `probe-module/` (new `planclosed.go`, `planexec.go`, `structural_cycle3.go` and their tests). Nothing staged, committed or published.
- STATUS: ready for review, cycle 4. Probe green: 23 cases, 32 closure checks, 14/14 expected-red controls, 56 structural checks, exit 0.
