### 0644 — The suite is not slow; one GOROOT walk is, and it runs five times per install

- ROOT CAUSE: `cmd/curator` aggregate duration is `internal/godriver.fingerprintToolchain`, not test setup. CPU profile of one compiled-install test: 8.24s of 13.18s samples (**62.5%**) inside it — `os.(*Root).Open` 3.61s, `fs.WalkDir` 3.26s (`os.(*Root).Lstat` 2.83s), and only 1.34s in `copyWithContext`. **84% of the cost is path resolution, not reading or hashing.**
- FINDING: it re-resolves the *full* root-relative path one `O_NOFOLLOW` component at a time, three separate times per entry — in `fs.WalkDir(root.FS(), …)`, again in `root.Lstat(name)`, again in `root.Open(record.filesystem)` (`fingerprint.go:34-107,126`). At GOROOT's average depth that is ~15 `openat` per file across 14502 files.
- FINDING: it runs once per read-only plan (`godriver.Probe`, `install/builddeps.go:154`, documented "does not memoize"), once per staging session (`Establish`, `builddeps.go:170`), and three more times per staged build (`VerifyToolchain` at `godriver/build.go:148,219,253`, plus `install/plan.go:252`). A `status` pays one full GOROOT hash; an install pays five.
- EVIDENCE: cost atoms measured from `go test -count=1 -timeout 30m -json ./cmd/curator` (exit 0, **533.463s**, 62 tests, 15 of them ≥1s accounting for 531.1s): one compiled-scope `status` = ~3.1s, one real compiled install = ~12.5s. The same scope *without* a compiled command does a full install plus four status calls in **1.08s**, so all non-build CLI machinery is ~0.2–0.3s per operation.
- FINDING: a directory-scoped walk — descend through per-directory `*os.Root` handles, reuse the handle of the directory both phases are inside, keep the identical record set, sort, and byte stream — computes the **identical SHA-256** and is **2.2x faster** (1.64s → 0.72s per GOROOT, three rounds). Prototype and A/B harness: `.temp/TASK-260729-2kaopg/probe/main.go`. Resolving each component once also narrows the TOCTOU window between walk, `Lstat`, and `Open` rather than widening it.
- DECISION: not applied. `internal/godriver` is the `go-v1` trust boundary and sits outside `TASK-260729-2kaopg`'s declared surface (`cmd/curator` global status and its CLI tests); the change also moves `internal/install` (289s) and `internal/install/atomicity` (423s) and needs `go-v1` conformance re-attestation, so it belongs in its own reviewed task.
- FINDING: the prescribed test-side levers cannot reach ≤480s. Immutable setup is already shared (the two largest tests build one fixture and one installation for 14 and 6 subtests); the remaining per-subtest cost is three CLI invocations that *are* the assertions. Nothing is memoizable — the fingerprint is deliberately not, and the operation-private `GOCACHE` (`install/private.go`) is a hermeticity property. Packaging cannot split: the tests live in `package main`, call `run()` in-process, and assert on unexported identifiers, so a split means moving `run()` out and rewriting ~1500 lines against string literals, losing compile-time coupling to the constants.
- NOTE: supersedes the 0607 recommendation. The right fix is neither an explicit CI timeout nor a package split — it is the 30-line walk change, projected at ~35% off `cmd/curator` (533s → ~350s).
- BLOCKED: `TASK-260729-2kaopg` awaits authorization for option 1 (separate godriver-owned task), option 2 (this task makes the change under its own review), or option 3 (accept the duration and set an explicit package timeout). Product surface unchanged and green throughout: `gofmt`, `git diff --check`, `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...` all exit 0; focused `^TestGlobalStatus` exit 0 in 147.322s with `cmdGlobalStatus` 96.9%, `globalStatusPlan` 100%, `statusReport` 86.2%.

### 0609 — Compiled repair review cycle 2: cache replacement still sits outside rollback

- REVIEW: `TASK-260720-1nlmvv` changes requested. Focused CLI/install tests, vet, formatting, and diff checks pass, but the explicit cycle-2 atomic-repair and diagnostic contracts remain open.
- ROOT CAUSE: `runCommit` publishes build winners before target staging or journal preparation (`internal/install/commit.go:537-585`). `buildcache.Publish` quarantines a corrupt/untrusted live entry and selects the replacement immediately (`internal/buildcache/publish.go:148-174`), but neither the transaction journal nor rollback snapshots own the cache. A later target-stage, journal-prepare, or target-commit failure restores the install while leaving the referenced cache entry replaced and the old entry quarantined. The corrupt-repair E2E tests cover a pre-publication toolchain refusal, not injected failures after publication.
- FINDING: build-related commit/publication errors still enter `Result.Errors` through raw `failf` (`internal/install/install.go:565-571`, `internal/install/global.go:328-334`) rather than `RedactDiagnostic`; publication errors can contain operation-private artifact/cache paths and are not bounded.
- FINDING: a toolchain-refusal inventory omits the known `go-v1` driver and already-validated build-source digest (`internal/install/plan.go:352-374,407-426`), so missing-Go status rows do not meet the per-command diagnostic field contract.
- FINDING: `build-state-changed` fingerprints install markers only. Cache receipt/artifact state is inspected once during the unlocked dry-run plan and is not revalidated before `status --check`, so a concurrent same-marker cache mutation can still produce a stale currentness verdict.
- REQUIRED: make corrupt/untrusted replacement rollback-owned through every post-publication fault; redact and bound every build-related `Result.Errors` path; preserve known driver/source identity in toolchain inventory rows; bracket or revalidate cache evidence for the final currentness verdict; add production-path fault/race regressions.
- EVIDENCE: reviewer `go test` focused install group exit 0 (5.174s), focused real CLI group exit 0 (354.202s), full diagnostic-focused install group exit 0 (2.632s), focused `go vet` exit 0, task-file `gofmt -l` empty, and `git diff --check 17804ce` exit 0.

### 0607 — The repository outgrew Go's default per-package timeout; late `cmd/curator` tests are innocent victims

- ANOMALY: `TASK-260729-2kaopg` ran `go test -count=1 ./...` twice as standalone foreground gates. Both exited 1 at Go's fixed 10-minute timeout in `cmd/curator`, but the active late test differed: `TestInstallRepairsUntrustedCompiledStateAndPreservesTheOldInstall` had run 12s in the first run, and `TestDryRunNeverClaimsACompletedCompilerCheck` had run 6s in the clean-load replay. Every other package, including `internal/install` and `internal/install/atomicity`, passed in both runs.
- EVIDENCE: the two alleged timeout victims passed in isolation with exit 0 in 40.0s and 9.3s. The focused global-status group passed in 144.5s, and `internal/install/atomicity` passed in 371.7s. The first full run overlapped an unrelated predecessor-worktree race gate; the second ran with no competing `go test` process and timed out later in the package, proving the remaining defect is aggregate package duration rather than that load event.
- DISK: the prior cacheable-run failure did not recur under `-count=1`. Free space moved from 14,785,080 KiB to 14,575,380 KiB across the clean-load full run, nowhere near exhaustion.
- RECOMMENDATION: give repository CI an explicit package timeout (30 minutes is sufficient for the current fault-injection suite), or split `cmd/curator` integration tests into independently timed packages. Never report the default-timeout command as green; its real exit is 1 until the suite is split or the gate timeout is raised.

### 0602 — A Cargo profile key starts a fourth process, and the dangerous value is the *unrecognized* one

- ROOT CAUSE: `TASK-260728-12pnm1` decision 0009 rev 3 admitted `[profile]` tables wholesale, arguing that a release toolchain gates per-profile `rustflags` behind `-Z`. Correct and irrelevant — the process a profile starts arrives without a flag. Measured on macOS 26.5 arm64 / Rust 1.91.0 under the contract's exact pinned pipeline: `[profile.release] debug = 2` + `split-debuginfo = "packed"` resolves `dsymutil` through `PATH`; the shim ran, returned 127, and `cargo build` still exited **0**. Falsified the three-executable closure claim.
- FINDING: the obvious fix is wrong. With `split-debuginfo = "wat"`, `""` or `"PACKED"`, cargo forwards **no** `-Csplit-debuginfo` at all (captured via a logging `RUSTC` wrapper), and rustc's own `aarch64-apple-darwin` default is `packed` — so `dsymutil` runs anyway, silently, exit 0. A deny-list naming `"packed"` is bypassed by any garbage string. Corollary: rev 3 was safe only because cargo happens to emit `unpacked` when the key is absent, i.e. the property rested on a toolchain default the contract does not control.
- FINDING: cargo accepts an unknown profile key (`totally-unknown-key = 42`) with no diagnostic, so a key deny-list cannot stay closed against a future stabilization. And `cargo metadata` reports **no** profile information, so no graph row G0–G11 can ever see this surface.
- FIX: two layers. Stage P gains a closed **positive allowlist** (3 table shapes, 11 keys with per-key value sets, `split-debuginfo` rejected outright at every value) decided over every manifest in the build root before any cargo process; `CARGO_ENCODED_RUSTFLAGS` gains a platform-resolved `-Csplit-debuginfo=<pin>`, `off` on macOS. Reference §4.7, §6, §7.5, §13.1 step 4; decision §2, §8, §9. Diagnostic reused (`build_rust_manifest_key_forbidden`).
- FINDING: over-rejection bounded by corpus rather than by hope — of 506 published crate manifests in the host registry cache, 37 carry a profile table, they use 7 keys between them, **0** use `split-debuginfo`, all 37 pass. A profile table in a path or vendored dependency was measured inert and is rejected anyway (stated over-rejection, same kind as the `[patch]` row).
- FINDING: the vendor checksum is not a pre-compile gate. Editing a vendored `Cargo.toml` without repairing `.cargo-checksum.json` leaves `cargo metadata` at exit 0 and only fails at `cargo build`.
- ROOT CAUSE: G11 compared against "the Stage P admitted set", which the contract never constructed — an ordinary auto-discovered `src/main.rs` had no verdict, forcing an implementation to either reject positive builds or copy Cargo's target discovery into the security boundary.
- FIX: the set is now defined by the walk Stage P already performs — every path the link-free walk enumerated under the build root, plus the leaves §4.3 admitted and permitted to be absent. Sufficiency measured over 14 discovery shapes: every reported path is either an existing file under the root or a declared path; a name-only target with no file makes the graph exit **101** rather than report a phantom `src_path`, so there is no third case. Reference §4.8.
- DECISION: the pin is recorded in the policy object (`debuginfo_packaging`) and enters cache identity, because `CARGO_ENCODED_RUSTFLAGS` participates in cargo's fingerprint — measured byte-identical under one pin, different across pins. Windows/Linux pin values are qualification obligations, not `off` by assumption: MSVC writes a PDB through the pinned linker, Linux `packed` runs an external `dwp`.
- SCOPE: 2 Markdown docs to rev 4 and the standalone Go probe (`profilegate.go`, `admitset.go`, `profilepipeline.go`, `controls4.go`, `vectors4.go`, `rework4_test.go`) in `.temp/TASK-260728-12pnm1/rework4`. 0 product files.
- STATUS: cycle-4 rework handed to review. Probe green: 19/19 cases, 24 closure checks 0 verdicts, **15/15** controls failing as required (C13/C14/C15 new), 124 host-independent vectors 0 divergences, 16 structural checks 0 divergences. Curator build/vet/test/gofmt 0. `make check` 2 and `validate.py` 1 expected-red, 0 occurrences of this task ID; clean-tree validate baseline 0; scoped link check 0 of 4.

### 0600 — A qualification gate split across host and corpus will silently self-contradict unless the split is written into the rule

- ROOT CAUSE: `TASK-260728-168smo` decision 0010 rev 3 stated "a tuple enters `platforms` only when a run on that exact tuple produces all of A1–A9", then recorded A1–A7 passed and assigned A8/A9 to a future task — and still called the tuple qualified, filled `platforms`/`compatibility` as shipped values, and declared the platform retirement branch closed. The rule and its own result table disagreed inside one section.
- FINDING: the defect is structural, not editorial. A8 (allow-list classifier corpus) and A9 (conformance-family result) execute no compiler and need no host, so "a run on that tuple produces all of A1–A9" is unsatisfiable as written. A gate whose requirements have two different discharge mechanisms must name them: A1–A7 host requirements, A8–A9 corpus requirements, and a defined intermediate state.
- FIX: introduced **A1–A7 host-qualified candidate** as a defined term used consistently; `platforms` and `compatibility` became candidate sets under one binding rule (previously only `compatibility` had one) that admits both in the change where `TASK-260728-251p01` lands the conformance corpus; both retirement branches re-armed. `decisions/0010-kotlin-native-driver-pair.md` §10/§11/§12, `docs/kotlin-native-build-drivers.md` §1.4/§11.1/§11.3/§11.5.
- FINDING: a duplicated closed set is a fail-open channel. The measured Windows base-installation DLL allow-list was `{KERNEL32.dll, msvcrt.dll}` in the normative table and `{KERNEL32.dll, msvcrt.dll, ADVAPI32.dll, USER32.dll}` in the obligation table. Both extra entries are reachable through the distribution's other platform klibs, so picking the wider row would publish artifacts with dependencies no A6 run ever produced. Fixed by naming one normative source and making the two extra DLLs *required rejection* vector cases, so re-admitting them silently now fails the corpus.
- DECISION: took the reviewer's deferral option over running A8/A9 here — those vectors belong to `TASK-260728-251p01`, and authoring them from this task would move the same claim without adding evidence.
- SCOPE: two Markdown docs to rev 4 in `.temp/TASK-260728-168smo/curator-spec-worktree`. 0 Go/schema/vector/release files. No measurement re-run; no host touched.
- STATUS: cycle-4 rework handed to review. Baseline `validate.py` 0; worktree 1 (expected-red, sibling copied-in doc, 0 from this task); scoped link sweep 0; spec unittest 0; spec `go test ./tools/...` 0; curator build/vet/gofmt/test 0; `make check` 2 (expected-red, 754 gofmt files, all under other tasks' `.temp/`).

### 0635 — Swift cycle-3 review: toolchain macro admission contradicts the accepted additional-language boundary

- REVIEW: `TASK-260728-1yhuqi` changes requested and routed to `analysis`. Cycle-2 repairs replay cleanly: 23/23 cases, 32 closure checks with no verdict, 12/12 expected-red controls, 44/44 structural checks, and executed native P2 admission all green.
- ROOT CAUSE: accepted Decision 0008 requires every additional driver to reject package-selected procedural and compiler macros before compile, but Decision 0011 explicitly admits source-selected toolchain macros. The probe's `S7` proves `@Observable` loads `libObservationMacros.dylib` in-process and compiles successfully; fingerprinting the implementation constrains provenance but does not satisfy the rejection requirement.
- ROOT CAUSE: the `swiftc -###` verifier accepts unknown flag-shaped tokens after checking its enumerated buckets. An unrecognized joined or opaque path carrier can therefore pass even though the prose calls the grammar fail-closed; this is unsafe across the admitted Swift `(6,3)` compatibility family.
- REQUIRED: reject actual macro/plugin loads at graph time, close the plan to a measured per-job flag grammar that rejects every unknown token/channel, add expected-red controls for both retired behaviors, and rerun review. If fingerprinted toolchain macros are intentionally desired, Decision 0008 must be explicitly reopened and amended rather than silently overridden.
- EVIDENCE: board outcome `TASK-260728-1yhuqi_review-verdict-cycle-3.md`; reviewer scratch `.temp/TASK-260728-1yhuqi/review-cycle-3.LWgiWg/`.

### 0554 — A corrupt protected build-cache entry was unrecoverable; repair is now the install path

- ROOT CAUSE: `BuildOutcome.blocking()` counted `corrupt` as blocking, so `planBuilds` returned before `stageBuilds`. Curator has no repair command, `buildcache.Publish` already had quarantine-and-replace, and the path never reached it — the only way out of a corrupt entry was deleting it by hand. `internal/install/plan.go:50-60`. `TASK-260720-1nlmvv` cycle 2.
- FIX: `buildable()` is now every state a rebuild resolves (cold miss, untrusted, corrupt); `blocking()` is only what a rebuild cannot (`unsupported`, `toolchain-unavailable`). Ordering is unchanged — the rebuild still runs after all 16 gates, stages privately, and publishes under the manager-home lock. Part of the observable rc.4 contract: the planner vocabulary changed meaning.
- FINDING: the install marker records no prior build input, so a logical cache-key mismatch cannot be attributed. The key is one opaque digest over schema, driver, source, build root, command, source dir, target+tuning, toolchain identity, and policy. `build-target-mismatch` was replaced by `build-input-drift` plus a `cause` subcode that is `build-root` or `target` only where the marker's own recorded roots/artifact path prove it, and `unattributed` otherwise. A truthful "the toolchain moved" verdict needs a marker-schema change, not a status change. `cmd/curator/builds.go:403-433`.
- FINDING: a strict validator that returns only "refused" destroys diagnostics. `marker.Read` collapsed unknown-schema and non-`go-v1`-driver markers into `invalid-marker`. `markerRefusal` is a presentation-only second look at the same bytes — it never accepts what the reader rejected, it only picks the stable code. `cmd/curator/builds.go:452`.
- DECISION: reporting a verdict is not a failure. Plain `curator status` exits zero when the read-only plan refused but still described every active compiled command (`BuildsComplete`); the raw detail becomes a `warning:`. A refusal that leaves a command undescribed keeps the legacy non-zero exit. `--check` is the only surface that turns a non-current verdict non-zero. `cmd/curator/main.go:636-653`.
- FINDING: token-based redaction leaks. Splitting on whitespace missed `source=/private/...`, `file:///private/...`, and `error=C:\Users\name\...`. Replaced with a single left-to-right scan that starts a path after any opener (`= : " ' \` ( [ { < > , ; |`) or space, in Unix, URI, Windows-drive, and UNC form. `Result.failBuild` now routes every build-phase failure through it, so `Result.Errors` cannot bypass what the plan line redacts. `internal/install/diagnostics.go`.
- ANOMALY: this suite is disk-hungry enough to look like a product defect. A `-race` sweep with ~9 GiB free filled the disk and three `internal/install` tests failed with `no space left on device` surfaced through `Result.Errors` as an ordinary installation failure — it reads exactly like a transaction-journal bug. The Go build cache alone reached 8.9 GiB. CI needs a free-space floor. Relevant to `TASK-260720-1pvfj5`.
- ANOMALY: PowerShell 5.1 mangles `-test.run` when invoking a native binary — the argument arrives as `-test` and the test binary exits 2 with a usage dump that reads like a product failure. Drive Go test binaries on host `win` through `cmd.exe /c` or `--%`.
- NOTE: `internal/install` and `internal/install/atomicity` exceed the default `go test` timeout under load (427s / 577s without `-race`; 764s for `internal/install` with it). An unpinned timeout makes the failure read as a hang.
- SCOPE: new `cmd/curator/builds.go` + `internal/install/diagnostics.go`; `cmd/curator/main.go`, `internal/install/{plan,install,global}.go`, `README.md`. Follow-ups filed: `TASK-260729-2kaopg` (global-status build awareness, exclusion documented in README), `TASK-260729-3jku56` (compiled install idempotence).
- STATUS: cycle-2 rework handed to review. gofmt/build/vet/lint/full-suite and all three `-race` gates exit 0; `GOOS=windows go vet` is red only in sibling-owned `internal/runtimestore`, identically on the untouched base.

### 0540 — Swift runtimeLibraryPaths names the OS runtime, so "everything inside a fingerprinted root" would reject the qualified host

- FINDING: measured, `swiftc -print-target-info -target arm64-apple-macosx26.0` returns `runtimeLibraryPaths = ["<toolchain-root>/usr/lib/swift/macosx", "/usr/lib/swift"]`. The second entry exists, is a directory, and is outside every fingerprinted root — macOS ships Swift in the OS. The obvious admission rule the cycle-2 reviewer proposed would have rejected the only host this contract is qualified on. `TASK-260728-1yhuqi`.
- DECISION: closed three-class partition instead — **A** in-closure, **B** base-installation inside a registry-declared per-platform prefix set (macOS exactly `/usr/lib/swift`, empty everywhere else so unmeasured platforms fail closed), **C** reject. Non-empty list, every entry resolves to a directory, at least one class A, class B enters identity under role `platform-base-installation` so a runtime moving between OS and closure moves the cache key, and a base prefix may not lie inside a fingerprinted root.
- FINDING: the hatch is narrow and that is measured, not argued — the class-B path appears as **0 tokens** in the verified `swiftc -###` job plan. The linker job's `-L` paths are the class-A entry and the presented SDK's `usr/lib/swift`. Plan verification stays total and admits no base-installation path in any bucket.
- FINDING: on this host P1 and P2 return byte-identical JSON. P2 is run and bound anyway; the equality is a property of a host, not of the contract.
- SCOPE: board outcomes `TASK-260728-1yhuqi_{decision-0011-swift-driver-pair,swift-build-drivers-reference,rework-cycle-2}.md`.

### 0538 — Swift plan grammar: trusted compiler output held stricter than untrusted manifest input

- DECISION: the `swiftc -###` job plan rejects a bare CR anywhere and requires a mandatory terminal LF; `Package.swift` line 1 still has exactly one trailing CR removed. The two rules are deliberately different and the difference is stated. `TASK-260728-1yhuqi`.
- ROOT CAUSE: the manifest is untrusted input authored on an unknown host, where CRLF is an ordinary fact and rejecting a build over it is a false positive. The plan is output the trusted compiler just produced on this host, measured to carry **zero** CR bytes — so a CR there is evidence the read or the producer is not what the contract assumes, and the fail-closed answer is rejection.
- FINDING: measured plan shape for the driver's own vector: 4808 bytes, final byte 0x0A, 3 LF, 0 CR, 0 other control bytes, 0 non-ASCII, longest line 1990 bytes. The mandatory terminal LF is what removes the `LF?` ambiguity — "exactly one trailing empty element after splitting" becomes a consequence rather than a convention.
- NOTE: bytes `0x80-0xFF` are admitted inside a plan line and compared byte-exactly. A POSIX path is a byte string; the plan is not required to be valid UTF-8.

### 0521 — Kotlin/Native cycle-3 review: measured Windows closure conflicts with the normative qualification record

- REVIEW: `TASK-260728-168smo` changes requested and routed to `analysis`. The retained Windows evidence supports A1–A7: ETW reports zero lost events and buffers, only in-bundle `clang++.exe` and `ld.lld.exe` below the compiler JVM, and the PE samples import exactly `KERNEL32.dll` and `msvcrt.dll`.
- ROOT CAUSE: the implementation-ready reference fixes the `windows/amd64` base-installation allow-list to exactly `{KERNEL32.dll, msvcrt.dll}` in section 10.1, then widens K-9 in section 11.6 to include `ADVAPI32.dll` and `USER32.dll` without the required A6 re-qualification.
- ROOT CAUSE: the contract says a tuple enters `platforms` only after A1–A9, while the same artifacts claim `windows/amd64` passed A1–A9 and is qualified even though A8 is assigned to a future corpus and A9 remains conditional on those vectors passing.
- REQUIRED: choose one measured DLL allow-list everywhere; then either attach passing A8/A9 evidence before retaining the qualified claim, or describe Windows as an A1–A7 host-qualified candidate and keep admission/retirement conditional. Evidence: board outcome `TASK-260728-168smo_review-verdict-cycle-3.md`.

### 0518 — `curator global status` plans read-only to reuse the one currentness vocabulary

- DECISION: `curator global status` gains `--json` and `--check` with exactly the `curator status` meaning, and runs the same read-only plan `curator global install --dry-run` runs. `TASK-260729-2kaopg`.
- ROOT CAUSE: compiled currentness cannot be read off an install marker. The logical cache key is one opaque digest over the whole build input, and only a plan derives the *current* one. A marker-only classification could report at most build-command drift and context exposure — the second, weaker vocabulary the task forbids.
- FINDING: the price is that a command which previously only read markers now resolves the machine-wide closure and passes the read-only audit and registry gates. It runs no compiler and writes no installation target, cache entry, or trust state; `Fetch` stays false under `DryRun`, so it performs no repository fetch either. Documented in `README.md:194-222`.
- DECISION: plain `global status` keeps its historical always-report / always-exit-zero contract. The declared-skill map still comes from `scopeStatusDrift` over install markers, never from the plan, so a scope without compiled commands prints the lines it always printed even when the plan refuses — the refusal is a `warning:` on stderr, not an `error:`. `--check` is the only surface that turns a verdict non-zero. This differs from `curator status`, which exits non-zero on an incompletely-described plan even without `--check`; that asymmetry is the price of preserving the pre-existing global surface.
- FINDING: `--check` fails closed twice: for every code that is not `up-to-date`/`current` (`checkFailed`), and when the plan refused before describing every compiled command (`Status == "failed" && !BuildsComplete`) — such a run cannot even prove whether the scope *has* compiled state.
- SCOPE: `cmd/curator/main.go` (`statusScope`/`projectStatusScope`/`globalStatusScope`, `statusReport`, `installedSkillDir`, `cmdGlobalStatus`, `globalStatusPlan`; `statusStores` folded in and removed), `cmd/curator/global_status_test.go` (new, 7 tests), `README.md`.
- NOTE: the machine-readable document carries `alias`, `skills`, and `builds` only — no `path`. The machine-wide scope has no operator-supplied root and the manager home is never published; two tests assert the home never reaches `--json`.
- STATUS: ready for review. gofmt/vet/build/lint/naming gate and the 7 focused CLI tests all exit 0.

### 0512 — Kotlin/Native windows/amd64 qualifies; kernel tracing replaces iterative denial as the admission method

- MILESTONE: `windows/amd64` passed A1-A7 of decision 0010 on a real host (Windows 10 Pro 10.0.19045.6456, AMD64, reached over `ssh win`). `platforms = {(windows, amd64)}` and `compatibility = {(2,4)}` are now filled from measurement rather than left empty. `TASK-260728-168smo`.
- DECISION: A3 now requires a **kernel-level process trace that records every process start by resolved absolute image path**, and names iterative sandbox denial inadmissible for *admitting* a tuple. ETW `Microsoft-Windows-Kernel-Process` keyword `0x10` via `logman` + `tracerpt` is the Windows instrument; `ImageName` is an NT device path resolved through `QueryDosDevice`. Iterative denial proves required-on-path and sufficiency, never completeness — enough to exclude a tuple, not enough to qualify one.
- FINDING: below the compiler JVM a successful `mingw_x64` compile spawns exactly two children, `clang++.exe` and `ld.lld.exe`, both inside the fingerprinted bundle. No `cmd.exe`, `powershell.exe`, `link.exe`, `cl.exe`, `lib.exe`, `vswhere.exe` or MSYS shell. The overlay materialises dependencies as junctions and the kernel reports the resolved target, so an image reached through the overlay is recorded at its bundle path.
- ROOT CAUSE: the cycle-2 "exactly seven macOS externals" claim was a conflation. `run7` (seeded empty) observed seven; `run8` (seeded with four) succeeded with six. The difference is path-dependent — an allowed `/usr/bin/xcode-select` removes the `xcrun xcodebuild -version` fallback. Union 7, sufficient 6; the archive was right and the prose was wrong.
- FINDING: `icacls /deny "SID:(OI)(CI)(W,D,DC)"` denies `SYNCHRONIZE` as part of the simple right `W` and makes the JDK unrunnable ("Access is denied" on process start). Use explicit advanced rights `(DE,DC,WD,AD,WEA,WA)` instead. Even then a per-user deny does **not** stop a delete when the same account is in `BUILTIN\Administrators`, which grants `FILE_DELETE_CHILD` on the parent — read-only must be ownership, not a deny ACE. Recorded as obligation K-11.
- FINDING: `konan-data/dependencies/.extracted` is a **required** part of a Kotlin/Native offline closure, not a cache artefact. With every dependency directory present but that 103-byte file absent, `airplaneMode=true` fails `Cannot find a dependency locally: lldb-2-windows` with zero download attempts. Reconstructing it from the four dependency names, LF-separated with a trailing LF, reproduced the 2.4 GB bundle tree digest byte for byte.
- FINDING: a compile against a writable bundle data dir mutates it in exactly one place — a 0-byte `dependencies/cache/.lock` — out of 27,867 files. That is the whole reason the operation-private overlay exists, and the overlay's `cache/` must be a fresh directory rather than a link back into the bundle.
- SCOPE: `curator-spec` `decisions/0010-kotlin-native-driver-pair.md` and `docs/kotlin-native-build-drivers.md`, both at revision 3. No Go, schema, vector or release file touched.
- STATUS: ready for review. Host footprint fully reverted: `C:\kn168` removed, deny ACE lifted, ETW session stopped, scoped firewall rule deleted and verified absent, free disk restored to 78.7 GB.

### 0440 — Current official Go is newer than the accepted go-v1 family, and ambient GOROOT can misidentify explicit launchers

- FINDING: the official 2026-07-29 catalog exposes supported Go 1.26.5 and 1.25.12, but accepted Curator `internal/godriver/session.go` allowlists only family `1.25`. The correct bootstrap for current `go-v1` qualification is Go 1.25.12; Go 1.26 must fail closed until the family passes the same conformance vectors and the allowlist is deliberately expanded.
- FINDING: local PATH resolves a 411-byte goenv shell shim before two native launchers. Three real arm64 trees exist — goenv 1.25.5, Homebrew 1.25.5, official `/usr/local/go` package 1.25.1 — but none is current or automatically trusted. The shell exports goenv `GOROOT`; without clearing it, direct Homebrew and `/usr/local/go/bin/go` calls both reported the goenv root. Empty-environment `GOENV=off GOTOOLCHAIN=local` probes restored the correct roots.
- FINDING: read-only `ssh win` inventory identifies Windows 10 Pro x64 but no `go.exe` on process/user/machine PATH, no Go MSI/uninstall registry entry, and no tree at conventional MSI/archive/package-manager roots. Windows qualification needs a human-installed, manager-approved Go 1.25.12 amd64 root; PATH search or automatic installation is not a product remedy.
- FINDING: late read-only `ssh lev` inventory identifies Ubuntu 26.04 LTS x86_64 with no Go on PATH, no conventional Go root or installed `golang*` package, and root-owned `0755` parents under `/usr/local` and `/opt`. Ubuntu offers Go 1.26, which is outside the accepted family; later Linux qualification needs a manually installed/approved official Go 1.25.12 linux-amd64 tree.
- DECISION INPUT: official archive SHA-256 authenticates the downloaded artifact but does not replace `curator-go-toolchain-v1`, which fingerprints the admitted full tree plus normalized `go version`. Catalog guidance must keep URLs/checksums/absolute roots manager-owned, render commands without executing them, set `GOTOOLCHAIN=local`, and reject missing, ambient, package-selected, wrapper, or unknown-family toolchains.
- EVIDENCE: `.research/260729_official-go-bootstrap-guidance.md`; official `go.dev/dl`, install, manage-install, toolchain, and release-policy sources retrieved 2026-07-29. Local and corrected encoded Windows SSH inventories exited 0; the first Windows attempt exited 1 on PowerShell quoting. The first Linux inventory exited 127 after an unescaped read-only `dpkg-query` format tripped `set -u`; its corrected package/root/permission probe exited 0. Neither failed attempt mutated a host or supplied an accepted conclusion.

### 0432 — Swift cycle-2 review: green boundary evidence still left toolchain identity open

- REVIEW: `TASK-260728-1yhuqi` changes requested after the cycle-1 fixes. Independent replay matched the producer exactly: 23/23 cases, 32 closure checks with no verdict, 9/9 expected-red controls, and 30/30 structural checks.
- ROOT CAUSE: the compiler banner has three different admission rules — decision `\(.*\)`, reference `\(.+\)`, and a probe that parses only the numeric token and ignores the remaining suffix. The probe therefore does not enforce the identity grammar it claims to test.
- ROOT CAUSE: native target probe P2 is normative but absent from the executable probe. The contract ignores empty and out-of-root `runtimeLibraryPaths`; `StdlibPresent` is recorded from P1 but does not gate `green`.
- ROOT CAUSE: `curator-swift-toolchain-v1` serializes macOS `usr/bin/ld` and one SDK root, while the portable closure is plan/P3-derived and Windows permits one or more SDK roots. Windows can stay unqualified, but its canonical identity cannot be deferred to implementers.

### 0431 — Compiled-language cycle is a story-projection artifact, not a task-DAG defect

- ROOT CAUSE: the Rust, Swift, and Kotlin stories each mix early language-design tasks with later Curator/CocoaSkills implementation and qualification; the toolchain story similarly mixes wire specification, implementation, and deferred Linux qualification. Correct task ordering collapses to `protocol → language → toolchain → protocol` at story level.

### 0430 — Swift driver: a metadata rule that reads one line cannot be implemented by a whole-file scan

- ROOT CAUSE: decision 0011 declared the manifest body "never a metadata input" while the classifier called `scanForHeader` over every later line to tell `rejected-absent-header` from `rejected-non-canonical-header`. A line-2 header therefore changed the manager verdict through bytes the normative contract said were not input.
- FINDING: measured `swift package tools-version` = `9.9.0`, exit 0, for a manifest whose only specification sits **inside a multi-line string literal** on line 3. Upstream's scan reaches string constants, so a declared whole-file scan would let arbitrary manifest body bytes set the version the manager compares.
- DECISION: classify from line 1 only; drop the scan. `rejected-absent-header` now means "line 1 carries none", not "the file carries none". A specification below line 1 is that class and stays in the security partition `F`, which grows from 7 to 8 members in two shapes (six line-1 reinterpretations, two below-line-1). `scanForHeader` survives renamed `upstreamScanForHeader`, used only to predict the oracle's output.
- FINDING: two nearby cases bound the narrowing and both agree with upstream — a canonical line 1 followed by `// swift-tools-version:99.0` on line 2 yields `6.0.0` (line 1 decides for both), and a specification inside a *single*-line string literal is found by neither. Neither is in `F`.
- SCOPE: `curator-spec` worktree `.temp/TASK-260728-1yhuqi/curator-spec-worktree` (uncommitted on `57c1f56`) — `decisions/0011-swift-driver-pair.md` §6/§10, `docs/swift-build-drivers.md` §7.1–7.3; probe `classifier.go`, `run.go`, `cases.go`.

### 0429 — `swiftc -###` output is POSIX-quoted argv, and a newline in a source name breaks the plan grammar

- FINDING: the plan is written to **stdout**, one job per line, stderr empty. Quoting is POSIX single-quote: a value containing a space or `#` is wrapped in `'…'`, an embedded quote renders as `'\''`, a backslash inside a quoted run is literal.
- FINDING: a source named `new\nline.swift` splits its job across physical lines — the graph command still exits 0 while the plan carries 7 physical lines for 3 jobs. A line-oriented verifier cannot recover from this after the fact.
- DECISION: reject ASCII control bytes in compiled source relative paths at Stage A, before the graph phase, and admit everything else. Quotes, spaces, `#` and a leading `@` were each measured to round-trip through the plan grammar, so restricting them would be unearned.
- FINDING: `swiftc` honours `@file` response files (measured: `-swift-version 6` from a response file reaches the plan), but a source named `@resp.swift` reaches the compiler as an absolute path and compiles. The channel is live and unreachable, which is why the matrix now carries an explicit row rather than silence.
- SCOPE: probe `plan.go`, `plan_test.go`; reference §3.1, §4.1.1.

### 0428 — The macro plugin tree derives three levels up from `-sdk`, so presentation depth is load-bearing

- FINDING: the external plugin path is derived from the `-sdk` argument by walking **three** ancestor levels up and then into `Developer/usr` — not `<sdk>/../../usr` as cycle 1 recorded. With the SDK at `<base>/sdkroot/SDKs/MacOSX.sdk` the derived tree lands at `<base>/Developer`, a *sibling* of the presentation directory rather than inside it.
- FIX: the presentation moved to `<staging>/sdk/present/SDKs/<name>`, so three levels up is `<staging>/sdk/present` and every derived tree stays inside `<staging>/sdk`, which the manager creates and keeps empty apart from the chain. Five explicit guarantees replace the previous one.
- FINDING: corrected two cycle-1 counts. Under the declared SDK path **2 distinct** plugin paths exist outside every fingerprinted root (the plugin directory and the server), not three — the third derived path (`usr/local/…`) does not exist. Under the presentation the plan carries 14 components but only **6 distinct**: 2 existing and both inside the toolchain root, 4 absent. The earlier "6 inside / 8 absent" counted per-job repeats.
- NOTE: the security property is unchanged in both directions — 0 existing outside a fingerprinted root under the presentation, a live external plugin tree under the declared path.
- SCOPE: decision §4, reference §2.2; probe `structural.go` S5/S26, control C6.

### 0427 — Plan verification is only a boundary if it is total over the plan's path surface

- ROOT CAUSE: the cycle-1 verifier silently dropped every line whose first token was not absolute, ignored a plugin flag with no value, returned tokens for an unmatched quote, and tested containment with a lexical prefix. Every "ignore" was a path entering the compile unchecked; a symlink out of a root passed.
- FIX: `plan.go` is a rejection engine. Closed token grammar with hard errors for an unterminated quoted run, a dangling backslash and any control byte; every **path-shaped** token must be claimed by exactly one of five buckets (executable, plugin, search, source, output) and boundary-checked; an unclaimed one rejects. `-new-driver-path` joined the executable bucket after the measurement showed the frontend names `swift-driver` by absolute path.
- FIX: containment symlink-resolves both sides and compares byte-exactly on components. The root itself counts as inside (`--sysroot` names it directly); a case variant on a case-insensitive volume fails closed; a dangling symlink — exists, resolves nowhere — rejects.
- FIX: every verified path is bound with its resolved path and file identity, every absent plugin path recorded as absent, and `Reverify` re-checks both immediately before the compile child starts. The contract states plainly that this narrows the window and that the ownership requirement on the declaration channels is what closes it.
- FINDING: measured 20 malformed plans covering every failure family are rejected while the plan the toolchain actually emits verifies with 0 rejections (buckets `executable=5 plugin=14 search=5 source=4 output=5`). The retired lenient scan admits **16 of the 20**. A grammar that refused everything would prove nothing.
- DECISION: totality is claimed over *path-shaped* tokens and the limit is stated rather than hidden — a future toolchain introducing a path channel in an unrecognised shape is not covered, but every unclaimed path-shaped token rejects, so the failure direction is closed.
- SCOPE: probe `plan.go`, `plan_test.go`, `structural.go` S17–S22, controls C7/C8; reference §4.1.1–4.1.5.

### 0426 — A command key and a Swift module name are different grammars, and the obvious mapping is not injective

- ROOT CAUSE: the contract said only that the module name is "manager-derived through `^[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]{0,63}$`" and gave no mapping. Protocol keys allow dots, hyphens, leading digits and unbounded length; replacing every non-alphanumeric with `_` merges `my-tool`, `my.tool` and `my_tool` into one module identity the protocol keeps distinct.
- FIX: `curator-swift-module-v1`. Prefix-free escape into `[A-Za-z0-9]` (`z`→`zz`, `.`→`zd`, `-`→`zh`, `_`→`zu`), then `Sk_`+escaped when the escape fits 61 bytes, else `Tk_`+40 hex of a domain-separated SHA-256 of the **whole** key+`_`+20 readable bytes. Injective on the short branch by construction — `DecodeModuleName` inverts it — and branch-separated so short and long results cannot collide.
- FINDING: a bare escape would map the key `wift` onto `Swift`, the standard-library module. The `Sk_`/`Tk_` tags prevent it and force an uppercase first letter, so no derived name can be a Swift keyword. Measured: 0 of 341 module and framework names inventoried from the toolchain root and platform SDK carry either prefix.
- DECISION: bind the consuming **command key** into the canonical build input alongside `module_name`, so cache identity does not depend on the derivation being injective at all. Receipt negatives grew 10→12.
- SCOPE: probe `modulename.go`, `modulename_test.go`, `structural.go` S23/S24, control C9; decision §6/§12/§16, reference §3.2/§8/§13.

### 0425 — `swift` is in the toolchain root and is not in the driver's closure

- ROOT CAUSE: the decision's Windows contract listed four closure members while reference §2.1 and acceptance test 12.1 required five by additionally demanding `swift`. `swift` is the SwiftPM launcher, which the same contract forbids from every stage.
- DECISION: the required set is **four** on macOS — `swiftc`, `swift-frontend`, `clang`, `ld`. `swift` is probe-only: forbidden from the pipeline, never invoked by the driver, still covered by the root fingerprint, used solely as the conformance probe's upstream oracle.
- DECISION: the rule is structural rather than a list — every executable the verified plan names, plus the linker `clang -print-prog-name` resolves, must lie inside the root. That is what lets Linux add a measured fifth member (`swift-autolink-extract`) and lets the Windows contract decline to assert a count for a plan nobody has measured.
- NOTE: "four executables, three of them one file" was also wrong once `swift` left the set — `swiftc` alone symlinks to `swift-frontend`, so it is two.
- SCOPE: decision §3/§13, reference §2.1/§12.1/§12.2/§12.3; probe S2b.


### 0428 — A build script that no manifest key declares, and a byte limit that decides admission from line endings

- FINDING: `cargo metadata`/`cargo build` 1.91.0 auto-discover a build script from a **regular file named `build.rs` in the manifest's own directory** when `package.build` is absent. Measured end to end: `custom-build` target reported, script compiled and **executed** (marker file written). A forbidden-key gate has nothing to reject — Stage P's `package.build` row never fires — so before this fix only graph row G2 saw it, i.e. after Cargo had already read and resolved the tree. That invalidated the reference's collective claim that every row G2 through G8 has a snapshot-byte counterpart.
- FINDING: discovery scope measured exhaustively and is narrower than folklore suggests — a differently named file, a nested `build.rs`, and a *directory* named `build.rs` are all ignored; `build = false` suppresses discovery; edition 2024 behaves as edition 2021.
- DECISION: the pre-Cargo rule is one `lstat` per manifest directory **on the name alone**, ignoring `package.build` and entry type. Stating it on Cargo's actual discovery semantics would bind the contract to behavior that can change between releases. It over-rejects exactly two measured-inert shapes (`build = false` beside a `build.rs`; a directory named `build.rs`), neither produced by `cargo vendor` — whose manifests carry `build = false` precisely when no script file was packaged.
- ROOT CAUSE: `curator-rust-toolchain-v1` applied its 4096-byte limit to the **raw** `rustc -vV` stream and folded CRLF afterwards, so the limit decided admission from line endings. Measured: an LF stream of 4093 `x`,LF,`A`,LF is 4096 raw bytes and was admitted; its CRLF form is 4098 raw bytes and was rejected, though folding it yields the first byte for byte. Over 410 lines the same 4096-byte folded stream expands to 4506 raw. A Windows-hosted and a Unix-hosted manager could disagree about whether one distribution is admissible — the exact opposite of what the fold exists for.
- FIX: raw capture bound 8192 → UTF-8/NUL → fold → semantic bound 4096 on the *folded* stream. Non-lossy by construction (`len(folded) >= ceil(len(raw)/2)`, so raw > 8192 always folds > 4096) and 8192 is the smallest such bound: 4096 CRLF pairs is exactly 8192 raw, folds to exactly 4096, and was measured to reach the terminal-LF rule rather than the limit. All four published digests unchanged.
- ANOMALY: Stage P required "the canonical form of the joined path must remain under R" for a leaf the same paragraph permits to be **absent**. No such form exists — `realpath(3)` returns NULL/ENOENT, `filepath.EvalSymlinks` fails. Worse than impossible: canonicalizing an absent leaf below a symlink **reports a path outside the package directory** before failing, so canonicalization was never a containment check.
- DECISION: containment moves to the lexical grammar plus a non-following component walk rejecting any symlink/reparse at any depth including the leaf, stopping at the first absent or non-directory component. Canonicalization applies only to the deepest existing ancestor — always defined, worst case the build root — as a cross-check for case folding and Windows short names.
- NOTE: both new controls (C11 superseded byte-order, C12 key-only build-script gate) also re-check the *replacement* on the same inputs and report themselves as not-failing on regression. A control that only demonstrates the old defect stays green after the new rule breaks.
- SCOPE: `TASK-260728-12pnm1` artifacts only — decision 0009 rev 3, reference rev 3, probe rev 3 (`controls3.go`, `rework3_test.go`). Conformance vectors 134 → 150, controls 10 → 12, probe host-independent vectors 68 → 81, structural checks 13 → 14. No new diagnostic, schema member or pin.
- STATUS: handed to review cycle 3. Probe green on macOS 26.5 arm64 / Rust 1.91.0: 19/19 cases, 24 closure checks 0 verdicts, 12/12 controls failing as required, 81 vectors 0 divergences, 14 structural 0 divergences. `platforms` still holds only `(macos, arm64)`; Windows and Linux remain qualification obligations. Nothing staged, committed, published or pinned.

### 0411 — Repeated compiled installs now prove currentness instead of rewriting context

- ROOT CAUSE: `internal/install.stageNode` called `marker.Current` without `marker.BuildCurrentness`. Schema-2 markers carrying build state therefore failed closed on every run even when planning independently reported an exact `cache-hit`; the context target was rewritten and reported `installed`.
- FIX: project and global target staging now pass independently derived raw-snapshot validation, protected-cache inspection, cloned planned inputs, and complete static context/runtime file sets. `stageNode` forwards that single proof into `marker.Current`; nodes without compiled commands retain the legacy call shape.
- DECISION: only a node whose entire read-only build plan was already `cache-hit` is eligible for currentness proof. Any miss, untrusted cache rebuild, corrupt/unsupported state, or incomplete proof passes no build state and retains `marker.Current`'s fail-closed non-current behavior, even if commit later publishes equivalent artifact bytes.
- BOUNDARY: context selection is enumerated without writing operation-private context staging; runtime enumeration includes every file copied from active script runtime roots, including names that the prompt-context whitelist excludes. Build roots remain absent from both sets.
- REGRESSION: the focused test was red before the fix (exit 1, second plan `outcome=cache-hit` but message `installed`) and green afterward. Project and global repeated installs now report `up-to-date`, stage no build, and leave the installed context byte-identical. Source, native-target tuning, toolchain identity, cache provenance, and prompt-context drift each re-stage; raw snapshot removal after trust finalization fails without changing the installed context.
- INTEGRATION: task worktree `.temp/TASK-260729-3jku56/worktree` is an exact composition of accepted `TASK-260720-1ljev5` plus four task-owned files. Concurrent `TASK-260720-1nlmvv` changes only build diagnostics in the overlapping `install.go`/`global.go` files at this checkpoint; the staging hunks do not overlap and `targets.go` is unchanged there.
- EVIDENCE: focused normal and race tests, `go test ./internal/install`, repository `go test ./...`, `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...`, gofmt check, and `git diff --check` exit 0. Repo-wide `golangci-lint` initially exited 1 on two unused test callback names, then exited 0 with zero issues after correction.
- STATUS: `TASK-260729-3jku56` implementation is prepared for developer handoff; no files were staged, committed, published, or copied into the concurrent worktree.

### 0512 — A named digest algorithm is not a digest algorithm: hardened TCB completeness rested on two undefined constructions

- ROOT CAUSE: `hardened-tcb-v1` required "two materially different trusted computing bases MUST NOT produce the same record" and identified each mutable component by `{kind, name, algorithm, content_sha256}`, but `curator-hardened-component-file-v1` and `curator-hardened-component-tree-v1` appeared only as enum values and two half-sentences (`protocol/hardened-execution.md:167,197,198`). Two implementations could hash different projections of one installed tree — following links or not, folding mode in or not — and both claim the same algorithm identity, so equal `content_sha256` did not mean equal component.
- ROOT CAUSE: section 8.5 stated the observed `tcb.backend.version` MUST be at or above a claim's `minimum_version`, and nothing compared them. Both were free `nonEmptyString`. Reviewer probe: observed `0` against declared `999999` gave 0 schema errors and `check_claim_qualification` ACCEPTED. Second probe: a linux claim whose `tcb.host.identity` was `windows` also passed — `host` was bound into the digest but related to no other member.
- FIX: `protocol/hardened-execution.md` §2.3.1 defines both constructions byte for byte in the `curator-build-source-v1` / `curator-go-toolchain-v1` house style; §2.3.2 closes algorithm-to-kind; §2.3.3 narrows the host record; §2.3.4 defines `hardened-backend-version-v1`. Schema relations and `check_tcb_record` / `check_claim_qualification` enforce all four.
- DECISION: the tree construction hashes the entry kind (`D`/`F`/`L`) alongside path and payload. Mode, owner, timestamp, ACL and xattr stay out because they change without changing what the component does; the kind stays in because it changes what the component *is* — which is what makes a symbolic link replaced by a regular file holding the referent's exact bytes a different tree rather than the same one.
- DECISION: `host.kind` narrowed to a constant `operating-system` and `hypervisor` deleted rather than left unenforced. Every backend the revision declares is an OS-kernel mechanism, and §2.2 already forces a hypervisor-supplied backend to mint a new profile and execution-policy identity — so the value could never legally appear, and keeping it only offered a way to detach the observed host from its platform.
- DECISION: version comparison needed a grammar before it could exist. `hardened-backend-version-v1` is `series "-" number(.number){0,3}` with the series closed against the backend; missing components are zero; components compare as integers. Cross-series comparison is *invalid*, not lower or higher. The lexical trap is the point: `cgroup2-6.9` sorts after `cgroup2-6.10` and is below it.
- FINDING: writing mutation probes found two defects in the fix itself. Widening the `hardenedHostIdentityValueV1` enum was undetected because the platform relation still covered the three declared platforms — a backstop nothing proved; `validate_schema_closed_value_sets` now pins every closed value set and requires each relation to branch on exactly the declared members. And the version pattern accepted `"sandbox-2.0\n"`: Python's `re` lets `$` match before a trailing newline, so the schema passed while only the conformance validator caught it. Pattern tail is now `(?![\s\S])`; Go RE2 was already correct.
- FINDING: reproducibility of a published digest is only credible with two implementations. The generator computes 10 component fixtures in Go; the validator recomputes every one in Python from the published bytes, and no trusted-component digest in the suite may come from anywhere else. The invented constants `sha256:3333…/4444…/5555…/8888…` are gone.
- FINDING: coverage granularity was the cycle-3 complaint and is a general trap — `test_every_mutable_bound_field_has_a_rotation` credited any rotation of the `trusted_components` array as coverage for kind, name and algorithm alike. Coverage is now per facet (kind, name, algorithm, content, tree-membership, entry-type, link-substitution, component-set), and a rotation cannot be credited with a facet it does not declare.
- SCOPE: `curator-spec` worktree `.temp/TASK-260728-1jafds/curator-spec-worktree` (uncommitted on `57c1f56`) — `protocol/hardened-execution.md`, `profiles/manager-hardened.md`, `schemas/hardened/v1/{hardened-common,hardened-conformance-claim-v4}.schema.json`, `tools/generate-hardened/`, `tools/validate_hardened.py`, regenerated `conformance/hardened/v1/**`.
- STATUS: `TASK-260728-1jafds` handed to review cycle 4. Python tests 113 → 151, suite files 79 → 92, TCB relations 8 → 12. Schema mutation probe 7/7 distinct detections; validator probe 6/6. `conformance/v1`, `schemas/v1` and `release/1.0.0-rc.5.json` byte-identical to the accepted predecessor, manifest `9ba9b8ec…` still equals the rc.5 pin. No platform qualified, no native validation run, nothing staged or committed.


### 0406 — Kotlin/Native cannot be admitted on macOS: seven host executables outside every fingerprintable root

- FINDING: under `sandbox-exec` exec containment allowing only the JDK, the Kotlin/Native 2.4.10 distribution and its hydrated `KONAN_DATA_DIR`, a hello-world `konanc -produce program -target macos_arm64` fails at `CurrentXcode.bash(Xcode.kt:144)`. Iterating the allow-list to exit 0 yields exactly `/bin/bash`, `/usr/bin/xcode-select`, `/usr/libexec/PlistBuddy`, `/usr/bin/xcrun`, `<Xcode>/…/usr/bin/xcodebuild`, `<Xcode>/…/XcodeDefault.xctoolchain/usr/bin/{ld,dsymutil}`, plus the Xcode SDK as sysroot.
- FINDING: no manager-fixed input removes them. With `ignoreXcodeVersionCheck=true` and `targetToolchain`/`targetSysRoot`/`additionalToolsDir` all overridden to absolute local paths, the compile still spawns `/usr/bin/xcrun` at `AppleConfigurablesImpl.getAbsoluteTargetToolchain(Apple.kt:45)`.
- ROOT CAUSE: `konan/konan.properties` declares the Apple toolchain, sysroot and addon as `remote:internal`, and `AppleConfigurablesImpl$XcodePartsProvider` has exactly two implementations — `InternalServer` (gated on `KONAN_USE_INTERNAL_SERVER`, pointing at `https://repo.labs.intellij.net/kotlin-native`) and `Local` (the host's Xcode). No third source exists. `linux_x64` and `mingw_x64` do not share this: their toolchain and sysroot resolve under `$KONAN_DATA_DIR/dependencies`.
- DECISION: decision 0010 rev 2 records macOS as permanently unsupported for `kotlin-native-v1`/`kotlin-native-repository-v1` at Protocol 1.0, on two independent grounds — the process closure, and an unfingerprinted Xcode SDK that would alias in the cache key. The pair is NOT retired: that would be a fabricated negative about `windows/amd64` and `linux/amd64`, and retirement is one-way because decision 0008 §2 closes the identifier space.
- FINDING: the trusted-root model changed to an operator-curated bundle (`jdk/` + `kotlin-native/` + prehydrated `konan-data/`), which satisfies decision 0007 §3 literally via `jdk/bin/java` and drops the `jdk` companion entirely. Measured: hydrated closure is byte-unchanged by a compile; a read-only data dir fails on `dependencies/cache/.lock`; a symlink overlay over a read-only bundle works; `-Xoverride-konan-properties=airplaneMode=true` fails closed instead of downloading.
- FINDING: two corrections to earlier claims. Response-file expansion is decided by the argv token's first character, not by absoluteness — `@/abs/path/file` does expand. And distribution platform libraries (`import platform.posix.*`, `platform.Foundation.*`) link with no `-library`/`.def` and change the artifact's dynamic dependency set, so decision 0008 §3's base-installation clause now needs a post-link, pre-publish gate.
- SCOPE: `curator-spec` `decisions/0010-kotlin-native-driver-pair.md`, `docs/kotlin-native-build-drivers.md` (uncommitted, task worktree). New board task `TASK-260729-2vfvgi` owns `windows/amd64` qualification.
- STATUS: `TASK-260728-168smo` handed to review; distribution and 688 MB dependency closure deleted after measurement, host disk restored.

### 0401 — CocoaSkills Go parity is mapped, but its rc.4 briefs must be reconciled with accepted rc.5 Curator execution

- FINDING: all 17 pre-existing CocoaSkills Go tasks map cleanly to concrete Curator packages/tests and form a valid two-root dependency graph. After Curator gate `TASK-260720-1pvfj5`, the only valid first tasks are schema model `TASK-260720-z9j4c9` and transaction engine `TASK-260720-z2z795`, in parallel.
- CONTRACT DELTA: the CocoaSkills briefs and `TASK-260720-3ag6pi` still name rc.4, while accepted Curator `TASK-260720-1zntv0` implements the human-authorized rc.5 `manager-worker-v1` amendment from `TASK-260728-zb2s4z`. Schema-6 declaration/build-source/metadata/receipt/marker bytes remain frozen, but execution-policy identity, portable controls and conformance identity are rc.5. A board owner must explicitly retarget the CocoaSkills briefs/dependencies to rc.5 (recommended) or authorize literal rc.4 publication before implementation.
- GATE: Curator start dependency is `1nlmvv` currentness rework → `jrrgw9` conformance plus `2qqq0w` docs → `1pvfj5` cross-platform acceptance. Shared CocoaSkills vectors additionally need an authorized real protocol ref and an accepted verification task for the selected release line.
- PROVENANCE: Curator accepted composite is still an uncommitted `TASK-260720-1ljev5` worktree on `17804cea…`; curator-spec accepted candidates are uncommitted on `57c1f568…`; CocoaSkills local main is clean at `edce8816…`, two commits behind verified `origin/main` `6fc2fd97…`.
- VALIDATION: the task-local full Curator `go test ./...` rerun is red, not green. `internal/install` hit `no space left on device`, and the irrecoverably red still-running command was terminated with exit 143. Prior accepted platform gates remain historical reviewer evidence only.
- EVIDENCE: `.research/260729_curator-go-to-csk-parity-delta.md` (`TASK-260729-1t1z2l`).

### 0400 — A corrupt protected build cache entry used to brick install permanently

- ROOT CAUSE: `BuildOutcome.blocking()` in `internal/install/plan.go` returned true for `BuildCorrupt`, so `planBuilds` failed before `stageBuilds`. Curator has no repair command, so a corrupt entry could not be resolved by any invocation, while `README` claimed it was rebuilt and `buildcache.Publish` already quarantined corrupt winners before publishing.
- DECISION: re-cut `buildable()`/`blocking()` along the only line that matters. Buildable = every state a rebuild resolves (cold miss, untrusted provenance, corrupt). Blocking = only what a rebuild cannot resolve (`unsupported` — no provable boundary to publish into; new `toolchain-unavailable` — nothing can be built). Matches the story decomposition's "corrupt or stale artifact rebuild".
- SCOPE: `internal/install/plan.go`, `internal/install/stage_test.go`, `internal/install/private_test.go` (two cases used `Corrupt` only to force a planning failure; switched to `Unsupported`).
- STATUS: resolved. `TestInstallAndUpgradeRepairCorruptCompiledState` covers {install, upgrade} × {corrupt receipt bytes, corrupt artifact bytes} end to end, including gate-refuses-first with a byte-identical cache.

### 0359 — A cache-key mismatch cannot be attributed to the target, and the old code claimed it could

- FINDING: `buildmeta.Input` binds schema version, driver, build source, build root, command, source directory, target and tuning, toolchain identity, and the fixed build policy into one opaque digest. An install marker records none of that, so the old `build-target-mismatch` code was a guess for every mismatch except the driver and build source, which are compared earlier.
- DECISION: renamed to `build-input-drift` and added a stable `cause` subcode. Only two components leave marker-side evidence: `build_roots` proves `build-root`, and `artifact_path` (whose form derives from the target OS) proves `target`. Everything else is `unattributed`.
- FINDING: build schema version and the fixed policy cannot be varied at all — this manager refuses to derive a key for them — so in production they can only appear as a key recorded by another manager version, i.e. unattributable by construction.
- SCOPE: `cmd/curator/builds.go:attributeInputDrift`, adversarial coverage in `cmd/curator/builds_test.go`.

### 0358 — Whitespace-token redaction leaked embedded and URI paths

- ROOT CAUSE: `install.RedactDiagnostic` split on whitespace and only inspected token starts, so `source=/private/cache/x`, `file:///private/cache/x`, and `error=C:\Users\name\cache` survived intact. Blocking plan errors also interpolated the raw cache reason straight into `Result.Errors` after the redacted plan line.
- FIX: single left-to-right scan recognising a path start after any opener (`= : " ' \` ( [ { < > , ; |`) or whitespace, in Unix, URI, Windows-drive, and UNC form; `Result.failBuild` now routes every build-phase failure through the same bounded redaction. `BuildDiagnostic` still carries the stable code unredacted.
- FINDING: relative declaration state survives for free — a path character that follows an ordinary character never starts a path, so `root=assets/build-tool` and `target=linux/amd64` are untouched.
- STATUS: resolved. Verified natively on Windows 10.0.19045 (`install.test.exe` redaction suite exit 0).

### 0350 — Rust cycle-2 rework is green on the measured host but still has two non-total boundary rules

- REVIEW: `TASK-260728-12pnm1` cycle 2 changes requested and routed to `analysis`. The macOS arm64 host replay is green (19/19 cases, P1/P2 true, 24 closure checks, 10/10 expected-red controls, 68 vectors, 13 structural checks), and repository Go build/vet/test gates pass.
- FINDING: `curator-rust-toolchain-v1` checks the raw 4096-byte limit before CRLF folding. An LF stream of exactly 4096 bytes is admitted while its CRLF-equivalent is 4098 bytes and rejected before folding, despite both having the same normalized payload. N2 covers only the 192-byte sample and N12 is not an LF/CRLF-equivalence boundary.
- FINDING: Stage P permits a non-dependency target path not to exist, then requires its canonical filesystem form in all cases. A non-existent leaf has no canonical form, so the physical algorithm cannot be implemented literally. The contract also overstates that every G2–G8 row has an earlier Stage P counterpart; auto-discovered `build.rs` is only covered by G2 unless an explicit file rule is added.
- REQUIRED: bound the normalized stream (with a separate raw capture bound if needed), add paired LF/CRLF vectors at the accepted and rejected limits, and define a total absent-leaf physical walk over existing ancestors with symlink/reparse vectors.
- EVIDENCE: board artifact `TASK-260728-12pnm1_review-verdict-cycle-2.md`. Published V/C payload and framed-record hashes independently reproduce; producer decision/reference/probe SHA-256 values remained unchanged.

### 0344 — rc.5 can supersede the unpublished rc.4 gate, but only the pre-toolchain snapshot is releasable as rc.5

- FINDING: authoritative `curator-spec` remote refs still stop at `v1.0.0-rc.3` / `origin/main` `57c1f568…`; no rc.4 or rc.5 tag exists. Unwrapped rc.4 `q5oy3o`, rc.5 `2kp3tv`, and later `2jaw7h` release gates each exit 1 because every accepted snapshot remains dirty atop rc.3. Clean main rejects rc.4 and rc.5 as the wrong README version, also exit 1.
- FINDING: internal validation is green (rc.4: 35 schemas/189 vectors/27 tests; releasable rc.5: 42/422/29; later candidate: 48/592/169). The blocker is a real commit/ref, not candidate consistency.
- DECISION: recommend rc.5 supersession, not stale rc.4 publication. Existing compatibility policy permits pre-1.0 correction and explicitly says neither candidate was released/pinned; rc.5 preserves the five schema-6 wire artifacts byte-for-byte while amending the pre-publication execution policy.
- BOUNDARY: the exact rc.5 publication input is accepted `TASK-260728-2kp3tv` (`release` `b32ee9d…`, manifest `9ba9b8e…`, index `2faa2ba…`). `TASK-260728-2jaw7h` keeps those frozen but adds versionless `conformance/next` and schema 8; its release workflow archives those directories, so tagging that tree rc.5 would publish deferred work owned by `TASK-260728-251p01`.
- AUTHORIZATION: a board owner must approve rc.5 substitution for the literal rc.4 verification briefs; a curator-spec maintainer must land the exact `2kp3tv` snapshot and later sign/publish `v1.0.0-rc.5`. No dirty-worktree shim, disposable commit, guessed SHA, tag, release, or pin was created.
- IMPACT: `TASK-260720-3ag6pi` has 2 direct and 47 total downstream task dependents. Critical release order remains candidate consumers/parity → real manager releases → `3pvihp` → spec implementation pins `vs6den` → signed protocol release → `25d05o` → manager released-suite pins → final `22ynoi`.
- EVIDENCE: `.research/260729_protocol-publication-gate-for-parity.md` (`TASK-260729-1kq1rd`).

### 0339 — Cargo reads outside the build root before any verdict can be computed, and an ancestor manifest needs no accomplice

- FINDING: `cargo metadata` **opens, parses and reports** a manifest outside the build root. A build root declaring `outside = { path = "../outside", package = "exfiltrated-outside-name" }` exits 0 and reports that package with its outside `manifest_path`; with the outside manifest replaced by malformed TOML the same command prints `error: unclosed table, expected ]` and `--> ../outside/Cargo.toml:1:9`. A rule that reads Cargo's output prevents compilation, not the read.
- FINDING: an ancestor `Cargo.toml` reaches Cargo with **no byte inside the build root naming it**. `parent/Cargo.toml` with `[workspace] members = ["build_root"]` and `[patch.crates-io] cfg-if = { path = "evil" }` redirected a vendored dependency to an outside path package carrying `build = "build.rs"`, while `workspace_members` stayed length **1**, that member **was** the build-root package, and `resolve.root` matched. Every graph row the contract had passes that shape. `workspace_root` is the only field that exposes it.
- DECISION: added Stage P — a manager-owned closure over snapshot bytes with the manager's own TOML parser, before any cargo process: manifest inventory including vendored manifests, a forbidden-key gate, a closed seven-rule relative-path grammar decided **on the declared string before any filesystem call**, dependency-origin and lock/vendor closure. Graph rows demoted to a consistency cross-check plus `workspace_root == build_root` and an admitted-set agreement row.
- DECISION: ancestor `Cargo.toml` joins ancestor `.cargo` as a manager **staging obligation**, not a package check, failing `build_execution_control_unavailable`.
- NOTE: `package.build` is admitted only when exactly `false` — `cargo vendor` normalization writes `build = false` explicitly, so rejecting the key's presence would reject ordinary vendored crates.
- SCOPE: `curator-spec` `decisions/0009-rust-driver-pair.md` §8, `docs/rust-build-drivers.md` §4.
- STATUS: ready for review (`TASK-260728-12pnm1` rework cycle 2).

### 0338 — Three Rust contract claims were false in a way only measurement showed

- FINDING: the graph vector lacked `--all-features`, so an optional `proc-macro` dependency behind a non-default feature was **absent from `packages[]` entirely** — `['feat','winonly']` without the flag, `['feat','implicit','pm','winonly']` with it. The contract claimed a union over every feature and required a vector that rule could never satisfy. Not a false sentence: a hole.
- FINDING: the flag buys the union over every feature of the **root** package only. Measured: a proc macro optional on a *dependency*, behind that dependency's own feature, is absent from `Cargo.lock` and from the `--all-features` graph unless a root feature names it. The subset property still holds — default features ⊆ all features, activation is additive, no `--filter-platform`, dev/build kinds are reported but not built, and `cargo vendor` (which has no `--all-features` flag) vendors the whole lock.
- FINDING: `curator-rust-toolchain-v1` required the `V` record to carry normalized `rustc -vV` stdout while forbidding every LF but one terminator. Real stdout is 192 bytes over **7** lines, so no implementation could compute the published identity. Replaced with a closed multiline rule (CRLF folded to LF, bare CR rejected, exactly one terminal LF) and a reproducible worked example: payload 191 bytes, `sha256 7d8e0833…`, framed record 208 bytes, `sha256 7fc35c11…`.
- DECISION: the claim that no package-derived byte reaches the manager-written `$CARGO_HOME/config.toml` is **withdrawn** — `build_root` comes from the consuming manifest or the external descriptor. Containment is now a closed representability rule plus a TOML **literal** string (no escape sequences, so the writer is a concatenation) plus a mandatory write-back re-parse. Measured: an unescaped `\` in a basic string fails with `could not load Cargo configuration`, and a naive writer emitted a file really carrying `[net] offline = false` and a `[junk]` table.
- FINDING: operator Cargo state is load-bearing, not hygiene. A build root with **no** `vendor` directory resolves exit 0 with the operator's `HOME`/`CARGO_HOME` visible and fails exit 101 under operation-private ones.
- SCOPE: `curator-spec` `decisions/0009-rust-driver-pair.md` §2/§6/§7, `docs/rust-build-drivers.md` §2.3/§5/§6.1/§7.1.
- STATUS: ready for review (`TASK-260728-12pnm1` rework cycle 2).


### 0334 — Swift review: the measured pipeline is viable, but its classifier and plan verifier are not yet closed

- REVIEW: `TASK-260728-1yhuqi` cycle 1 changes requested. The standalone probe replayed green (20/20 cases, 32 closure checks with no verdict, 6/6 expected-red controls, 18/18 structural checks), and independent runs confirmed `describe`, `show-dependencies`, `show-executables`, `show-traits`, and `dump-package` all execute `Package.swift`; the decision to omit SwiftPM remains supported.
- FINDING: the normative contract says the manager reads **only the first line** of `Package.swift` and never parses the body, but classifier class 1/2 and probe `scanForHeader` inspect later lines to distinguish absent from non-canonical headers. A line-2 header therefore changes the verdict through bytes the contract says are not metadata input.
- FINDING: the `swiftc -###` verifier is not demonstrated fail-closed. The probe silently ignores non-absolute/unknown job lines, unmatched quoting and missing plugin-flag values, and uses lexical prefix containment for planned executables/plugin paths rather than resolved containment. No negative vector covers malformed output, relative executables, symlink escape, or absent-path/permit-time mutation.
- FINDING: valid protocol command keys allow dots, hyphens, leading digits and unbounded Swift-incompatible shapes, while the Swift reference only states the target module-name regex. It defines no total collision-safe mapping or rejection rule, so exact argv, local/external equivalence and cache identity are underspecified.
- REQUIRED: choose one coherent manifest-read rule; define a closed plan grammar with resolved containment and permit-boundary revalidation; add adversarial parser/symlink/TOCTOU controls; define module-name derivation for every valid command key; explicitly close response files, `unsafeFlags`/scripts/configurations and the inconsistent four-vs-five-member Windows closure.
- EVIDENCE: board artifact `TASK-260728-1yhuqi_review-verdict-cycle-1.md`.
- ROUTE: `analysis`.

### 0325 — Swift's SDK argument silently selects an executable outside the toolchain

- FINDING: `swiftc` injects macro plugin search paths into every frontend job unasked, and the **external** ones are derived from the `-sdk` argument as `<sdk>/../../usr/lib/swift/host/plugins` with a server at `<sdk>/../../usr/bin/swift-plugin-server`. Passing the declared Xcode SDK path directly puts **3** existing plugin paths outside every fingerprinted root into the closure, and `#Predicate` loads `FoundationMacros` **through a `swift-plugin-server` process** there — as does the toolchain's own `ObservationMacros`.
- DECISION: the manager never passes the declared SDK path. It presents the SDK through a directory it owns entirely (`<staging>/sdkroot/SDKs/MacOSX.sdk -> <declared root>`). Measured with that presentation: 14 plugin paths, 6 inside the toolchain root, **8 absent**, 0 outside; `#Predicate` fails to compile; `@Observable` still compiles at exit 0 loading `libObservationMacros.dylib` **in process**, no server.
- DECISION: the presentation is a defence, not the proof. The manager also **verifies** every job executable and every `-plugin-path` / `-external-plugin-path` / `-in-process-plugin-server-path` / `-load-plugin-{library,executable}` / `-load-resolved-plugin` value from the `swiftc -###` plan it is about to execute. A derivation rule can change in a patch release; the plan cannot lie about what will run.
- NOTE: same shape as the Rust `cc`/`xcrun` pinning and the Kotlin launcher result — the escape is a path the build tool computes for you, not a flag anyone passed.
- SCOPE: `curator-spec` `decisions/0011-swift-driver-pair.md` §4, `docs/swift-build-drivers.md` §2.2 and §4.1.
- STATUS: ready for review (`TASK-260728-1yhuqi`).

### 0324 — SwiftPM has no code-execution-free metadata query, so the Swift driver drops SwiftPM entirely

- FINDING: `swift package dump-package` **executes** `Package.swift`. Measured with a manifest that writes to stderr and attempts a filesystem escape: executed under both sandbox settings; the escape write was blocked by SwiftPM's macOS `sandbox-exec` policy and **landed** with `--disable-sandbox`. Tool-supplied, platform-specific containment that one flag removes.
- FINDING: `swift package tools-version` returns exit 0 and `6.0.0` for a manifest whose body is `this is @@ not swift (((`, while `dump-package` on the same file exits 1. The only datum obtainable without running the package is the comment on line 1.
- DECISION: decision 0008 §7 disqualifies a driver whose package-selected execution surfaces cannot be decided pre-compile, so `swift-v1` invokes **no** SwiftPM subcommand in any stage. The compiler is driven directly, the manager enumerates `Sources/**/*.swift` itself, and `Package.swift`'s first line is the bound metadata — read as text, never as a program.
- NOTE: this is the opposite result to Rust, where `cargo metadata` reports `custom-build`/`proc-macro` targets **without** running them. The trade buys a stronger graph phase: `swiftc -###` exits **0** and prints its plan for an unreadable source and for an absent one, writing nothing. Cost: single-module, dependency-free programs only.
- SCOPE: `curator-spec` `decisions/0011-swift-driver-pair.md` §2 and §6.
- STATUS: ready for review (`TASK-260728-1yhuqi`).

### 0323 — Swift's compiler-boundary position depends on whether the vector links

- FINDING: `TASK-260729-rhjxtx` measured a `swift-frontend` job spawning for `x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu` before the standard-library failure. That reproduces exactly — for a **compile-only** vector (`-c -v`: 1 frontend job, then `unable to load standard library`).
- FINDING: under a **linking** vector — which is what the driver uses — the same target fails at job planning with `error: unableToFind(tool: "swift-autolink-extract")` and spawns **0** frontend jobs. Both facts are true; the vector separates them. The job trace is on **stdout**, not stderr, which is what makes this easy to measure backwards.
- CONSEQUENCE: because the driver always links, the `-###` graph phase refuses an unserved target before any compiler child, and the Stage A stat of `runtimeLibraryPaths` refuses it earlier still. `-print-target-info` remains representability-only: exit **0** for a target whose stdlib directory does not exist.
- CONSEQUENCE FOR LINUX: `swift-autolink-extract` is a **fourth** executable a Linux qualification must show resolving inside the fingerprinted root.
- SCOPE: `curator-spec` `decisions/0011-swift-driver-pair.md` §5 and §13.
- STATUS: ready for review (`TASK-260728-1yhuqi`).

### 0302 — Official Kotlin/Native distribution disproves the proposed empty offline closure

- REVIEW: `TASK-260728-168smo` cycle 1 changes requested. Kotlin/Native is the only selected backend and the JVM/runtime-bundle rejection remains sound, but the reserved pair is not yet implementable.
- FINDING: checksum-verified Kotlin/Native 2.4.10 is JVM-hosted: the official distribution exposes shell launchers, `kotlin-native-compiler-embeddable.jar`, and `org.jetbrains.kotlin.cli.utilities.MainKt konanc`. The `kotlin` primary therefore has no regular executable in its own fingerprinted root; companion `jdk/bin/java` does not satisfy decision 0007's per-entry primary-executable invariant.
- FINDING: official `konan.properties` names remote LLVM, sysroot, target-toolchain, additional-tools, and LLDB dependencies. A direct macOS arm64 compile with empty `KONAN_DATA_DIR` and `airplaneMode=true` exited 2 on missing `lldb-4-macos`, produced no artifact, and still created `dependencies/.extracted` plus `dependencies/cache/.lock`. Q4's "empty and unchanged" data directory and the submitted `[kotlin,jdk]` identity/process closure are false for the official distribution.
