- FINDING: distribution-owned platform `.klib` libraries make the submitted "stdlib only / no C interop" statement incomplete; source imports can reach POSIX, Win32, or Apple bindings without package `.def`/`.klib` files or package-controlled argv.
- ROUTE: return to `analysis`. Select and measure one exact trusted closure, fill the registry/platform fields and pass at least one Q1–Q9 tuple, or retire both Kotlin identifiers under the current accepted architecture.
- EVIDENCE: `TASK-260728-168smo_review-verdict-cycle-1.md`.

### 0301 — A Kotlin build-root filename is an arbitrary-code-execution channel

- FINDING: the Kotlin CLI expands a bare `@`-prefixed argument as a response file. A file named `@inject` containing `-script`, plus any `.kts` in the same directory, made `kotlinc` **execute** package code at build time — marker written, exit **0**. No Gradle, no manifest field, no plugin. Measured on `kotlinc-jvm 2.4.10`.
- FINDING: the same path in absolute (`/abs/…/@inject`) or `./@inject` form does **not** expand — exit 1, `source entry is not a Kotlin file`. Expansion triggers only on the argument's first character.
- FINDING: a *missing* response file is `warning: argfile not found` with exit **0**, so a partial mitigation fails silently.
- DECISION: the `kotlin-native-v1` rejection matrix is a closed **allow-list over name shapes**, not a deny-list of filenames — the vector is a shape. Build root admits only directories, `*.kt` regular files, and one `kotlin-native-module.json`, all under a leading-character class that excludes dotfiles and `@`. Absolute-path argv kept as a measured structural backstop, not as the rule.
- NOTE: any Kotlin build integration that enumerates package files and passes them positionally has this hole.
- SCOPE: `curator-spec` `decisions/0010-kotlin-native-driver-pair.md` §6, `docs/kotlin-native-build-drivers.md` §7.
- STATUS: ready for review (`TASK-260728-168smo`).

### 0300 — The shipped Kotlin launcher resolves its own compiler from PATH

- FINDING: `<kotlin_root>/bin/kotlinc` is a bash script that resolved **4** names from `PATH` under a shim `PATH` — `uname`, `dirname`, `java -version`, `java <compile line>` — plus `bash` from its `#!/usr/bin/env bash` shebang. The compiler process is `java` **by name**. It also reads `JAVACMD`, `JAVA_HOME`, `JAVA_OPTS`, `KOTLIN_RUNNER`, `KOTLIN_COMPILER`, `KOTLIN_TOOL`; `KOTLIN_COMPILER` selects the compiler main class, i.e. an environment-selected backend.
- FINDING: invoking `<jdk_root>/bin/java -cp <kotlin_root>/lib/… <MainClass>` directly recorded **0** `PATH` resolutions, exit 0, artifact produced. `bin/java` is a `Mach-O 64-bit executable arm64`.
- DECISION: the Kotlin driver never runs any launcher script, on any platform, including for the version probe. Trusted launcher is the fingerprinted JDK's `bin/java`; `jdk` becomes a REQUIRED companion under a **native** artifact model — correcting decision 0007's "`jdk` if JVM" expectation, because the Kotlin/Native *compiler* is JVM-hosted while the produced artifact needs no runtime. Six environment channels close structurally rather than by neutralisation.
- NOTE: same shape as the Rust linker/SDK pinning result — whole process closure inside fingerprinted operator-trusted roots.
- SCOPE: `curator-spec` `decisions/0010-kotlin-native-driver-pair.md` §5, `docs/kotlin-native-build-drivers.md` §3.
- STATUS: ready for review (`TASK-260728-168smo`).

### 0259 — A regeneration that rewrites both the artifact and its pin proves nothing

- ROOT CAUSE: `curator-spec` generates `conformance/v1/manifest.json` by walking the suite directory, and `release/1.0.0-rc.5.json` pins the SHA-256 of that manifest. One `go run ./tools/generate-vectors` writes both, so any new corpus file moves the released digest *and* updates the document pinning it. The release gate compared the two against each other, so the rewritten pair passed. `TASK-260728-2jaw7h` cycle 1 shipped exactly this: rc.5 moved `b32ee9d3…` → `32114a32…` with a green `release_gate.py`.
- DECISION: an internal consistency check cannot establish preservation. The comparison must be against a record generation cannot move. `release/frozen.json` is authored, never generated, and holds per released version the release-document digest, the suite-manifest digest, the schema-case-index digest and the suite root the document must name.
- FIX: enforced at three moments — `assertFrozenReleaseIdentity` at the end of `tools/generate-vectors/main.go` (a rewrite fails instead of being written), `validate_frozen_releases` in `tools/validate.py` (every run), and the same in `tools/release_gate.py` (before a tag). Unreleased corpus moved to a second suite root `conformance/next`, whose manifest deliberately carries no `protocol_version` because naming one mints one.
- FINDING: the generator writes case files and never prunes them. `agent-skill-v8/valid-non-primary-identifier-is-a-runtime-code.json` and its `csk-skill-v8` twin were left over from an earlier generation, named by no index entry — so nothing validated them — yet the manifest directory walk hashed both into the pinned digest. Cycle 1's manifest listed 593 files; two were orphans.
- FIX: `validate_schemas` now requires the case files present under each schema-case root to equal the set the index names, in both directions.
- EVIDENCE: reproduced the cycle-1 defect in a scratch repo — manifest extended, document repinned to agree — gate exits 1 with `frozen release 1.0.0-rc.5 was rewritten`. `conformance/v1` byte-identical to the accepted predecessor. 169 tests (was 141), validate/vet/gofmt/compileall/go test all exit 0, boundary probe green on Go 1.25.1 and 1.25.5.
- SCOPE: `curator-spec` `tools/generate-vectors/main.go`, `tools/validate.py`, `tools/release_gate.py`, `release/frozen.json`, `conformance/next/`.
- STATUS: ready for review (cycle 2).

### 0247 — A synthetic closed vocabulary is not a reachable CLI contract

- REVIEW: `TASK-260720-1nlmvv` cycle 1 changes requested. Focused normal, race, vet, formatting, and diff gates pass, but the CLI does not satisfy the currentness/repair contract.
- FINDING: real `corrupt` and `unsupported` cache outcomes are blocking in `planBuilds`; `cmdStatus` therefore exits before `statusReport`. Human output never carries `state=corrupt-build-cache` / `unsupported-build-platform`, and `status --json` serializes an empty result instead of the promised build row. Missing or incompatible Go fails even earlier at toolchain probing with only stderr guidance and no machine-readable build diagnostic.
- FINDING: two documented codes are unreachable from persisted state. `marker.Read` rejects every unknown schema before `scopeStatusDrift` can emit `unsupported-marker`; marker validation likewise rejects any driver other than `go-v1` before classification can emit `unsupported-build-driver`. Unit tests call classifiers with states the production reader cannot return, so they prove enumeration rather than reachability.
- REGRESSION: install/upgrade do not repair a corrupt receipt or artifact. `BuildCorrupt.blocking()` returns before staging even though the cache publication layer already supports quarantining and replacing corrupt state. The new README says corrupt state is rebuilt, while the CLI explicitly reports it was refused.
- FINDING: an opaque cache-key mismatch is always labelled `build-target-mismatch`, although the key binds build root, command, source directory, target, toolchain, and policy. The marker does not retain enough prior input evidence to attribute the cause, so the diagnostic overclaims.
- FINDING: `RedactDiagnostic` only checks whitespace-delimited tokens. Embedded and URI forms such as `source=/private/...`, `file:///private/...`, and `error=C:\Users\...` survive unchanged; blocking plan errors also interpolate the raw cache reason into `Result.Errors` after the redacted plan line.
- EVIDENCE: task-scoped verdict `TASK-260720-1nlmvv_review-verdict-cycle-1.md`; focused packages pass at 196.644s / 260.263s / 28.658s, focused race pass at 135.109s / 1.969s / 1.560s, vet/format/diff checks pass. Routed to `to-dev`.

### 0230 — Rust can be admitted only because `rust-lld` ships inside the toolchain, and `cargo metadata` refuses to run the package

- FINDING: on macOS `rustc` resolves its linker as the bare name `cc` and locates the SDK by running `xcrun` — both `PATH` lookups outside any fingerprinted tree. Measured with 20 logging shims on `PATH`: unpinned, the control run resolved `xcrun --sdk macosx --show-sdk-path` then `cc <full link line>` and failed `error: linking with 'cc' failed: exit status: 127`. Pinned with `SDKROOT` and `-Clinker=<root>/lib/rustlib/<target>/bin/rust-lld -Clinker-flavor=ld64.lld`, both the graph and the compile phase produced **zero** resolutions and a running `Mach-O 64-bit executable arm64` whose only dynamic dependency is `/usr/lib/libSystem.B.dylib`.
- DECISION: the whole process closure is therefore `cargo -> rustc -> rust-lld`, three executables inside one fingerprinted distribution, and the platform SDK is a **data-only** second fingerprinted root that starts nothing. That avoids coining a sixth toolchain identifier: decision 0007's closed set stays `{go, rust, swift, kotlin, jdk}`, the `rust` entry declares no companion, and `curator-rust-toolchain-v1` fingerprints an ordered closure of roots with a domain-separated `closure_sha256` over `(role, tree digest)` pairs.
- FINDING: `cargo metadata --format-version 1 --offline` executes **no** package code. A path dependency with a marker-writing `build.rs` and a second with a marker-writing `proc-macro` lib produced neither marker and exited 0 — while the same output reported `kind:["custom-build"]`, `crate_types:["proc-macro"]`, `links` and per-package `source`. That is the only reason the decision 0008 §7 pre-compile rejection matrix is computable at all, and it is the opposite of the Swift `dump-package` result.
- FINDING: `cargo metadata` **without** `--locked` writes `Cargo.lock` into the source tree — a write to the frozen snapshot. With `--locked` and no lock file it exits 101 and writes nothing. `--locked` is mandatory on both vectors for that reason, not for reproducibility.
- FINDING: a package `.cargo/config.toml` carrying `[build] rustc-wrapper` **executed** the named script three times during `cargo build`, and an **ancestor** `.cargo/config.toml` above the build root is discovered too. `RUSTC_WRAPPER=` (set, empty) in the manager environment neutralises both, and a config `[env] ... force = true` cannot override it. The file is still rejected outright: `[source]`, `[registries]`, `[patch]` and `[http]` have no environment counterpart and a package config outranks the manager's own `$CARGO_HOME` config.
- FINDING: `rust-toolchain.toml` carrying **both** `path` and `channel = "nightly"` is completely inert against a directly resolved `cargo` — build succeeded, 0 `PATH` resolutions. It is a live selector only through the `rustup` shim, which is what admits `channel` as `compared` and keeps `path` `forbidden`.
- DECISION: decision 0007's expected `Cargo.toml` `rust-version` row moves from `compared` to `classified`. Measured, the field has **three** host-independent acceptance layers — TOML type, `rust-version` grammar, and an edition floor (2018 -> 1.31.0, 2021 -> 1.56.0, 2024 -> 1.85.0; 2015 admits `"1"`) — plus a separate host gate. Pairwise independence is measured, not argued. Unlike Go, isolation needs no harness surgery: `cargo metadata --no-deps` reports `rust_version` `1.99` on a 1.91.0 host with exit 0, so it structurally cannot be applying the host gate. Security partition `F` is empty, so P1/P2 collapse to the satisfiable equality `C = Upstream`.
- NOTE: recognition had to be a **pair** — one predicted first line plus one predicted caret source line — because three distinct grammar rejections share the first line `error: expected a version like "1.32"` and none of the first lines names the value under test. Control C2 measures exactly that: lead-only recognition matched 6 diagnostics produced for a different value.
- FINDING: two surfaces are admitted with bounds rather than closed, and one has no home. `include!`/`include_str!`/`include_bytes!` can read outside the build root; they are reads not code execution, no sound pre-compile rejection exists (the macro name is a token, so `include ! ( )` defeats a byte scan while the substring rejects ordinary comments), and **none of the six deferred hardened guarantees covers compile-time filesystem reads**. `STORY-260728-327soo` needs a seventh.
- NOTE: measured fingerprint cost is real and stated rather than optimised away — Rust root 657 MB / 167 files / 1.73 s, macOS SDK 261 MB / 32,345 files / 9.01 s, per operation, per root.
- SCOPE: `TASK-260728-12pnm1`. New `decisions/0009-rust-driver-pair.md` + `docs/rust-build-drivers.md` in worktree `.temp/TASK-260728-12pnm1/curator-spec-worktree`; standalone probe module `.temp/TASK-260728-12pnm1/probe`. No normative spec file modified, nothing staged or pinned.
- STATUS: ready for review. Probe green at exit 0 — 19/19 cases, P1 and P2 hold, 24 closure checks with 0 verdicts, 6 of 6 controls failing as required, 8 structural checks with 0 divergences. `platforms` holds only `(macos, arm64)`; macOS amd64, Windows and Linux are qualification obligations with a stated acceptance test, not claims.

### 0224 — Marker v2 silently broke every schema-1 installation's status, and a repeated compiled install still rewrites what it just proved current

- REGRESSION: since marker v2 landed, `scopeStatusDrift` compared the recorded schema against `marker.SchemaVersion` alone, so every unchanged schema 1 through 5 installation reported `unsupported-marker` and failed `status --check`. `marker.Current` had always accepted schema 1 for those installs — only the CLI's own drift check disagreed. FIX: accept `marker.LegacySchemaVersion` too; a schema-1 marker that would have to describe a compiled command is demoted to `needs-install` by the build classification, the only place that can see the plan. `cmd/curator/main.go` `scopeStatusDrift`.
- FINDING: a repeated `curator install` over an unchanged compiled skill is **not** idempotent. `stageNode` calls `marker.Current(live, expected)` with no `marker.BuildCurrentness` value, and that call fails closed for any marker carrying build state, so the context directory is re-staged every run. Measured: second install reports `installed`, not `up-to-date`, while the plan line still reports `outcome=cache-hit` — nothing recompiles, only the context tree is rewritten. `internal/install/targets.go:42`. Owned by the staging tasks, not by `TASK-260720-1nlmvv`; worth its own task.
- DECISION: `status` classification compares the recorded driver, then the build-source identity, then the logical key, and only *then* reads the cache inspection. That ordering is what makes "wrong target or toolchain" reportable as itself: if the source identity still matches but the derived key does not, the only remaining inputs are target, toolchain, and the fixed policy. A `cache-hit` alone is not accepted — the entry's receipt identity, artifact path and artifact hash must equal the marker's, which is how `corrupt-build-receipt` and `build-artifact-drift` separate from a hit.
- DECISION: compiled state may only **demote** a skill from `up-to-date`, never overwrite a code an ordinary check produced. That is simultaneously the correctness rule (a missing or tampered skill keeps the more actionable instruction) and the legacy-compatibility guarantee: the `skills` object and the human lines are byte-unchanged for any closure without build commands, and the new `builds` key is absent rather than empty.
- FINDING: `internal/install` exceeds the default 10-minute `go test` timeout under `-race` (709s observed, exit 0 with `-timeout 40m`). The default produces a timeout panic that reads like a failure but reports no data race. CI should pin the timeout.
- ANOMALY: `TestRegistryRevocationDeniesInstall` failed once under a loaded parallel `go test ./...` with "registry test-reg snapshot timestamp is too far in the future"; passed in isolation, in a full `./internal/install` run, on the untouched base, and in two later full `./...` runs. Snapshot clock-skew tolerance looks load-sensitive. STATUS: unexplained, not reproduced, worth its own task rather than a silent retry.
- SCOPE: `TASK-260720-1nlmvv`, worktree `.temp/TASK-260720-1nlmvv/worktree` over the accepted `1ljev5` tree. New `cmd/curator/builds.go` + `internal/install/diagnostics.go`; `cmd/curator` gained a `TestMain` dispatching the hidden worker mode, so CLI tests now run **real** compiled installations through the identity-verified process graph instead of a mock.

### 0219 — A payload that cannot name the source that failed is not a diagnostic, and the two accepted prerequisites merged without a single conflict

- MILESTONE: `TASK-260728-2jaw7h` landed the decision 0007 toolchain contract on the wire: manifest schema 8 and `skill-build.json` schema 2 carrying a REQUIRED and an OPTIONAL `toolchain` of exactly `{id, version}`, the manager-owned `toolchain-registry-v1`, `toolchain-guidance-catalog-v1` and `toolchain-diagnostic-v1` schemas, `protocol/core.md` section 4.2.3 (10 sub-sections), and five vector suites covering the whole section 8 inventory by case identifier.
- FINDING: the two accepted prerequisites are disjoint on disk. `1g0z69` touches `CHANGELOG.md` + two docs; `2spy93` touches `decisions/0008` + `tools/validate.py` + `tools/test_validate.py`. Merging both onto the `2kp3tv` base ran green on all four gates before the first edit — 42 schemas, 422 vector files, 91 tests.
- FINDING: `2spy93` had already pinned the three shapes it reserved (`buildCommandV8`, `repositoryBuildCommandV2`, `skillBuildTargetV2`) to their exact member sets and to a single `$ref` at `toolchainRequirementV1`. Minting them required **no** relaxation of that guard — it went from checking three absent definitions to checking three present ones. Reservation tables that constrain a shape before it exists are worth the cost.
- DECISION: `source_ref.surface` gains a fourth token `registry`. Reference section 5.1 fixes three; section 2.3 makes the registry baseline a contributing source of the intersection. The commonest unsatisfiable case is a manifest range below the baseline, where the failing bound *is* the baseline's — and `requirement_unsatisfiable` must name every source achieving the bound. Three tokens cannot. Recorded in the reference header as a completion, not a restatement.
- DECISION: a value classifier's `absent` class is marked `matches: "absence"`. Section 3.1.1 requires forbidden classes first; sections 4.2.2/4.2.3 both put `absent` at position 1 ahead of the forbidden ones. The precedence rule is over *values*, and the absence class classifies no byte string. Explicit marking beats special-casing a class name.
- DECISION: `toolchainRequirementV1.id` is closed to `{go, kotlin, rust, swift}`, not to the driver's primary. Binding it to `const "go"` would make `id_not_primary` and `unknown_id` unreachable as runtime violations and turn inventory case 21 into a schema rejection, which the inventory explicitly says it is not.
- REGRESSION: adding `import toolchain_gate` to `validate.py` and `release_gate.py` wrote `tools/__pycache__`, leaving the checkout dirty and failing the release gate's own clean-checkout requirement. Caught by the clean git-init probe, not by reasoning about it. FIX: `sys.dont_write_bytecode = True` before the import in both tools.
- FINDING: the fixture/probe agreement check does not need a Go toolchain. `tools/toolchain_gate.py` parses the landed probe's own `goCases`/`tcCases` literals — 29 cases — and holds every fixture row marked `probe_measured` to the probe's class and disposition in both directions. The reference's rule ("a fixture that disagrees with a probe run is a defect in the fixture") is now enforced on any runner.
- SCOPE: `curator-spec` worktree `.temp/TASK-260728-2jaw7h/curator-spec-worktree`, 47 entries vs the accepted base. 31 frozen schema files, every pre-existing schema case, vector, fixture and expected file byte-identical; `common.schema.json` purely additive (20 new `$defs`, 0 modified).
- STATUS: ready for review. `validate.py` 0 (48 schemas, 593 vector files), 141 tests 0, `go test`/`vet`/`gofmt` 0, clean-probe `make release-check VERSION=1.0.0-rc.5` 0, boundary probe green and all five expected-red controls failing as required. `1.0.0-rc.6` deliberately not minted — that stays `TASK-260728-251p01`.

### 0145 — A boolean inferred from a non-zero exit is not a measurement, and it got the Swift compiler boundary backwards

- ROOT CAUSE: `TASK-260729-rhjxtx` rework cycle 1. `swiftTargetCases()` computed `BuildStartedCompilation` as `compile.ExitCode != 0 && targetPrint.ExitCode == 0` — an inference from failure. It reports compilation for *any* target the compiler recognises and then refuses, on any host, without ever observing a compiler process.
- FINDING: the measurement contradicts the cycle-0 prose. Under `swiftc -v`, the driver's own job trace shows it **does** execute `swift-frontend -frontend -c -primary-file …` for `x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu` before failing on the standard library, and executes **no** frontend job for `not-a-real-triple`. Rust is therefore *not* the only corpus case where upstream rejection arrives after compilation starts; both Rust and Swift reject a known-but-unserved target only after starting a compiler, and only the unknown-triple case is refused by the driver alone.
- FIX: `Observation.BuildCompilationEvidence` now carries the exact output line that proves a compiler ran; `finish()` derives the boolean from it and overwrites anything a builder set. Both streams are scanned — cargo writes `Compiling` to stderr, the Swift driver writes its job trace to stdout. `.temp/TASK-260729-rhjxtx/probe/classify.go`, `swift.go:281`.
- NOTE: a compiler's exit code cannot locate the boundary. Both Swift target cases exit 1; only the job trace separates "the driver refused the triple" from "a frontend ran and failed".

### 0144 — An expected classification that varies with the host that ran the probe is not an expectation

- ROOT CAUSE: `TASK-260729-rhjxtx` carried each case's identity twice — a stub list producing `not_run` records, and the executed builder. They drifted: `swift.negative.selector-swift-version-file` was `package_selector` in the stub list and `none` in the builder, so the macOS and Windows fixtures disagreed about the same case ID. The classifier can only produce a divergence if the expectation is fixed independently of the measurement, so a host-varying expectation silently disarms the whole design.
- FIX: one canonical `caseTable()` in `.temp/TASK-260729-rhjxtx/probe/cases.go`; `notRunLanguage()` and every builder derive from it, and `finish()` re-applies the declaration to every emitted case. Regressions compare declared against executed against **real** local toolchains. Verified on the two produced fixtures: 19 shared case IDs, 0 cross-host contract divergences.
- DECISION: `.swift-version` is `none`, which is the measurement — the file does not redirect a directly resolved toolchain. A negative control whose expected outcome is "no effect" is still a negative control, and that inertness is what admits the field as `compared` rather than `forbidden`.

### 0143 — Kotlin has no legal project-metadata file, and that absence is the decision input

- FINDING: decision 0008 section 4 requires every local driver to bind exactly one closed project-metadata file in the build root, as `go-v1` binds `go.mod`. Kotlin/Native has no candidate. `kotlinc-native` consumes `.kt` sources and an output path and defines no manifest; the ecosystem's manifest is a Gradle script.
- FINDING (measured, needs no Kotlin/Native): answering the pure metadata query `gradle properties` compiled the build script as a program source unit (`_BuildScript_`) before it could answer. Same disqualification `swift package dump-package` earns. Contained via `GRADLE_USER_HOME` inside the probe work dir with `--offline --no-daemon`; the operator `~/.gradle` was verified untouched. `build.gradle.kts` was *not* measured — resolving the Kotlin DSL needs network fetches the probe refuses.
- DECISION: record the boundary rather than invent a candidate. `TASK-260728-168smo` must either mint a curator-owned metadata file for the build root, or accept that decision 0008 section 10's retirement clause applies to both Kotlin identifiers. The probe proposes no file and no field.
- NOTE: no Kotlin/Native distribution exists on either probed host. The supplied-root branch is implemented and unit-tested against a *synthetic* root, which verifies probe logic only; the `konanc` normalization rule and the `-list-targets` parser stay `UNVERIFIED` and every Kotlin/Native path degrades to `not_run` with a stated reason rather than a plausible wrong answer.

### 0120 — A digest label is not a cryptographic identity, and a declared minimum is not enforced merely because both strings are present

- REVIEW: `TASK-260728-1jafds` cycle 3 changes requested. Cycle-2 rework closes the manager-parent, host, backend, component-record, platform/backend, target/platform, and configuration-binding gaps; all mechanical gates and rc.5 parity checks pass.
- FINDING: `curator-hardened-component-file-v1` and `curator-hardened-component-tree-v1` are enumerated but never defined. There is no normative byte stream, domain separation, tree traversal/order, path/type/mode/link treatment, or failure rule. A `content_sha256` field therefore cannot be independently reproduced, and the tree form cannot distinguish every materially different trusted component under a common contract.
- FINDING: the protocol requires observed `tcb.backend.version` to be at or above the claim entry's `minimum_version`, but claim schema 4 constrains both only as non-empty strings and `check_claim_qualification` never compares them. An independent mutant with backend version `0` and minimum `999999` produced 0 schema errors and the semantic validator accepted it. The same probe showed a Linux TCB with host identity `windows` is accepted, so the observed-host relation is asserted but not closed.
- COVERAGE GAP: TCB rotations change a component digest and add a component, but do not rotate a valid component `kind`, `name`, or digest algorithm even though cycle 3 explicitly required component-kind/digest mutation.
- REQUIRED: define reproducible component file/tree digest algorithms and adversarial fixtures; define backend-specific version ordering and enforce the minimum-version relation; close the host/platform observation relation or explicitly narrow it; rotate every component identity subfield and prove cache/receipt/marker/claim behavior.
- EVIDENCE: `TASK-260728-1jafds_review-verdict-cycle-3.md`; task routed to `to-dev`. `make validate` 0 (113 tests), Go tests/vet/format/diff 0, clean-probe regeneration and rc.5 release gate 0, portable manifest SHA-256 `9ba9b8ec…`.

### 0119 — Go's `invalid GOTOOLCHAIN %q` diagnostics quote the environment setting, not the go.mod name — so recognising that lead plus any tail fabricates verdicts

- FINDING: `TASK-260728-1g0z69` review cycle 7. In `cmd/go/internal/toolchain/select.go`, three `base.Fatalf` sites render `invalid GOTOOLCHAIN %q` while *interpreting the environment setting*, before `go.mod` is read — `:155` (`…: invalid minimum toolchain %q`), `:157`, `:163` (`…: only version suffixes are +auto and +path`). All three quote `gotoolchain` = the env value. Under `GOTOOLCHAIN=local+path` none is reachable at all (`min` is `local`; the suffix is `path`). Only `:198` (`invalid toolchain %q in %s`), `:282` (`invalid GOTOOLCHAIN %q`, after `gotoolchain` is reassigned from go.mod at `:171`/`:208`) and `:351` (`cannot find %q in PATH`) quote a go.mod-derived name. Verified on Go 1.25.1 and 1.25.5.
- ROOT CAUSE: the boundary probe's `toolchain` classifier recognised the exact `invalid GOTOOLCHAIN "v"` form *and* `HasPrefix(line, "invalid GOTOOLCHAIN \"v\":")`. That prefix is not one upstream outcome; it is every message upstream might later render behind that lead, none of them measured. It answered `rejected` — and for any value the isolated `gover` measurement already rejects, the fabricated verdict *agrees*, so the isolated-vs-command crosscheck compared equal and the row stayed green.
- FINDING: the same defect was present in three more places nobody had challenged — `invalid toolchain "v" in <anything>` (lead), `invalid go version 'v':` (substring anywhere in output), and the TooNew lead-and-tail matcher. Cycle 6 had closed the *fall-through*, not the *matching*.
- DECISION: verdict-laundering has two directions and neither is safer. Fabricating `accepted` hides behind an isolated-accepted value; fabricating `rejected` hides behind an isolated-rejected one. A control that only exercises one direction is not evidence about the other, so the probe now counts and reports them separately.
- FIX: recognition is a finite list of whole diagnostic lines *predicted before the command runs* from the value under test plus probe-fixed constants (module file name, directive line number, `GOTOOLCHAIN` setting, the probed toolchain's own local version). New measured `classifier closure` section — 331 checks per toolchain over real unrelated command failures, cross-fed measured outcomes, and measured diagnostics structurally extended. New expected-red `-red open-classifier` restores the four retired families: 9 direction-A + 15 direction-B laundered fabrications per toolchain.
- NOTE: fail-closedness cannot be measured end-to-end — it is a claim about outcomes upstream has not written yet. Constructing them from text upstream *did* emit, and labelling the construction, is the honest form of the check; asserting the property from the recognised set is not a check.
- SCOPE: worktree `.temp/TASK-260728-1g0z69/curator-spec-worktree` — `CHANGELOG.md`, decision 0007, `docs/compiled-build-toolchain-requirements.md`; probe at `.temp/TASK-260728-1g0z69/probe/main.go`.
- STATUS: handed to review cycle 8.

### 0118 — Hashing a TCB digest is worthless unless the record behind it distinguishes the bases that actually differ

- ROOT CAUSE: `TASK-260728-1jafds` review cycle 2. `hardened-tcb-v1` bound only supervisor bytes, worker bytes, a backend *label*, platform and toolchain. Four adversarial instances validated with **0 schema errors**: a macOS TCB carrying the Linux backend; a `darwin` build target with a `linux` TCB; a trusted component declared as the bare string `"mutable-interpreter-with-no-cryptographic-identity"`; a macOS TCB with a Windows backend inside a conformance claim. Two materially different trusted bases therefore produced one digest, one cache key, one receipt binding, one marker, one claim — while every test stayed green.
- FINDING: the gap was not the hashing (domain-separated `curator-hardened-tcb-v1` was correct) but *record completeness*. Missing: the installed manager parent, the observed OS/hypervisor identity+version+build, the enforcement-backend version and the configuration the qualification depends on. Present but useless: `additional_trusted_components` as `array of nonEmptyString` — a name is not an identity.
- FIX: record is now 12 closed members incl. `parent_sha256`, `host{kind,identity,version,build}`, `backend{version,configuration[{setting,observed_value}]}`, and `trusted_components[{kind,name,algorithm,content_sha256}]`. The string array is deleted and a schema case proves it cannot be reintroduced. `schemas/hardened/v1/hardened-common.schema.json`.
- DECISION: bind the observed host/backend rather than narrow the normative claim. An implementation that cannot say which OS, which backend version, and which components it trusted cannot support the claim that an artifact carries the revision's guarantees.
- FINDING: three one-to-one relations *are* expressible in Draft 2020-12 via `allOf` + `if/then` `$ref`s and are now schema-enforced — platform↔backend, claim OS↔backend, and receipt `input.target.goos`↔`tcb.platform`. Narrowing the hardened input's `goos` to `{darwin,linux,windows}` makes the third total instead of partial. Two relations (claim TCB names a declared OS; claim's required configuration observed in its own TCB) are not expressible and are validator-enforced — `tcb_completeness.relations` labels each relation `schema` or `conformance-validator` so a reader is not misled about where a check lives.
- FINDING: a mutant that trips two rules proves neither. First pass at `validate_tcb_schema_relations` set a platform/backend mismatch while leaving the target `darwin`, so the *target* relation fired and the platform↔backend rule was never exercised. Fixed by moving the target with the platform. Verified by deleting each relation from the shipped schema in a sandbox and requiring a distinct failure — 5/5 caught. `tools/validate_hardened.py:validate_tcb_schema_relations`.
- DECISION: 13 rotation cases, one per mutable member; **11 change nothing a package can see**, so key movement is attributable to the trusted base and not to the build. The two that necessarily move a visible value (`toolchain`, also a portable input member; `platform`, bound to the receipt target) carry `package_visible_input_changed: true` + a stated reason, and the validator rejects a misreport in either direction.
- NOTE: `rotate-worker-identity` reproduces the `hardened_rotated_tcb` contract key by construction — 18 keys, 17 distinct. Same trusted base, so identical is correct, not an alias.
- SCOPE: worktree `.temp/TASK-260728-1jafds/curator-spec-worktree`. Hardened suite 48→79 files, schema cases 33→75, Python tests 87→113. `conformance/v1` + `schemas/v1` + `release/1.0.0-rc.5.json` byte-identical to the accepted predecessor; manifest sha256 still `9ba9b8ec…`.
- STATUS: handed to review cycle 3. No platform qualified, `claims_emitted: []`, no native adversarial test run on any host.

### 0037 — A compiler's "do you know this target" command answers representability, and both Rust and Swift let it pass for a target they cannot build

- FINDING: `TASK-260729-rhjxtx`. `rustc --print target-libdir --target x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu` exits **0** and prints a plausible path on a host where that target's std is absent; `swiftc -print-target-info -target x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu` exits **0** and emits full JSON. Both then fail at compile — Rust with `E0463: can't find crate for 'std'` *after* `Compiling`, Swift with `unable to load standard library for target`. Using either print command as a preflight admits a build that runs a compiler on package source before failing.
- FINDING: the admission check is a manager-side stat inside the fingerprinted tree in both cases — `<root>/lib/rustlib/<target>/lib` for Rust, the `paths.runtimeLibraryPaths` the same Swift command reports for Swift. Only unknown triples are refused early by the compilers themselves (exit 1). This is the target-level twin of decision 0007's representability-vs-host-version split.
- FINDING: `rust-toolchain.toml` `channel = "nightly"` made the rustup shim **attempt a network download and install** (`info: syncing channel updates for 'nightly-aarch64-apple-darwin'`); `path = "/nonexistent/…"` made it fail with that path. The same directory through the directly resolved root `<root>/bin/rustc` exits 0 and returns the host toolchain unchanged. Decision 0007 §3 measured in both directions, not argued. Download blocked by pointing `RUSTUP_DIST_SERVER`/`RUSTUP_UPDATE_ROOT` at a closed loopback port; `rustup toolchain list` identical before and after.
- FINDING: `swift package tools-version` reads the header line only — exit 0 and `6.0.0` against a `Package.swift` with a syntax error *and* against one whose body is `fatalError`. `swift package dump-package` compiles the manifest into a `<name>-manifest` executable (full `swiftc` argv incl. `-plugin-path …/host/plugins/testing` in its own diagnostic) and runs it: `Fatal error: PROBE-MANIFEST-EXECUTED`. So a Swift driver's graph phase is bounded at the header; products and targets must come from the wire surface.
- FINDING: three of four version probes do not put the version where a naive rule looks. `swift --version` splits one banner across stdout and stderr (stderr fragment has no trailing newline, so merged output concatenates the two into one line and defeats an anchored rule). `kotlinc -version` writes to **stderr** with an `info:` prefix and leaves stdout **empty**. `rustc --version` mixes the commit hash into the version line. Registry `probe` entries must name their stream.
- FINDING: Swift `target.triple` is `arm64-apple-macosx26.0` while host macOS is 26.5 and the SDK is 26.5 — the platform component is the deployment default, and SwiftPM compiles manifests at `arm64-apple-macosx14.0`, a third value. `unversionedTriple` is the only form usable as a native-target identity.
- REGRESSION: cargo resolves `rustc` **by name from PATH** — under a manager-owned minimal PATH every invocation died with ``could not execute process `rustc -vV` (never executed)``. Found by the probe's own first run. A Rust driver must pass `RUSTC` explicitly or the second node of its `manager-worker-v2` process graph is chosen by PATH order.
- ANOMALY: `TOOLCHAINS=nonexistent.toolchain.id xcrun --find swiftc` exits 0 and silently returns the default toolchain rather than failing. "The selector did not error" is not evidence that no selector applied.
- BLOCKED (evidence, not the task): Kotlin/Native absent on both hosts — Homebrew `kotlin` ships jvm/js/wasm front ends only, and the Windows host (`win`, 10.0.19045) carries no Rust, Swift, Kotlin, JDK, or Go at all. Nothing installed. 4 Kotlin cases and all 19 Windows cases reported `not_run`, never as passes; the konanc normalization rule is named `…UNVERIFIED`. No Kotlin metadata file/field proposed, and no Windows platform claim is evidence-backed today.
- DECISION: model the JVM front end as a third availability state `wrong_backend`, distinct from `unavailable` — "a Kotlin compiler is here and it is the wrong one" is a different operator problem, and accepting any `kotlinc` banner would let a JVM archive satisfy a driver whose only artifact class is `native-executable-v1`.
- SCOPE: `.temp/TASK-260729-rhjxtx/probe/` (standalone module, 34 tests); board artifacts `TASK-260729-rhjxtx_{results.md,probe.tar.gz,fixture-macos.json,fixture-windows.json,command-evidence.log,windows-inventory.log,gate-log.txt}`. No curator-spec, module, pin, or dependency touched.
- STATUS: handed to review. macOS 15/15 matched, 0 divergences, exit 0, verdicts identical across two independent runs; Windows exit 0 all not_run; probe gofmt/vet/test/golangci-lint 0; curator `go build`, `make vet`, `make test` 0. `make check` exits 2 at its `gofmt -l .` stage on 7 files in *other* tasks' `.temp/` scratch trees — 0 from this task, all tracked `.go` files clean.

### 0031 — An open classifier's fall-through branch is an assertion wearing a measurement's clothes

- ROOT CAUSE: `TASK-260728-1g0z69` cycle 6. The boundary probe's `toolchain`-directive command classifier started at `accepted` and let unrecognized non-zero outcomes fall through to it. The reviewer flagged it as a latent hole; instrumenting the branch showed it was already firing.
- FINDING: under `go build ./...`, `toolchain default` and `toolchain go1` exit 1 with `go: updates to go.mod needed` on both Go 1.25.1 and 1.25.5 — the module loader runs *after* `toolchain.Select`, and both values leave the `toolchain` line redundant against `go 1.23`. Confirmed by rerunning with `-mod=mod`: exit 0, `toolchain` line dropped. Four of twenty-six toolchain measurements were scoring module tidiness as upstream acceptance of a version name.
- FINDING: the isolated-versus-command agreement check could not catch it. A fabricated verdict agrees with the isolated measurement exactly as well as a real one does — which is why closure has to be a separate property, not a consequence of cross-checking.
- FIX: `go version` replaces `go build ./...` for that position (whole failure surface is `Select`, so exit 0 measures acceptance rather than absence of unrelated failure); `cmdUnknown` added as a fourth state that is not a verdict and always fails; recognition is per-diagnostic-line and must echo the value under test in upstream's own `%q`. The `go`-directive classifier closed too — its fall-through named *rejection*, which launders just as effectively for values the isolated measurement already rejects.
- FINDING: requiring the echoed name is load-bearing beyond this defect. `Select` renders two further `invalid GOTOOLCHAIN %q` messages quoting the *environment* value (`local+path`), never the `go.mod` name; those correctly fall to unknown.
- DECISION: falling through to rejection is not safer than falling through to acceptance — it only moves the laundering. Recognized sets are drawn from upstream's own `Fatalf` forms; a future message change turns the probe red rather than quietly changing what it measures.
- SCOPE: `.temp/TASK-260728-1g0z69/probe/main.go`; candidate delta still exactly `CHANGELOG.md`, `decisions/0007-…`, `docs/compiled-build-toolchain-requirements.md`. Reserved rust/swift/kotlin/jdk entries now owe the same closed set — `cargo`, `swift build` and Gradle all run substantial work after their version check.
- STATUS: handed to review (cycle 7). Probe 58 cases exit 0; four expected-red controls each exit 1; validate/unittest/go test/vet/gofmt/diff 0; clean-probe regeneration ×2 and rc.5 release gate 0; rc.5 bytes identical to the accepted predecessor.

### 0028 — Proving a boundary and re-deriving it are different acts: bind the verdict, not just the mutation

- ROOT CAUSE: `TASK-260720-1ljev5` cycle 2 bound the build-cache *removal* to the proven cache-root object but let the *decision* that authorises it start a fresh traversal from the manager-home pathname (`internal/buildcache/collect.go` → `store.inspectEntry(entryPath, …)`). On Unix an open descriptor does not pin later pathname resolution, so a cache-root exchange between the parent assertion and the classification made the exact-content, receipt, and artifact-hash checks validate a *planted replacement* while `retireEntry` removed the unproven original: validate one directory, delete another.
- FIX: `inspectProvenEntry` + `openProtectedEntryFrom` classify from the candidate's own descriptor — `openat`/`O_NOFOLLOW` for receipt, `bin`, and artifact; the exact-members listing reads the caller's borrowed proven handle. `Store.Inspect` keeps the pathname entry point; `classifyEntry` is the single decision procedure both share so they cannot drift.
- FINDING: `json.Decoder.DisallowUnknownFields` is not a strictness gate — Go struct decoding accepts a **repeated** known member and keeps the last one. `{"schema_version":1,"consumers":["/live"],"consumers":[]}` read as an empty *trusted* consumer registry, which maintenance would rewrite empty, forgetting the checkout whose marker protects a build key. `parseConsumers` now walks tokens and refuses any repeated member; repetition inside the array stays accepted because dedup cannot drop a checkout.
- FINDING: a "retained" test outcome proves nothing on its own. The first classification-swap test wrote into the entry *after* backdating it, so the candidate was retained for being young; only the negative control exposed that the test was measuring the wrong branch.
- SCOPE: `internal/buildcache/{cache,collect,protection_unix,protection_windows,protection_unsupported}.go`, `internal/scopes/consumers.go`, README maintenance section, four test files.
- STATUS: handed to review (cycle 3). macOS full/race/lint gates 0; native Windows scopes and `curator gc` 0; both negative controls red by construction; `GOOS=windows go vet` red only on the sibling-owned `internal/runtimestore` defect the accepted base reproduces identically.

### 0024 — A corroborating command classifier must fail closed on unknown outcomes

- REVIEW: `TASK-260728-1g0z69` cycle 6 changes requested. The isolated Go representability harness and the `go`-directive accepted/too-new/rejected classifier close the cycle-5 blocker, and all positive/negative gates pass.
- FINDING: the `toolchain`-directive cross-check initializes the command result as accepted and leaves every unrecognized non-zero outcome accepted (`.temp/TASK-260728-1g0z69/probe/main.go:489-500`). Only `invalid toolchain`, `invalid GOTOOLCHAIN`, `cannot find`, and shape rejection are classified explicitly.
- IMPACT: for a shape-valid, isolated-representable name, an unrelated command failure or a future upstream rejection layer is reported as accepted and agrees with the isolated result. The probe can therefore stay green while the command composition drifts, contradicting the decision's claim that neither measurement is authoritative alone and disagreement catches composition errors.
- REQUIRED: make the command-outcome classifier closed. Accept only exit 0 or a structurally validated `cannot find <tested name> in PATH` outcome; reject only the recognized invalid-name outcomes; treat every other non-zero result as unknown and fail the probe. Add a runnable expected-red control injecting an unrelated failure for an isolated-representable, shape-valid name.
- EVIDENCE: reviewer artifact `TASK-260728-1g0z69_review-verdict-cycle-6.md`; task routed to `analysis`.

### 0001 — An exit status is a conjunction of every gate a command applies, and cannot be decomposed back into one of them

- ROOT CAUSE: `TASK-260728-1g0z69` cycles 3–4 measured the Go semantic acceptance layer — "can upstream *represent* this version" — with the exit code of `go mod tidy`. That command also applies the running toolchain's TooNew gate, so a shape-valid, `gover.Parse`-valid future release scored as ecosystem-invalid. Reproduced on Go 1.25.1 and 1.25.5: a module with `go 1.99.0` gives `go mod edit -json` exit 0 and `go mod tidy` exit 1 with `go.mod requires go >= 1.99.0 (running go 1.25.x)`.
- FINDING: upstream keeps the two apart and orders them — `cmd/go/internal/modload/modfile.go` raises `*gover.TooNewError` only *after* `modfile.Parse` returns and `gover.Compare(f.Go.Version, gover.Local()) > 0`. Representability is a property of the value; buildability is a property of the host. `$GOROOT/src/internal/gover/gover.go` imports only `cmp` and never consults the local version.
- FIX: the probe now lifts `internal/gover/gover.go` whole and `FromToolchain` verbatim out of the *probed* toolchain's own `GOROOT`, builds them with that toolchain, and evaluates representability directly; the real command is retained only as corroboration with its outcome classified into accepted / too-new / rejected. The two must agree wherever the shape layer accepts the value.
- DECISION: the contract must not fold a host-dependent gate into a grammar layer, because Curator already reaches the same conclusion at its own firing site — a future release is a class-2 comparison whose base triple is above the resolved toolchain, reported with a *derived canonical assertion*, not the `unclassifiable` token of a grammar failure. Folding would classify one fact twice under two payloads.
- FINDING: this failure mode is green until the case corpus first names a version above the runner's, then goes red on the release gate for a defect entirely in the test. Three superseded classifications are now runnable controls inside the probe binary (`-semantic tidy-exit`, `-red patch-prerelease-compared`, `-red c-equals-upstream`), each required to fail; a control that stops failing is the alarm.
- SCOPE: `curator-spec` `docs/compiled-build-toolchain-requirements.md` §§ 4.2.1, 4.2.1.2, 4.2.2, 8; `decisions/0007-compiled-build-toolchain-preflight.md` § 4 and rejected alternatives; `.temp/TASK-260728-1g0z69/probe/main.go`.
- STATUS: handed to review (cycle 6).

## 2026-07-28

### 2358 — A guarantee self-test cannot precede the process that is contained by the thing it tests

- ROOT CAUSE: `TASK-260728-1jafds` cycle 1 published a 15-phase list where `domain-guarantee-self-test` (phase 6) ran "from inside the freshly created domain" while `domain-entry` was phase 7, and the domain-root worker was defined as the first process inside the domain. No actor could perform phase 6. The list enumerated phases but never named who runs each one, so the contradiction was invisible.
- FIX: every phase now carries an `actor` (`manager-parent`, `hardened-supervisor`, `domain-root-worker`), and one rule is enforced mechanically — no phase whose actor is inside the build domain may precede `domain-entry`. Order is `domain-establishment` → `domain-entry` → `in-domain-guarantee-self-test` → `go-list`. `validate_ordered_phases` and `TestNoInDomainActorRunsBeforeDomainEntry` reject the old ordering.
- DECISION: split the overclaimed single boundary into two honest ones — *before domain entry* (phases 1–7, where every capability/qualification/TCB rejection lands) and *before package exposure* (phases 8–9). Conflating them was the only way to keep the old phrasing, and it was false. Both remain strictly before `go list`, `go build`, and any compiler.
- FINDING: the mirror insight is why the split is necessary at all — creating containment requires privileges the domain must not have, so it belongs to an uncontained supervisor; *observing* containment requires being contained, so it belongs to the worker. Verification runs from both sides.
- SCOPE: `protocol/hardened-execution.md` §§ 4.1, 4.3, 7.2, 7.3; `profiles/manager-hardened.md` § 2; `tools/generate-hardened/main.go`; `tools/validate_hardened.py`.
- STATUS: handed to review (cycle 2).

### 2358 — Reproducing a reserved example key is not a reason to leave the identities that decide the guarantee unhashed

- ROOT CAUSE: `TASK-260728-1jafds` cycle 1 kept `hardened-profile-v1` and the `hardened-tcb-v1` record out of the hashed build input and out of receipts, specifically so the hardened cache key would reproduce `sha256:13736230…`, the value rc.5 recorded for the reserved hardened policy slot. That contradicted the task contract requiring both identities to bind cache, receipt, marker, and claim.
- FINDING: rc.5 marks that reserved input `schema_valid: false` (`conformance/v1/vectors/go-host-execution-policy.json#cache_identity.reserved_hardened`). It was only ever a demonstration that the policy slot alone does not alias — never a valid hardened build input. Treating it as a constraint was the drafting error.
- FIX: `hardened-identity-binding-v1`. The hardened build input adds exactly one closed member `{profile, tcb: {algorithm: curator-hardened-tcb-v1, content_sha256}}`. `curator-hardened-tcb-v1` is domain-separated and length-framed over CCJ-1 bytes, house style matching `curator-build-source-v1`, so the digest can never be confused with a cache key over the same bytes. Receipts v3/v4 now require the complete record; the suite proves five distinct non-aliasing keys instead of three.
- DECISION: accepted per-TCB cache-key divergence as the price. An artifact carries exactly the guarantees of the trusted code that produced it, so reusing across a supervisor update — including one fixing a containment defect — would silently transfer a guarantee claim. A hardened miss costs a compile; a wrong attribution costs the guarantee.
- FINDING: this does not reopen `decisions/0006`. That decision kept *per-operation capability evidence* out of portable cache identity so a key could not be read as capability proof. That rule still holds — `hardened-capability-evidence-v1` remains the one result-only identity. What is hashed here is identity (which trusted code, which backend, which toolchain), verifiable independently and constant within an operation.
- STATUS: resolved.

### 2358 — Two documents restating the same normative sequence will drift; parse them instead

- ROOT CAUSE: `protocol/hardened-execution.md` and `profiles/manager-hardened.md` each published an exact 15-step sequence in prose. They disagreed — the manager profile added a toolchain probe the protocol omitted and merged permit with build — and nothing detected it.
- FIX: one authoritative 17-phase table in `protocol/hardened-execution.md` § 7.2; the manager profile became a phase-keyed obligation table that publishes no ordering. `validate_phase_list_documents` parses the numbered table out of protocol § 7.2 and the phase column out of manager § 2, and fails on drift or on a reintroduced numbered list.
- FINDING: closing the drift surfaced two genuinely missing phases — `toolchain-probe-and-snapshot-freeze` (present only in the manager profile) and `build-input-and-cache-lookup` (in neither, but required once the TCB binds the key and once an exact hit must skip domain creation).
- NOTE: prose cross-checked by a parser is the cheapest anti-drift device available in a spec repo; the regex is anchored to a named section so unrelated tables cannot match.
- STATUS: resolved.

### 2358 — Dirty-worktree gates can be resolved without staging anything

- FINDING: cycle 1 reported `make regenerate-check` 2, `make regenerate-hardened-check` 2, and `release_gate.py` 1 as permanently expected-red, because all three compare against committed `57c1f56` while the whole rc.5 candidate is uncommitted and the directive forbids committing.
- FIX: copied the task worktree into a throwaway scratch git repo under `.temp/clean-probe`, committed there, and re-ran the gates. All four (`make validate`, both regenerate checks, `release_gate.py --version 1.0.0-rc.5`) exit 0. `diff -r` between probe and worktree was empty, so the evidence applies to exactly those bytes; the probe was deleted and nothing was staged in `curator-spec`.
- FINDING: the passing rc.5 release gate also answers a standing review question — adding hardened CI gates does not alter rc.5 qualification.
- NOTE: `git commit` in the probe needed `-c commit.gpgsign=false`; the repo-wide signing key prompts for a passphrase. Also: the gate reads `git status --porcelain`, so log files written into the probe made it fail until they were moved out.
- STATUS: resolved; reusable pattern for any no-commit task whose gates diff against HEAD.

### 2352 — Pinning a `$ref` is not a closure: the reference target has to be pinned too

- ROOT CAUSE: `TASK-260728-2spy93` held `buildArtifactV1`'s three properties to exact `{"$ref": ...}` values and then proved the closure behaviourally against the compiled receipts. Neither layer constrained the *referenced* definitions, so a one-keyword widening of `portablePath`, `sha256`, or `nonNegativeSafeInteger` left the `$ref` intact, left every sampled negative rejected, and left every generated positive case valid.
- FINDING: reproduced all three reviewer mutants against the shipped gate — `maxLength` 4096→4097, digest alphabet gaining uppercase, `maximum` +1. In each case the shipped `build-receipt-v2` validator rejects the corresponding artifact and the mutated one accepts it, with the positive case unaffected. A finite set of rejected samples cannot see a bound that moved by one.
- FIX: in curator-spec, `ARTIFACT_REFERENCE_TARGETS` pins the three canonical schemas keyword for keyword and `check_artifact_reference_targets` enforces them plus the gate's own table consistency (`tools/validate.py`, constants at 135 and checker at 893); decision 0008 sections 3, 11.11 and Security impact now state structural enforcement instead of claiming the behavioural proof covers it.
- FINDING: the reviewer's `maximum + 1` mutant cannot be planted on disk — `load_json`'s CCJ-1 safe-integer parse guard rejects the file before the boundary gate reads it. The same widening as `maximum` removed is caught only by the new pin, and the +1 case is exercised in memory.
- DECISION: chose structural pinning over a schema equivalence/subsumption checker — three small closed schemas, deterministic and diffable against the shipped JSON, versus a new unaudited proof surface.
- SCOPE: still exactly three files vs the accepted `TASK-260728-2kp3tv` base; rc.5 manifest and release digests byte-identical; tests 83 → 91, six of them verified red with the new checker stubbed out.
- STATUS: handed to review (cycle 5 rework).

### 2347 — A command-level Go probe must distinguish version representation from the running toolchain's TooNew gate

- REVIEW: `TASK-260728-1g0z69` cycle 5 changes requested. The contract defines Go directive acceptance as `modfile` shape plus `gover` representability, but its required probe treats any nonzero `go mod tidy` exit as semantic rejection.
- FINDING: `go 1.99.0` is accepted by `GoVersionRE` and `gover.Parse`; on both Go 1.25.1 and 1.25.5, `go mod edit -json` exits 0 while `GOTOOLCHAIN=local go mod tidy` exits 1 only because the running host is too old (`go.mod requires go >= 1.99.0`). The probe would therefore mark a valid compared value as upstream-rejected and falsely fail P1.
- REQUIRED REWORK: isolate `gover.Parse` with an exactly sourced equivalent, or classify the command's `TooNewError` as representable while preserving semantic rejection for invalid/panic cases. Add a future-release-on-older-host adversarial case on both toolchains and an expected-red regression for the old exit-code-only classifier.
- EVIDENCE: all submitted green gates independently pass (42 schemas/422 vectors, 29 Python tests, Go tests/vet/format/diff, two-toolchain 26-case probe, deterministic regeneration twice, rc.5 release gate); the accepted-predecessor directory diff remains exactly `CHANGELOG.md`, decision 0007, and the toolchain reference.
- STATUS: routed to `analysis`; verdict artifact `TASK-260728-1g0z69_review-verdict-cycle-5.md`.

### 2346 — Handle-bound deletion is insufficient when the decisive classification reopens the cache pathname

- REVIEW: `TASK-260720-1ljev5` cycle 2 changes requested. Rework cycle 1 closes the original post-validation pathname mutation, conservative two-pass cases, and Windows evidence contradiction; independent focused, race, vet, format, diff, and full-tree Go gates pass.
- P1 FINDING: `inspectUnexpected` proves an entry parent and reads its receipt through handles, but then calls `store.inspectEntry(entryPath, ...)`, which reopens the manager home and cache entry by pathname on Unix. A cache-root exchange in that window can validate a replacement entry and then retire a different entry through the original root-bound mutator. The current exchange seam fires only after classification and cannot cover this window.
- P1 FINDING: `parseConsumers` uses `encoding/json` struct decoding with `DisallowUnknownFields`, which still accepts duplicate known fields. A document with an earlier live `consumers` list and a later empty list is treated as trusted and rewritten empty, recreating the pass-one-forgets/pass-two-sweeps hazard.
- REQUIRED REWORK: make the entire receipt/structure/artifact classification operate from the already-proven entry/root object and add a swap-during-classification negative control; reject duplicate registry keys (preferably exact canonical bytes or duplicate-aware token parsing) and add a two-pass duplicate-field regression for `Collect`, `RecordConsumer`, and `StageConsumer`.
- EVIDENCE: verdict artifact `TASK-260720-1ljev5_review-verdict-cycle-2.md`; routed to `to-dev`.

### 2334 — A conservative GC pass that consumes its own uncertainty is not conservative: fail-safe has to survive the second pass

- ROOT CAUSE: `scopes.Collect` warned about an unprovable reference set and then destroyed the state that produced the warning — it rewrote `consumers.json` before checking `marked.uncertain`, and dropped any project whose markers could not be read. Pass one refused the build sweep; pass two saw a clean-looking registry and could sweep entries that were still referenced. Every single-pass test was green throughout.
- FINDING: leniency in the registry parser was the amplifier. Any JSON that was not the canonical document decoded to zero consumers, and "unknown consumers" is indistinguishable from "every project was legitimately removed" — the exact reading that authorizes pruning live checkouts and then collecting their artifacts.
- FIX: `pruneConsumers` never rewrites an untrusted registry (in `Collect` *and* `CollectRuntime`); a consumer is unregistered only once its scope is proven absent or valid-and-empty; `parseConsumers` accepts only the exact shape Curator writes (`internal/scopes/gc.go`, `internal/scopes/consumers.go`). `RecordConsumer`/`StageConsumer` fail closed rather than merging into an empty set.
- DECISION: split scope observations into blocking `uncertain` and advisory `notes`. A non-directory member (`.DS_Store`) cannot hold a marker, so treating it as uncertainty would disable build-cache GC permanently on any machine browsed in Finder. Symlinks and reparse points *can* hide a marker, so they block.
- FINDING: Go has mapped Windows junctions onto different mode bits across releases, so `ModeSymlink|ModeIrregular` alone is not a reliable reparse test; `internal/scopes/redirect_windows.go` also checks `FILE_ATTRIBUTE_REPARSE_POINT`.
- NOTE: general rule for this repo — a fail-safe branch needs a two-consecutive-pass regression. Reverting just the two pruning decisions fails 8 subtests that a single-pass suite could not see.
- SCOPE: `TASK-260720-1ljev5` rework cycle 1, worktree `.temp/TASK-260720-1ljev5/worktree`.
- STATUS: resolved, handed to review cycle 2.

### 2333 — An open directory handle proves a boundary on Windows and proves nothing on Unix: bind mutation to the object, not the pathname

- ROOT CAUSE: `buildcache.Sweep` validated the cache root with `O_NOFOLLOW` handles held open for the whole pass, then retired entries with pathname `os.CreateTemp`/`os.Rename`/`os.RemoveAll`. An open fd does not pin later pathname resolution on Unix, so exchanging the cache-root path between validation and retirement redirected both the rename and the deletion into the replacement tree — outside the proven boundary the comment claimed to guarantee.
- FIX: `sweepRoot` pairs the validated `protectedDir` with an `os.Root` opened on the same path and accepted only when `os.SameFile` says it is the same directory object; all mutation goes through `root.mutator.Rename`/`RemoveAll`, and `inspectUnexpected` asserts the candidate's parent is that same object (`internal/buildcache/collect.go`).
- FINDING: `os.Root` (Go 1.24/1.25) is the right primitive — handle-relative, no-follow, escape-refusing rename and recursive removal on both platforms — but `os.OpenRoot` still resolves its own argument by pathname, so it needs an independent identity check against a separately validated handle to close the window.
- FINDING: Windows closes it one component earlier. Handles opened without `FILE_SHARE_DELETE` make the cache root un-renamable while the sweep holds it; on the native run the adversarial swap was refused by the OS.
- NOTE: negative control is what proved the test — restoring pathname `Rename`/`RemoveAll` makes the swap test fail with "the sweep acted inside the replacement tree".
- SCOPE: `TASK-260720-1ljev5` rework cycle 1; `internal/buildcache/collect.go`, `protection_{unix,windows,unsupported}.go`.
- STATUS: resolved, handed to review cycle 2.

### 2332 — Single-run cross-platform comparison cannot distinguish a regression from a coin flip

- ANOMALY: cycle-1 Windows evidence showed `TestAtomicPublicationConflictingRace` failing on the task tree and passing on the accepted base, read as a possible regression.
- FINDING: five runs of each binary in the same non-elevated session — task tree 3 pass/2 fail, accepted base 4 pass/1 fail; `TestAtomicPublicationIdenticalRace` 1/4 and 0/5. Both tests are nondeterministic on both trees. The single-run comparison caught opposite sides of the same coin flip.
- ROOT CAUSE: identical message on both trees — `prepare protected cache root: untrusted cache provenance: ... DACL is not protected from inheritance`. `ensureProtectedBase` creates a directory and applies its protected DACL in two steps, so a concurrent publisher observes the gap. Owned by `TASK-260720-3pwg2w`; untouched by this task.
- FINDING: two Windows coverage gaps were fixture problems, not platform limits. Protected-state fixtures can be built outside `internal/buildcache` by applying an inheritance-protected owner-only DACL directly, which un-skips the `internal/scopes` end-to-end tests; directory reparse coverage does not need `SeCreateSymbolicLinkPrivilege` because a **junction** (`mklink /J`) needs no privilege at all. A *file* reparse point still does.
- NOTE: an elevated SSH session makes Windows assign `BUILTIN\Administrators` as owner of everything the process creates, so every protected-state fixture is rejected for reasons unrelated to the code. `schtasks /rl LIMITED` is the local, self-cleaning way to get a limited token.
- SCOPE: `TASK-260720-1ljev5` rework cycle 1; host `win` (Windows 10 19045).
- STATUS: resolved — contradiction explained, inherited failures kept separate from the task gate.

### 2331 — Pinning to one upstream artifact is not pinning to upstream behavior: Go accepts a go.mod version through two independent layers

- ROOT CAUSE: `golang.org/x/mod/modfile.GoVersionRE` is only the *shape* layer of the `go` directive. The go command separately requires `internal/gover.Parse` to represent the value it parsed. The two are **incomparable**: the regex accepts `1.23.4rc1` (prerelease after an explicit patch) which `gover.Parse` rejects by design (`gover.go` comment: allowing it would order opposite to `1.21 < 1.21rc1`), and `gover.Parse` accepts a bare major `1` which the regex rejects. A classifier defined by either alone admits values the toolchain cannot use.
- FINDING: measured on Go 1.25.1 and Go 1.25.5 — `go 1.23.4rc1` gives `go mod edit -json` exit 0 (file parses), then `go mod tidy` and `go list -mod=mod` exit 2 with `panic: go: internal error: missing go root module`. Read-only commands instead emit the misleading `updates to go.mod needed; to update it: go mod tidy`.
- FINDING: `go build ./...` and `toolchain default` both produce that same "updates to go.mod needed" message for unrelated reasons, so it is useless as an acceptance signal. `go mod edit -json` (shape) versus `go mod tidy` (semantics) is the clean two-layer discriminator.
- FINDING: the `toolchain` directive's semantic layer can be probed **offline** with `GOTOOLCHAIN=local+path` — a representable name yields `cannot find "X" in PATH`, an unrepresentable one yields `invalid toolchain "X" in go.mod` before any lookup. No download, no installed toolchain needed.
- DECISION: upstream acceptance is the *conjunction* of both layers at both directive positions. Values in shape-minus-semantics get a typed `build_toolchain_metadata_mismatch` at Stage B value classification — not at the file-shape gate, because the file parses and the field extracts; what rejects them is version semantics.
- DECISION: alignment with an ecosystem grammar cannot be stated as set equality when a security partition subtracts from it. Upstream accepts `go1.21.0-custom`; Curator forbids custom-distribution names. Split into P1 `C ⊆ Upstream` (no widening) and P2 `Upstream \ F ⊆ C` (no narrowing outside the forbidden partition), giving `C = Upstream \ F`. `F` is neither a subset of nor disjoint from `Upstream`, so only `C` is pinned.
- FINDING: fixture vectors cannot establish either property — a fixture's "upstream" column records what the spec *believes*, so a shared misreading yields a green suite. That is exactly how the `GoVersionRE`-only classifier survived a full review cycle. Both properties are now measured by an executable probe against real toolchains.
- NOTE: general rule for this repo — when a contract cites an upstream grammar, cite the *acceptance pipeline*, and prove it with a probe. A regex lifted from one upstream file is evidence about that file, not about the tool.
- SCOPE: `TASK-260728-1g0z69`, worktree `.temp/TASK-260728-1g0z69/curator-spec-worktree`; `decisions/0007-compiled-build-toolchain-preflight.md`, `docs/compiled-build-toolchain-requirements.md` §4.2.1–4.2.3; probe at `.temp/TASK-260728-1g0z69/probe/main.go`.
- STATUS: resolved, handed to review (rework cycle 4; all gates green, rc.5 artifacts byte-identical).

### 2321 — Object-keyword closure escape: a "closed member set" without an exact type assertion is decoration

- ROOT CAUSE: `properties`, `required`, and `additionalProperties` are JSON Schema *object* keywords — they constrain nothing when the instance is a string, number, array, or boolean. Any gate that asserts a closed member set without also asserting `"type": "object"` exactly (never omitted, never unioned with a scalar, never a boolean schema) accepts a definition that keeps every member name while admitting a package-controlled value of any shape. All shipped positive cases keep validating, so the widening is invisible to a member-name comparison.
- FINDING: this same class has now escaped four independent review cycles on `tools/validate.py` in three different places — `toolchainRequirementV1` (cycle 3), conformance claim assertions (cycle 3), and `buildArtifactV1` (cycle 4). Each time the fix was local and each time the next unguarded site was found by the reviewer, not by the gate.
- FINDING: an emptied `required` list is the same failure from the other side — the names stay available and the definition admits `{}`.
- FINDING: pinning a property to `{"$ref": "#/$defs/x"}` is satisfied by a reference to a definition that has itself been opened, so structural pinning alone is not a closure. Only asking the compiled `Draft202012Validator` what it accepts catches a widened reference target.
- FIX: `check_closed_member_set` (`tools/validate.py:686`) owns the object-type and non-boolean assertions for every caller, so new closed definitions inherit it. `check_build_artifact_closure` + `check_build_artifact_rejections` add the structural and behavioural halves for `buildArtifactV1`.
- FINDING: `validate_schemas`, `validate_manifest`, and `validate_vector_semantics` are all blind to an on-disk `buildArtifactV1` widening, and `validate_schemas` runs first in `main()`. The boundary gate is the only layer that sees it — the frozen schema-case corpus is not a backstop for this class.
- NOTE: general rule for this repo — when adding a definition to any exact member-set table, the type assertion and a compiled-validator negative are part of the closure, not polish.
- SCOPE: `TASK-260728-2spy93`, worktree `.temp/TASK-260728-2spy93/curator-spec-worktree`; `tools/validate.py`, `tools/test_validate.py`, `decisions/0008-additional-language-driver-boundary.md`.
- STATUS: resolved, handed to review (83 tests, all gates green, rc.5 digests unchanged).

### Hardened profile review — identity binding and pre-entry ordering remain unresolved

- REVIEW: `TASK-260728-1jafds` changes requested. The candidate passes its 64-test validation suite, regenerates byte-stably, preserves `conformance/v1`, `schemas/v1`, and `release/1.0.0-rc.5.json`, and closes the package-influence surface.
- FINDING: the normative profile and executable identity vector explicitly exclude `hardened-profile-v1` and `hardened-tcb-v1` from the cache identity and forbid TCB data in receipts. This conflicts with the task DoD requiring hardened profile/TCB identity binding across cache, receipt, marker, and claim state; the closed receipt schemas make the omission structural.
- FINDING: `domain-guarantee-self-test` is required to execute from inside the fresh build domain before the subsequent `domain-entry` phase, while the fixed process graph says the domain-root worker is the first process inside the domain. No actor exists that can execute the phase-6 test under the specified graph.
- FINDING: `protocol/hardened-execution.md` and `profiles/manager-hardened.md` each prescribe an exact 15-step sequence but disagree: the manager profile adds a toolchain-probe/freeze step and combines build permit with `go build`; the protocol and executable vector omit the former and split the latter.
- REQUIRED REWORK: define one executable pre-package domain-entry/self-test state machine; make both normative documents and vectors share it; bind the required hardened identities through every reusable state surface or obtain an explicit acceptance-contract revision; add negative validation for both invariants.

### 2304 — rc.5's own pin forces the hardened profile into a separate suite root

- FINDING: `conformance/v1/manifest.json` (`sha256:9ba9b8ec…`) is hashed into `release/1.0.0-rc.5.json` and consumed downstream, so any hardened vector or schema case placed under `conformance/v1` moves an accepted pin. Independently, `tools/validate.py:172` requires every schema in `schemas/v1` to have positive and negative cases under `conformance/v1`, so a hardened schema cannot live there either.
- DECISION: hardened material is a separately versioned candidate `hardened-1.0.0-rc.1` with its own suite root `conformance/hardened/v1`, `schemas/hardened/v1`, `release/hardened-1.0.0-rc.1.json`, generator, and validator. Matches EPIC-260728-2m6dqo ("separately versioned") and decision 0006's follow-up clause.
- FINDING: rc.5 preserved exactly — `conformance/v1`, `schemas/v1`, and `release/1.0.0-rc.5.json` are `diff -r` identical to the accepted predecessor *after* regenerating with `tools/generate-vectors`.
- SCOPE: `TASK-260728-1jafds`, worktree `.temp/TASK-260728-1jafds/curator-spec-worktree`.
- STATUS: resolved, handed to review.

### 2304 — rc.5 reserved the hardened cache key, so the hardened build input is constrained to one substituted value

- FINDING: `conformance/v1/vectors/go-host-execution-policy.json#cache_identity.reserved_hardened` already carries a hardened cache key `sha256:13736230…` over a concrete build input with `execution_policy: "hardened-worker-v1"`.
- DECISION: the hardened profile changes exactly that one value inside the canonical build policy object and adds, removes, and reorders nothing. That reproduces the reserved key instead of inventing one, and keeps pre-revision / portable / hardened at three non-aliasing keys. Enforced by a Go test and a Python test that both recompute it.
- DECISION: the enforcement backend, platform, and TCB record are deliberately *not* hashed into a build identity — same reasoning decision 0006 applied to portable capability evidence. Cache-key fragmentation per host adds no guarantee and invites reading a key as capability proof; the real risk (a host silently losing a capability) is answered by re-probing every operation.
- SCOPE: `TASK-260728-1jafds`.
- STATUS: resolved.

### 2304 — Aggregate resource bounds cannot be stated as "the kernel enforces it" without excluding a real implementation

- ROOT CAUSE: self-review of the first draft. Combined stdout/stderr and wall-clock bounds are naturally accounted for by the supervisor reading a pipe, not by a kernel facility. Requiring kernel accounting for all seven quantities would have made the guarantee unimplementable on every platform, including Linux.
- DECISION: `hard-aggregate-descendant-resource-bounds` admits host accounting *or* supervisor accounting, and names the one property that makes them equivalent — no domain member can evade the accounting, prevent the enforcement, or survive it. That property is supplied by `domain-membership-enforcement` plus `domain-atomic-termination`, which is exactly what the portable profile lacks (a detached descendant escapes the parent's deadline).
- FINDING: applying that lens corrected two platform declarations. macOS blocks on `domain-membership-enforcement`, `domain-atomic-termination`, and `aggregate-resource-bounds` (process group / session is escapable; no aggregate accounting), not just the last. Windows blocks on `exec-path-allowlist` *and* `aggregate-resource-bounds` (no supported facility bounds bytes written below the private build root — the same gap rc.5 records as an unavailable `per-file-size-limit`). An earlier draft also wrongly listed `active-capability-probe` as blocked on macOS; a probe that reports a class unavailable is a working probe.
- SCOPE: `TASK-260728-1jafds` — `protocol/hardened-execution.md` §5.4/§6.3, `tools/generate-hardened/main.go`.
- STATUS: resolved.

### 2304 — `regenerate-check` and `release_gate.py` are structurally red in any uncommitted candidate worktree

- FINDING: `make regenerate-check` exits 2 because its `git diff --exit-code -- conformance/v1 release/1.0.0-rc.5.json` compares against committed `57c1f56`, and the whole rc.5 candidate is uncommitted. Verified not caused by this change: the diff output is byte-identical in the read-only predecessor worktree, both exit 1. `release_gate.py` exits 1 with `release gate requires a clean candidate checkout` for the same reason.
- FINDING: `git diff` also does not see untracked files, so `git diff --exit-code -- conformance/hardened` passes vacuously. Byte stability of a new untracked suite must be proved by regenerating twice and comparing digests, not by that line.
- NOTE: applies to every producer task under this epic that is forbidden to commit, not only this one.
- SCOPE: `TASK-260728-1jafds`.
- STATUS: resolved, reported as expected-red with real exit codes.

### 2302 — Go uses two different version grammars in `go.mod`, one per directive position

- FINDING: `modfile.GoVersionRE` governs the `go` directive and requires a nonzero major plus a **required** minor (`go 1`, `go 0`, `go 1.023`, `go 1.23rc` all rejected). `modfile.ToolchainRE` (`^default$|^go1($|\.)`) governs the `toolchain` value shape, and the version part it yields is validated by `cmd/go/internal/gover.Parse`, which *accepts* a bare major (`go1` → `1.0.0`) and *rejects* a prerelease after an explicit patch (`1.21.3rc1`). Verified on host against `golang.org/x/mod@v0.38.0/modfile/rule.go:329,338` and `GOROOT/src/cmd/go/internal/gover/{toolchain,gover}.go`, not from memory.
- FINDING: consequence — one shared literal grammar for both directives is wrong in *both* directions simultaneously: too wide for `go`, too narrow for a toolchain name.
- FINDING: `modfile` reports a repeated `go` or `toolchain` statement as a **parse error**, so a repeated directive never yields a value to classify. It belongs to a file-shape gate, not to a value classifier class.
- SCOPE: `TASK-260728-1g0z69` — `docs/compiled-build-toolchain-requirements.md` §4.2.1–4.2.3 in the curator-spec worktree.
- STATUS: resolved, handed to review cycle 4.

### 2302 — "Assertion, not selector" defends selection but not deferral

- ROOT CAUSE: `TASK-260728-1g0z69` review cycle 3. The contract deliberately used a Go version grammar wider than upstream's, arguing the extra values were harmless because Stage B metadata is compared and discarded, never used to pick a toolchain. Sound for selection, unsound for ordering: a wider grammar turns a value Go itself rejects into a *permitted comparison*, so it passes the pre-compiler cross-check, reaches cache lookup and a compiler child, and fails as a compiler error after the manager has committed to the build.
- DECISION: a pre-compiler cross-check may be neither wider nor narrower than the ecosystem's own grammar at each position. Wider defers a typed rejection to the compiler; narrower fails a valid package for a grammar reason.
- STATUS: resolved.

### 2302 — Disjoint diagnostics are a property of inputs, not of outcomes or ordering
