- FINDING: three separate cycle-3 blockers on `TASK-260728-1g0z69` were the same defect — a rule written over *outcomes* (or resolved by step ordering) rather than over *inputs*, so one input satisfied two triggers and the reported code depended on which rule an implementation consulted first.
- FIX: partition by what each step reads. Stage A resolution became 3a channel map → 3b channel entry value plus holding state (no filesystem) → 3c the filesystem object that value denotes. `PATH` is not a declaration channel at all, so a toolchain installed and on `PATH` with no channel entry is `unavailable`, deterministically, on every host.
- FIX: same shape for platform applicability — the host-pair half reads only registry data and moved ahead of relpath resolution and probing, because a per-OS relpath is undefined on an unsupported host and the diagnostic was otherwise unreachable on exactly the hosts it describes.
- DECISION: once a code can fire at more than one site, "payload shape is a function of the code" becomes false rather than restrictive. Re-keyed the diagnostic union on the firing site `(code, stage, discriminant)`, which also converted the last editorial either/or in the union into a derived branch.
- SCOPE: `decisions/0007-compiled-build-toolchain-preflight.md`, `docs/compiled-build-toolchain-requirements.md`.
- STATUS: resolved, handed to review cycle 4.

### 2301 — A closed member set over a non-object closes nothing

- ROOT CAUSE: `TASK-260728-2spy93` review cycle 3. `check_closed_member_set` asserted `properties`, `required` and `additionalProperties: false` and never asserted `type`. JSON Schema object keywords do not constrain non-objects, so `toolchainRequirementV1` declared `type: string` kept the exact closed member set while accepting any package-supplied text in the reserved `toolchain` slot.
- FIX: the object type is now part of the closure itself at `tools/validate.py:638` — every closed member-set definition must declare `"type": "object"` exactly; string, omitted, and `["object","string"]` union all reject. All nine shipped closed definitions already declared it, so no wire shape moved.
- FINDING: the same failure class was latent on conformance-claim driver assertions, which never required `type: object` either. Closed there too.
- SCOPE: `decisions/0008-additional-language-driver-boundary.md`, `tools/validate.py`, `tools/test_validate.py` in the `TASK-260728-2spy93` curator-spec worktree.
- STATUS: resolved, handed to review cycle 4.

### 2301 — Draft 2020-12 `prefixItems` is a second element path, not a style choice

- ROOT CAUSE: `check_claim_driver_admission` read `items.oneOf` and stopped. Under Draft 2020-12 `items` applies only to elements `prefixItems` did not cover, so one `prefixItems` entry exempts element zero from the closed `oneOf` entirely. Verified against the compiled validator: the shipped conformance-claim-v3 schema rejects `kotlin-native-repository-v1` with 1 error; the same schema plus one `prefixItems` entry accepts it with 0.
- ROOT CAUSE: currency was decided *after* filtering out claim schemas lacking `build_drivers`, so a newest claim that dropped the member handed currency back to a frozen predecessor and asserted nothing while the gate reported the older schema as covering the admitted set.
- FIX: `check_claim_driver_container` in `tools/validate.py` closes the array to keywords that cannot reach an element (`type`, `items`, `minItems`, `maxItems`, `uniqueItems`, annotations), so `prefixItems`, `contains`, `unevaluatedItems` and any applicator added later reject as unlisted rather than being interpreted. The newest schema is selected before inspection and must declare the member.
- DECISION: allow-list the container keywords rather than deny-list the known-dangerous ones — a deny-list is only correct until the next JSON Schema applicator exists.
- FINDING: on-disk plants against shipped claim v3 never reach this check; the reserved-name absence scan or the frozen schema-case corpus rejects first. The container closure owns the case those layers cannot see — a newly minted claim version whose identifiers and negative cases are authored by the same change. Proved by unit test and by isolating the boundary gate on a planted file (cycle-2 checker exit 0, shipped checker exit 1).
- EVIDENCE: `validate.py` 0 (42 schemas / 422 files), 76 Python tests 0 (was 71), `go test` / `go vet` / `gofmt` / `git diff --check` 0, clean-checkout `make validate` + `make regenerate-check` twice + `make release-check VERSION=1.0.0-rc.5` all 0 at probe commit `86ec275`. rc.5 manifest `9ba9b8ec…` and `release/1.0.0-rc.5.json` byte-identical to the accepted base.
- STATUS: resolved, handed to review cycle 4.

### 2238 — Build-cache GC: provenance is the protected boundary, never the receipt

- MILESTONE: `TASK-260720-1ljev5` — maintenance GC extended to immutable build cache entries. New `internal/buildcache/collect.go` (`Sweep`), a shared mark phase plus `Collect` in `internal/scopes/gc.go`, `openProtectedDir`/`openProtectedChildFile` on all three protection backends, and `cmd/curator gc` now locked and recovery-first.
- DECISION: the receipt is read only to learn which logical input an entry *claims*. That input is independently re-derived into a cache key that must equal the directory name, then the existing full `inspectEntry` re-checks structure, canonical receipt, artifact hash, and size. Provenance stays with the protected boundary, so an internally consistent receipt proves nothing — the §5.6 requirement stated as code rather than as a comment.
- DECISION: `buildcache.DefaultGrace` is a documented 24h Curator constant, not a config key. The accepted contract says "the manager's documented grace period" without a number, and `internal/config` plus the pinned `internal/interop` wire shape make the config schema closed. Sweeping too eagerly costs one rebuild, never correctness; the only entry that can be unreferenced and still needed is one published by a process that crashed between publication and journal preparation.
- DECISION: removal is a rename to a private `.sweep-<name>-<random>` sibling inside the same proven root. That rename *is* the atomic removal; the `RemoveAll` behind it is resumable cleanup the next sweep finishes. Both paths are direct children of one validated root, so a deletion cannot escape the cache root even when the entry contains links.
- DECISION: runtime entries are marked from every supported marker schema; build keys only from marker v2 entries that carry them, plus in-flight journals. Required by `TASK-260720-17llva_review-verdict-cycle-2.md` — a still-current schema-1 install must not lose its runtime entry, and it contributes no compiled-artifact reference.
- FINDING: an unreadable marker, skill directory, or consumer registry makes the reference set unprovable, so the build sweep is skipped entirely and reported. Runtime collection keeps its established behaviour unchanged.
- EVIDENCE: darwin/arm64 `gofmt`, `git diff --check`, `go build`, `go vet`, `go test ./... -count=1` (38 packages), four race gates, and pinned `golangci-lint` v2.4.0 repo-wide all exit 0 with **0 issues**. `GOOS=windows go vet ./...` exits 1 on a pre-existing sibling defect (`internal/runtimestore/targets_windows_test.go:97: undefined: decodeHelperOutput`) that the untouched accepted base reproduces identically.
- SCOPE: `internal/buildcache`, `internal/scopes`, `internal/install/commit.go`, `cmd/curator/main.go`, `README.md`. 15 files, 9 of them new tests.

### 2238 — Windows protected-state tests need a non-elevated session, not a host policy change

- ROOT CAUSE: an elevated Windows session makes the OS assign `BUILTIN\Administrators` as owner of every object the process creates, so `buildcache.validateWindowsOwner` correctly rejects every fixture. Running the suite over SSH to host `win` is elevated by default, which is why `TASK-260720-1zntv0_portable-worker-results.md` recorded seven `internal/buildcache` failures all reading `owner does not match the effective user`.
- FINDING: those failures are an environment property, not a product defect. A temporary `schtasks /create ... /rl LIMITED` task running the same cross-compiled test binary non-elevated turns them green: with it, every new sweep test passes natively on Windows, and `internal/scopes` and the `cmd/curator` gc serialization tests exit 0.
- DECISION: prefer the scheduled-task recipe over setting `HKLM\...\Lsa\nodefaultadminowner`. It is local, self-cleaning, and changes no host security policy.
- FINDING: three `internal/buildcache` Windows failures survive non-elevated — `TestAtomicPublicationIdenticalRace`, `TestAtomicPublicationConflictingRace`, `TestWindowsProtectedStateMatrix/artifact_has_inherit-only_owner_allow`. The accepted base binary run in the same session fails the same tests; five repeated `-test.run TestAtomicPublication` runs exit 1 on both. Pre-existing, `TASK-260720-3pwg2w`-owned.
- NOTE: host `win` has no Go toolchain and no `git`. Test binaries must be cross-compiled on macOS; `internal/managerlock` subprocess tests then fail with `cannot run executable found relative to current directory`, and `TestCLIEndToEndInstallStatusAndTamperCheck` fails for the missing `git`. Both are harness artifacts.

### 2238 — A corrupt consumers.json wipes the consumer registry

- FINDING: `scopes.LoadConsumers` returns nil for a registry that exists but does not parse, and `CollectRuntime` then calls `ReplaceConsumers(home, nil)` — one corrupt `consumers.json` silently deregisters every project checkout.
- DECISION: left unchanged by `TASK-260720-1ljev5`. It is established runtime GC behaviour and that task requires runtime compatibility. The new `scopes.readConsumers` distinguishes absent from unreadable, and the build-cache sweep treats unreadable as an uncertainty and refuses to sweep.
- STATUS: pending — needs its own task to decide whether runtime GC should fail closed here too.

### 2237 — Driver boundary rework 02: a member set is not a contract

- ROOT CAUSE: `TASK-260728-2spy93` cycle-2 review. The boundary gate closed *which* names the three reserved wire shapes may carry and never checked what those names mean, so `toolchain: {"type":"string"}` in `buildCommandV8`, `repositoryBuildCommandV2` and `skillBuildTargetV2` passed while decision 0008 claimed the closed `toolchain-requirement-v1` object was enforced. The test helper minted exactly that shape, so the positive test certified the hole.
- FIX: placement made structural. Decision 0008 allocates one shared definition `toolchainRequirementV1`; each slot's property schema must be exactly `{"$ref":"#/$defs/toolchainRequirementV1"}` with no sibling keyword, and the definition exactly `id` + `version`, `additionalProperties` false. `tools/validate.py` gains `EXACT_PROPERTY_SCHEMAS`, `check_exact_property_schemas`, `check_toolchain_requirement_definition`; a dangling reference and a reserved shape minted without a `driver` (which would not be driver-bearing, so it would escape every table) now fail.
- FINDING: decision 0007 names the requirement object but fixes no `$defs` identifier. Naming it in 0008 is boundary ownership — "where it lands" — not a redefinition; the two members enforced are the two 0007 itself states, and the `version` grammar stays 0007's.
- DECISION: claim membership is defined over the admitted set, never over a count. Section 8 said claim schema 4 admits all eight identifiers while section 2 lets a rejected contract retire its identifiers unused — so a rejected Kotlin contract forced integration to admit a retired identifier or violate section 8. Claim schema 4 now admits exactly the admitted wire driver set at minting: eight iff all six contracts are accepted, six if both Kotlin identifiers retire, and a retired identifier is structurally unassertable.
- FIX: `check_claim_driver_admission` + `DRIVER_EXECUTION_POLICIES` (all eight identifiers, one policy each). Every claim schema asserting build drivers may assert only admitted identifiers, must pair each with its table policy, must keep assertions closed to four members with no duplicate driver; the highest claim version must assert every admitted driver, older frozen claims may assert a subset. Reachable today against shipped claim v3 and self-adjusting when integration admits a driver.
- EVIDENCE: task worktree exit 0 for `validate.py` (42 schemas / 422 vectors), 71 Python tests (was 60), `go test`, `go vet`, `gofmt -l`, `compileall`, `git diff --check`. Clean probe `c12d0d8`: `make validate`, `regenerate-check` ×2, `release-check VERSION=1.0.0-rc.5` all 0. rc.5 manifest digest `sha256:9ba9b8ec…` and `release/1.0.0-rc.5.json` unchanged. Plant-and-revert CLI probes rejected the string-typed toolchain and an extra claim-assertion member, both files restored byte-identical.
- SCOPE: `.temp/TASK-260728-2spy93/curator-spec-worktree` — still exactly `decisions/0008-additional-language-driver-boundary.md`, `tools/validate.py`, `tools/test_validate.py`.
- STATUS: handed to review cycle 3; nothing staged, committed, published or pinned; no platform or native-execution claim.

### 2233 — Toolchain contract review cycle 3: Stage-A partition and late descriptor gate remain incomplete

- REVIEW: `TASK-260728-1g0z69` changes requested and routed to `analysis`; verdict evidence is `TASK-260728-1g0z69_review-verdict-cycle-3.md`.
- FINDING: Stage A sub-step 2a asks whether a root was declared by an *admissible* origin, while 2b says a declaration from a forbidden origin is `build_toolchain_untrusted`. A PATH-only declaration therefore satisfies both the 2a `unavailable` wording and the vector-31 2b `untrusted` wording. The intended declaration-presence partition is not yet expressed by the algorithm.
- FINDING: an external descriptor requirement joins only at Stage B, but Stage B merely recomputes the intersection and then applies metadata rules. `build_toolchain_incompatible` is defined as Stage-A-only. A non-empty descriptor intersection that excludes the already-resolved host version therefore has no specified rejection before cache/compiler work.
- FINDING: platform applicability is checked after OS-specific executable-shape resolution and the probe. For a registry entry without a relpath/probe on the current unsupported host pair, an implementation can report `untrusted` or fail to construct the probe before reaching `platform_unsupported`.
- FINDING: the complete Go classifier intentionally accepts ecosystem-invalid short/zero-major values and defers their rejection to the compiler. This contradicts its implementation-ready/fail-before-compiler claim and leaves malformed file shapes such as a repeated `go` directive without a typed Stage-B outcome.
- EVIDENCE: non-mutating gates pass — validator 42 schemas/422 vectors, 29 Python tests, Go tests/vet/format, `git diff --check`, frozen conformance and rc.5 byte comparisons, and the rc.5 release gate. The task delta remains exactly decision 0007, its reference guide, and one changelog bullet.

### 2223 — Toolchain contract rework 2: overlapping diagnostics partitioned, Go directive classification made total

- ROOT CAUSE: `TASK-260728-1g0z69` cycle-2 review. Three of the four blockers were the same defect in different places — a rule stated over *outcomes* instead of over *inputs*, so two rules could both be true of one input. A wire `version` literal holding a path was simultaneously a bad literal and smuggled influence; a configured root with a missing executable was simultaneously "not resolvable" (`unavailable`) and "not a regular executable" (`untrusted`); a catalog could be read as requiring one `any` entry or one per OS.
- DECISION: partition each on a property of the *input*, never on severity or ordering. Wire surface: unknown key → existing schema rejection, malformed value → `requirement_invalid`, so `package_influence_forbidden` becomes Stage-B-only. Stage A step 2 splits into 2a declaration / 2b origin trust / 2c shape, with `unavailable` meaning nothing was declared and `untrusted` meaning something was declared and is unusable — a declared root that does not exist is `untrusted`. Guidance classes already agreed with that cut: `host` = obtain a toolchain, `configuration` = fix the configuration.
- FINDING: the "no such field may be added" half of the wire rule cannot be a runtime code at all — a field that does not exist produces no value to diagnose. It moves to a release gate over published schema property names. A runtime code for an input a closed schema cannot admit was dead text.
- DECISION: diagnostic payload shape is *derived*, not chosen — a code carries exactly what is established where it fires, and the stage steps are totally ordered, so shape is a function of the code. `effective_requirement` absent in exactly the 2 codes firing before an interval exists; `resolved_version` absent in exactly the 5 firing before normalization. Removes the sentinel/omission divergence the reviewer predicted.
- DECISION: a diagnostic payload never reproduces an unvalidated package byte. `requirement_invalid` carries a location plus a closed violation token; `requirement_unsatisfiable` carries the individually validated fragments, which are safe precisely because they validated.
- FINDING: canonicalizing Go's `1.21` and `1.21rc1` both to `(1, 21, 0)` looks lossy — Go's real order is `1.21 < 1.21rc1 < 1.21.0` — but is exact here. The orders disagree only for a comparand strictly between a language version and its release, every such value is a prerelease, and Stage A rejects a prerelease host before Stage B runs.
- DECISION: a custom Go toolchain name (`go1.23.4-bigcorp`) is `package_influence_forbidden`, not a metadata mismatch. A mismatch asserts "this claim is false about the resolved toolchain", which invites a later implementation to verify the claim by consulting the named distribution — reopening the selection path the contract exists to close. `toolchain default` is the opposite case: permitted and never honored, because it asserts exactly Curator's own unconditional behavior.
- FINDING: `go.work` carries the same `go`/`toolchain` directives but is deliberately not a metadata source — `protocol/core.md` §4.2 already fixes `GOWORK=off`, so a workspace file cannot reach the build. Adding it would create a rejection for a file the compiler never reads.
- DECISION: hybrid guidance coverage (`any` fallback plus per-OS overrides) is valid. Coverage is defined by the selection function rather than beside it, and the gate checks resolution plus **reachability** — an exact entry for an OS outside the registry, or an `any` entry shadowed everywhere, fails. Forbidding hybrid would reject catalogs that already resolve deterministically; reachability is the property actually worth gating.
- FIX: "append-only" and "retirement flips `active`" reconciled by stating both against the catalog *version*: a published version is immutable in whole, all change is an N → N+1 transition, the entry set only grows, and `active`/`superseded_by` are one-way monotone. "Exactly one active per tuple" relaxes to "at most one", since retirement without a successor is now defined for a tuple the gate no longer requires.
- NOTE: Go grammar taken from on-host primary sources, not memory — vendored `modfile.ToolchainRE = ^default$|^go1($|\.)`, `modfile.GoVersionRE`, and `cmd/go/internal/gover.FromToolchain` (suffix introduced by `-`, space or tab; path separator rejected).
- SCOPE: `.temp/TASK-260728-1g0z69/curator-spec-worktree` — still exactly `decisions/0007-compiled-build-toolchain-preflight.md`, `docs/compiled-build-toolchain-requirements.md`, one `CHANGELOG.md` bullet. Vector inventory 58 → 95 cases. `conformance/v1` and `release/1.0.0-rc.5.json` byte-identical to the accepted predecessor; clean-probe `regenerate-check` ×2 and `release-check VERSION=1.0.0-rc.5` all exit 0.
- STATUS: handed to review.

### 2210 — Additional-driver boundary rework: a second sibling execution policy, not a widened one

- DECISION: `manager-worker-v1` stays frozen and Go-only. Decision 0006 binds it to `<GOROOT>/bin/go`, to `<GOROOT>/pkg/tool/...`, and to exactly one `go list` plus one `go build`; re-reading it generically for Rust/Swift/Kotlin would change what a frozen rc.4/rc.5 label means after the fact. `TASK-260728-2spy93` mints `manager-worker-v2` for the six reserved drivers only.
- FINDING: the previous draft's rejection of `manager-worker-v2` ("it would change every `go-v1` cache key and invalidate rc.5") was false as stated. That cost belongs to *migrating Go* to v2, not to minting v2 for new drivers. Minting for new drivers alone moves no Go byte — verified: rc.5 manifest digest `sha256:9ba9b8ec...` unchanged, two clean regenerations byte-identical, `make release-check VERSION=1.0.0-rc.5` exit 0.
- DECISION: v2 is a **concurrent sibling**, not a successor — identical mandatory controls, identical `rc5-native-control-inventory-v1`, identical failure boundary. Only two things differ: the two lower graph nodes bind to the driver's fingerprinted toolchain closure, and v2 admits *at most* one graph phase where v1 requires exactly one. A reader MUST NOT rank the two by integer; the non-ordering rule is normative rather than left to naming convention.
- FINDING: minting a second policy forces `capability-evidence-v2`. The v1 record is normatively closed to `execution_policy: manager-worker-v1` **and** to one record per operation, and manifest schema 8 admits mixing Go and additional-driver commands in one operation. Resolution: same closed member set, one record per distinct active policy, result-only so no frozen byte moves.
- ANOMALY: `capability-evidence-v2` is already in the frozen rc.5 corpus and release gate as the *negative* example — `unknown-evidence-record-version-is-rejected` in `conformance/v1/vectors/go-host-execution-policy.json:266` and the "drifted capability-evidence record" case in `tools/test_release_gate.py:256`. Correct at rc.5, but it means an absence scan is the wrong gate for a reserved *version*. Non-admission is proved positively instead: the gate requires the frozen corpus to keep rejecting it. `TASK-260728-251p01` must move both literals when rc.6 mints the record, without touching rc.5 bytes or the pin.
- ROOT CAUSE: the boundary gate false-accepted optional `language` on `buildCommandV6`, arbitrary `command` on `skillBuildTargetV1`, and `runtime_files` on `buildRecordV2` because it checked a fixed deny-list of member names. A deny-list accepts everything nobody thought to forbid.
- FIX: `tools/validate.py` now holds every driver-bearing definition to a **closed exact member set** — property set, required set, and `additionalProperties: false` must match — and a driver-bearing definition *missing from the table* is itself a failure. The reserved `buildCommandV8` / `repositoryBuildCommandV2` / `skillBuildTargetV2` shapes live in the same table, so decision 0007's REQUIRED/OPTIONAL `toolchain` placement is enforced the moment those definitions are minted. All three demonstrated false accepts now reject.
- FINDING: decision 0007 §2.2 requires `toolchain` REQUIRED on both schema-8 manifest commands and OPTIONAL on the descriptor schema-2 target, while decision 0008 said the local command is exactly three members and the descriptor adds none — `TASK-260728-2jaw7h` could not have satisfied both. Reconciled by naming the three reserved definitions and fixing their exact shapes. The REQUIRED fourth member causes no rebuild: the requirement is a gate, not a build input, so a `go-v1` command moving from schema 7 to 8 produces a byte-identical canonical build input.
- SCOPE: `decisions/0008-additional-language-driver-boundary.md` (912 lines), `tools/validate.py` (+460), `tools/test_validate.py` (+491, 60 tests total) in the `TASK-260728-2spy93` worktree. Nothing under `conformance/`, `schemas/`, `release/`, `protocol/`, or `profiles/` differs from the accepted base.
- ANOMALY: putting the task venv first on `PATH` and running bare `python` does **not** select it — an interactive alias binds `python` to `/opt/homebrew/bin/python3`, which has no `jsonschema`, so `tools/validate.py` exits 1 with `ModuleNotFoundError` and reads as a gate regression. One confirmation run was taken that way, discarded, and re-run with the interpreter's absolute path. Same class of trap as the Windows `%ERRORLEVEL%` parse-time expansion already logged: always invoke the interpreter by absolute path when recording a gate exit code.
- STATUS: handed to review.

### 2150 — Toolchain review cycle 2: a total reason table is not yet a deterministic diagnostic contract

- REVIEW: `TASK-260728-1g0z69` changes requested and routed to `analysis`; evidence is `TASK-260728-1g0z69_review-verdict-cycle-2.md`.
- FINDING: the completed Go metadata entry handles only version-like `go.mod` `toolchain` values. Official Go syntax also admits `toolchain default` and named/custom toolchains, so Stage B has no defined result for valid non-version `ToolchainName` values. The Go entry cannot be called complete until that classifier and its vectors are exhaustive.
- FINDING: wire values such as a URL in a version field are simultaneously `requirement_invalid` and `package_influence_forbidden`; Stage A likewise overlaps `unavailable` and `untrusted` for an absent/non-regular configured primary executable. The contract fixes Stage-B forbidden/compared precedence but never partitions these validation and Stage-A cases.
- FINDING: requiring every diagnostic to carry an effective requirement is unrepresentable for an invalid requirement and for an unsatisfiable empty intersection. The payload needs a typed union or explicit omission semantics.
- FINDING: guidance selection prefers exact OS then `any`, while the totality gate does not decide whether exact-plus-`any` hybrid coverage is valid; retirement also mutates an old entry while the catalog is called append-only within a version. Both need explicit modes and transition vectors before the catalog owner can implement them.
- EVIDENCE: task artifacts match the worktree; delta remains exactly decision 0007, its reference, and one changelog bullet. Validation passes (42 schemas / 422 vectors, 29 Python tests, Go tests/vet/format, diff check); conformance and rc.5 release metadata remain byte-identical to the accepted predecessor.

### 2148 — The artifact class, not the language, is what closes the additional-driver boundary — and it is what makes Kotlin/JVM inadmissible

- DECISION: `TASK-260728-2spy93`. Next surface is `1.0.0-rc.6`: manifest schema 8, descriptor `skill-build.json` schema 2, receipt schema 3 (local) / 4 (external), marker schema 4, claim schema 4. Six closed identities reserved — `rust-v1`, `rust-repository-v1`, `swift-v1`, `swift-repository-v1`, `kotlin-native-v1`, `kotlin-native-repository-v1`. Reservation is explicitly **not** admission; a rejected contract retires its identifier unused rather than freeing it for reuse.
- DECISION: exactly one artifact class, `native-executable-v1` — one bounded regular file, manager-named `bin/<command>`. `runtime-bundle` rejected. The load-bearing reason is not taste: receipts, markers, shims, currentness and GC all key on **one** artifact path and **one** digest, so a bundle redefines five identities at once; and a manager-generated launcher would be manager-authored executable content derived from package data (main class, classpath), reopening exactly the install-time execution surface decisions 0004/0005 exist to close.
- FINDING: the currentness argument cuts harder than the identity one. A bundle plus an unfingerprintable external runtime can be reported **current** by a marker while the JRE it needs is gone. A single self-contained executable is fully described by a digest the manager itself computed.
- FINDING: the three candidate languages are not Go-shaped. `cargo build` runs `build.rs` and expands proc macros; `Package.swift` is a Swift **program** SwiftPM compiles and runs; Kotlin's mainstream path is Gradle, a general-purpose script engine. `SECURITY.md:66-72` already forbids a manager to invoke all three. So the question was never "how do we accommodate them" — it is "can each driver be defined without them at all", and a surface that cannot be rejected deterministically **before** the compile phase disqualifies its driver.
- DECISION: keep `manager-worker-v1`; do not mint `manager-worker-v2`. The two lower graph nodes were always per-operation values bound by the toolchain identity inside the build input, never constants of the policy, so restating them generically changes no `go-v1` byte. Minting a new identity would change every `go-v1` cache key and invalidate the frozen rc.5 candidate for zero security gain. Verified: regeneration byte-identical, pin still `sha256:9ba9b8ec…`.
- DECISION: the local command object stays exactly `{type, driver, source_dir}` and the descriptor target exactly `{driver, build_root, source_dir}` — no driver may add a member. Consequence accepted deliberately: a driver that cannot map `source_dir` to exactly one program without a new package-controlled field is **rejected**, not accommodated. The consuming command key is the sole naming authority.
- FINDING: Swift-on-Windows and Kotlin/JVM both fail the artifact class for the same reason (published sidecar runtime), and both are now a platform/driver exclusion with a typed diagnostic (`build_artifact_class_unsupported`) rather than a sidecar. That is the honest outcome, and it is `TASK-260728-1yhuqi` / `TASK-260728-168smo` to resolve inside the class.
- FIX: `tools/validate.py` `validate_additional_driver_boundary()` (+216) — reserved identifiers are banned from every surface outside `decisions/`; `driver` must be a `const` in every driver-bearing def; `buildArtifactV1` must stay `{path, sha256, size}` with no bundle member; reserved schema slots must stay unallocated; and 66 assertions prove against the compiled validators that v6/v7 manifests, descriptor v1, receipts v1/v2, markers v2/v3 and claim v3 each reject each reserved id. Names are assembled from family + suffix so the guard never matches its own source. 15 new tests (29 → 44).
- ANOMALY: numbering collision — `TASK-260728-1g0z69` had already taken `decisions/0007` in its own worktree. Independent worktrees hide this until merge. Renumbered to 0008; four references moved. Parallel spec tasks should claim decision numbers on the board, not in a worktree.
- SCOPE: `curator-spec` @ `57c1f56` + rc.5 candidate. Exactly three files: `decisions/0008-additional-language-driver-boundary.md` (new), `tools/validate.py`, `tools/test_validate.py`. No schema, vector, generated byte, release file or pin touched.
- STATUS: handed to review. Wire landing is `TASK-260728-251p01`; qualification is `TASK-260728-2bu2q6`.

### 2138 — A version constraint cannot carry a tested-release allowlist: ordering is not evidence of testing

- ROOT CAUSE: `TASK-260728-1g0z69` rework 1. The cycle-0 `go` registry entry declared only `baseline at_least 1.23.0`, which silently weakened the released `profiles/manager.md` §2.2 rule. That rule rejects "an otherwise unknown release, including a newer release merely ordered after a known one" — but an untested Go 1.99.0 satisfies `at_least 1.23.0`. An interval gate expresses *order*; the allowlist expresses *evidence of testing*. Neither can substitute for the other.
- FIX: REQUIRED registry field `compatibility` — a closed manager-owned set of tested release families, matched by **exact set membership, never ordering**. `go` = `{(1, 23)}`. Independent of the requirement: a constraint can neither widen nor narrow it, and nothing on the wire surface can reach it. New twelfth diagnostic `build_toolchain_untested_release`, carrying the admitted family set (manager data, never package data).
- DECISION: fixed Stage A precedence — effective requirement (step 6) before compatibility (step 7). Go 1.22.0 reports `incompatible`; Go 1.99.0 passes step 6 and reports `untested_release`. Both gates stay reachable and the reported code is deterministic. Because a conforming manager MAY admit more families than another, the set is a gate, not a build input, so vectors declare it as fixture input.
- FINDING: "is a channel forbidden or compared?" was unanswerable because the question conflated two surfaces. On the **manager-defined wire surface** Curator owns a closed field set, so no channel field exists to compare and any such value is forbidden. On the **source-ecosystem metadata surface** Curator does not own the field set, so each entry declares a closed disposition table (`forbidden` / `compared` / `ignored`). `forbidden` is reserved for values naming *where* a toolchain comes from; a channel names *which version*, so it is `compared` — and refusing to honor it is precisely what makes it harmless.
- DECISION: forbidden-disposition fields evaluate before compared ones, in Unicode-scalar order of source path then field path. So `rust-toolchain.toml` carrying both `path` and `channel = "nightly"` is always `package_influence_forbidden`, never `metadata_mismatch`. Relaxed one rule deliberately: `channel = "stable"` is now permitted and never honored rather than a mismatch — it asserts exactly the release-only property v1 already enforces, so rejecting it would fail nearly every real Rust package while proving nothing.
- FINDING: a `(toolchain, reason, platform)` guidance ID cannot name a second entry, so the "new identifier on a change of meaning" lifecycle rule was unimplementable — `superseded_by` had no distinct target. Fixed with an immutable revision component `toolchain.<id>.<reason>.<platform>.r<N>`, exactly one active entry per tuple, and `superseded_by` required to name a strictly greater revision of the same tuple. Selection still resolves the tuple, not the identifier.
- FIX: guidance totality made mechanical — `reason` is exactly the diagnostic code suffix, so the code-to-reason mapping is the identity and stays total as codes are added. Five previously uncovered reasons (`requirement_invalid`, `requirement_unsatisfiable`, `version_undetermined`, `package_influence_forbidden`, `changed`) now resolve. New `guidance_class` (`host` / `configuration` / `authoring`) fixes the admissible `primary_source` per class, because the original "the language's own official origin" rule could not apply to an authoring error.
- ANOMALY: "cache hit with an incompatible toolchain gets rebuilt" was a self-contradiction against fail-fast. Stage A precedes cache lookup, so that hit is unreachable. Split into two cases: an incompatible current toolchain fails at Stage A with no lookup, no candidate, no rebuild, no mutation; a *different cached toolchain identity* under a compatible current toolchain is an ordinary key miss and rebuild.
- SCOPE: `curator-spec` rc.5 candidate, task worktree `.temp/TASK-260728-1g0z69/curator-spec-worktree`. Still exactly three files: `decisions/0007-compiled-build-toolchain-preflight.md`, `docs/compiled-build-toolchain-requirements.md`, one `CHANGELOG.md` bullet. Vector inventory 40 → 58 cases. `conformance/v1` and `release/1.0.0-rc.5.json` byte-identical to the accepted predecessor after regeneration.
- STATUS: handed back to review. Normative landing remains `TASK-260728-2jaw7h`.

### 2116 — Toolchain version constraints are safe only because they filter; every language ships a selector that must be demoted to an assertion

- FINDING: `TASK-260728-1g0z69`. The three pending compiled drivers each ship a package-controlled path from source bytes to "which executable compiles this": `rust-toolchain.toml` names a channel and can name a `path`, `swift-tools-version` / `.swift-version` select a compiler, the Gradle wrapper downloads its own distribution, and Go's `GOTOOLCHAIN` fetches and switches on demand. Ecosystem-normal, and each one is the exact boundary decisions 0004/0006 exist to hold.
- DECISION: admit a **version constraint**, reject every **selector**. The load-bearing property is that a constraint can only *filter* the manager-trusted set — it can never add a candidate. Declared metadata is retained as a cross-check assertion (better diagnostic than a compiler error) and discarded as a selector. `rust-toolchain.toml` `path` is a hard package-influence rejection.
- DECISION: prereleases satisfy nothing and cannot appear in a requirement. Cross-language determinism dies at prerelease ordering — Go writes `1.24rc1` with no separator, Rust uses semver `-nightly`, Kotlin `-Beta1`, Swift dated snapshots. No shared order exists, so reject rather than rank. Comparison happens only over a canonical `(major, minor, patch)` triple normalized per-toolchain at the entry boundary.
- FINDING: the ordering fix costs nothing for Go. Stage A reuses the three package-independent bootstrap probes `protocol/core.md` §4.2 already runs from the manager parent (`go telemetry off`, `go version`, `go env -json …`), and `go version` stdout is already the `V` record of `curator-go-toolchain-v1`. The five Go argument-vector forms stay five; `manager-worker-v1` is untouched. Only the phase changes.
- FINDING: current `profiles/manager.md` §2.1 resolves the toolchain in phase 9 and §11.6 acquires the whole external repository before touching a compiler — so a host with no Rust clones, proves, hashes and audits an external repo before discovering it can never build it. Moving availability/version to phase 4 is gate-neutral: it consumes only manager/operator config plus one already-validated manifest field.
- DECISION: the effective requirement is a **gate, not a build input** — same reasoning that kept host capability evidence out of the key in decision 0006. No bypass exists because Stage A gates before cache lookup. Consequence: zero frozen artifacts change (`curator-go-toolchain-v1`, receipts v1/v2, markers v2/v3, rc.4 digests, every published cache key), and adopting the contract causes no rebuild.
- ANOMALY: one unavoidable asymmetry — a descriptor-declared requirement is unreadable at Stage A because the descriptor arrives *with* the repository. Stage A gates on baseline ∩ manifest; the descriptor requirement joins at Stage B. That is precisely why the consuming-manifest requirement is REQUIRED in the next schema: the cheap gate must always have something to evaluate.
- NOTE: `make regenerate-check` cannot pass inside a task worktree carrying the uncommitted rc.5 candidate — it runs `git diff --exit-code` against `57c1f56`. Prove determinism instead by diffing regenerated `conformance/v1` against the accepted predecessor and by running the gate twice in a clean `git init` probe.
- SCOPE: `curator-spec` @ `57c1f56` + rc.5 candidate. Two new files (`decisions/0007-compiled-build-toolchain-preflight.md`, `docs/compiled-build-toolchain-requirements.md`) plus one CHANGELOG bullet. No schema, vector, or generated byte changed.
- STATUS: handed to review. Normative landing is `TASK-260728-2jaw7h`; schema version numbers are `TASK-260728-2spy93`.

### 2049 — `curator-build-source-v1` is a digest algorithm, not the descriptor — it must survive the `skill-build.json` rename

- FINDING: `TASK-260728-2kp3tv`. The `curator-build` stem names **two unrelated things** in the rc.5 candidate. `curator-build.json` is the schema-7 repository-root descriptor (unreleased, renameable). `curator-build-source-v1` is the schema-6 **build-source snapshot digest algorithm** — the domain-separation prefix hashed over the frozen external snapshot, defined at `schemas/v1/common.schema.json:303` and referenced 113 times.
- FINDING: the digest algorithm is bound into rc.4 byte-frozen artifacts. `conformance/v1/schema-cases/install-marker-v2/valid.json` carries it and is digest-pinned at `538d12bb89d2...` by `TestRC4CompiledArtifactsRemainByteFrozen` (`tools/generate-vectors/main_test.go`). A naive `s/curator-build/skill-build/g` breaks the schemas-1-6 byte freeze and fails that guard.
- ANOMALY: negative fixtures mutate the algorithm's **version suffix** — `curator-build-source-v2` appears in `build-receipt-v1/invalid-build-source-algorithm.json` and `install-marker-v2/invalid-build-source-algorithm.json`. An exclusion written against the exact `-source-v1` string is not enough; the exclusion must cover the whole `-source` namespace. First wiring of the absence guard went red on exactly this.
- DECISION: renamed the descriptor family only (`curator-build.json`, `curator-build-v1{,.schema.json}`, `curatorBuildTargetV1`, two generator helpers); left the digest-algorithm namespace untouched. All nine rc.4 frozen digests re-verified unchanged.
- FIX: three guards now encode the distinction explicitly rather than incidentally — `validate_repository_descriptor_identity` (`tools/validate.py`), `validate_repository_descriptor` (`tools/release_gate.py`), `TestRepositoryDescriptorIsManagerNeutral` (`tools/generate-vectors/main_test.go`). Each assembles the retired stem from parts (`"curator" + "-build"`) so it can scan its own source without matching itself, and each asserts the `-source` namespace is *still present* — over-reach fails as loudly as under-reach.
- FINDING: the descriptor path is part of the external build input, so the rename is a real cache-identity revision, not cosmetic. External `go-repository-v1` cache key `07dd911a…` → `4abc903b…`, external receipt hash `11d2bf4d…` → `0f8f910a…`. Local `go-v1` carries no descriptor and is byte-identical (`1a887eb6…`), which is what scopes the change to the external driver.
- SCOPE: `curator-spec` @ `57c1f56` + rc.5 candidate; 71 files, one schema file and one schema-case directory renamed. New candidate pin `sha256:9ba9b8ec…`.
- STATUS: handed to review.

The predecessor worktree on disk pins `sha256:58f8d229…` in both `candidate_protocol_pin.manifest_sha256` and `downstream_consumption.required_manifest_sha256`, and `shasum -a 256 conformance/v1/manifest.json` confirms `58f8d229…`.

- FINDING: the on-disk predecessor state is internally consistent; only the summary artifact is stale. Baselines taken from that worktree are sound.

### 2010 — An optimistic generation must identify the bytes that were parsed, not the path they came from

- ROOT CAUSE: `TASK-260720-2284br` R5. All four declaration observations digested the path, read and parsed the path as a *separate* operation, then compared a second path digest under the home lock. That catches A → B but not A → B → A around the read: the closure is resolved from the transient B while both samples are A, so `recheck` reports nothing and the run commits context, shims, adapters and consumer state for a declaration set that exists in **neither** the recorded nor the current state.
- FINDING: the window is wide open on this codebase because every supported writer rewrites its manifest **in place** with `os.WriteFile` — `manifest.writeJSON:355` (`curator add|remove`, `curator global add|remove`), `scopes.AddHybridDecl:185` / `RemoveHybridDecl` (`curator hybrid add|rm`), and hand edits of `Skillfile.dev.json`. Truncate-and-rewrite is visible to a reader that already holds the file open, so a parse genuinely consumes B.
- DECISION: rejected the reviewer's alternative before/read/after path-sampling protocol. before=A, after=A, parsed=B *is* the ABA case, so no number of path samples closes it. Only binding the generation to the parsed bytes does.
- FIX: `internal/install/generation.go` — `readDocument` opens once, reads from that handle, and returns the payload together with the generation of exactly those bytes; the parser is handed the returned payload. Byte-level entry points added to the owning packages so no loader reads a path twice: `manifest.ParseBytes`, `devsub.ParseBytes`, `scopes.ParseHybridDecls`, each the function its own `Load` now delegates to. Call sites `internal/install/install.go:180,220,257` and `internal/install/global.go:81`.
- FIX: the invariant is structural, not conventional. `observations` is one record per key (generation + path + reader) instead of parallel maps, `observation.current()` is the single recheck door and re-reads through the reader that recorded the entry, and the four declaration keys have their own `documentKey` type — so a document can only enter through `observeDocument`, which takes a generation, and cannot be passed to `observe`, which takes a path. Reintroducing the ABA-prone form is now a compile error.
- FINDING: a torn read is safe by construction. Whatever bytes came back are what the parse saw and what the generation covers, so the settled file differs and the run restarts; it cannot commit.
- FINDING: two deliberate behaviour changes fall out of digesting bytes instead of a path. A `chmod` no longer restarts a run (mode selects nothing installed). A **symlinked** manifest is now revalidated by content — `transaction.DigestPath` refused a link and pinned the observation to an `unreadable:` marker that compared equal to itself forever, i.e. a symlinked manifest was previously not revalidated at all.
- NOTE: the fix deletes the window it closes, so no permanent install-level test can hook "between the digest and the read". The invariant is locked at the read instead, with a one-shot `afterDocumentOpen` seam (nil in production, same convention as `godriver.controlSeamFault`), and the negative control runs **both** orderings: digest-before-read fails all four install-level ABA cases *by committing the transient declaration*, digest-after-read fails the rename binding case. The byte-identical-rewrite control passes in every variant, so the cases are not merely restart-happy.
- SCOPE: `internal/install/{generation,commit,install,global}.go`, `internal/manifest/manifest.go`, `internal/devsub/devsub.go`, `internal/scopes/hybrid.go`; new `internal/install/{generation,aba}_test.go` (10 cases).
- STATUS: handed to review, cycle 5. Repo-wide lint 0, all suites 0, zero data races; `internal/godriver` again red only on its contention-sensitive 15s probe cap (exit 0 isolated). Darwin/arm64 only.

### 1913 — Rework 3 gates re-run first-hand; godriver's 15s probe cap is contention-sensitive

- EVIDENCE: `TASK-260720-2284br` cycle-3 final-tree gates, re-run rather than inherited because the session that wrote the code was killed mid-gate-run. gofmt 0, `git diff --check` 0, `go build ./...` 0, `go vet ./...` 0, `test-install` 0 (369s), `test-atomicity` 0 (388s), `test-rest` 0 across the remaining 37 packages, four race gates 0 (revalidation 119s, activation 95s, concurrency 61s, staging+transaction 52s), pinned `golangci-lint` v2.4.0 repo-wide **`0 issues`**. Both reviewer overlay repros — cycle-3 project/global manifests and cycle-2 hybrid — pass unmodified.
- ANOMALY: `internal/godriver` exited **1** in the driver run and again on an isolated re-run (304s, 11 failures), every one `process_timeout` at exactly ~15.02s with no assertion failure. `-parallel 1` → exit 0 in 26s; default parallelism after the machine quieted → exit 0 in 25s.
- ROOT CAUSE: `defaultProbeTimeout = 15s` at `internal/godriver/session.go:34` is a **hard wall-clock cap** — `session.go:136-137` clamps any larger caller value back down to it. Concurrent probe subprocesses competing for CPU blow it. The machine was carrying ~400% of unrelated CPU (`gramdrive-agent` 99%, `suggestd` 98%, `iTerm2` 74%, `fileproviderd` 61%), load average 9-12.
- DECISION: not patched here. `internal/godriver` is sibling-owned (`TASK-260720-6i3cya`), untouched by this task (newest file 08:01 vs this cycle's 18:14-18:15 edits), and raising a security-relevant probe bound to make a test pass would be a forced fit. Reported red, diagnosed, and flagged for its owner. `go test ./...` on a loaded machine can fail this package.
- FINDING: `golangci-lint -v` reports `Issues before processing: 79, after processing: 0`, which looks like gate-gaming and is not. `.golangci.yml` is byte-identical to base `17804ce` (dated Jul 21, predating this story); its gosec excludes (G306, G301, G122, G703, gosec-off-in-tests) and the 104 per-site-justified `#nosec` directives are pre-existing. Cache-cleared re-run still `0 issues`. Worth re-checking this way whenever a lint gate reports 0 in ~1s.
- NOTE: negative control re-verified as an honest minimal diff — `diff` against the real sources is exactly three deleted `observed.observe` lines, nothing else; five mutation cases fail without them, control still passes.
- STATUS: handed to review, cycle 4. Darwin/arm64 only.

### 1820 — Rework 3: closure inputs must be classified by their writers, not by their directory

- ROOT CAUSE: `TASK-260720-2284br` R4. The closure-input audit in `internal/install/commit.go` reasoned from location — "the project `Skillfile.json` is in the locked checkout", "the global `Skillfile.json` is under `GlobalRoot(home)`, the global scope's own operation identity". Both are true and both prove nothing: `curator add/remove` (`cmd/curator/main.go:432,460`) and `curator global add/remove` (`872,883`) call `manifest.AddDecl`/`RemoveDecl` directly and take **no** lock. A lock is a witness only against writers that take it.
- FINDING: the same re-audit surfaced a third input in the same class that no earlier cycle had named — `Skillfile.dev.json` has *no* Curator writer at all, so nothing serializes it against a run, and a substitution redirects a declaration at a local checkout while also deciding the strict-audit refusal.
- FIX: `manifest/project`, `manifest/global` and `substitutions/project` join the optimistic observation set, digest-before-read, at `internal/install/install.go:177,217` and `internal/install/global.go:79`. No control flow changed — `recheck` already routes any generation difference to `restartClosure` before cache publication and target staging.
- FIX: `observations` now owns the path to re-read each key (`internal/install/commit.go:301-334`); the parallel `generationPaths` map and `commitRequest.generationPaths` are gone. "Every observed key must also be registered in the paths map or it is silently never rechecked" was a convention; it is now structural.
- FINDING: a `path` dev substitution does **not** escape the write-once snapshot property — `closure.resolveNode` (`internal/closure/closure.go:263-273`) resolves the local checkout to a concrete HEAD commit and snapshots under it, so only *which* checkout is pointed at needs observing.
- DECISION: `.gitignore` is deliberately left unobserved and no lock is claimed over it. It is a hygiene gate, not a closure input, and this operation rewrites it itself under `--fix-gitignore`; observing a file the run writes would manufacture restart loops. Audit trust state, registry attestations and MCP results are likewise documented as gates or pinned evidence rather than defended with a lock that does not exist.
- SCOPE: `internal/install/{install,global,commit}.go`; new `internal/install/revalidation_test.go` (6 cases, mutations driven through the real `manifest.AddDecl`/`RemoveDecl` writers).
- NOTE: the regressions went into `internal/install`, not `internal/install/atomicity` — that package already runs ~411s plain against the 600s default timeout, which the cycle-2 reviewer flagged.

### 1818 — Atomic-install review cycle 3 found project/global closure inputs outside the claimed lock invariant

- REVIEW: `TASK-260720-2284br` changes requested. The cycle-2 hybrid observation closes its narrow R3 reproduction, and focused `transaction`/`staging`/`adapters` tests confirm R1/R2 remain closed.
- FINDING: the closure-input audit in `internal/install/commit.go` claims project and global manifests are stabilized by canonical operation locks, but the public writers bypass those locks: `curator add/remove` calls `manifest.AddDecl`/`RemoveDecl` directly at `cmd/curator/main.go:432,460`; `curator global add/remove` does the same at lines `872,883`.
- REPRODUCTION: read-only Go overlay tests remove `skill-b` in `Options.OnStaged`. Both project and global installs return `ok`, emit no closure restart, and commit stale `skill-b`. Scratch: `.temp/TASK-260720-2284br/review/stale_global_manifest_test.go` and `global-overlay.json`.
- REQUIRED: observe/recheck the exact project/global manifest generations or make every supported writer acquire the same canonical operation lock; route mismatch to `restartClosure`; add permanent removal plus added/retargeted regressions; audit closure inputs against their actual writers rather than directory location.

### 1745 — Rework 2: hybrid activation is now an optimistic observation; digest-before-read is the safe order

- ROOT CAUSE: `TASK-260720-2284br` R3. `projectAttempt` merges machine-level hybrid declarations into the effective closure at step 5, but the optimistic observation set was not built until step 16 and only ever held installed marker generations and cache outcomes. `curator hybrid add|rm` call `scopes.AddHybridDecl`/`RemoveHybridDecl` straight from `cmd/curator/main.go:985,994` under **no** project, key, or home lock, so the manifest could move at any point between closure resolution and commit and the run would commit the closure it planned against.
- FIX: the observation set and its path map now start at step 5, and `scopes.HybridManifestPath(cfg.Home())` is observed under key `activation/hybrid-manifest`. No control flow changed — `recheck` already maps any generation difference to `restartClosure`, and `runCommit` already calls it after journal recovery and before both cache publication and target staging.
- DECISION: **digest, then read** — not read, then digest. A writer that races the read then leaves a recorded digest that no longer matches the file, so the recheck restarts. The reverse order records the writer's bytes while the closure came from the bytes actually read, which is a stale commit the recheck cannot see. Cost is a bounded spurious restart, which is always the safe direction.
- FINDING: closure-input audit completed and recorded in the `observations` doc comment. Seven inputs are either observed or provably stable under the canonical project operation lock / commit-addressed snapshots. The eighth — user configuration — is documented as a **boundary, not a freshness proof**: observing it would only produce a restart loop, since a restart re-reads no configuration. Per-attempt reloading is a separate decision.
- EVIDENCE: negative control run — with the single `observe` line removed, all three mutation regressions fail and the control still passes; restored and re-verified. The reviewer's own overlay repro passes unmodified.
- ANOMALY: `setsid` does not exist on macOS, so detached gate runners silently produce nothing. Long gate shells were also killed at exit 144. Fix that stuck: bounded foreground commands plus a durable `<name>.exit` file written as the runner's last action, so a missing `.exit` reads as "killed", never as "passed".
- FINDING: `gates-cycle2/gate-test-full.log` was green but finished at 17:02:45 while `commit.go` was last touched at 17:15:33 — stale by 13 minutes. Mtime-vs-log-time comparison is worth doing before inheriting any prior suite result.
- EVIDENCE: gofmt 0, `git diff --check` 0, `go build ./...` 0, `go vet ./...` 0, all 40 packages green across three recorded chunks (atomicity 411.3s, install 236.0s, other 38 ok), three bounded race gates 0 (activation 427.0s, restart/concurrency 56.3s, staging/transaction), pinned `golangci-lint` v2.4.0 repo-wide **`0 issues`**.
- NOTE: `internal/install/atomicity` is at 411.3s plain against the default 600s per-package timeout, and 427.0s for the four activation cases alone under `-race`. The next case added there needs a split decision, not another four installs.
- STATUS: handed to review, cycle 3. Darwin/arm64 only; hardened containment `STORY-260728-327soo` stays separate and non-gating.

Cycle-1 R1/R2 are closed: `KindEntry` journals default/explicit symlink mirrors and restores exact link destinations; stale adapter entries are class-80 removals before class-90 consumer state; the project+hybrid and global class sweeps cover their required target surfaces.

The commit comment claims stale activation is revalidated, but the hybrid activation manifest is absent from `generationPaths`.

The install returns `ok`, emits no restart, and commits the stale hybrid context. Log: `.temp/TASK-260720-2284br/review/stale-activation.log`; scratch test: `.temp/TASK-260720-2284br/review/stale_activation_test.go`.

Pinned `golangci-lint` v2.4.0 reports `0 issues` repo-wide; the six lint-expanded packages pass their tests. Submitted archive records full `go test ./...` and the broad race run at exit 0.

### 1621 — Rework 1: symlink mirrors moved into the journal; acceptance suite split into its own package
- ROOT CAUSE: `TASK-260720-2284br` cycle-1 review rejected the "journal the ledger, not the entry" decision from entry 1253. The real cause was one layer down: `internal/transaction` refused a symbolic link in every role, so the production default `adapter_mode: auto` had no journaled way to publish a mirror entry. Consequences the reviewer traced: a link failure between `Prepare` and `Commit` returns `failed` while leaving a **prepared** journal that recovery later commits; a rollback cannot restore a mirror `Mirror.Link` already replaced; stale pruning ran after the consumer ledger.
- DECISION: reversed entry 1253. `internal/transaction` gained an explicit second target kind — `KindBytes` (unchanged contract, links refused, path fully resolved) and `KindEntry` (a managed directory entry that may be a link; digested by its **destination string** with kind `'l'` and mode 0, leaf-preserving namespace resolution, identity read with `Lstat`). Additive: `kind`/`link_target` are `omitempty`, existing digests unchanged, links inside trees still refused everywhere. Every accepted transaction test passes untouched, including the two that assert `DigestPath`/`copyTarget` reject links.
- FIX: `internal/adapters/stage.go` — `Mirror.Link`/`Mirror.PruneStale` deleted. Mirror entries are `ReplaceEntry` targets in class 60, stale entries are `RemoveEntry` targets in class 80, both before the consumer ledger at 90. An entry that is already the exact link produces no target at all. `runCommit` now runs `Prepare` immediately followed by `Commit`, so no ordinary post-prepare failure path can leave a journal behind. Removed the superseded direct-mutation API (`RefreshProject`, `RefreshGlobal`, `refresh`, `refreshEntry`, `symlinkRel`, `removePath`, `writeLedger`) — it had no production caller and was the only non-atomic mirroring path.
- FINDING: the old fault sweep could not fail a **removal** class at all. It injected at `PointBeforeInstall`, which the engine skips when the desired state is already reached — true for every removal once its preimage is moved aside. `PointAfterBackup` is emitted once per target and is the correct universal injection point.
- FINDING: `auto` mode probed symlink support in the **temp stage root**, not where the link is actually created. The transaction sidecar and the published entry both live in the adapter root, so the probe now covers both filesystems.
- FINDING: `existingNamespaceAncestor` returned a dangling symbolic link, and `pathconf` on it answers `ENOENT`. Exposed, not introduced, by the new kind. Now skips links.
- DECISION: the acceptance suite moved to `internal/install/atomicity` (`doc.go` + tests only, exported API only). Every case drives a complete real installation, and the combined runtime pushed `internal/install` past Go's default 10m per-package timeout — `go test ./...`, the exact CI command, failed at 739s with a timeout panic and no assertion failure. After the split: `internal/install` 212.7s, `internal/install/atomicity` 360.1s, both green at default timeouts. CI runners are slower and the atomicity package uses ~60% of its budget here; that is the number to watch.
- EVIDENCE: `gofmt -l` 0 files, `go build ./...` 0, `go vet ./...` 0, `go test ./... -count=1` **0**, `go test -race -timeout 45m` over install/atomicity/transaction/managerlock/staging/adapters **0** with zero data races. Fault injection passes for all 7 project+hybrid classes and all 5 global classes in `auto` mode, plus `auto`/`symlink`/`copy` adapter cases. `golangci-lint` task-scope (now including `transaction`) 0 issues; repo-wide still 1 with 45 issues, byte-identical per-linter counts to the pre-change base — zero new.

### 1253 — Atomic cross-scope commit landed; adapter symlinks are the one non-journaled class
- MILESTONE: `TASK-260720-2284br` — install phases 20–23 (consumer-first, piecemeal materialization, cleanup, adapters, GC) replaced by one manager-home-locked transaction over staged targets, consumer ledger last. New `internal/staging`, `internal/install/{commit,targets}.go`; staging producers in `envfiles`, `adapters`, `globalbins`, `scopes`, `runtimestore`.
- FINDING: the four direct blockers had each landed in a **separate uncommitted worktree**, never in one tree. Union of `.temp/TASK-260720-1zntv0/worktree` (godriver + 3itlly staging) and `.temp/TASK-260720-31nl14/worktree` (transaction + managerlock + marker v2). 128 shared files, 10 divergent — every divergence was one chain at base while the other advanced, so the union is a per-file superset with no content merge. Built twice into independent worktrees and compared by SHA-256 manifest to prove the composition.
- DECISION: `internal/transaction` refuses links as targets and inside trees (`digest.go:33`, `files.go:23`), with an accepted test asserting it (`validation_test.go:736`). `adapters` defaults to `adapter_mode: auto` = symlink-first. Contract §6.1 step 12 names **"adapter ledgers and hybrid/global mirrors"** as the class — not each entry — so ledgers and copy-mode entries are journaled; symlink entries are created after `Prepare`/before `Commit` and pruned only after success. A rollback therefore never deletes a live mirror, and a leftover entry stays recognizably ours via `unmanagedConflict`. Did **not** extend `transaction` — out of scope, and the contract does not require it.
- ROOT CAUSE: rollback left state non-identical to the prior one. `journalPlan` ran `os.MkdirAll` for target parents (e.g. `<home>/runtime/<skill>/`); those are not journaled targets, so an aborted commit left them behind. Caught by the per-target rollback sweep.
- FIX: `makeMissingDirectories`/`removeCreatedDirectories` (`internal/install/commit.go`) record exactly the directories the commit created and drop them deepest-first, only while still empty, unless the commit succeeded.
- DECISION: `CachePublisher` now requires `Inspect` as well as `Publish`, and `CommitDeps.resolve` defaults the publisher to the planning inspector when it can publish. Two separately constructed `buildcache.Store`s over one home could otherwise disagree about whether the entry a launcher points at exists — which is exactly how the first integration run failed.
- FINDING: `managerlock`'s `processState.reserve` rejects a home acquisition while any project acquisition is pending **in the same process**, so two concurrent installs cannot share a process. The two-consumer concurrency assertion therefore runs as separate processes (`TestInstallHelperProcess`), matching the pattern in `internal/transaction/subprocess_test.go`.
- FINDING: three fixture facts in the accepted `stage_test.go` modelled states the product cannot reach — a fabricated `linux/amd64` target on a darwin/arm64 host, cache hits with an invented `ArtifactPath` and no receipt, and a cache that could not publish. Staging tolerated all three; the commit phase legitimately rejects them. Fixtures corrected without weakening an assertion.
- EVIDENCE: `go build`, `go vet`, `gofmt -l`, `go test ./... -count=1`, and `go test -race` over `install`/`transaction`/`managerlock` all exit 0. `golangci-lint run ./...` exits 1 with 45 issues — **the same 45, with identical per-linter counts, as the pre-change composed base** (`gate-lint-baseline.log`); the 3 issues this task did add were fixed. Net zero new lint issues; the 45 are inherited from `buildcache`, `buildsource`, `snapshot`, `runtimestore`, `gitignore`, `scopes`.
- SCOPE: 8 new files (~2290 lines incl. 736 of tests); modified `install.go`, `global.go`, `plan.go`, `adapters.go`, `envfiles.go`, `globalbins.go`, `consumers.go`, `runtimestore/targets.go`, `stage_test.go`.
- STATUS: handed to review. Darwin/arm64 only — no native Windows runtime evidence claimed.

### 1037 — One operation-private root per install; global scope gained the MCP and registry gates
- DECISION: an installation now opens exactly one ephemeral root, `curator-install-private-*` (`internal/install/private.go`). The dry-run closure workspace, the toolchain probe base, and the staging session base are all subdirectories of it. Replaces the separate `curator-dry-run-*` / `curator-global-dry-run-*` roots at `install.go:205` and `global.go:60`.
- FINDING: a literal "dry run creates only the toolchain probe root" is unreachable. Closure resolution must hand a real on-disk tree to `skillspec.Load`, `skillcheck.Validate`, `audit.Gate*`, `hashing.ContentSHA256`, and `buildsource.Validate` — the last exists to detect on-disk mutation and holds directory handles, so no virtual FS can back it. The tree can only come from `git archive`, and dry-run may not write the persistent snapshot cache (`TestDryRunTouchesNothing` asserts `<home>/cache` stays absent). The probe root cannot host it either: `Toolchain.Probe` runs after closure, and a closure with no build command never probes. The real choice is one ephemeral root versus two, not one versus none.
- FIX: `BuildDeps.resolve` takes the `privateRoot`; `goToolchain.Probe`/`Establish` allocate `go-probe-base-*`/`go-build-base-*` inside it. Defer order is `releasePrivateRoot` first, `releasePlan` second, so LIFO drops the staging before the root.
- FIX: `Global` was missing MCP verification and registry-attestation resolution entirely, so a machine-wide build could establish the toolchain, inspect the protected cache, and compile before either gate. Both now run in the project order — MCP after the dependency checks, registry after audit, both above `BuildDeps.resolve` — through the shared `mcpVerifier` helper so the two scopes cannot drift. Global's MCP surface is `{ProjectRoot: GlobalRoot(home), UserHome: userHome}`. Global markers carry the evidence instead of `nil, nil`.
- EVIDENCE: revert-to-prove on both fixes. Restoring the scratch roots fails the containment tests with `temporary roots during the dry run = [curator-dry-run-2191237997]`; removing the global gates yields `Status:ok` with `outcome=would-preflight-and-build`, a staged artifact, and `build-skill tag v1 ... installed` on a run whose MCP/registry callback returned an error.
- SCOPE: added `internal/install/private.go`, `private_test.go`; modified `builddeps.go`, `install.go`, `global.go`, `stage_test.go`. No cross-package product code touched.
- STATUS: cycle-4 rework handed to review; `go test ./...` green across 36 packages, `golangci-lint` clean in `internal/install`.

The task permits temporary mutation only inside the removed toolchain probe root; existing tests enumerate persistent paths but not all `TMPDIR` creations.

A global miss can therefore compile before those required gates.

### 1004 — Teardown split from the trust verdict; no drift can be reported after live mutation
- ROOT CAUSE: `godriver.Session.Close` conflated two responsibilities — re-fingerprinting the toolchain and removing the operation-private root. `install` deferred that bundled close across every live write, so a toolchain that drifted after the cycle-2 pre-handoff check surfaced as a `failed` install *after* the skill was already installed.
- FIX: extracted `removePrivateState` and added cleanup-only `godriver.Session.Release` (`internal/godriver/session.go:327`); `Close` keeps its old verify-then-remove behavior for `godriver.probe`. `BuildSession.Close` → `Release` and `goSession.Release` now goes through the driver's cleanup-only path (`internal/install/builddeps.go:31,182`). `BuildPlan.Close` → `Release` (`internal/install/plan.go:220`).
- FIX: `releasePlan` no longer touches `result.Status` — a private root that outlived its operation is a scope-prefixed message, not a verdict on state that already changed (`internal/install/plan.go:239`). Trust stays finalized by `BuildPlan.Verify` at the end of `stageBuilds`, before `OnStaged` and before phase 20 `scopes.RecordConsumer`.
- FINDING: the reviewer's suggested test shape ("release returns drift → assert no state changed") is unreachable after the fix, since release cannot produce a drift verdict at all. Asserted the complement instead: a fake session that fails *every* verification after the first must still finish `ok` with `verified == 1`. Any re-check reintroduced into teardown flips it.
- FINDING: mutation runs reproduce the old bug verbatim — `Status:failed`, `Errors:[toolchain tree changed during operation]`, in a result whose own messages already read `build-skill tag v1 ... installed`.
- SCOPE: `internal/install/{builddeps,plan,stage_test}.go`, `internal/godriver/{session,session_test}.go`. Cross-package touch into `godriver` (owned by `TASK-260720-6i3cya`/`1zntv0`, both `done`) is additive only; `TestToolchainMutationFailsRecheck` passes unchanged.
- EVIDENCE: `gofmt -l internal cmd`, `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...`, `go test -count=1 ./internal/install/`, `./internal/godriver/`, `./...` (36 packages), scoped `golangci-lint@v2.1.6` on both touched packages, and `git diff --check` all exit 0. Repo-wide lint still exits 1 on the same 45 pre-existing issues, none in the touched packages.
- STATUS: `TASK-260720-3itlly` handed back to review, cycle 3. Still uncommitted in `.temp/TASK-260720-1zntv0/worktree` at base `17804cea`.

### 0949 — Cycle-2 review found the release path still re-verifies after mutation
- REVIEW: `TASK-260720-3itlly` changes requested again. The new pre-handoff toolchain/source verification closes the cycle-1 races, and the independent build, vet, full tests, scoped lint, formatting, and diff gates pass.
- FINDING: `BuildSession.Close` is documented as cleanup-only, but `goSession.Close` still calls `godriver.Session.Close`, which runs `VerifyToolchain` again. Because `Project` and `Global` defer `releasePlan` across consumer/install/runtime/shim/adapter/GC mutations, a toolchain change after the new pre-handoff check is returned as a failed install only after live state changed.
- TEST GAP: `TestSessionReleaseFailureIsReported` checks only the late failed status/error. The preservation tests inject failure into pre-handoff `VerifyToolchain`; neither proves a release-time toolchain error preserves project/global live state.
- REQUIRED: make release genuinely cleanup-only (or otherwise eliminate post-mutation trust failures), retain private staged-output lifetime through handoff, and add project/global late-release regressions asserting all live surfaces remain unchanged.
