### 1122 — Trust finalization moved ahead of staged handoff
- FIX (R1): `BuildSession` gained `VerifyToolchain(ctx)`, split out of `Close`. `stageBuilds` calls `BuildPlan.Verify` as its last step, so the toolchain is re-fingerprinted through the last build child *before* `Options.OnStaged` and before phase 20 (`scopes.RecordConsumer`), the first persistent write. `godriver.Session.Close` still re-verifies, but it is now a backstop rather than the only check. `internal/install/builddeps.go:24`, `internal/install/plan.go:177`, `internal/install/stage.go:130`.
- FIX (R2): `planOne` wraps the protected-cache lookup in `buildsource.Token.Use`, so a reuse verdict is rechecked immediately before and after the decision; `BuildPlan.recheckSources` then rechecks every planned source once, lexically by node name, inside `Verify`. A raced lookup now fails inside read-only planning, and a source that moves after its own bracket is caught before handoff. `internal/install/plan.go:193,340`.
- FINDING: a naive "mutate the snapshot during `Inspect`" test does **not** discriminate the bracket — the final all-source recheck catches it anyway and the run still fails clean. The bracket is only observable through *where* the failure lands: with it, planning stops at that command (one inspection, zero planned builds, zero builder calls, error names `build-skill.alpha`); without it, a second command is planned and its miss is compiled first. The test asserts the former.
- FINDING: trust must be finalized even when nothing is compiled. A plan of pure cache hits establishes a session, skips the builder entirely, and previously reached live mutation with no re-verification at all. `TestCacheHitOnlyPlanStillFinalizesToolchainTrust` pins it.
- SCOPE: `internal/install/{builddeps,plan,stage}.go` + `stage_test.go`; comment-only touches in `{install,global}.go`. Five new regressions plus an ordering assertion inside `OnStaged` (verified exactly once, not yet closed).
- EVIDENCE: `gofmt -l`, `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...`, `go test -count=1 ./internal/install/`, `go test -count=1 ./...` (36 packages), scoped `golangci-lint@v2.1.6 run ./internal/install/...`, and `git diff --check` all exit 0. Repo-wide lint still exits 1 on the same 45 pre-existing issues outside `internal/install`. Both fixes were temporarily reverted to confirm the new tests fail without them.
- STATUS: `TASK-260720-3itlly` handed back to review, cycle 2. Still uncommitted in `.temp/TASK-260720-1zntv0/worktree` at base `17804cea`.

### 0938 — Read-only plan review found trust finalization after mutation
- REVIEW: `TASK-260720-3itlly` changes requested. The build plan and staging order are well covered and the full suite is green, but two trust failures can still be detected too late.
- FINDING: `Project` and `Global` defer `releasePlan` until function exit, after consumer/context/runtime/shim/adapter mutations. `BuildSession.Close` re-fingerprints the trusted toolchain, so a toolchain drift error is currently converted to a failed result only after live state changed. The existing release-failure test checks the status/error but not preservation of prior state.
- FINDING: build-source tokens are validated before cache lookup, but cache inspection is not bracketed by `Token.Use`, and `BuildPlan.Close` closes tokens without a final `Recheck`. A cache hit can therefore be selected from one source identity and then proceed to staging handoff/live materialization after the snapshot changes; misses likewise lack one final all-source recheck immediately before handoff.
- REQUIRED: finalize/revalidate toolchain and every frozen source before `OnStaged` or the first shared mutation, retain cleanup ownership separately, and add mutation-injection regressions proving the prior project/global install, consumer ledger, runtime, and live build cache remain unchanged.
- EVIDENCE: reviewer independently ran `git diff --check`, `go test -count=1 ./internal/install/...`, `go test -count=1 ./...`, `go vet ./...`, and scoped golangci-lint; all exited 0.

### 0920 — Install split into a read-only plan and an operation-private staging pass
- DECISION: `install.Project`/`install.Global` now run every gate — manifest, skill check, closure, collision, system, MCP, audit, registry, moved tag, toolchain, cache — before the first persistent write. Build planning is phase 17, private staging is 19, and the first mutation (`scopes.RecordConsumer`) is 20. `internal/install/plan.go`, `internal/install/stage.go`.
- DECISION: staging does not publish. `Staged` describes what a later atomic commit may install; `Options.OnStaged` is the seam `TASK-260720-2284br` takes over. Until it lands, a schema v6 build skill installs its context and stages its artifacts but exports no runnable command.
- FINDING: `godriver.Build` already stages inside `Session.operation`, so "delete staging on failure" needs no separate staging area — releasing the session removes every artifact staged earlier in the same pass. The manager only has to own the private *base* it hands to `godriver.Config.PrivateBase`. `internal/install/builddeps.go:143`.
- DECISION: that private base is an `os.MkdirTemp("")` root, not a path under the manager home. `godriver.validatePrivateBase` requires the base to already exist, so a home-relative base would make a dry run create persistent state — which the dry-run contract forbids. `internal/install/builddeps.go:126`.
- FINDING: `Session.Close` re-verifies the fingerprinted toolchain, so discarding its error hides drift behind a successful-looking install. `Project`/`Global` now use named returns and `releasePlan` reports the release failure. `internal/install/plan.go:196`.
- FINDING: `BuildOutcome` is deliberately a mirror of `buildcache.Result.DryRunOutcome()` rather than an independent enum — a plan must not be able to invent an outcome the read-only cache inspection did not report. `corrupt` and `unsupported` fail closed during planning; `would-rebuild-untrusted-cache` never exposes a reusable artifact path.
- SCOPE: new `internal/install/{builddeps,plan,stage}.go` + `stage_test.go`; modified `internal/install/{install,global}.go`. Narrow injected boundaries: toolchain, cache inspector, builder, clock, generation reader.
- EVIDENCE: `gofmt -l .`, `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...`, `go test -count=1 ./internal/install/`, `go test -count=1 ./...` (36 packages) and scoped `golangci-lint run ./internal/install/...` all exit 0. Repo-wide lint still exits 1 on 45 pre-existing issues in runtimestore/snapshot/buildcache/buildsource/scopes/gitignore, none in `internal/install`.
- ANOMALY: one combined `go test ./...` printed `testing: can't write …/testlog.txt: file too large` for `internal/godriver` *after* that package printed `PASS`. The package exits 0 alone, cached and uncached; it is a testlog-size artifact of its very large output, not a failure.
- STATUS: handed to review as `TASK-260720-3itlly`. Work is uncommitted in `.temp/TASK-260720-1zntv0/worktree` at base `17804cea`.

### 0912 — Portable go-v1 control installation moved to the manager parent
- ROOT CAUSE: review cycle 1 (R1) was right that the pre-worker failure boundary was structurally open. Native controls were applied *inside* the running worker, so on Windows `AssignProcessToJobObject` and the actual `PROC_THREAD_ATTRIBUTE_HANDLE_LIST` installation happened after launch, and on macOS `setsid`/`setrlimit` were first attempted in the already-running worker. A supported host could therefore emit `build_execution_control_unavailable` *after* the worker started, and evidence could report `applied` for a mechanism that had not been installed.
- DECISION: control ownership moved to the manager parent. New rule, enforced in code: only the parent, before worker execution, may emit `build_execution_control_unavailable`; once the worker runs, a mismatch between installed and observed is `build_execution_capability_evidence_invalid`, and a refused process creation is a bounded child result. `rawWorker.expectFailure` fails any test whose worker-side rejection is the mandatory-control code, so the invariant is checked on every worker rejection scenario.
- FINDING: Windows needs a suspended launch. The parent creates the Job Object first, starts the worker with `CREATE_SUSPENDED`, `OpenProcess` + `AssignProcessToJobObject`, then resumes every thread via `CreateToolhelp32Snapshot`/`Thread32First`/`OpenThread`/`ResumeThread` (x/sys/windows has all of them; `IsProcessInJob` does not exist there, so the worker confirms with `QueryInformationJobObject(0, …)` which queries the caller's own job). An install failure terminates the worker while it is still suspended, so it never executes an instruction. `controls_windows.go`.
- FINDING: macOS has no post-`Start` install step at all once you use the fork boundary. `SysProcAttr.Setsid` makes the kernel create the private session between fork and exec, and lowering `RLIMIT_FSIZE` in the manager across the fork (under a mutex, restored immediately) gives the worker and every descendant the bound by inheritance. Both failures therefore surface before the worker exists. `controls_darwin.go`.
- FINDING: probes now perform the *exact* operation of the operation — the real Job Object with this build's `ActiveProcessLimit`/memory limits, and a real `setrlimit` to this build's byte bound followed by a restore — rather than a generic capability check.
- FIX (R2): `runGo` dereferenced `command.ProcessState` on any `Run` error, but it is nil when process creation fails. Now nil-safe: a refusal sets `StartFailed`, `ExitCode = -1`, and the parent classifies it as the existing `go_list_failed`/`go_build_failed`. `Started` is documented as "times the single permitted creation site was used", so a refused attempt still consumes one and the exact-count process-graph guard is unchanged. A launcher identity failure now ends the session with its own stable code instead of a result with `Started == 0` that the parent misreported as a graph violation.
- FINDING: a real, portable injection for the nil-`ProcessState` path is a manager-owned working directory that is inside the frozen snapshot but does not exist — the OS refuses `chdir` on both macOS and Windows and leaves no process state. On Windows the Job Object `ActiveProcessLimit = 1` additionally drives the same refusal through a complete `Build`, which is the closest thing to the scenario the review described.
- ANOMALY: `cmd … & echo %ERRORLEVEL%` over ssh expands at parse time and always printed `0`. Every Windows gate reading taken that way was discarded and re-run with runtime `&& echo EXIT_0 || echo EXIT_NONZERO`. Related: `set VAR=1 && …` in `cmd.exe` keeps the trailing space, so `CURATOR_REAL_GO_BUILD_TEST` was `"1 "` and the real-build test silently skipped; the quoted `set "VAR=1"` form fixes it. Both are traps for any future native Windows evidence run.
- EVIDENCE: local macOS arm64 build/vet/gofmt/full/`-race`/rc.5/rc.4/real-build/scoped-lint all exit 0. macOS amd64 (`ssh relux`) and Windows amd64 (`ssh win`) verified byte-identical (143 `cmd`+`internal` Go files, per-file SHA-256 diff clean) with build, vet, task packages under the rc.5 root, and the real vendored build all exit 0. Windows `go test ./...` still exits non-zero in the same five pre-existing packages outside this task.
- STATUS: handed to review.

### 0740 — Curator implements portable manager-worker-v1 go-v1
- MILESTONE: `internal/godriver` now runs every source-aware Go command inside one identity-verified hidden-mode re-execution of the manager (`cmd/curator` `__curator-go-worker-v1`), with a nonce-bound list → parent graph validation → HMAC build permit → build state machine. The declarative `HostPolicy` adapter the earlier review rejected is deleted; its environment/argv/root checks now live in the worker where they govern a real process.
- DECISION: dropped the old `source_not_readonly` permission-bit gate. rc.5 defines the portable source mechanism as frozen snapshot + identity re-verification; a permission-bit rejection would be an extra containment claim the portable profile may not make.
- FINDING: `RLIMIT_FSIZE` is genuinely applied and inherited. Tests that set `FileBytes` to 64–1024 bytes failed with `unexpected EOF` on the stub's own log and `go_build_failed` instead of `artifact_size_limit` — the kernel truncated writes inside the Go child. Positive proof the control is real, not declared. `controls_darwin.go:85`.
- FINDING: teardown order matters. The process group must be killed **before** `cmd.Wait()` reaps the worker; after reaping, the pid is reusable and `kill(-pid)` could hit an unrelated group. `workerclient.go` drains the response pipe to EOF (worker exit closes it without reaping), then kills, then waits.
- FIX: `filepath.IsAbs("/etc/passwd")` is false on Windows, so a rooted toolchain symlink was classified `toolchain_link_dangling` instead of `toolchain_link_absolute`. Added explicit leading `/` and `\` checks. `fingerprint.go:83`. First native Windows run of this package exposed it.
- FINDING: Go's `os/exec` on Windows always installs `PROC_THREAD_ATTRIBUTE_HANDLE_LIST` (`syscall/exec_windows.go:374`), so the inventory's `explicit-handle-inheritance-list` mechanism is what the standard library already does — the worker only has to add no handle.
- DECISION: worker tests drive a real subprocess. `TestMain` gives the test binary the same hidden mode the manager has, and `testdata/stubgo` is a real compiled launcher inside a fingerprintable fake `GOROOT`. Consequence: measured `internal/godriver` coverage reads 72.1 % because worker-side statements execute out of process and are not attributed to the parent profile.
- EVIDENCE: portable cache key reproduces `sha256:529370122ae11e2e961d5265b1a020e046bcd43165b2eb96b05e73a51187ac9b`; canonical receipt is byte-identical to the accepted `schema-cases/build-receipt-v1/valid.json`. 222 cases pass under the rc.5 root. Green on macOS arm64 (incl. `-race`), macOS amd64, and Windows amd64 — all three with a real Go 1.25.5 vendored build that is verified and never started.
- ANOMALY: first native Windows full-suite run exposed pre-existing gaps in packages this task does not own — `buildcache` (`owner does not match the effective user` on temp roots), `buildsource` (Windows rename/path semantics), `globalbins` and `runtimestore` (`script command is not executable`, POSIX exec-bit assumption), `shell` (PowerShell hook). All pass on both macOS hosts. Reported, not fixed.
- NOTE: Linux is out of `rc5-native-control-inventory-v1`, so the driver rejects there with `build_execution_control_unavailable` before the worker. Linux coverage is compile/vet/test-link plus that rejection path only; no Linux host was used.
- STATUS: handed to review.

### 0549 — Portable execution policy rework: absolute enforcement text reconciled, native-control vocabulary closed
- ROOT CAUSE: the first amendment added the portable deferral without touching the pre-existing absolute wording. `protocol/core.md` §4.2, `profiles/manager.md` §2.2, and `SECURITY.md` still said only the fingerprinted graph may start and source is read-only to every child, while the new §4.2.1 deferred kernel read-only and exact allowlisting and forbade rejecting on their absence. Two incompatible conforming-manager readings.
- FIX: every portable rule now states the mechanism and, separately, the kernel guarantee it is not — a normative table in `protocol/core.md` §4.2.1, mirrored in `SECURITY.md`, `decisions/0006`, and `docs/portable-go-execution-policy.md`. `network: "none"` is defined in §9.1 as fixed offline Go module/proxy/checksum-db/VCS configuration, not network denial.
- DECISION: exactly one failure boundary, stated identically in five documents and enforced by the vector's new `failure_boundary` section — a missing mandatory portable control rejects before the worker; an unavailable inventory control or any of the six deferred guarantees never rejects, warns, or blocks publication.
- FIX: "every native control the host actually provides" replaced by the exhaustive versioned `rc5-native-control-inventory-v1` inventory (5 controls, closed per-platform `{availability, mechanism, unavailable_reason}` records) and the closed `capability-evidence-v1` record (exact fields, states, `pre-worker-launch` probe timing, per-operation probe scope, result-only exposure, 8 consistency rules).
- DECISION: extending the inventory is a specification revision with a new inventory version, NOT an execution-policy revision — inventory membership never enters a build input, artifact, or hashed identity. Both versions are pinned in `release/1.0.0-rc.5.json` and gated by `tools/release_gate.py`.
- FINDING: Go's `json.Decoder.UseNumber()` makes `entry_count != float64(1)` silently misclassify every valid case. Compare decoded numbers as text in `main_test.go`, or the oracle inverts.
- SCOPE: `conformance/v1/vectors/go-host-execution-policy.json` (5 new sections), `tools/generate-vectors/main.go` + `main_test.go`, `tools/validate.py` (3 new validators), `tools/test_validate.py` (13 → 40 mutations), `tools/release_gate.py` + tests, `protocol/core.md`, `profiles/manager.md`, `SECURITY.md`, `decisions/0004`/`0006`, `docs/portable-go-execution-policy.md`, `cli/curator.md`, `conformance/README.md`, `COMPATIBILITY.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- NOTE: candidate pin moved `sha256:bfe49f25...` → `sha256:58f8d229...`. All CCJ-1 oracles the reviewer independently recomputed (portable/hardened/pre-revision keys, receipt-v2 key and hash) are byte-identical. Suite still 42 schemas / 422 files.
- ANOMALY: the reviewed worktree carried two untracked scratch artifacts — a 4 MB `generate-vectors` binary at the root and `tools/__pycache__` — which had been committed into the previous clean release probe. Removed before the final probe; the probe now holds 514 tracked product files only.
- STATUS: handed to review again; no manager implementation exists, so inventory availability is a normative specification statement, not measured platform evidence.

### 0506 — go-v1 execution policy: portable manager-worker-v1, hardened deferred
- DECISION: curator-spec rc.5 `go-v1`/`go-repository-v1` now declare a named execution policy, `manager-worker-v1`, implemented on macOS and Windows. Replaces the earlier `TASK-260720-1zntv0` proposal of hardened-Linux-only real builds with `host_profile_unsupported` everywhere else.
- DECISION: rejected shipping hardened-Linux-only — it would withhold the entire compiled-skill feature from the two platforms this release targets in exchange for a guarantee no supported host currently offers. Recorded in `decisions/0006-portable-manager-worker-execution.md`.
- FINDING: six hardened guarantees (total network denial, read-only source/toolchain, private-build-root-only writes, hard aggregate descendant bounds, exact executable allowlisting, fail-closed preflight) are named in the spec and attributed to `STORY-260728-327soo`. Their absence never rejects a portable build; recording them as applied is an error.
- FINDING: host capability evidence is deliberately NOT a cache input. Putting it in the key would fragment portable cache identity per machine without adding any guarantee a reader may rely on.
- FINDING: binding `execution_policy` into `goBuildPolicyV1` necessarily changes every `go-v1` logical cache key, so the rc.4 receipt example `93217cf1...` could not stay byte-frozen. Handled by moving it out of `TestRC4CompiledArtifactsRemainByteFrozen` into a dedicated non-alias guard that pins the old digest as negative evidence.
- FINDING: marker v2 keeps its frozen rc.4 record shape. Its execution-policy binding is transitive through the recorded cache key and `receipt_sha256`, which is sufficient — a record written under one execution contract cannot validate against another.
- SCOPE: curator-spec `protocol/core.md` §§4.2/4.2.1/4.2.2/9.1/9.2/9.3/10/12.3, `profiles/manager.md` §§2.2/2.2.1/2.4/11.8, `SECURITY.md`, `decisions/0004`–`0006`, `schemas/v1/common.schema.json`, `conformance-claim-v3.schema.json`, new vector `conformance/v1/vectors/go-host-execution-policy.json`, `docs/portable-go-execution-policy.md`.
- NOTE: rc.5 candidate pin moved from `sha256:33fd7aed...` to `sha256:bfe49f25...`. Suite grew 411 → 422 files. Candidate still emits zero claim-v3 tuples.
- STATUS: handed to review as `TASK-260728-zb2s4z`; no manager implementation exists yet, so native macOS/Windows qualification stays downstream.

### 2026-07-30 — Build-source Windows identity rework after post-landing CI
- DEVELOPER REWORK: `TASK-260720-3c0ss2` uses the existing CocoaSkills task worktree at signed, published base `2734beff1a0c93d725c00b1c66ef6ad22c3a780a`; the published commit was not amended and the candidate remains unstaged/uncommitted for re-review.
- ROOT CAUSE: Windows `os.DirEntry.stat()` intentionally reports zero `st_dev`/`st_ino`, so comparing its cached record with physical `os.lstat()`/`os.fstat()` identities rejected every ordinary file as mutated. NTFS also interprets `bad:name` as a named data stream and exposes only base entry `bad` to directory enumeration.
- FIX: the path scanner now records every descendant with no-follow `os.lstat()` while retaining the existing open/read/after-read physical identity checks. A Windows-only boundary enumerates `$DATA` streams with `FindFirstStreamW`/`FindNextStreamW`, accepts only the unnamed default stream, and fails closed on named streams or unexpected enumeration errors for the root and every descendant.
- REGRESSION: a platform-independent test prevents reuse of cached `DirEntry` metadata; a Win32 API seam proves default-stream filtering and handle closure; the native NTFS case proves a hidden named stream is rejected. The pre-fix physical-stat regression exited 1 as expected; the corrected source-boundary suite exited 0 with 19 passed / 1 Windows-only skip.
- LOCAL GATES: accepted conformance/task-focused pytest exited 0 with 198 passed / 1 Windows-only skip; full accepted-root pytest exited 0 with 707 passed / 2 platform skips; strict mypy exited 0 over 58 source files; build, Twine, wheel/sdist inventory, compileall, and diff hygiene all exited 0.
- WINDOWS FOLLOW-UP: origin CI run `30500306639` is the red baseline at published commit `2734beff`. The repository exposes only `push(main)` and `pull_request(main)` CI triggers, while the operator directive requires the new fix commit only after re-review. Native origin Windows validation therefore follows review-authorized commit publication; no temporary commit, branch, PR, SSH session, or unrelated remote mutation was fabricated.

### 2026-07-30 — Protected Windows cache review finds late hard-link admission and unsafe inherited mutation rights
- REVIEWER: `TASK-260720-8nxlgx` run `RUN-260730-e46794` on CocoaSkills base `15860e3f309888845b9271a257fb95f7c2825b56`; exact local/Windows candidate hashes matched. No CocoaSkills product source or repository test file was modified.
- GREEN BASELINE: local focused POSIX/Windows cache tests passed 42 / skipped 16; full pytest passed 853 / skipped 74; strict mypy passed 65 files; task Ruff passed. The exact native Windows snapshot passed all 16 focused cases and isolated win32 mypy.
- HARD-LINK RACE: deterministic native hooks created hard links immediately after the receipt/artifact byte-verification helpers returned and immediately before publication-source final validation. Lookup still returned `hit` for multiply linked receipt and artifact files, and publication returned `published` for a multiply linked source because final handle/path revalidation checks identity and DACL but not `number_of_links`.
- DACL INHERITANCE: a manager-home ACE granting Everyone `(OI)(CI)(IO)F` was accepted as a `miss`. `INHERIT_ONLY_ACE` is ineffective on the home itself but becomes effective on newly created children; `os.mkdir` creates cache roots without an explicit security descriptor before the backend seals them. The resulting creation window violates the trusted-principal-only mutation boundary.
- VERDICT: changes requested; route `to-dev`. Evidence and the executable native probe are attached as `TASK-260720-8nxlgx_review-verdict_RUN-260730-e46794.md` and `TASK-260720-8nxlgx_reviewer-late-hardlink-probe.py`.
- REWORK RESOLUTION: final file validation now queries `FILE_STANDARD_INFO.NumberOfLinks` on both the retained receipt/artifact handle and the independently reopened selected path after identity, DACL, and attribute checks. Publication applies the same post-security query to the retained source and selected source path at every final stability boundary. Late links therefore produce an untrusted lookup or `cache_publication_invalid` without returning candidate bytes or accepting publication.
- INHERITANCE RESOLUTION: untrusted mutating allow ACEs are rejected when effective on the manager home or inheritable through either `OBJECT_INHERIT_ACE` or `CONTAINER_INHERIT_ACE`, including `INHERIT_ONLY_ACE`; inherit-only manager ACEs no longer count as effective manager-home grants.
- REWORK EVIDENCE: exact hashes `cache.py=28b977d4…`, `cache_windows.py=43a413ab…`, and `test_build_cache_windows.py=611aefdc…` matched the native host. Native Windows passed 23 focused tests, 790 full-suite tests with 144 skips, and isolated strict win32 mypy. Host gates passed 43 focused tests with 22 skips, 854 full-suite tests with 80 skips, strict mypy over 65 source files, Ruff, compileall, build, Twine, and diff hygiene.

### 2026-07-31 — BUG-260731-3a5q1p rc.6 multi-project dry-run binding: review changes requested
- REVIEW: `BUG-260731-3a5q1p` PR 14 head `d345420` on base `main` `3a047d5`. Independent reproduction on darwin/arm64 with Go 1.25.5 over fresh `git archive` checkouts, rc.6 root `b07ef1d51dc6ebcc04cf59a20375d191bf82f6bb` (tip of published `release/v1.0.0-rc.6`): `main` + rc.6 fails with `published dry-run scope "multi-project" has no executable binding`; PR head + rc.6 passes all three dry-run cases and `go test ./internal/install/...` exits 0 (install 406.7 s, atomicity 365.5 s); PR head + the rc.3 `SPEC_PIN` still exits 0. Both `default:` branches remain fatal and the new case is not skipped, so the AC's no-weakening constraint holds.
- ANOMALY — NEW CROSS-PLATFORM COUPLING: the multi-project binding drives the real `goToolchain`, so `internal/install` now depends on a working trusted-GOROOT probe on every runner. When `godriver.Probe` cannot resolve the trusted root, `planBuilds` emits `toolchain-unavailable`, which rc.6 `reported_build_outcomes` does not admit. Reproduced directly by running the compiled test binary against the rc.6 root with an unresolvable `GOROOT`: `dryrun_conformance_test.go:291: project 0 reported build outcome "toolchain-unavailable"`. That is exactly the windows-latest condition recorded in `BUG-260731-fs3dht`, and `internal/install` is never platform-excluded, so advancing `SPEC_PIN` to rc.6 would turn Windows CI red on this test until `fs3dht` lands. The three-platform `candidate-conformance` lane that would have caught this was never dispatched for the branch, although rc.6 is an immutable published ref and sibling tasks dispatched the same lane the same day.
- SPEC READING CORRECTED: rc.6 `profiles/manager.md` §2.5 governs project *lock acquisition* order (implemented by `managerlock.AcquireProjects` via `CanonicalProjects`), not planning-iteration order. Production `install --all` iterates targets in alias order (`cmd/curator/main.go` `selectProjectTargets`, `sort.Strings(aliases)`), and a dry run takes no lock at all, so §2.5 imposes no ordering obligation on it. The test's canonical-identity ordering is a deterministic test choice, not production fidelity.
- NOT A REGRESSION: PR 14 and `main` fail the identical Windows required-case set (7 × `cmd/curator`, `internal/buildsource TestFrozenTokenRejectsRootReplacement`, `internal/install TestEndToEndInstall`, 3 × `internal/install/atomicity`), owned by `BUG-260731-27h1yc` and `BUG-260731-fs3dht`. Lint, Race, Interop, Naming, Gate self-test, and the ubuntu and macOS Test jobs are green.

### 2026-07-31 — BUG-260731-33v6zz Curator Windows lane unowned packages: ready for review
- SCOPE CLOSED: `BUG-260731-33v6zz` PR 13 head `c6cb2df` on `main`. All 14 owned failures are green on native `windows-latest` (run `30630564858`, artifact `test-evidence-windows-latest` id `8793545541`, parsed from raw `test/go-test.json`): `cmd/curator` 8→0, `internal/buildcache` 2→0, `internal/managerlock` 2→0, `internal/globalbins` 1→0, `internal/staging` 1→0. Each of the 14 named cases verified individually as `pass` — none `skip`, none absent.
- TWO PRODUCT DEFECTS THAT MADE WINDOWS UNUSABLE, not test-only issues. (1) `go-v1` refused the host's own GOROOT: the Actions tool cache publishes Go behind a **directory junction** (`attributes=0x410`, `reparseTag=0xa0000003` = `IO_REPARSE_TAG_MOUNT_POINT`). Since Go 1.23 a junction is a reparse-tag name surrogate, so `os.Lstat` reports `ModeIrregular` and `filepath.EvalSymlinks` — which follows only `ModeSymlink` — left it unresolved; `EvalSymlinks` also fails `ENOTDIR` for a junction mid-path, breaking `<junction>/bin/go.exe`. Now resolved through `physicalPath` (`GetFinalPathNameByHandle` on Windows). Stricter, not looser: the fingerprint and the `verifySelectedRoot` `os.SameFile` anchor now bind to the physical target, so retargeting the junction cannot move the trusted toolchain under a live session. (2) **No manager home was ever protected** — the home is made by `os.MkdirAll`, a Windows directory made that way inherits its parent's ACEs, and `ensureProtectedBase` requires an owner-only de-inherited DACL, so **no publication on Windows could ever succeed**. `internal/buildcache`'s own suite passed only because its fixture calls `protectTestHome` first.
- SECOND-ORDER DEFECT FROM THE FIX: hardening the home with a non-inheritable ACE gave every later child an **empty** DACL, making ordinary manager state unopenable by its own owner and surfacing as `Access is denied` on a manager lock, far from the cache. Container ACEs now propagate, file ACEs do not, and `validateWindowsDACL` accepts propagation flags while still rejecting `INHERITED_ACE`; an inherit-only ACE contributes no effective rights, so an inherit-only DACL remains a refusal. Boundary not weakened: `WRITE_DAC` is owner-only, the owner check runs first and is unchanged, reparse points are refused, and `TestWindowsPublicationRefusesAHomeOwnedByAnotherPrincipal` pins that another principal's home is still rejected rather than seized.
- ALSO: `internal/buildcache` create-then-protect TOCTOU closed by atomic `windows.CreateDirectory` with `SE_DACL_PROTECTED` (16 racers assert it); `internal/identity` rejected `C:\...` as a malformed network source (hunk byte-identical to PR 12 so both merge cleanly).
- NO LEDGER RELAXATION: `git diff origin/main -- .github/` is **empty**. `ledger-consistency: ok` (56 rows). The 4 `t.Skip` calls this branch adds are host-capability guards inside **new** Windows tests, and none fired on the runner — all three new tests ran and passed. The 2 `TestCompiledProjectStatusRepairRollbackRecovery` subtest skips are pre-existing on `main` (`cmd/curator/status_test.go:1098`, byte-identical message).
- ZERO REGRESSIONS: set-diff of failing cases base vs head shows no case that passed at base now failing. 17 went red→green — the 14 owned, `internal/runtimestore` (merged PR 10), and 2 `internal/install` dry-run cases the identity fix incidentally repaired.
- SCOPE CORRECTION CONFIRMED (raised by the `BUG-260731-27h1yc` reviewer): this bug's description claimed "None of the 14 appears in `.github/ci/platform-cases.tsv`". **False** — `platform-cases.tsv:149-155` carries 7 `cmd/curator` rows required on windows, identical on `main` and this head. This strengthens the result rather than weakening it: the platform-case gate now reports `ok` for all 7.
- ANOMALY — UNOWNED GAP THAT STILL BLOCKS THE STORY: `Test (windows-latest)` remains red at 74 cases, none in an owned package. `internal/install` 58 + `internal/install/atomicity` 8 + `internal/buildsource` 2 belong to `BUG-260731-27h1yc` (PR 12, in review). **`internal/transaction` (5) and `internal/godriver TestFingerprintReportsUnreadableDirectoryIdentically` (1) are unowned**: they were mapped to `BUG-260731-lepevi`, whose AC covered only the **Linux** lane; that bug is `done` and these survived it. The lane will **still be red after both PR 12 and PR 13 merge** until someone owns those 6. Needs its own board item.
- OWNERSHIP CROSSING, FLAGGED: `internal/godriver` was nominally out of scope but was touched, because 7 of the 8 `cmd/curator` failures were the GOROOT junction defect and only `cmd/curator` exercises the real `build.Default.GOROOT` path (`install` uses `fakeToolchain`, `godriver` injects `hostFacts`). `BUG-260731-lepevi` PR 11 has merged, this branch is rebased onto it, and PR 11 touched `controls_other.go` not `session.go`, so nothing collides. Called out in the PR body.

Local darwin/arm64 go1.25.5, each command standalone with its real exit code: `go build ./...` 0, `go vet ./...` 0, `GOOS=windows go build ./...` 0, `GOOS=windows go vet ./...` 0, `go test ./internal/{buildcache,globalbins,managerlock,staging}/...` 0, `no-broad-suppression.sh` 0.
- ANOMALY — LOCAL TIMEOUT, NOT A DEFECT: the first local `go test ./cmd/curator/...` hit the **default 600s package timeout** (real exit 1) at host load average ~19 with 11 competing build processes. macOS CI passes the same package in 475s and `ci.yml` uses `-timeout 30m` with an in-file comment calling it "headroom for a slower hosted" runner. Rerun with `-timeout 40m`: **exit 0 in 1219s**. `golangci-lint` is not installed on this host, so lint was not reproduced locally and rests on the green pinned CI job.

A CI runner cannot create a foreign-owned directory without elevation, so the gap is understandable — the name should match what is proven.

Mitigated on three counts: on unix the helper is a plain `filepath.Join` so those hosts lose nothing, `identity_windows_test.go` asserts the folding rule *behaviorally* through case-alias contention, and the branch adds an independent alias-twin assertion.
- REVIEW OBSERVATION — FIRST-RUN ASYMMETRY: only `ensureProtectedBase` repairs the manager home; `openProtectedEntry` does not. A cold `Inspect` on a never-published-into Windows home therefore reports `UntrustedProvenance` rather than a plain miss until the first publication self-heals it. Strictly better than the prior state (nothing on Windows worked at all) and both classifications lead to a rebuild — but establishing the boundary where the home is created (`managerlock.prepare`) would be the tidier home for it.

### 2026-08-22 — TASK-260822-1l4r4f decision 0008 open questions resolved (revision 2)
- CORRECTION TO A PRIOR PASS, VENDOR-SOURCED: revision 1 of this task's analysis marked `descendant-exec-denial` **available on Windows** via the all-or-none child-process creation policy, and recorded as an open maintainer item that the Windows attribute had not been checked against vendor documentation. It has now been checked. Microsoft's `UpdateProcThreadAttribute` reference states `PROCESS_CREATION_CHILD_PROCESS_RESTRICTED` "is only effective in sandboxed applications (such as AppContainer) which ensure privileged process handles are not accessible to the process" and is bypassable given `PROCESS_CREATE_PROCESS`/`PROCESS_VM_WRITE` handle access. A curator-launched interpreter runs as the ordinary user, so the control is **unavailable on Windows** (`child-process-policy-requires-appcontainer`) and only reachable through the decision-0007 verified-provider path. Reporting it `applied` would have been exactly the false claim decision 0006 forbids.
- CASCADE FROM THAT CORRECTION: with macOS also unable to provide it unprivileged (deprecated `sandbox_init`/`sandbox-exec`; App Sandbox is entitlement- and packaging-dependent), a two-platform script inventory would have `descendant-exec-denial` `unavailable` on **every** column at birth — which decision 0006 forbids, since unmet guarantees live in the deferred list, not the inventory. Resolution: the script inventory ships a **Linux column** (`macos`/`linux`/`windows`), where Landlock verifiably provides execute-right and write restriction unprivileged under `no_new_privs` since 5.13, inherited by every descendant. `rc5-native-control-inventory-v1` and `go-v1`'s macOS/Windows scope stay byte-frozen.
- NEW EVIDENCE-MODEL EXTENSION PROPOSED: rc5 asserts availability as a fixed normative per-platform value that the probe confirms. That cannot express Landlock, cgroup-v2 delegation, or unprivileged netns, which vary between hosts of the same platform — Ubuntu 23.10+/24.04 default to `kernel.apparmor_restrict_unprivileged_userns=1`, denying capabilities inside an unprivileged user namespace and so blocking netns on the exact `ubuntu-latest` lane the suite runs on. A third availability state `host-conditional` (inventory says "may provide", probe decides) is confined to the new `script-capability-evidence-v1` record version; no existing bytes change.
- Q3 STRENGTHENED, NOT CHANGED: host globs stay reporting-only. Added the kernel-side half of the argument — Landlock's network support restricts TCP bind/connect **ports** (ABI 4) and UDP (ABI 10), never hosts, and a netns is total denial rather than host filtering. So no supported host provides unprivileged host-granular filtering in any form, which is the exact test that places it in the deferred list rather than the inventory.
- ANOMALY — SILENT PRIOR ARTIFACT: this task's board status was `backlog` while a complete 44 KB `TASK-260822-1l4r4f_analysis.md` already existed on the board from an earlier spawn (`RUN-260822-178366` / `RUN-260822-9f1145`, both spawn logs 0 bytes). The producer wrote its outcome resource but never transitioned status or handed off. A `resource add` on a re-run fails with "resource already exists" rather than clobbering — check for an existing artifact before assuming a re-run starts clean, and use `resource update` to supersede.
- UNRESOLVED, HANDED TO THE PROSE TASKS: Job Object nesting on Windows (can `active-process-count-limit` be applied inside a job the manager did not create?) is unconfirmed and blocks that mechanism string becoming normative; cgroup-v2 delegation is unverified and is therefore mapped `host-conditional`.
- EVIDENCE: `TASK-260822-1l4r4f_analysis.md` (board outcome resource), `.research/260822_decision-0008-open-questions.md`.

- VERDICT: **accepted** (`RUN-260822-378a01`, read-only). AC met — all five decision-0008 open questions answered with one recommendation each, anchored rationale, and rejected options. - CORRECTION — LEDGER CLAIM 21 OVERSTATES THE VERSION RANGE: the analysis asserts "Ubuntu **23.10+**/24.04 default to `kernel.apparmor_restrict_unprivileged_userns=1`" and marks it verified. Its own cited blog says the opposite for 23.10 — the restriction shipped **opt-in** on release day. **24.04 LTS is where the default lands**, and Canonical's 24.04 material confirms the substantive half precisely: unprivileged user namespaces are allowed for all applications but "access to any additional permissions within the namespace are denied". No recommendation changes (`ubuntu-latest` is 24.04, so `network-isolation-domain` stays `host-conditional`) — but narrow the range before `TASK-260822-3fkfmf` or `TASK-260822-f4qv7w` cites it. General trap: a claim can be right about the mechanism and wrong about which release turned it on.
- UNFLAGGED FOURTH CONFLICT WITH 0008: the analysis lists three conflicts the prose must resolve rather than inherit (F1 schema default, F2 evidence record, F5 Linux cell) — but Q3 is a fourth. Decision 0008 §3 says kernel-grade host filtering "**enters the native inventory per platform**"; Q3 recommends no host-filtering inventory entry at all. Q3 is right on the merits; the divergence still belongs in the conflict list, or `TASK-260822-1f533i` inherits it silently and a maintainer diffing prose against the accepted decision finds it unannounced.
- CONFORMANCE-GATE MISMATCH TO SETTLE IN PROSE, NOT AT GATE TIME: the analysis routes every `host-conditional` control to platform-case class `host-capability`, but `.github/ci/platform-cases.tsv` defines that class as "the runner **filesystem** cannot create the artefact the vector needs (symlink, hard link, reparse point, FIFO, non-UTF-8 name)". A missing Landlock/cgroup/netns kernel feature is not that, and `platform-case-gate.sh` checks the class against the reason text the test actually printed. `TASK-260822-f4qv7w` needs a widened class or a new one (e.g. `host-kernel-feature`).
- EVIDENCE: `TASK-260822-1l4r4f_review-verdict.md` (board outcome resource).

### 2026-08-22 — TASK-260822-27bvo4 reviewer verdict cycle 2: ACCEPTED, and how to prove a ledger row actually bites
- VERDICT: **accepted** (`RUN-260822-abf447`, read-only) on `.temp/TASK-260822-27bvo4/RUN-260822-507162`, branch `task/TASK-260822-27bvo4-symlink-launcher-507162`, base `6a9b201`, 4 modified files + 2 new test files, 95 insertions. Both cycle-1 gate-wiring findings closed; the product fix (`identity.go:56` → `physicalPath(absolute)`) and the test set were accepted in cycle 1 and are unchanged.
- TECHNIQUE WORTH REUSING — `make ledger-check` green does **not** mean a `platform-cases.tsv` row protects anything. `ledger-consistency.sh` only proves the named case is *declared* in that GOOS's build; whether the gate would actually go red is a separate question. Prove it by mutating the captured `go test -json` stream and re-running `platform-case-gate.sh` on the mutant: (a) `sed` the case name to something else → must fail `required case never ran on <goos>`; (b) rewrite one subtest's `pass` event into an `output` reason line + a `skip` event → must fail `ledger case skipped where the ledger does not tolerate it`. Both mutations here behaved correctly, which is what closed the finding. Gotcha: the gate's awk `jstr()` parses `"Key":` with no space, so synthetic events must be emitted with `json.dumps(..., separators=(',', ':'))` or the gate silently ignores them and the mutation looks like a pass.
- A `must_run_on=X / skip_allowed_on=X` ROW IS NOT AUTOMATICALLY A LOOPHOLE. Cycle 1 asked for `skip_allowed_on=-` on the new rows; the rework used `windows` + `host-capability` and that is the correct call. Four of the five shared identity cases skip at the **parent** level on a Windows host without `SeCreateSymbolicLinkPrivilege`, so `-` would have re-created exactly the CI-fatal condition finding 1 was about. The shape is already repo convention (two `internal/buildcache` reparse rows, one `internal/scopes`). The enforcement that matters comes from the row existing at all (rename/delete fails by name) plus the `Parent/*` rows, which make a **darwin** subtest skip fatal while tolerating the privilege-dependent Windows one.
- PRE-EXISTING RED, ISOLATED BY CONTROL RUN: `platform-case-gate.sh` exits 1 on both platforms for `TestCandidateGoV1SourceAwareContract` (skips `root-unset`, row tolerates `root-content`) because `CURATOR_CONFORMANCE_ROOT` is unset locally. Running the same Windows stream against the **base** ledger from `HEAD` reproduces the identical single failure — that control is what separates "my change broke the gate" from "this gate cannot be green off-CI", and is cheap enough to always run.
- NATIVE WINDOWS (`ssh win`, DESKTOP-3PBO632, go1.25.5 windows/amd64): full `internal/godriver` suite exit 0, 297 pass / 0 fail / 17 skip, both junction cases passing with **zero** skips among the seven identity cases; darwin 296 pass / 0 fail / 18 skip, likewise zero. `go test ./...` not run — data volume at 99%, and earlier runs on this task died with `no space left on device` at full-suite parallelism.
- MINOR, LEFT OPEN: `identity_test.go:62` declares `skipWin bool` in the launch-shape table struct; nothing sets or reads it — dead field left over from merging the two implementation lanes, lint-clean, worth a one-line sweep when the file is next touched.
- EVIDENCE: `TASK-260822-27bvo4_review-RUN-abf447.md` plus five gate logs (board outcome resources); local logs `curator/.temp/TASK-260822-27bvo4/review-abf447/`.

### 2026-08-23 — TASK-260822-4p3dcq docs-build-ssh-surface: reviewer verdict ACCEPTED, and what a docs review actually has to re-run
- VERDICT: **accepted** (`RUN-260822-3dc3b7`, read-only) on `.temp/TASK-260822-4p3dcq/worktree`, branch `task/TASK-260822-4p3dcq-docs-build-ssh`, base `origin/main` `6a9b201`. Delta charged to the task is two files: `docs/build-ssh.md` (new, 494 lines) and one `README.md` bullet. The rest of the branch is the accepted `96m5pj`+`2505vo`+`3pkc80`+`b0wg3a` chain carried staged.
- TECHNIQUE THAT MADE THE REVIEW WORTH ANYTHING — for a docs page whose AC is "examples match actual CLI output", reading the page against the source is not enough. Build the binary from the producer's own worktree (`go build -o review-curator ./cmd/curator`), point `CURATOR_CONFIG` at a throwaway config, and **replay every transcript**. All of them matched byte-for-byte including exit codes: the four `add` lines, `replaced` vs `added`, the sorted tab-separated `list`, the empty-listing stderr line at exit 0, both `remove` cases, both refusals at exit 2, the three `-build-ssh-*` help lines, and all seven fail-closed parse messages regenerated from my own malformed configs. The page's `build_ssh` JSON block is literally what the scratch config holds after the four adds.
- GOTCHA WHEN REPLAYING `curator config build-ssh`: it needs an existing config file. On a host with **no** config at all every subcommand exits 1 with `global config not found: <path>`, not the documented `curator: no build_ssh scopes are configured`. Seed a minimal `{"schema_version":1,"skills_root":"~/.curator/skills","projects":{}}` first or you will misread the surface as broken.
- THE PRODUCER CORRECTED A REVIEW FINDING INSTEAD OF TRANSCRIBING IT, AND WAS RIGHT. `b0wg3a` finding 3 said a repository namespace can contain characters `scopeSegmentRE` rejects (citing `identifiers.PortableComponent`), so the suggested default scope must widen to the host. Re-derived independently: `PortableComponent` governs *skill* sources; build repos gate SSH raw paths through `validRepositoryPath`/`sshPathRE` (`internal/buildrepo/buildrepo.go:124,151`), which is exactly the `scopeSegmentRE` alphabet — so the **path** half cannot diverge on the only transport `build_ssh` selects for. The **host** half does: `hostRE` (`buildrepo.go:36`) admits `git.example.com.`, `git..example.com`, `git-.example.com`, which `scopeHostRE` (`internal/config/buildssh.go:49`) rejects — and widening to the host does **not** rescue it, because the host is not a valid scope either. Such a repository is coverable only by `--build-ssh-*` or `CURATOR_BUILD_SSH_*`. General lesson: a checklist item quoting a symbol name is a hypothesis, not a fact; check which package the symbol actually governs.
- SPEC DRIFT THE PAGE FLAGS, CONFIRMED AT FIRST HAND: `profiles/manager.md §11.3` on `curator-spec` `main` says the authentication tail "is exactly either" the identity-only or the agent-only form — **two** tails. The pinned-agent third tail this repo implements (`internal/buildrepo/admission.go:168`, accepted in `6a9b201`) exists only on the unmerged branch `spec/pinned-agent-authentication-tail`, head `38232d3`. Writing that into the page rather than presenting three tails as settled protocol is the right call and is exactly the kind of thing a docs page normally gets wrong silently.
- ANOMALY FOR A FOLLOW-UP ON THE CODE, NOT THE PAGE: the "agent requested but `SSH_AUTH_SOCK` is not set" failure is **coded on one path and uncoded on the other**. Run-wide `--build-ssh-agent auto` fails through `runWideCredentials` (`internal/install/buildssh.go:274`) with the stable `build_repository_ssh_credential_missing` prefix; a configured `"agent": true` scope in the same situation fails through `scopeCredentials` (`buildssh.go:314`) with a bare message that `matchScope` wraps, carrying no code at all. Same operator condition, two diagnostics, only one machine-readable.
- "DOCS BUILD/LINT GREEN" HAS NO GATE IN THIS REPO AND THE PRODUCER SAID SO. There is no markdown linter, no link checker, no docs build step: `docs/` holds two files and no index, no `make docs`, no docs job in `ci.yml`. The AC was met by the gates that do exist and apply to prose — naming gate, the no-em-dash/guillemet rule (`CONTRIBUTING.md:29`, `docs/implementation-plan.md:44`), and an explicit link/anchor pass — all re-run by the reviewer at 0 misses. Declaring the absent gate beats claiming a green one.
- `CHANGELOG.md` `Unreleased` IS EMPTY AND THIS WHOLE OPERATOR-CREDENTIAL SURFACE LANDS WITHOUT AN ENTRY. Deliberately **not** charged to this task: the file has not been touched since 0.12.5 (2026-07-14) while schema 7, the seamless manager lifecycle and the pinned-agent tail all landed without entries, there is no CI check, and `CONTRIBUTING.md` does not require it. A story- or repo-level decision, not docs rework.
- GATES RE-RUN BY THE REVIEWER: `go build ./...` 0; `gofmt -l .` clean; `golangci-lint run ./...` `0 issues.`; naming gate clean; own link/anchor script over four markdown files 0 misses incl. all ten in-page TOC anchors; `go test -count=1 -timeout 30m ./internal/config/ ./internal/install/... ./internal/buildrepo/ ./cmd/curator/` all `ok`, EXIT=0 (`cmd/curator` alone 376s — the 30m timeout is mandatory here).
- EVIDENCE: `TASK-260822-4p3dcq_review.md`, `TASK-260822-4p3dcq_review-gotest.log` (board outcome resources); local scratch `curator/.temp/TASK-260822-4p3dcq/`.

### 0046 — TASK-260822-3nvx91: module-root prose and shared Schema 8 handoff

- NUMBERING COORDINATION: decision 0009 shares manifest Schema 8 with decision 0008. The local `go-v1` branch now reaches a new closed `$defs.buildCommandV8`; no Schema 9 was created, and schemas/case directories 1-7 remain byte-unchanged.
- LOAD-BEARING GO FORMAT DETAIL: `vendor/modules.txt` does distinguish versioned-left from unversioned-left directory replacements, but only through a second annotation. For `replace example.com/lib => ../lib`, Go writes both `# example.com/lib v0.0.0 => ../lib` (selection metadata) and `# example.com/lib => ../lib` (the unversioned directive). For `replace example.com/lib v0.0.0 => ../lib`, it writes only the first line. Treating every `=>` line as the same directive would silently accept the forbidden versioned-left form. Protocol Core 4.2.3 now reconciles the two annotations and rejects an unmatched two-token left side.
- GENERATOR GOTCHA: `go run ./tools/generate-vectors -root .` rewrites current outputs but does not delete stale case files. A red iteration that temporarily added legacy schema case names left those files in the suite and polluted `manifest.json` until they were explicitly removed and the generator rerun. Do not infer that regeneration prunes removed cases.
- GATES: validator exit 0 (52 schemas, 686 vector files); Python unit suite exit 0 (95 tests); Go tool suite exit 0; gofmt and `git diff --check` exit 0; lychee exit 0 (40 OK, 0 errors); deterministic second regeneration proved by identical full generated-tree SHA-256 `6175eeb18e6a1f4885ac82114a96421a4e7115e0a7e6af379ecffeeafca58544` before/after.

### 0057 — TASK-260825-2fy132: HTTPS credential operator documentation

- DOCUMENTATION BOUNDARY: HTTPS selection is resolved for the whole planned closure before its first repository fetch, but an uncovered HTTPS repository remains anonymous. The delivered surface has no install-time HTTPS credential discovery or interactive candidate prompt; only explicitly configured sources and the `login` command can select a token. The operator page states this boundary instead of borrowing the SSH prompt behaviour.
- SECURITY: `CURATOR_BUILD_HTTPS_TOKEN` without `CURATOR_BUILD_HTTPS_HOST` is identity-unbound and can reach every HTTPS build-repository host in the run. The same plain-language warning is present in the operator page and the CHANGELOG entry, with `Spec core §12.2` cited.
- EVIDENCE: delivery-checkout `go run ./cmd/curator config build-https --help`, `add`, and replacement transcripts all exited 0; primary-checkout `make lint` exited 0. The isolated docs worktree lacks the untracked `agents/skills/skill-go-testing-tools/tuitestkit` Go replacement, so its `make lint` exited 2 before linting the changed documentation.
- CORRECTION TO THE DOCUMENTATION BOUNDARY: the previous boundary incorrectly
  denied the shipped terminal candidate prompt. Before the first fetch,
  an operator-terminal run offers every uncovered HTTPS repository the
  presence-only detected Git host credential (when available) or entry of a
  token now. The operator explicitly selects a candidate and then either
  persists its scope or uses it for this run only; abort stops the run. Only
  headless, non-terminal, and dry-run runs leave an uncovered repository
  anonymous. The operator page now states the shipped behaviour.
